> **⚠️ Maintain this file.** Whenever the architecture, crate layout, stack, conventions, or current phase meaningfully changes, update the relevant section here in the same session — this file is the durable source of truth loaded every session. Keep it accurate; prune anything that becomes wrong.

## What this is

**Rustroom** is an open-source alternative to Adobe Lightroom (Classic model): a non-destructive RAW photo editor. **Linux first**, then Windows/macOS from one codebase.

- Ambition: a **serious, usable** tool on a focused feature set — not a toy, not a full LR clone.
- Focus order: **Develop (editing) first, Library (catalog/DAM) later.**
- **Reuse proven libraries** (libraw, LittleCMS, lensfun) rather than reimplementing decoding/color science.

## Stack

| Concern | Choice |
|---|---|
| Language | **Rust** (Cargo workspace) |
| RAW decode | **libraw** via bindings (swappable; `rawler` is the pure-Rust alt) |
| Color management | **LittleCMS** (`lcms2`) |
| Lens corrections | **lensfun** (later phase) |
| CPU parallelism | **`rayon`** |
| Image export I/O | **`image`** + jpeg/tiff/png crates |
| GUI | **`egui` + `wgpu`** (Slint reconsidered later) |
| Catalog DB (later) | **SQLite** via `rusqlite` |
| Edit persistence | sidecar **JSON** now → catalog DB later |

**Why Rust over C++/Qt:** cross-platform single codebase, native perf w/o GC, memory/thread safety, and — the deciding factor — `wgpu` is one GPU compute API across Vulkan/Metal/DX12/GL, making the later CPU→GPU port share one abstraction.

## Core architecture (the load-bearing decisions)

1. **Non-destructive.** Originals are never modified. An edit = a parameterized entry in an ordered **op-list**. Rendering replays the op-list. This gives history, virtual copies, and presets almost for free.
2. **Scene-referred, linear, float32 working space.** Math in linear light; display/output transform applied only at the end.
3. **Staged pipeline behind a `PipelineStage` trait.** Each stage is an independent, unit-testable `apply(image, params) -> image`. A stage's CPU impl can later be swapped for a `wgpu` compute shader **without changing callers.**
4. **Interactivity via previews.** Edit downscaled previews for instant feedback; render full-res only on export / 1:1 zoom.

Pipeline order:
```
decode → linearize/black-white → white balance → demosaic →
input color profile → working space (linear f32) →
geometry (crop/straighten) → noise reduction → exposure →
tone (highlights/shadows/curve) → color (HSL/grading) →
vignette → sharpen → output transform → encode
```

**Geometry runs first**, not last, so every adjustment acts on the final cropped pixels —
which is also what makes the vignette post-crop for free, with no separate mode. (Lens
corrections will join it there when `lensfun` lands.)

**NR early, sharpening late** — the two halves of "detail" belong at opposite ends. Denoising
the source is far easier than denoising after Exposure/Shadows/the tone curve have amplified
the grain, and it prevents HSL chroma speckle instead of smoothing it after the fact.
Sharpening is last because it must not amplify anything a later stage would then stretch.

## Planned crate layout (Cargo workspace)

```
opensource_lightroom/
├── Cargo.toml                # [workspace]
├── CLAUDE.md                 # this file
├── crates/
│   ├── core-image/           # ✅ ImageBuf (f32 RGB) + Capture (EXIF facts, both decoders) + map_samples/map_pixels/map_pixels_xy/to_rgb8/to_rgba8 (rayon-parallel) /crop/downscale_to + Thumbnail
│   ├── raw-decode/           # ✅ libraw(rsraw); DecodeOptions{linear_scene,display,preview(half_size),demosaic}; Demosaic tiers -> user_qual; extract_thumbnail
│   ├── image-decode/         # ✅ JPEG/PNG/TIFF → linear ImageBuf (image + srgb_to_linear) + thumbnail; EXIF orientation applied in both
│   ├── color/                # ✅ sRGB transfer fns + Oklab (lcms2/ICC still deferred)
│   ├── edit-model/           # ✅ serde Edits op-list (orientation/WB/exposure/contrast/tone/curve/saturation/hsl/local_contrast/dehaze/NR/sharpening/vignette/geometry) + EditGroups partition + merge_from
│   ├── pipeline/             # ✅ PipelineStage + 14 stages (incl. geometry, noise_reduction, dehaze, saturation, hsl, local_contrast, vignette, sharpening) + spatial::{box_blur, gaussian_blur, wide_blur, min_filter, guided_filter} + build_pipeline / build_pipeline_split(_in_region) / build_prefix_steps + PrefixStep/PrefixStepKey/PrefixKey + Region + tail_kernel_radius + golden tests
│   ├── export/               # ✅ to_rgb8 + save (JPEG/PNG/TIFF) + metadata::{ExportMetadata, FileMetadata, MetadataScope} — the full XMP packet (M6.2) + watermark + output_sharpen
│   ├── xmp/                  # ✅ stem.xmp read / merge-write: rating, label, keywords, title/caption/rights/creator (M6.3 + M6.2); zero GUI, zero catalog
│   ├── rustroom/             # ✅ CLI crate; binary `rustroom` — `decode` (dump) + `render [--edits]` (RAW)
│   ├── app/                  # ✅ GUI `Rustroom` (eframe/egui 0.35 + wgpu). ⚠ main.rs is 681 lines after W0 (2026-09-01); RustroomApp's methods live in session_flow/sources/smart_sources/library_state/catalog_ops/copies/snapshots/transfer/undo/export_flow/settings_apply/interaction/decode. lib=spot_tool/spot_source/visualize/grade_view/history/action_log/edit_history/selection/crop_tool/histogram/render_engine/thumb_loader/thumb_cache/thumb_window/metadata_scanner/date_tree/rule/rule_form/library_filter{RatingSet,FlagSet,LabelSet}/decode_worker/buffer_cache/view_region/full_res/format_filter/presets/export_presets/safe_name/settings/edit_clipboard/sidecar/sync/batch_export/photo_id/entries/file_rename; bin-only modules=theme/modals/name_edit/develop{mod,panel,canvas}/library/filmstrip/thumb/shell (egui lives here, not in the lib)
│   └── catalog/              # ✅ scan_folder → RAW+JPEG/PNG/TIFF entries (+ mtime); SortOrder/sort (name/modified/edited, case-insensitive, name tie-break); is_raw/is_supported (in-memory; SQLite DAM later) + Description per file (v7, M6.2)
├── docs/
└── tests/                    # integration + golden reference images
```

Key rule: **`pipeline`, `color`, `export` have zero GUI dependency** and are driven headless by `cli` and tests.

## Conventions & engineering discipline

- **CI on Linux from day one:** `cargo build`, `cargo test`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt --check` must stay green.
- **Golden-image regression tests** (established at plan milestone M1.2): render a fixed RAW with a fixed op-list, assert output matches a checked-in reference within tolerance. Never abandon these — they make the GPU port safe.
- **Keep crates small and single-purpose.** A file growing large signals it's doing too much.
- **⚠⚠ DECIDE WHERE NEW CODE GOES *BEFORE* WRITING IT — never dump into `main.rs` and leave the split for later** (standing instruction, 2026-09-01). Every oversized file in this repo got that way one reasonable-seeming addition at a time; `main.rs` reached **4,375 lines** and `canvas.rs` **1,890** exactly like that. An agent that adds 300 lines to `main.rs` has not finished its task, it has moved work onto whoever splits it next. **The full placement table is in `docs/PARALLEL-WORK.md` §5**; the short form:
  - **Crate first, and it is a hard rule.** `pipeline`/`color`/`export`/`core-image`/`edit-model`/`catalog`/decoders have **zero GUI dependency**. `crates/app/src/lib.rs` and its modules have **no egui** — that is where unit-testable logic lives. egui belongs only in the bin modules (`theme`, `modals`, `develop`, `library`, `filmstrip`, `thumb`, `shell`, `rail`).
  - **A stage** → `pipeline/src/stages/<name>.rs`, one per file. **An edit parameter** → `edit-model`, *plus* its `EditGroups` group, `merge_from`'s destructure and the three exhaustive fixtures. **Logic behind a gesture** → a lib module taking plain types (the `crop_tool`/`mask_tool`/`panel_order` pattern); **the gesture itself** → the bin module.
  - **Two questions that catch most misplacements:** *can this be tested without opening a window?* (if yes and it is in a bin module, it is in the wrong place) and *would a second caller want this?* (if yes it is a lib module, not a private helper).
  - ⚠ **If a file you are editing already exceeds ~600 lines, say so in your report** rather than silently adding to it.
- **Color correctness is first-class** — validate against references early.
- **Design the GPU seam now, build it later.** Don't leak CPU-specific types into the pipeline interface.
- Avoid platform-specific assumptions (paths, file dialogs, GPU backend) so Win/macOS stay cheap.

> **✅ M7.4 IS DONE (2026-09-04), and the shape of the feature changed twice on the way.** Track C's detector was held out of W4 because it "put the guides at random". Two rounds fixed real faults in it and **neither made Auto usable**; the third round changed what the button is for. Specs `2026-09-03-detector-contrast-floor-design.md` and `2026-09-04-guide-refinement-design.md`.
>
> **⚠⚠ THE LESSON OF THE WHOLE MILESTONE: EDGE QUALITY AND EDGE SELECTION ARE DIFFERENT PROBLEMS, AND ONLY THE FIRST IS ALGORITHMIC.** The contrast floor and the contiguity rule both made the detector find *genuine* edges. Neither could make it know which edges are the **building**, because a Hough accumulator cannot tell a colonnade from a cliff face or a person's silhouette. That is semantic knowledge, and the only thing in the room that has it is the person looking at the photograph. **Two rounds were spent improving quality before that was recognised.**
>
> - **Round 1 — an absolute contrast floor on display-encoded luminance.** ⚠⚠ An absolute floor on **linear** luminance would be meaningless: 0.01 in shadow is a visible edge, the same 0.01 near white is nothing. **This project already had that exact bug on record in the Whites slider**, where perceptual thirds were read off linear luminance. So `verticals` display-encodes before the Sobel, after which `MIN_EDGE_LEVELS` is expressible in **8-bit levels across an edge**, divided by Sobel's step gain of 4 so it means what it says. ⚠ The encode lives **inside `detect::verticals`**, so a caller cannot hand it the wrong colour space — `LensCorrectionStage`'s reasoning for building its own `Corrector`.
> - **Round 2 — a candidate must have one CONTIGUOUS run of support.** `MIN_LENGTH_FRACTION` counted votes *anywhere* along the infinite line, so a candidate reached its threshold from fragments scattered over the frame. Measured, the winning lines had **24–41% of rows voting but a longest contiguous run of 7–13%** — statistically collinear noise, not edges. Hough finds candidates; a walk along each survivor now verifies them.
> - **Round 3 — Auto became a refinement of guides you draw.** `Auto` with no guides, **`Refine guides`** once any exist. Each near-vertical guide is replaced by the strongest real edge within `SNAP_RADIUS`, or **returned bit-identical** with the status line saying so.
>
> **⚠⚠ THE TWO CONSTANTS TRADE OFF, AND THAT MOVED THE FLOOR BY A FACTOR OF THREE.** `MIN_EDGE_LEVELS` was tuned to **24** while the length test still counted scattered votes — it was the only thing rejecting noise. With contiguity doing that job, **8** fires on 26% of 70 frames against 24's 29%, *and* recovers Weltenburg Abbey, whose stonework is faint under an overcast sky and which is lost above about 10. **A constant chosen to compensate for a different bug moves once that bug is fixed.**
>
> | levels | 2 | 4 | 6 | **8** | 12 | 16 | 24 | 32 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | fires, votes counted anywhere | 97% | 96% | 93% | 84% | 53% | 34% | 29% | 27% |
> | fires, run required contiguous | 54% | 37% | 29% | **26%** | 20% | 16% | 13% | 11% |
>
> **⚠⚠ `detect_overlay` IS THE DIAGNOSTIC THAT ENDED THE GUESSWORK, AND IT IS KEPT.** It writes the plane `detect::verticals` actually consumes as a PNG with the chosen lines drawn on it, which separates two completely different bugs: *the lines are wrong* against *the lines are right and mapped wrongly*. **Two rounds of reasoning had gone to the wrong cause; one picture settled it in a minute.** `--refine` additionally draws a guide as drawn (amber) against refined (green). ⚠ Reach for it before theorising about detector behaviour.
>
> ⚠ **Two hypotheses were tested and REJECTED rather than assumed**, which is why they are worth recording: the offset mapping is **not** aspect-broken (every fixture was square 256×256 while the real plane is 512×342, and this project has recorded an aspect bug four times — but a vertical line on a wide frame comes back where it was painted), and the floor is **not** what excluded the colonnade (8, 12 and 24 give Walhalla the identical pair).
>
> **⚠⚠ A GUIDE'S TWO X VALUES ARE ITS POSITIONS AT THE Y IT WAS DRAWN BETWEEN — and reading them as the frame's top and bottom is a bug that is INVISIBLE on a full-height guide.** For a short one they describe a different line, with its lean understated by the ratio of the extents: tracing the top fifth of an edge leaning 8° read as **1.7°**, so a perfectly-placed guide was rejected on divergence outside where it was drawn. Reported in use as refinement taking only one of two guides *and* redrawing them full-height. The gap is measured at the **drawn endpoints** now, which is also simpler — the drawn line's x at those y *is* `from[0]` and `to[0]` — and the refined guide keeps the y it was drawn between.
>
> ⚠ `SNAP_RADIUS` is **0.03 and measured, not asserted**: guides traced 1–3% off the true edge moved 0.011–0.027 of frame width across both known frames, while the competing wrong edge — Walhalla's sky line against its building corner — sits **0.16 away, five times the radius**. ⚠⚠ The first measurement showed the abbey never snapping and **the fault was the measurement**: probing a 16°-leaning edge with a straight *vertical* guide leaves a large gap at one end however well placed the line is. `--refine` takes `top:bottom:y0:y1` for that reason.
>
> **⚠⚠ FOUR SABOTAGES PASSED WHILE PROVING NOTHING IN THIS MILESTONE, AND THREE WERE THE FIXTURE'S FAULT RATHER THAN THE GUARD'S.** (1) **My own change disarmed the one test that could see the direction gate** — `a_gradient_sky_casts_no_votes` carried 0.64 levels per row, far below the new floor, so the floor rejected every pixel before the gate saw one and deleting the gate outright left all 13 tests green; it is a repeating ramp now, ~25 levels per row. (2) The direction gate *inside* the contiguity walk: gaps filled with Gaussian bands, but **a symmetric band has zero vertical derivative at its own centre**, so it bridged five rows of a twenty-one row gap. (3) The horizontal-guide check: a **full-width** horizontal guide sweeps x across the frame, so the radius rejects it whether or not the family check exists — the fixture is a short shallow stub now, the only shape where that check is load-bearing. (4) A metric sabotage that was **lenient rather than equivalent** to the old code. ⚠ **If a sabotage does not fail, suspect the fixture before concluding the guard is redundant.**
>
> ⚠ **And a test that passed against BOTH implementations**: the first fixture for the drawn-extent metric used a guide crossing a *vertical* edge, where the misreading only understates a lean that is near zero anyway. The discriminating case is a guide tracing part of a **leaning** edge — the ordinary one.
>
> ⚠ **What M7.4 does NOT deliver, stated rather than glossed:** Auto's own two picks are still often wrong — on both known frames one line lands on the building and the other on a cliff or a silhouette, which are real contiguous vertical edges no edge-quality rule can reject. **Auto stopped being an answer and became a suggestion**, and refinement is what makes a wrong suggestion cheap to fix. ⚠ The **rectangle-constrained re-detect** the user proposed is deliberately not built: it supplies roughly the same information as drawing a line roughly, at more interaction cost. Revisit if refinement proves insufficient.

Guide Mode was the exception for exactly this reason; there is no longer an exception.

> **✅ AND FOUR FOLLOW-UPS FROM USING IT (2026-09-03), all merged. 1853 → 1858 tests.** Every one was reported by eye, and none by a test — the thirteenth time this file records that about GUI work.
>
> - **The crop rectangle stays visible while the straighten mode is armed, and in Guide Mode**, drawn muted and non-interactive. It used to vanish outright, because the dispatch replaced `draw_crop_overlay` entirely as Guide Mode does — which left the composition guides dividing a rectangle nobody could see. ⚠ **Muted deliberately drops the dimmed surround** rather than lightening it: a straighten line is very often traced along a horizon that runs *outside* the crop, and darkening exactly that region fights the gesture the marks exist to support. ⚠ This is **not** the composition-guide rule, which stays — a guide dividing a rectangle nobody keeps is worse than none, and drawing the rectangle is a different thing from dividing it.
> - **The loupe is up whenever straighten or Guide Mode is active**, not only while dragging. Gated on a live drag, the one point you could not place precisely was the **first** one. `loupe::loupe_at` owns the rule so the two overlays cannot drift.
> - **The readout is a colour swatch plus a bare triple**, `■ 10 128 250`. The swatch does the job the `R`/`G`/`B` prefixes did badly — it says *which colour* at a glance — and costs less width. `Session.readout` stores the **value**, not a formatted string, so the swatch and the numbers are two renderings of one fact.
> - **⚠⚠ THE READOUT'S WOBBLE TOOK TWO ATTEMPTS, AND THE FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS THE WRONG SHAPE.** Reported as the readout jumping as the pointer moved. Padding each channel to three characters and drawing it **monospaced** was attempt one and was not enough — reported again as the swatch still moving when the **red** channel changed digit count while green and blue looked stable. ⚠ That is where a width error *surfaces*, not where it originates: the cluster is laid out **right-to-left**, so the label's right edge is pinned and any discrepancy accumulates **leftward**, into the swatch. The fix is to **reserve a width measured from the widest label that can occur**, which removes the dependency on font metrics altogether rather than assuming padded spaces and digits measure alike.
>
> ⚠ **Two egui facts, corrected by the compiler:** `Ui::fonts` hands out a shared `&Fonts`, but **both** `glyph_width` and `layout_no_wrap` need a **mutable** one to populate the glyph atlas. **`Painter::layout_no_wrap` takes `&self`** and does that internally — it is the one that works for measuring text.
>
> ⚠ **What no test here covers:** that the reserved width is actually applied, and that the label is drawn monospaced. `shell.rs` cannot be driven headlessly, because **`eframe::Frame` is not constructible in a test** — the tier-2 gap this file already records. The padding and `WIDEST_LABEL` have tests; the layout that consumes them does not.
>
> **⚠⚠ FIVE OF MY OWN SABOTAGES PASSED WHILE PROVING NOTHING, IN ONE SESSION, AND THEY NAME FIVE DISTINCT WAYS IT HAPPENS.** Worth more than the fixes: (1) run against **`--bin`** when the module lives in the **lib** — the filter matched eight unrelated tests and printed `ok`; (2) `cargo test "a\|b"` — cargo takes a **substring, not a regex**, so it matched nothing and printed `0 passed … ok`; (3) a `sed` whose pattern did not match, so no sabotage was applied; (4) a **malformed Python** whose write silently failed, leaving the file untouched; (5) the named test **did not exist**, because an earlier scripted edit's second replacement had silently no-opped. ⚠ Same root every time: **trusting that a scripted edit landed, and trusting a `test result: ok` line without checking what it ran.** After any scripted edit, assert the new text is present; after any sabotage, confirm the *named* test failed rather than that some suite passed.
>
> ⚠ **A sixth, of a different kind: two scripted edits spliced by computed index cut in the wrong place** — once duplicating a whole region, once landing on an inner brace. This file already records the `sed`-matched-nothing trap; this is its mirror, an edit that matches **too much**. Replace a block by its **exact literal text** and check it compiles.
>
> **⚠⚠ THE GREY-OUT WAS THE WRONG FIX TO A REAL PROBLEM, AND THE RIGHT ONE WAS ALREADY IN THE FILE.** A straighten line writes `geometry.angle`, one of the three values a guide solve owns, so drawn guides are left describing a solve that no longer holds — and `panel/geometry.rs`'s hand-edit check structurally **cannot** catch it, because it captures `solved_before` earlier in the *same* frame and an angle written by the canvas on the *previous* frame is already in `s.edits`. But thirty lines below sits the rule: *"editing by hand throws the guides away, because the solve no longer describes what you see."* A straighten line is a hand edit through another affordance. `straighten_overlay::settle` clears them and leaves the sliders alone, which is `Clear`'s own contract. ⚠ The gate was only ever chosen because track B was **barred from writing `Session.guides`** during W4; that ownership boundary ended at the merge.
>
> **⚠⚠ THE CROP RE-ANCHORING IS M3.1a's THREE-HEADED BUG AGAIN, AND IT BEHAVED THE SAME WAY: SABOTAGING ALL THREE, ONLY ONE WAS CAUGHT.** The heads are **placement** (`crop_screen_rect`), the **hit-test** normalisation, and the **drag deltas** — and only the deltas failed a test. Placement is invisible to any drag, because it only affects where the crop is *drawn*; the hit-test is invisible from the crop's **centre**, because a wrong normalisation still resolves to `Inside` and the drag still works. Each needed its own test: one asserting the **painted geometry**, one pressing an **edge** and checking the opposite edge stays put. ⚠ Two coordinates deliberately stay `img_rect` — the dimming and the clip — because they are about the painted texture rather than the frame.
>
> **⚠⚠ AND NONE OF IT IS TESTABLE AT FIT, WHICH IS WHY THE TESTS SEED A REGION.** `img_rect` and `frame_rect` are **bit-identical** whenever `rendered_region` is `None`, and no frame ever lands in the canvas harness — frames become textures in `shell.rs`, which the harness does not run. That is the same reason W4 track D's `frame_rect` sabotage came back **inert**. ⚠ The discriminator needs no absolute coordinates: **`frame_rect`'s on-screen size is independent of render resolution** because `pk` cancels against `pscale`, and `img_rect`'s is not — so the same drag must move the crop identically at two render resolutions, and the crop must be *drawn* in the same place.
>
> ⚠ **Found rather than assumed: a crop handle outside the painted slice cannot be grabbed at all.** The canvas allocates its response over `img_rect`, so with a region render on, a press outside the rendered slice is never delivered. That is a property of region rendering rather than of this change — you cannot interact with what is not drawn — but at high zoom the off-screen handles are unreachable until you pan to them.
>
> ⚠ **`loupe::loupe_at` owns one rule for both overlays** — the point a live gesture is placing, else the pointer while it is over the photograph — so the straighten and guide overlays cannot drift on it. `test_support.rs` already states why: "a helper duplicated per overlay is how the two would drift."
>
> **⚠⚠ CLIPPY CAUGHT WHAT A GREEN SUITE DID NOT, FOR THE EIGHTH RECORDED TIME, AND IT WAS REPORTED AS DONE BEFORE THE CHECK RAN.** The `meshes()` wrapper became dead code once every test moved to `meshes_hovering`, and `-D warnings` rejects that — 1853 tests passed while the build was failing. **Read the clippy exit code before claiming a gate is green**, and read it from a file, never through a pipe. ⚠ A second process note from the same round: **two scripted edits spliced by computed index cut in the wrong place**, once duplicating a whole region and once landing on an inner brace. This file already records the `sed`-matched-nothing trap; this is its mirror — an edit that matches **too much**. Replace a block by its exact literal text and check the result compiles.
>
> **🎯 W4 PARTLY LANDED (2026-09-03): B and D are merged into `main` fast-forward; C is HELD OUT and its branch kept.** **1815 → 1845 tests** (B +16, D +14 — the count matches exactly what the two tracks added, which is the assertion, not "still green"); **53 goldens byte-identical**, and 53 *named* golden tests confirmed to have **run** rather than being silently skipped; `fmt` / `clippy --all-targets --all-features -D warnings` / `test --all` all green **on the merged result**, which is the run that counts — a green run in each worktree is no evidence about the merge.

>
> **⚠⚠ B AND D ARE MERGED BUT *UNVERIFIED BY USE*, AND THIS PROJECT'S RECORD SAYS THAT IS EXACTLY WHERE ITS DEFECTS LIVE.** Neither agent could run its GUI checklist and neither can a session here: `AXIsProcessTrusted()` is **false**, so every synthetic mouse event is silently dropped (track B built a CGEvent driver in Swift and confirmed the cursor did not move), and `screencapture` returns `could not create image from display`. Granting Accessibility or Screen Recording is a system/security settings change an agent must not make. **The twenty checklist items in the two plans' final tasks are outstanding.** ⚠ The four that no test can cover at all: D's `img_rect`-vs-`frame_rect` behaviour at 1:1 while panning, D's numbers being **unchanged** under a Display P3 display space, D's readout still describing the photograph with Visualize Spots on, and both labels coexisting in the status cluster during an export.
>
> **⚠⚠ WHY C WAS HELD, WITH THE NUMBERS, BECAUSE THEY NAME A DESIGN FAULT RATHER THAN A TUNING ONE.** On Walhalla (DSCF6270) Auto wrote **Vertical −42.5, Straighten +7.75°** and left the columns leaning *left*: the two detected lines sat at x ≈ **0.68 and 0.85 — in the sky, not on the building** — near-parallel at +4.00° and +1.00°, and cleared `MIN_SEPARATION` 0.15 at **0.17**. So **the separation rule the spec introduced let through precisely the ill-conditioned pair `guides.rs` warns about**. On the cleanest converging-verticals frame available (Weltenburg Abbey, DSCF6296) it finds **0** and correctly reports "need two". Across **70 real RAWs** it fires on **54%**, and of those **47% ask for |Vertical| ≥ 50**; it fired on a blurred kitten close-up (+95.3), a flat wall poster (+88.9) and a tree trunk (−38.4). ⚠ **Root cause: every threshold is relative to the frame's own single strongest gradient**, which is simultaneously too permissive on soft frames and too strict on the abbey. The fix is an **absolute contrast floor**, and `MIN_SEPARATION` wants re-judging with it.
>
> ⚠ **C's one unambiguous win, and it is worth keeping: the lens step is real and is being applied.** Corrector max displacement on the 512 px analysis plane is **3.14 px** (XF23) to **15.08 px** (XF16-80 @ 80 mm), and guides differ ON vs OFF on **40 of 70** frames — on DSCF6270 decisively, +0.0 against −42.5. That was the decision no synthetic fixture could check, and `detect_probe` is what checks it.
>
> **⚠⚠ FOUR ERRORS IN THE PLANS, ALL FOUND BY EXECUTING THEM, ALL NOW RECORDED IN THE PLAN FILES.** Three in C's: the Hough code used gradient direction to **pick** the angle bin where the spec says **gate** (a noisy per-pixel estimate scatters one line across a dozen bins — it recovered **0°** for a line drawn at 7.1°); `OFFSET_BINS` spanned the half-diagonal, too small for a 30° line through a corner, silently dropping exactly the frames the wide window existed for; and the fixtures were hard-painted **staircases**, whose risers are genuine short verticals, which is why the out-of-window fixture "detected" one. One in B's: Task 3 kept a stale `StraightenDrag` enum from a pre-edit draft while Task 2 specified `-> bool` — the scripted-edit trap this file already records, landing on what was matched and not on what surrounded it. ⚠ **C also needed a sixth constant the plan did not anticipate**: a **relative** threshold structurally cannot say *"there is no vertical in this photograph"*, so a landscape always returned two lines. `MIN_LENGTH_FRACTION` counts **voting pixels**, not summed magnitude, because thresholding magnitude conflates length with contrast.
>
> **⚠⚠ TWO MORE INSTANCES OF A TEST NAMING WHAT IT COULD NOT DETECT — the standing lesson, now at SEVENTEEN.** (1) C: deleting the **direction gate** left the whole suite green, because `MIN_LENGTH_FRACTION` was silently doing that work; `a_gradient_sky_casts_no_votes` — a smooth vertical luminance ramp, strong vertical gradient everywhere, no lines at all — is what fails against it. (2) B: with `shown_geo` at its default, `from_frame`/`to_frame` are the **identity** and `shown_angle + a == a`, so *storing screen points instead of raw ones* and *dropping the `shown_angle +`* were **both invisible**; the fix was to the **test**, seeding a photo already straightened 5°, which is also the real workflow — never to the tolerance.
>
> ⚠ **Three sabotages were genuinely INERT and were reported as such rather than counted as passes**, which is worth more than a clean-looking table. B's `±45` clamp is **structurally unreachable** — each family's angle is strictly inside ±45 by construction, so no input can reach it; kept as defence in depth with the test's doc comment saying so. D's `frame_rect`-for-`img_rect` swap is inert **in the harness** because with `place_region == None` the two rects are bit-identical, diverging only during a region render at a native rung the harness never reaches. And C's skip-the-lens-step sabotage cannot be seen by any synthetic fixture, since none carries a lens profile.
>
> ⚠ **Two user-facing questions W4 raised and did not settle.** (1) **While B's straighten mode is armed the crop rectangle disappears** — outline, brackets and dimming — because the dispatch replaces `draw_crop_overlay` entirely, exactly as Guide Mode does; it lasts one gesture since the mode auto-disarms, but the *composition* guides still draw inside the crop rect, so the two are briefly inconsistent. **Lightroom keeps the crop rect visible.** (2) **D's label reads `R 10  G 128  B 250`** — the spec fixed the units and not the wording.
>
> ⚠ **Two corrections to the specs, from measurement.** (1) D's spec said "~27 MB at a native rung"; **retention does not scale with the photo**, because a native rung always requests a *region* — the visible rect plus `REGION_MARGIN` — so it is bounded by the **canvas viewport**. The fit figure is measured at **6.52 MiB**; the native one is not, since it needs a window. (2) In the sRGB case — every session where the OS colour-manages, i.e. macOS — `rgba` and `srgb` are **one shared allocation** (`Arc::ptr_eq` asserted), so retaining the readout bytes adds nothing while the frame is alive.
>

> **▶⚠⚠ THE PLAN WAS REWRITTEN ON 2026-09-01. PHASES 5 AND 6 ARE NO LONGER GPU AND RELEASE — THEY ARE NOW 8 AND 9.** The roadmap was re-planned after assessing the app **as a photographer** rather than as a codebase, and the finding reordered everything: **Rustroom is an excellent renderer wearing half a photo editor.** It was built inside-out — the imaging core is more rigorous than most shipping alternatives, and every gap is where a photographer actually touches the software. Accelerating an application that cannot yet produce a named, credited file is the wrong next problem, so three phases now sit in front of the GPU port:
>
> - **Phase 5 — Credibility.** M5.1 tone curve · M5.2 white balance · M5.3 spot removal · M5.4 a photo that looks right on open · M5.5 crop guides · M5.6 slider legibility.
> - **Phase 6 — Delivery & interchange.** M6.1 export that produces a deliverable · M6.2 metadata · M6.3 XMP · M6.4 soft proofing · M6.5 culling throughput.
> - **Phase 7 — Depth.** M7.1 range masks · M7.2 masked spatial adjustments · M7.3 interpolation quality · M7.4 Hough verticals · M7.5 creative LUTs · M7.6 tool gaps.
> - **Phase 8 — Performance** (cheap wins, then `wgpu`). **Phase 9 — Immich, Windows/Linux, contributor docs, `v0.1`, plugins.**

> **✅ EVERYTHING BELOW IS MERGED TO `main` AS OF 2026-09-01 — 1454 tests, 50 goldens.**
>
> **✅ M7.3 RESAMPLING IS DONE.** The kernel is a **`Settings` choice** — Bilinear / Mitchell / Catmull-Rom / Lanczos3, **defaulting to Catmull-Rom** — plus `rustroom render --resampler`. ⚠ **Both samplers honour it**: `geometry::sample_bilinear` (straighten *and* perspective) and `lens_correction::sample_channel`, which is a **separate per-channel** sampler that TCA needs. Lens correction is on by default, so a typical RAW resamples twice and covering only the first would have been half a feature. ⚠ **Thumbnails deliberately do NOT follow it** (user's call): invisible at 512 px, and honouring it dropped the whole folder's cache — ~34 s on 830 photos — for nothing. ⚠ Its section is **"Resampling"** below; 6 of 49 goldens moved and `crop_center`/`orient_*`/`lens_vignetting` correctly did not.
>
> **✅ FOUR FIXES FROM USING THE APP (2026-09-01), and the first is the instructive one.**
> 1. **⚠⚠ A CROPPED PHOTO WENT SOFT ON EVERY SLIDER MOVE.** `render_max_dim` fell back to `fit_cap()` whenever `requested_region` was `None` — but **"no region" stopped implying "the fit rung"** when the fit clamp was removed. A crop makes the image *smaller*, so `fit` is larger, the ladder climbs to a native rung, and the whole cropped frame still fits on screen: the canvas stored rung 3200 with no region, and every panel-driven render then asked for 1600. ⚠ **The canvas could not correct it** — neither the rung nor the region had changed from its side — which is exactly why nudging the zoom fixed it until the next slider move. It returns `render_rung` now.
> 2. **The tone curve's endpoints use the Fritsch–Butland three-point tangent**, not the naive one-sided slope. With `m[0] = d[0]` a three-point curve leaves (0,0) along the **straight chord** to the dragged point, so the first segment is dead straight and all the curvature piles up at the point — reported as the ends feeling "wonky". ⚠ Two points still take the single secant, which is what keeps a two-point curve bit-for-bit the old straight line.
> 3. **Composition guides are a View feature**, drawn under **every** tool rather than only Crop, and greyed/thinned (0.8 over a 2.0 halo, 190 at alpha 150). ⚠ **Guide Mode draws none** — there the preview strips the crop and no crop rect is drawn, so a guide would divide a rectangle nobody keeps, and one that divides the wrong rectangle is worse than none because it gets trusted.
> 4. **Every export token carries an explanation**, `{copy}` the longest since it was the one reported as unclear.
>
> **✅ M6.0 COPY LABELS IS DONE (2026-09-01), merged to `main`.** A copy carries a **label** — short human text, no extension, never a path — instead of a name that doubled as its export filename. Its section is **"Copy labels"** below. **1422 tests.**

> **⚠⚠ W0 PROVED THE PARALLEL MODEL AND GAVE ONE REUSABLE RECIPE.** Two agents refactored 6,265 lines concurrently in separate worktrees with **zero merge conflicts** — from one constraint given to both: **type *definitions* stay put, only `impl` blocks and free functions move.** An inherent impl only has to be in the same *crate*, so ~90 methods left `main.rs` with `self` staying `self` and no call-site churn, while `develop/`'s `use crate::RustroomApp` kept resolving. ⚠ **When two tracks must touch related code, look for the constraint that makes their files disjoint rather than assuming they must be serialised.**

It is also the cheapest large win in the project, because the maths, the LUT, the goldens and the edit-group membership are all built and tested — **M5.1a adds no `Edits` field at all.**

Answering it once retires all four. **(2)** M6.2's blocker dissolves under a narrower scope: EXIF/IPTC editing was blocked on "modify the original" vs "a second XMP sidecar", and **writing metadata only into the exported file is neither** — which is what M6.1b needs anyway.

>
> **⚠⚠ M5.2's MIGRATION QUESTION IS CLOSED, AND TWO FINDINGS CAME OUT OF IT (2026-09-01).** The call was *convert the existing values to Kelvin* — and then all **49 copies** in the author's folders proved to store `{"temp": 0.0, "tint": 0.0}`. **Nothing has ever been converted because nothing has ever been set**, which is the strongest argument for the milestone: the sliders are unusable enough that they have never once been moved. ⚠ Had a value existed, converting it would **not** have been well-defined — the current gains are hand-chosen coefficients, not a blackbody model, so the old and new models trace different surfaces in gain space and only a nearest fit is possible.
>
> **⚠⚠ AND THE LARGER ONE: KELVIN IS A RAW-ONLY CONCEPT, WHICH RESHAPES M5.2.** A Kelvin value derives from the camera's **as-shot neutral**; a JPEG has none, so it has no meaningful Kelvin — and **15 of the 49 copies are non-RAW**. So M5.2 does not *replace* the relative model, it **adds an absolute one beside it**, which is Lightroom's answer too. ⚠ Two consequences to settle in the spec rather than discover: the same control reads in different units depending on the source, and **copy/paste and sync across a RAW→JPEG boundary have no defined meaning** — the very interoperability the milestone exists to fix does not cross it. Detail is in the roadmap's M5.2 entry.

> ⚠ **The GUI binary is now `Rustroom`, not `rustroom-gui`** — `cargo test -p app --bin Rustroom`. The name is what macOS shows as the application menu's title.

**M7.1c boolean mask operations — the PANEL AND CANVAS (2026-09-10). ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE** — *"everything works now, confirmed by use"*, after one correction found by using it: "Show mask" for every kind, below. Composites are authorable: mode chips, a component list, per-component handles, and a tint that shows the composite. 2201 → **2218 tests**, 63 goldens byte-identical throughout — this half touches no pixel path. Plan `2026-09-09-boolean-masks-ui.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE FIRST SABOTAGE WENT UNCAUGHT, AND THAT WAS THE MOST USEFUL RESULT OF THE HALF.** Forcing `mask_components::component_mut` to always resolve the base left **all 129 panel tests passing** — the shape controls' routing had *no coverage at all*, so a build whose list showed "Subtract Radial" selected while the sliders silently edited the base would have shipped looking plausible. `the_shape_controls_edit_the_selected_component_not_the_base` drags the real Feather slider through the real panel and asserts on the **refinement's** feather *and* on the base being unchanged; a test checking only "something moved" passes against exactly that bug. ⚠ The canvas has the identical hazard on the side this file's record says the defects live, so it got the identical guard **before** its sabotage was run — and that one fired on the new test alone.
- **⚠ `Session::component_index()` clamps at the READ**, rather than the plan's enumerate-every-reset-site. The mask list can shrink under a stale index by four routes — Remove all masks, a snapshot restore, a paste, a sync — and enumerating them is how one gets missed. Clamping means a missed site degrades to *the base is selected* rather than to the panel editing one shape while the canvas drags another.
- **⚠ The chips are `Add to` / `Subtract from` / `Intersect with`, disabled without a selection.** All five kind buttons already begin with "Add", so a bare `Add` chip says nothing about which is which — the ambiguity that made the lens Scale slider "Lens scale". Disabled rather than falling back to New, because a control whose meaning depends on invisible selection state is the class of thing this file records as biting; the add path still fails toward *add a mask*, recoverable in one click.
- **⚠⚠ THE TINT NOW COVERS A RANGE COMPONENT, WHICH REVERSES `paint_mask`'s SHIPPED REFUSAL — corrected in place, not deleted.** That refusal's argument was exact when written: *"the only pixels the canvas has are the rendered texture, which already has this mask's adjustment applied"*, so a tint would show the mask selecting the tones it had just moved. **Mask isolation removed the premise** — while the tint is shown, `suppressed_mask` holds this mask's adjustments out of the view, so the displayed frame **is** this mask's base. ⚠ Two residual error terms are named rather than glossed: the frame is 8-bit sRGB **after the display rendering and the creative LUT**, and **later masks are still in it**. The tint approximates the render rather than previewing it — `TintBase`'s existing caveat, now inherited by two more variants.
- **⚠ A lone brush takes the tint path it always did, byte for byte** (one `rasterize_in`, keeping its bbox). Every other shape goes through the full-grid plane, because an analytic or pixel-dependent component covers an area no stroke bbox bounds. `a_lone_brush_keeps_its_bounding_box` is what distinguishes the two paths — and it is about **cost**, not about whether anything is drawn.
- **⚠⚠ "SHOW MASK" IS DRAWN FOR EVERY KIND, AND THE COMPOSITE-ONLY VERSION WAS A BUG — reported in use (2026-09-10): *"a single radial mask does not show any edits when moving a slider, only after going e.g. to the edits field it appears."*** The cause was not the tint at all. `develop::body` suppresses **whichever mask is selected** while `show_mask_tint` is on — *whatever kind it is* — and that flag defaults **on**; so selecting a radial has always held its own adjustments out of the view, and the checkbox that turns it off was drawn only in the brush block. ⚠⚠ **That is this file's own accepted caveat (1) of mask isolation arriving exactly as it predicted** — *"a slider belonging to the shown mask does nothing visible … the caveat most likely to be reported as a bug; untick Show mask to see its effect"* — with **no reachable control to untick** for any non-brush mask. The rule is now one sentence with no exceptions: the checkbox is drawn for every selected mask, so the tint and the suppression are two faces of one control and cannot disagree about which masks they apply to.
- **⚠ A range mask tints NOTHING until a frame has landed, and that is correct rather than a gap.** Its coverage is a function of the photograph's own hue; with no frame the sample reads black, whose chroma is zero, and the chroma gate — which exists precisely so a near-neutral pixel is not selected on noise — zeroes it. Inventing a hue would be the silent-mask failure `analytic_coverage`'s `Option` exists to prevent. The same degradation an auto-masked brush already has before its first frame; pinned by `a_range_mask_with_no_frame_tints_nothing` rather than left to be rediscovered.
- **⚠ Copy/paste does NOT carry masks, and that is by design rather than a defect** — reported in use the same day. `EditGroups::default()` has `masks: false` beside `geometry` and `retouch`, on the reasoning recorded at M3.1a that a mask positioned for one composition is meaningless on another. The route is **Paste…** with Masks ticked, after which Ctrl+V remembers the choice. Left as it is pending the user's call; flipping the default is one line.
- **⚠⚠ THE SERIAL TINT PLANE WAS A DEFECT, NOT A CAVEAT, AND ONLY MEASURING FOUND IT.** At 1400×930, best of 5:

  | composite | sequential | parallel |
  |---|---|---|
  | + 1 radial | 13.0 ms | **3.6 ms** |
  | + 1 luminance | 28.1 ms | **7.8 ms** |
  | + 1 colour | **62.1 ms** | **14.1 ms** |
  | + 3 colour | **99.6 ms** | **20.9 ms** |

  62 ms/frame is worse than the ~36 ms once reported in use as *"responsive with the mask hidden, sluggish with it shown"* — and **the memo does not save the case that hurts**, because dragging a component's slider changes the shape every frame and so misses every frame. ⚠ The cause was structural: the pipeline's equivalent has always gone through `map_pixels_xy`, which is rayon-parallel over rows, and the tint plane was the only place doing the same arithmetic serially. ⚠⚠ **And the projection it replaced was 5× optimistic** — derived from the render's own per-component numbers without noticing those were measured on a *parallel* loop. The arithmetic was right; what it extrapolated from was not comparable. **Seventh cost estimate in this area corrected by measuring.**
- **⚠ `pipeline` re-exports `lightness` and `hue_chroma` by name rather than opening `stages`** — the hue LUT's rule — so the tint cannot invent a second hue convention that would disagree with the render everywhere.
- **⚠⚠ AND I DESTROYED UNCOMMITTED WORK WITH `git checkout` — the exact trap this file already records from the display-profile work.** Undoing a sabotage in `masks.rs` with `git checkout -- <file>` reverted the whole file and lost every one of that task's edits to it, where every other sabotage in the milestone had correctly restored from a `cp` backup. Rebuilding took one pass because the edit scripts were still on disk; nothing about that was luck. **Take the copy first, every time.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) ✅ confirmed by use, after **one** correction — and it is worth recording that the correction was **not** in the new code: the radial report was mask isolation's own accepted caveat, which this file had predicted in writing and which the composite-only gate made unreachable; (2) the tint approximates the render, with the two error terms above; (3) components are **append-only with no reorder** while order is observable; (4) **one level** — a composite cannot contain a composite; (5) `masks.rs` (~700) and `mask_overlay.rs` (~700) both grew and neither was split.

**M7.1c boolean mask operations — the HEADLESS half (2026-09-09). ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE with the half above; M7.1c IS CLOSED, and PHASE 7 HAS ONLY M7.3b LEFT IN IT.** A mask carries `refine: Vec<Refinement>` — shapes composited onto its base with **Add / Subtract / Intersect** — correct through the pipeline, the coverage cache and the prefix key, with **no GUI at all**. 2172 → **2201 tests**, 60 → **63 goldens** with the 60 byte-identical. Spec `2026-09-09-boolean-mask-ops-design.md`, plan `2026-09-09-boolean-masks-headless.md`. ⚠ **Plan 2 (`2026-09-09-boolean-masks-ui.md`) is outstanding** — a composite is currently reachable only by hand-editing a sidecar, the same deliberate cost M3.1a's and the brush's headless plans each paid.

- **⚠⚠ THE ALGEBRA IS THE BRUSH'S OWN, AND THE RASTER NOW CALLS IT.** `Add` is exactly `brush/raster.rs`'s across-strokes paint step and `Subtract` is exactly its `erase`; `Intersect` is the third member of that product algebra. `min`/`max` were rejected because they agree with these rules at 0 and 1 and differ at **every partial coverage** — subtract-between-masks would disagree with erase-within-a-brush, a second rule wearing the first one's name. The raster's two inline expressions are `MaskOp::combine` calls now, bit-identically, which the 60 unmoved goldens prove. ⚠ The trade, stated rather than discovered: the product rules are **not idempotent** (`0.5 ∩ 0.5 = 0.25`), which is the reading of *"both conditions hold"* under independence and the thing most likely to be reported as "the intersection is too weak".
- **⚠⚠ THE `is_inert` SHORT-CIRCUIT STOPPED BEING AN OPTIMISATION.** An inert base stays inert under `Subtract` and `Intersect` and **does not** under `Add`, where the added component *is* the whole mask. Dropping that clause fails the rule's unit test and the end-to-end carrier and **nothing else** — the render is simply the unmasked photograph, which looks like nothing rather than an error.
- **⚠⚠ `capture_projection` HAD TO CARRY THE REFINEMENTS, AND THE COMPILER FORCED IT.** It builds a `MaskedAdjustment` **struct literal with no `..`**, so the new field was an `E0063` right there. Without it a subtracted component would change what the masked NR/dehaze covers while the cached prefix checkpoint stayed valid. Its test gained a third direction that the four existing ones structurally cannot see — a refinement is neither a capture correction nor a tail adjustment.
- **⚠⚠ THE PLAN WAS WRONG ABOUT THE COVERAGE KEY, AND THE SABOTAGE STEP IS WHAT ESTABLISHED IT.** It specified a component index in `MaskCoverageKey`; forcing that to `(i, 0)` left **all 52 tests passing**, including the one written to catch exactly it. Nothing caught it because there was nothing to catch: the key already holds **the brush itself** plus geometry, frame size, region and base identity — the complete list of things coverage is a function of. Two components with identical brushes have identical coverage and *should* share an entry. Removed, with the reasoning at `key`. ⚠ **Usually a passing sabotage means the test is blind; here it meant the code was doing nothing.**
- **⚠ `base_id` is unchanged and now says why.** Every component of a mask reads the **same** base, because components change the composite's coverage and never its pixels. A component index there would be wrong rather than redundant. What *did* change: `BrushCoverage` is `[mask][component]`, `MaskStage` deposits under `(i, j)`, and `cap` counts components — otherwise a composite of three brushes makes its own siblings evict each other on every zoom change.
- **⚠⚠ MEASURED, AND IT OVERTURNED THIS DESIGN'S OWN PREDICTION IN TWO PLACES — the sixth cost estimate decomposition has corrected in this area.** X-T4, best of 5, against `1 masked exposure (gradient)`:

  | extra component | fit @1600 | region 1400×900 |
  |---|---|---|
  | + 1 radial | +3.7 ms | +2.2 ms |
  | + 1 luminance | **+7.1 ms** | +4.7 ms |
  | + 1 colour | +16.5 ms | +13.6 ms |
  | + 3 colour | +32.1 ms | +20.1 ms |
  | + 1 brush (uncached) | +12.5 ms | +13.5 ms |

  **(1) "A luminance component is free once the base facts are shared" was FALSE** — the base is a *gradient*, which computes no lightness, so the luminance component is the **first** consumer and has nothing to share with. **Sharing pays from the second pixel-dependent component onward, never the first**; "shared" is not "free". **(2) "Three colour components cost one Oklab round trip" is TRUE and the inference was wrong** — the counter proves one conversion, and the cost still climbs ~+7.8 ms per extra component, so `ColourRange::coverage_at` dominates rather than the conversion.
- **⚠ What the sharing IS worth, measured by disabling the memo rather than modelled:** `+3 colour` goes **50.2 → 65.2 ms at fit (saves 15.0, 23%)** and **30.7 → 45.3 ms at the region (saves 14.6, 32%)**, while `+1 colour` measures 34.6 against 35.0 — identical within noise, which is exactly what a memo with nothing to share must do and is what confirms the mechanism rather than a coincidence.
- **⚠⚠ THE SHARING IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY PIXEL TEST**, because three conversions render **bit-identically** and merely cost 3×. Its guard is a **thread-local** conversion counter — a counter rather than a timing, because a benchmark cannot fail a build, and thread-local because libtest runs tests in parallel and a shared `AtomicUsize` would race into a flaky-or-vacuous guard.
- **⚠ The goldens were SWEPT, not eyeballed.** At the planned +0.55 EV a left-to-right gradient put the strongest lift where the chart is already near 1.0 and `Add` — which *widens* coverage — clipped **434 samples**; reversing the gradient cut that to 64. And **zero clipping was the wrong target**: `chart()`'s ramp reaches 1.0, so the *neutral* render already pins 24, and asserting zero would have been asserting something false about correct code. The guard computes the neutral floor and asserts the composite adds none of its own; **0.45 EV** is the largest lift that does, with 109–116 distinct values. ⚠ A second guard asserts each op differs from the other two *and* from the base mask alone — three plausible PNGs prove nothing, since a composite that ignored its refinements would render all three identically and pass forever.
- **⚠ Two dead-code traps, both predicted and both fired.** `Composite`/`BaseFacts`/`PixelCtx` are `pub(super)` in a **private module**, so they are dead code under `-D warnings` until their caller exists — the hue LUT's recorded trap arriving in a *library* crate — which is why Tasks 5 and 6 landed as one commit. And a `///` doc comment on a `thread_local!` invocation is `unused_doc_comments`, which `-D warnings` also rejects.
- **⚠ `stages/mask.rs` went 1080 → 1005** by moving `CoverageSource`, `lightness` and `hue_chroma` into `stages/mask_coverage.rs` **before** anything was added — a pure move (1 insertion, 76 deletions, no test edited), with the `⚠` comments diffed before and after to prove none was dropped.
- **⚠⚠ THREE PROCESS TRAPS, ALL VARIANTS THIS FILE ALREADY RECORDS, HIT ANYWAY.** (1) A quoted heredoc passes `'\\n'` verbatim, so Python wrote `mask.rs` as **one line of escape sequences** — an edit that landed perfectly and wrote corrupt content, where the recorded traps are edits that match nothing or match too much. (2) `/tmp/t.log` and `/tmp/c.log` — the names this file itself recommends — were **left over from an earlier session**, so a wait condition matched stale content instantly; use session-unique log names. (3) `cargo test --test golden "name_a name_b"` matched **nothing** and exited 0, because cargo takes a substring and not a list; caught only by reading `0 passed; 74 filtered out` rather than the word `ok`.
- **✅ PROVED END TO END THROUGH THE CLI, on a real X-T4 frame with a hand-written sidecar** — the check M3.1a's headless plan used, and the first real test that the serde format is usable by a human. A radial carrying +1.5 EV, rendered twice, the second with a smaller radial **subtracted** from its middle: centre `[220,212,210] → [137,133,132]` (the lift removed), the ring between the two radials **byte-identical**, outside both **byte-identical**. ⚠⚠ **And the differing area is 9.40% of 26 Mpx against the subtracted ellipse's analytic `π·0.15·0.2 = 9.42%`** — the region that changed matches the shape's own area to two decimal places, which is far stronger evidence than two pictures looking different.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **nothing is authorable yet** — plan 2 adds the panel and canvas; (2) the product algebra is not idempotent; (3) components are **append-only with no reorder**, while order is observable — `panel_order::{drop_index, moved}` already solves that gesture and can be reused; (4) **one level** — a composite cannot contain a composite, the nesting the recursive variant would have bought; (5) a brush component costs a cache entry and a rasterisation like any other brush; (6) no mask-to-mask reference — a refinement is a shape, not "mask 2".

**The auto-mask coverage cache (2026-09-09). ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE** — *"the caching works, the masked slider is much faster now"*. An auto-masked brush's coverage is no longer re-rasterised on every render. 2166 → **2172 tests**, 60 goldens byte-identical throughout, which is the proof this changed *when* coverage is computed and never what it is. Spec `2026-09-09-auto-mask-coverage-cache-design.md`, plan `2026-09-09-auto-mask-coverage-cache.md`.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED BEFORE DESIGNING, because `docs/BACKLOG.md` records a cost hypothesis in this exact area that measurement overturned by 6×.** `bench_auto_mask_cov` (kept), X-T4, rung 6246, region 1465×1053 (1.54 Mpx):

  | `rasterize_in` | clipped to the region | whole frame |
  |---|---|---|
  | plain brush | 12.32 ms | 21.62 ms |
  | **auto-mask brush** | **42.55 ms** | **80.81 ms** |

  **The gate costs 30.24 ms on top — 3.5×.** Against a ~23.5 ms region tail, **coverage was about 65% of a masked render.** ✅ After: `RUSTROOM_DEBUG_RENDER` over a real session shows masked renders reaching **21.5 ms at 1.7 Mpx — the same cost as an *unmasked* render at that size**, i.e. coverage free on a hit. ⚠ The session's median is 66.7 ms because it mixes in the misses; that is not a figure to quote as the win.
- **⚠⚠ THE SPEC'S DESIGN COULD NOT BE IMPLEMENTED AND THE PLAN SAID SO — the fifth plan-time correction this file records.** `BrushCache` lives in `render_engine` and **has no base buffer**; a gating brush's coverage is built inside `MaskStage::apply`, the only place holding the image, and `render_cached` cannot interpose because the masks loop is inside the tail it runs as one unit. **The cache can never build this coverage — only store one the stage built.** `BrushCoverage` gained a write side; the stage reports, the app keys and stores. ⚠ Two alternatives rejected: a `CoverageStore` trait puts cache lookup inside a stage, and splitting the tail at every mask boundary needs one tap per mask and multiplies with the proof path.
- **⚠⚠ PER-MASK ENTRIES, AND THE WIN DEPENDS ON IT.** One entry holding every mask's map would be invalidated by a change to any one of them — including **mask 0's own adjustment invalidating mask 0's own coverage**, which is exactly the case this exists to make free.
- **⚠⚠ THE KEY IS `Edits` TRUNCATED TO THE MASKS *BEFORE* THIS ONE, PLUS THE RUNG.** Sound because everything upstream of the base is in it — the prefix stages all live in `Edits`, so does the whole global tail, so does every earlier mask's shape and adjustments. **And the truncation is what buys the win**: dropping this mask drops its own adjustments, so moving its exposure leaves the identity unchanged. ⚠ It fails toward a **miss**, `PrefixKey`'s rule; the dangerous direction is a stale hit — a mask rendering positionally while the tint shows it stopping at an edge.
- **⚠⚠ WHAT IT DOES NOT BUY, AND AN EARLIER FRAMING OF MINE OVERSTATED THIS.** A **global** slider genuinely changes the base — the masks loop runs after the entire global tail — so it must rebuild and no cache can help. `docs/BACKLOG.md` already carried that as a correction to an earlier claim. **And panning is not helped either, structurally**: whole-frame coverage for a gating brush is not merely uncached but **unknowable** during a region render, because the gate reads base pixels the region render never computes. Provable rather than empirical, like the `chroma ≤ K · spread` argument beside it.
- **⚠⚠ THE RESTRUCTURE MADE TWO SHIPPED TESTS VACUOUS, AND ONLY RUNNING THEM SHOWED IT.** The five `BrushCache` tests compared the **outer** `Arc<BrushCoverage>`, which is now assembled fresh per call — so the three "must hit" ones failed loudly and the two "must miss" ones began passing **for free**, since `!ptr_eq` on two always-different `Arc`s is trivially true. They compare mask 0's **map** now, which is what "did not re-rasterize" means, and a third sabotage — a key that ignores the strokes — confirms they discriminate again. ⚠ **The behaviour did not change; the observable did.** A silently-passing test is worse than a failing one, and the failing three are what led to finding the other two.
- **⚠ `the_brush_cache_declines_a_brush_that_needs_the_image` is INVERTED, not deleted, and says what it used to assert.** The cache stores a gating brush's coverage now; it still never **builds** one, and that is the half keeping the dangerous state unrepresentable.
- **⚠ Clippy caught `remember` at 8 arguments while 2172 tests passed.** `CoverageSpec` bundles the four values `key`, `remember` and `get_or_build` all passed around — the `FrameSpec` answer, not an allowance. **At least the thirteenth time clippy has caught what a green suite did not.**
- **⚠⚠ A GATE WAS INVALIDATED BY EDITING WHILE IT RAN**, and the two tasks it covered had to land as one commit rather than back-dating a green result. It reported `TEST=101` with **`ok: 0`** — a compile failure that reads as a pass unless the count is read. ⚠ The `cargo fmt` reflow trap fired twice more, once making a scripted edit match nothing and once making a regex splice a rung *inside* a `Some(..)`; both were caught by asserting the edit landed, which is the rule that exists for it.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **panning is not helped**, structurally, and it is what prompted the work — saying so plainly is better than implying otherwise; (2) **a global slider drag is not helped** and cannot be; (3) the key over-invalidates when an *earlier* mask changes, which is sound and the cheapest sound rule; (4) it costs an `Edits` clone and comparison per mask per render — if that ever shows up the answer is a hash of the truncated `Edits`, never a looser key; (5) `render_engine.rs` is **~2,800 lines** and grew here; not split.

**Mask isolation, and the placement shift that was not there (2026-09-09) — M7.6 CLOSES WITH IT. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE** — *"everything works now"*, after **seven** rounds of defects found by using it, every one recorded below. While a mask's tint is shown that mask's adjustments leave the view, so painting needs no render and the rung never changes mid-stroke; plus the render-path defects the instruments found while looking for a reported placement shift. 2138 → **2166 tests**, 60 goldens byte-identical throughout, which is the proof no pixel path was touched. Spec `2026-09-09-mask-isolation-design.md`, plan `2026-09-09-mask-isolation.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE BRIEF WAS "FIX THE PLACEMENT SHIFT" AND THE PHOTOGRAPH DOES NOT MOVE. Two independent instruments say so, one of them on the reporter's own session.** Reported as: while drawing at zoom, *"the image noticeably shifts... slightly to the top left"* when the render quality changes.
  - **`RUSTROOM_DEBUG_PLACE=1`** (kept, `develop/canvas/mod.rs`, the `RUSTROOM_DEBUG_BRUSH` precedent) logs every input the placement is derived from, per frame. Over **836 real frames and 16 rung transitions**, holding pan and zoom fixed across each one: worst content shift at screen centre **0.25 px**, and **exactly 0.000 px vertically at every transition**.
  - ⚠⚠ **The vertical zero is STRUCTURAL, not luck.** `fit = avail.y / full.y` in the constrained axis, so `full.y * fit` is `avail.y` identically and the frame's on-screen height cannot depend on the rung. **This mechanism can only ever move things sideways, sub-pixel — so a report of a *top-left* translation is not this.**
  - **`rung_alignment`** (kept, `crates/app/examples/`) renders the same photo through the **real** prefix+tail at each rung — lens corrections ON, as the GUI has them, since they are the only geometric stage whose displacement field is rebuilt per resolution — resamples the higher rung onto the lower one's grid with the centre-aligned `downscale_to`, and measures the translation by SSD with a parabolic sub-pixel refinement. Worst case **0.020 fit px = 0.18 screen px** at the reporter's 8.88×; the **lens-off control reads exactly 0.000**, which is what makes the lens row believable.
- **⚠⚠ THE HYPOTHESIS THIS WORK STARTED FROM WAS REAL AND TWO ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE TOO SMALL — recorded so nobody re-derives it as the cause.** `fit_image_size` really did drift per rung (§ below), by 0.4 px. It cannot produce a noticeable translation and it cannot produce a vertical one at all.
- **What is large and real:** `drag_quality` is `Faster`, so a stroke takes the texture from **2.28 screen pixels per rendered pixel to 4.44** — a genuine 2× softening of the whole visible field, exactly as designed. ⚠ **A high-contrast edge going soft and sharp again reads as movement.** That is a property of the blur changing, not of anything being displaced, and nothing can be moved to fix it — only the resolution change can be removed, which is what this milestone does for the drawing case. ⚠ **It is a hypothesis about perception and only use can confirm it.** If a shift is still visible once the rung stops changing mid-stroke, it is not the render, and the next things to instrument are **the mask tint's own placement grid** and **the region-scoped coverage rasterisation** — neither has been measured.
- **⚠⚠ MASK ISOLATION ALONE DOES NOT STOP THE RUNG LADDER, and that is the thing most likely to be assumed.** `is_decidedly_dragging()` is true while painting, so the canvas still drops a rung and `max_dim != s.render_rung` still forces a render. The rung rule is not an addition to the feature; it **is** the half that delivers "full resolution while drawing".
- **`Session.suppressed_mask` is `suppressed_spot`'s mirror**, applied in `view_edits` in the same place — *"a patch leaves the view while it is adjusted"*. **Removed rather than neutralised**: a mask contributes to the picture only through its adjustments, so the two are the same thing. ⚠⚠ **One derivation site, never a set/clear pair**, recomputed unconditionally each frame in `develop::body` from the same three facts that decide whether the tint is drawn. The spot-removal flicker bug was two owners of one suppression; a single unconditional derivation cannot acquire a second.
- **⚠ The carrier test is the one that matters, and its sabotage proves it:** stubbing the derivation leaves **every** `view_edits` test green and fails only the carrier. That asymmetry is the shape that let creative LUTs ship fully tested and 100% non-functional.
- **⚠⚠ PANNING'S RUNG BEHAVIOUR IS DECIDED BY A MEASUREMENT, AND IT REVERSES WHAT THE INTERACTIVE RUNG ASSUMED.** `bench_pan_rung` (kept) prices a region tail with the prefix cached — the whole per-frame cost of a pan, since panning changes no edits — at the reporter's zoom on an X-T4:

  | rung | region | tail |
  |---|---|---|
  | 3200 | 488×277, 0.14 Mpx | **1.48 ms** |
  | 6246 | 953×541, 0.52 Mpx | **6.02 ms** |

  The drop buys **4.5 ms** — a full-rung pan frame is 6 ms, about **166 fps** — and costs a 2× blur across the whole view. ⚠ The interactive rung's **137.9 ms** was measured on a *fitted, whole-image* render of 6.8 Mpx, where the same drop was worth 98 ms. **Zoomed and region-bounded it is worth almost nothing**, which is why panning keeps the exemption.
- **⚠⚠ THE RELEASE FRAME BROKE THE RULE, AND INSTRUMENTING FOUND IT AFTER TWO WRONG GUESSES.** egui still reports a decided drag on the release frame while `press_origin` is already `None` — the edge `canvas/mod.rs` calls benign. It stops being benign once the exemption depends on *where the press began*: the gesture becomes unattributable, the exemption evaporates, the drop still applies, and **the rung dropped for one frame at the end of every pan**. The drop now also requires `press_origin().is_some()`, which additionally restores full quality one frame sooner for every gesture.
- **⚠⚠ THE PAN TEST WAS VACUOUS THREE TIMES, EACH FOR A DIFFERENT REASON, AND ONLY THE SABOTAGE FOUND THE FIRST.** (1) An **80×40 fixture gives a ONE-RUNG ladder** — `fit_cap` is 80, so `rung_below` returns the same rung whatever the drop and the assertion can never fire. (2) **No frame ever lands in the canvas harness**, so `rendered_region` stays `None` and `covered` is never true. (3) **Seeding a region while the whole image is visible** makes `want` `None`, which hits `covered`'s `_ => false` arm. It now raises `buffer_long` to 3200, zooms in, seeds the region, and **asserts the ladder really settled on its top rung** so it cannot go blind again in silence.
- **⚠ And a fourth, in the handover test: the fixture opens with `buffer_is_full_res: true`**, so the poll loop broke on its first pump, the handover never ran, and the fixture check asserted a flag that was already true. It passed against the unfixed code. The sibling handover test resets that flag; omitting the line was the error.
- **⚠ `fit_image_size` is carried on the frame now, not derived in `shell.rs`.** It was `frame_dims × fit_cap / frame.max_dim`, and a frame's dimensions come from `downscale_to`, which **rounds** — so the error was amplified by `rung / fit_cap`: measured **1068.000 at rungs 1600 and 3200 against a true 1068.204 at 6246**. ⚠⚠ **Deriving it in `shell.rs` from `Session::edits` would also be exact and would be WORSE**: it describes a crop the frame on screen does not have until the next one lands, so the texture would be misplaced for a frame or two after every crop change — trading a 0.4 px error for a visible one. Travelling with the pixels it describes makes that unrepresentable. ⚠ `PREVIEW_MAX_DIM` moved into `view_region` and the bin re-exports it, because the **lib** now computes this and needs the same cap the bin uses.
- **⚠ The full-resolution handover requested a whole-image FIT render whatever the zoom.** At 7.72× that landed an **8.9× magnified 1600 px texture held for 18 frames** — four times the softening `Faster` gives, once per photo. It requests at `s.render_rung` now. ⚠ Removing `s.render_rung = s.fit_cap()` left `Session::fit_cap` with no production caller and **clippy rejected it — which found a duplicate derivation**: the canvas and `commands.rs` were each computing `view_region::fit_cap(s.buffer_long, PREVIEW_MAX_DIM)` inline, exactly what that method's doc says must not happen. Both go through the accessor now.
- **⚠ `RenderedFrame` has FOUR construction sites**, not the two the plan named — `buffer_cache` and `test_app` build one each — and **`Session` has two exhaustive literals**. The compiler named all of them, which is what those literals are for.
- **⚠⚠ SEVEN ROUNDS OF DEFECTS FROM USING IT, AND THE TWO LARGEST WERE NOT THE FEATURE'S.** The fourteenth time this file records that GUI work's defects are found by use rather than by a test — and this time two of them were **years-old bugs in the render path** that only became visible because someone was finally looking at that area with instruments.
- **⚠⚠⚠ THE RENDER MARGIN HAD NEVER WORKED, SINCE THE DAY REGION RENDERING LANDED.** `covered` asked whether the rendered region contains `want` — and `want` is the **margined** rect. Both are `visible + 2 × margin`, so they are the same size, and one rectangle contains another of equal size **only when they are identical**: any pan at all made it false. `with_margin`'s own doc says why it exists — *"small pans reuse the existing texture instead of re-rendering"* — and the comparison defeated it completely. ⚠ Measured, not reasoned: `RUSTROOM_DEBUG_RENDER` over one pan showed **81 consecutive renders, one per frame**, regions drifting a pixel at a time, 21 ms each. It asks about the **visible** rect now; the margin is headroom, not part of the requirement. ⚠ **The canvas-level pan test cannot see this and says so**: it seeds `rendered_region` as the whole image, which contains the margined rect too, so restoring the old comparison leaves it green — verified by sabotage. The harness structurally cannot reach the case, since a 256 px margin needs an image far larger than any fixture that can reach a native rung. `view_region::a_small_pan_stays_inside_the_margin_but_not_inside_a_margined_rect` guards the rule instead.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE OTHER: THE FULL-RESOLUTION HANDOVER THREW EVERY ZOOMED VIEW BACK TO FIT RESOLUTION, ONCE PER PHOTO.** `s.request(.., s.fit_cap(), None)` — at 7.72× that is an **8.9× magnified 1600 px texture, measured held for 18 frames**, four times the softening `Faster` is meant to give. The explicit request is necessary (without it the canvas sees an unchanged rung and a covered region and asks for nothing, so the upgrade is invisible); only the resolution was wrong. ⚠ Removing `s.render_rung = s.fit_cap()` left `Session::fit_cap` with **no production caller and clippy rejected it** — which found a duplicate derivation: the canvas and `commands.rs` were each computing `view_region::fit_cap(s.buffer_long, PREVIEW_MAX_DIM)` inline, exactly what that method's doc says must not happen.
- **⚠⚠ THE MASKED EDITS APPEARED LATE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, AND IT WAS A DESIGN ERROR RATHER THAN A SLIP: A DERIVED VALUE WAS STORED.** Leaving the Masks tool rendered *without* the mask's edits, arriving rendered *with* them, each correcting itself only when something unrelated forced a frame. One cause — the suppression was updated **after** `select_tool`, which owns the re-render for a tool change, so every tool change requested with the suppression of the tool being **left**. Derived from `picked` above `select_tool` now. ⚠ The second half is a **change guard that asks for a render**: ticking "Show mask" and selecting a different mask change what `view_edits` returns with *no tool change at all*, so nothing requested and the screen kept the old answer — a latent bug that had not been hit yet.
- **⚠⚠ PANNING SHOWED BLACK RECTANGLES AT THE EDGES.** A region render paints only `img_rect`; outside it nothing was painted, so revealing new area showed black that snapped to image. The last **whole-image** texture is kept and painted at `frame_rect` *under* the region, so a revealed edge is a soft but correct picture that snaps sharp. Dropped when its `fit_size` no longer matches — it spans the whole frame rect, so a stale one is visibly wrong rather than subtly. ⚠ **Rendering the whole image at the native rung was measured and rejected**: 26 Mpx against a region's 0.5, about **300 ms** per render, paid on every slider move as well. The backdrop costs one texture and one `paint_at`.
- **⚠⚠ `PAN_MARGIN` IS 256 RENDERED PX, SWEPT RATHER THAN CHOSEN** (`bench_pan_rung`, kept): 583 pt of pan for 23.5 ms, where 1024 costs 97 ms for four times the travel. **The area grows as `(w + 2m)(h + 2m)` while the distance it buys grows only as `m`**, so it cannot simply be raised until panning is free. `max`-ed with the kernel reach: that floor is *comfort*, the reach is *correctness*, and trading the second for the first would be silently wrong. ⚠ It made `the_render_margin_follows_the_edits_it_will_render` read **"256 vs 256"** — one masked clarity's ~120 px sits under it, so the guard could no longer see the derivation it exists to guard; three chained masks reach ~360 and clear it. ⚠ `view_region`'s standing warning that a margin constant "must not come back" is **corrected in place**: a floor is not that constant, since the failure it guards needs a margin *smaller* than the reach.
- **⚠⚠ A MODE SWITCH RENDERS ONE RUNG LOWER FIRST AND SHARPENS ON THE NEXT FRAME, AND THIS IS THE 2026-08-13 PROGRESSIVE-FIT FEATURE DELIBERATELY RESTORED.** Measured: a full-quality render of a toggle is **95–300 ms** on a large window, and a single mask roughly doubles it. ⚠⚠ **The reason that feature was deleted does not apply**: it was removed because *"the user could not see it on or off"*, trading a 1.44× residual nobody noticed. This trades a measured 95–300 ms wait against one or two frames at 2× softer, and **the wait is what was reported**. It also cannot oscillate the way that one did — the gate is a change in `suppressed_mask`, which no render can affect. ⚠ No settle timer: setting `render_rung` low makes the canvas's own `max_dim != s.render_rung` disjunction restore full quality on the next frame. ⚠ `Session::soft_next` is a **baton across one frame, not a second owner**: `develop::body` runs the panels before the canvas, so without it the canvas sees the rung change on the very frame the drop was made and supersedes the soft render before it can be shown.
- **⚠ Holding the configured button erases for the duration of the gesture** — `Settings → Erase with`, right-drag / middle-drag / off, defaulting to right. Baked into the stroke at `drag_started`, exactly as `radius`, `hardness`, `flow` and `auto_mask` are, so a stroke keeps what it was drawn with. ⚠ **XOR, not an override**: with Erase already ticked, holding the button *paints* — a modifier rather than a second erase control, and a way to add back a slip without reaching for the panel. ⚠⚠ **The pan binding always wins, and `EraseDrag::button(pan)` decides both that and "is it off" in ONE function** — two places deciding it is how a build appears in which a right-drag both pans the photograph and erases into it. The dialog hint *says* when the gesture is unavailable rather than leaving a control that silently does nothing.
- **⚠⚠ THREE-FINGER ERASE WAS ASKED FOR AND IS NOT BUILDABLE THROUGH THIS STACK — checked in the vendored source, not the documentation.** `egui` exposes `multi_touch()` with `num_touches` and `egui-winit` forwards `WindowEvent::Touch`, but **winit's macOS AppKit backend never emits that event**: trackpad gestures arrive as scroll/magnify/rotate, never as raw touches. It needs AppKit code of the kind `macos_display_colorspace` required.
- **⚠⚠ PANNING WITH AN AUTO-MASKED BRUSH IS HEAVY FOR A SPECIFIC, FIXABLE REASON — and it is the next action.** Measured with `RUSTROOM_DEBUG_RENDER`: a 1.7 Mpx region costs **~21 ms with no mask and 19–156 ms with one**; a 6.8 Mpx whole image **94.9 ms against 192–301 ms**. `BrushCache` deliberately **declines** an auto-mask brush (`b.needs_base() => None`) because its coverage depends on pixel values, so the key (strokes + geometry) is insufficient — and it is therefore re-rasterised from scratch on **every render**. ⚠ **During a pan the base pixels do not change**, since the prefix output is cached and identical, so that coverage *can* be cached on the rung and region. Same insight `docs/BACKLOG.md` already holds for range-mask coverage.
- **⚠⚠ FOUR PROCESS SLIPS, EVERY ONE A TRAP THIS FILE ALREADY RECORDS, WALKED INTO ANYWAY.** (1) **`git checkout <file>` to undo a sabotage destroyed all the uncommitted work in that file** — the exact entry the display-profile work left. A copy is taken before each sabotage now. (2) **A `sed` matched nothing because `cargo fmt` had reflowed the condition across two lines**, so the sabotage came back green; the rule that catches it — *assert the edit landed* — is the one skipped. (3) **A sabotage that failed to compile read exactly like a pass.** (4) **`cargo test "a\|b"` matched nothing** — cargo takes a substring, not a regex — and printed `0 passed; 787 filtered out`, which reads as green unless the count is read.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **a slider belonging to the shown mask does nothing visible** — the deliberate cost of a one-sentence rule, and the caveat most likely to be reported as a bug; untick "Show mask" to see its effect; (2) **the reported shift may persist**, because the measurements say it is not a displacement — the next instruments are named above; (3) `fit_image_size` is now an ideal rather than a measurement of the frame, so it can differ from the real frame by up to half a rendered pixel — consistent beats exact for a placement reference; (4) **nothing here makes a render faster** — it removes renders that were never needed and stops one that was too coarse; (5) the suppression is per *selected* mask, so composing two masks against each other still means unticking the box; (6) ⚠ `develop/canvas/mod.rs` is **1,850 lines** and `main.rs` ~800 — both well past the ~600 marker, both grew here, neither split.

**The dehaze airlight tolerance (2026-09-08) — the last M7.6 gap but one. Merged fast-forward the same day (user's call); not pushed. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE** — *"works, confirmed by use"*: the white wall holds at +100 and the cloudy sky behaves as before. A pixel within **0.1** of the airlight in every channel (linear working space) is read as an object of that colour rather than as haze, and its transmission is raised toward 1. One constant, one line in `transmission`, two test seams, no `Edits`, no slider. 2111 → **2113 tests**; 59 goldens ran, **three re-authored** (`dehaze_plus` moved 19 of 255, `dehaze_minus` 5, `mask_dehaze` 4) and the other **56 byte-identical**. Spec `2026-09-08-dehaze-bright-tolerance-design.md`, plan `2026-09-08-dehaze-bright-tolerance.md`.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED BEFORE DESIGNING, AND THE MEASUREMENT RESHAPED THE BRIEF FOUR WAYS.** `dehaze_bright_probe` (kept) rendered all **415 RAWs** at +60 and +100 and scored each frame's bright achromatic pixels. (1) **Mild at +60, severe at +100**: over 183 frames with a meaningful bright area, median darkening 0.8% and worst 6% at +60; at +100 the white-kitten frames darken **15.7–18.7%** on average, **38–48%** in their worst decile, with a blue-grey tint (Oklab chroma +0.013).
