(2) **The dark ring toward the corners is flash falloff read as depth**: the airlight estimate *is* the wall's brightest part, a warm white, and every wall pixel below it is pushed further away by `(I − A)/t + A` — subtracting a warm airlight is what turns it blue. (3) ⚠⚠ **This catalogue contains no genuinely hazy landscape** — ranked by how much Dehaze changes the frame, the top ten are kittens in front of a flat lilac wall — so the "haze must still be removed" regression set is the stage's **synthetic** scene, and region detection was rejected partly because nothing here could check it. (4) **Cloudy skies are a different case**: their bright pixels are not near the airlight, and at +60 the sheets show the effect people reach for Dehaze to get; deliberately untouched (~15% at +100, unchanged).
- **⚠⚠ THE TOLERANCE WAS SWEPT, NOT CHOSEN.** At +100 on six frames: 0.06 buys half the benefit (10.4% / 3.8% on the two walls); **0.1** gives **3.4% / 0.8%** with the tint gone; **0.2 fails `negative_dehaze_is_depth_aware`** — the depth gradient of a flat scene is swallowed. The cost, measured: **13% less removal of dense haze** on the synthetic scene (residual 0.336 → 0.380 of the haze, bound 0.5), because dense haze is near the airlight by definition. Render cost inside noise: 42.2 → 42.8 ms on the dehaze-60 row, with the machine's own spread 40.5–48.4.
- **⚠⚠ THE TEST FIXTURE WAS CORRECTED THREE TIMES, EACH BY A FAILING ASSERTION, AND EACH CORRECTION TAUGHT THE MODEL.** (1) The spec put a patch at **0.92 × A**; the model's own arithmetic says the tolerance raises its transmission only 1.5× there and it still darkens 43% — the white-wall case is pixels within **~1%** of the estimate. (2) A **flat** patch near A *becomes* the airlight estimate and is untouched with or without the tolerance (`(Â − Â)/t + Â = Â`); the falloff is the whole mechanism, so the fixture is a wall with a 3% gradient, hazed 10% darker than the wall so the wall's top rows win the estimate as on the real frames. (3) ⚠ **The guided refinement's reach is twice its radius** — 120 px, the `local_contrast::kernel_reach` finding arriving in a new place — so a band 60 px below the wall's top edge read a transmission of 0.17 rather than ~0.06, averaged with the hazy rows above; the wall now spans the full image height and the band is inset 120 px. Measured then: **0.3% darker with the tolerance, 9.0% without**, and the premise bound is 5%, a measured 4.5× margin rather than the 10% the per-pixel arithmetic predicted. **Instrumentation settled the second and third within one run each, after reasoning had produced a wrong prediction both times.**
- **⚠ The continuity test moved from pixels to a pure function.** `tolerant_transmission(t, dist, K)` is the rule; sweeping it directly puts nothing between the sabotage and the assertion. A hard branch fails it with `jump of 0.7 at dist 0.1`.
- **⚠ Clippy caught what a green test run did not, again — and this time it was a warning the plan created.** The spec's `transmission` wrapper (delegating with the constant) lost its only caller when `apply_with` took the tolerance, and a `pub fn` in the private `stages` module with no caller is dead code under `-D warnings`. Removed; the constant enters at `apply`. ⚠ Task 1 was committed on `fmt` and tests alone and clippy was run only afterwards — the standing rule broken once more and repaired before anything was built on it.
- **⚠⚠ THE PROBE'S "HAZIEST FRAMES" LIST WAS NOT WHAT IT SAID.** Ranking by overall effect found flat lilac walls, not haze — the metric measures how much Dehaze *moved*, and a flat wall of one colour near the airlight is exactly what it moves most. Recorded in the probe's own doc so nobody re-runs it to learn the same thing.
- **⚠ `stages/dehaze.rs` is 597 lines** after this, up from 422 — the two fixture-heavy tests are most of the growth — and it now sits one test short of the ~600 marker; `golden.rs` untouched. No oversized file touched.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **dense haze near the airlight is removed ~13% less** — the trade, bounded by the 0.5 test; (2) **cloudy skies still darken ~15% at +100**, deliberately; (3) **a bright object whose frame's airlight is a *different* bright region is untouched** — DSCF6327's wall behind a window, 8.4% at +100, recorded as a limit and carried in the backlog; (4) **K is derived from six X-T4 frames and one synthetic scene**, the ceiling and the floor both measured; (5) the exemption is per photo, like the airlight; (6) three goldens moved, the second re-authoring of the dehaze references for a model change.

**The grade range view (2026-09-08) — the first of M7.6's five gaps. Merged fast-forward the same day (user's call); not pushed. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE — after one correction found by using it, the tint, recorded below.** A "Show range" checkbox under Balance tints the photograph where the selected `Shad │ Mid │ High` range reaches, alpha = that range's weight at each pixel, read at the grading stage's **input**. 2095 → **2111 tests**; 59 goldens ran and none moved. Spec `2026-09-08-grade-range-view-design.md`, plan `2026-09-08-grade-range-view.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE TWO CHEAP DESIGNS WERE WRONG BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THIS IS THE ONE PLACE IN THIS AREA WHERE THAT COULD BE SAID BEFORE MEASURING.** The tail runs grading → the masks loop → vignette → sharpening → **the display rendering S-curve** → the creative LUT → the output transform. The histogram and `pack`'s pixels sit past the S-curve; the ranges are selected on Oklab L before it. A view decoded from the output pixels, or a marker on the histogram's axis, misplaces every boundary by the whole curve. So the tap is at the grading stage's input: `TailHalves` (`build_pipeline_split_tapped`) returns the tail in two halves split there, and the old builder is a wrapper that concatenates them — **byte-identical by construction, and the 59 goldens are the proof.** `the_plane_is_taken_before_the_display_rendering` is the test that encodes it; sabotaged (plane read after the second half), it and `the_plane_is_taken_before_the_grade` both fail.
- **⚠ The split exists whether or not the grade is active** — Blending and Balance define the ranges before a wheel is turned, so "before the grade" is a structural position, after HSL and before the masks loop, not a search for a stage name.
- **⚠⚠ MEASURED, AND THE SPEC'S "SINGLE DIGITS" WAS THE FIFTH WRONG ESTIMATE IN THIS AREA.** `range_weights` per pixel is a `powf` for the width plus three `exp`: **15.3 ms at fit, 25.3 ms at a 2000×1400 region** (X-T4, release, best of 5; `bench_grade_view`, kept). A 256-entry linearly interpolated table — the stage's own design, for the stage's own reason — brought it to **8.8 / 14.0 ms**. What is left is three `cbrt` per pixel in the Oklab conversion, the floor a colour range mask pays too. Paid only while the box is ticked. ⚠ A nearest-entry table would **not** do: at Blending 0 the weight curve climbs ~12 of 255 between adjacent entries.
- **⚠⚠ THE TINT WAS TEAL, AND THE FIRST GUI CHECK SAID *"very subtle and a bit confusing"* — it is the brush tint's red now (user's call).** Teal was chosen so the two tints could never be confused. At the default Blending of 50 the weights sit around ⅓–½ over the whole frame, and a teal at half that alpha is a faint wash rather than a region. Red at the same alpha rule (`weight / 2`) reads at a glance and is the tint already learned to mean "where the tool reaches"; the two never coincide, since the brush tint is under Masks and this under Edit, and `select_tool` clears the view on the way across.
- **⚠ Two session fields, one derivation site.** `show_grade_range` is the box; `grade_view` is the wire value — the box AND a non-Global selection — and `Session::request` cannot see `app.grade_range`, so the panel derives it with a change guard. `drawing_the_panel_with_the_view_on_requests_nothing` pins the guard (sabotage: an unguarded write fails it); Global carries `None` and leaves the box ticked, so stepping back to a range brings the view straight back. Cleared unconditionally in `select_tool`, both directions tested, beside `visualize`.
- **⚠ The plane lands where the frame lands** (`shell.rs`), becomes a texture in the same assignment — so a frame without one drops it, no separate clear — and is painted at **`img_rect`**, the readout's exception: this overlay is frame-*sized*, every other one is frame-relative.
- **⚠ A sabotage the spec named was inert, corrected in place:** "dropping the clamp" on the lightness index changes nothing, because the weights are normalised and the highlight weight is ≈1 for `L > 1` either way. The clamp is parity with the stage's *table index*; the test pins the behaviour.
- **⚠ The canvas test counted meshes first and found 0 both with and without the tint** — `egui::Image::paint_at` emits a textured `RectShape`, not a mesh; only `painter.image` (the loupe) emits meshes. `textured_rects` counts brushes instead, and asserts the photograph is exactly one before the tint is added.
- **⚠ Clippy caught what a green test run did not, again:** the panel's `show` reached nine arguments and `clippy -D warnings` refused the bin while every test passed. `GradeUi` groups the selector, the box and the wire value — one concern — the `FrameSpec` precedent, and a fair complaint rather than a lint to silence.
- **⚠ `rayon` was not a dependency of `app` until this** — only `core-image` and `pipeline` pulled it; added from the workspace.
- **⚠ Four oversized files touched:** `render_engine.rs` (2594), `adjustments.rs` (989), `shell.rs` (1028), `canvas/mod.rs` (1697) — each a few lines at an existing seam; the logic is in `grade_view.rs`.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) one range at a time — the panel strip still shows all three; (2) no histogram feedback, and none that would be honest without the tap; (3) the plane is at render resolution, the brush tint's trade; (4) ~9 ms at fit while the box is ticked, never folded into the stage; (5) a masked exposure does not move the ranges — correct, grading runs before the masks loop, but a face lifted by a mask keeps the membership it had before; (6) resets per photo and per tool, like Visualize Spots.

**Decided off (2026-09-08) — the first M5.4b leftover, widened to the lens corrections. Merged fast-forward the same day (user's call); not pushed. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** `Edits.rendering` is `Option<Rendering>` and `Edits.lens_correction` is `Option<LensCorrection>`, `None` = **never decided**, `Some(..)` = decided, *including decided off* — so switching either off on an otherwise untouched photo survives a photo switch. 2083 → **2092 tests**; 59 goldens ran and none moved, which is the proof `Edits::default()` stayed neutral. Spec `2026-09-08-decided-off-design.md`, plan `2026-09-08-decided-off.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE BUG WAS CONDITIONAL, NOT UNCONDITIONAL AS THIS FILE HAD IT.** `is_neutral` compares against `Edits::default()`, and a fresh photo is seeded with **both** preferences, so with auto lens correction on, switching the rendering off left the lens corrections non-default, the sidecar survived, and "off" stuck. It failed only when nothing else was non-default — auto lens correction off, or a photo where it does nothing — and the lens corrections had the same failure the other way round. The model flaw the note named was right regardless: "never decided" and "decided off" were one value.
- **⚠⚠ UNDECIDED SEEDS `None`, NEVER `Some(off)`.** `Settings::initial_edits` seeds `Some(Camera)` / `Some(all three on)` only when a preference is **on**. Seeding `Some(off)` under an off preference would have written a sidecar for every photo ever opened, where today none is written — decided-off is a fact worth a file, and an off preference is not a decision about any one photo.
- **⚠ Two accessors are the ONE read rule.** `Edits::applied_rendering()` and `applied_lens_correction()` — `unwrap_or_default()` — so "undecided applies as off" is written once; the pipeline, the CLI, the panels, `perspective_detect`, the spot overlay and the golden harness all read through them. The compiler enumerated every site (the field's type changed), which is why Task 1 was one commit across five crates and every example: **a workspace must build at every commit, and a type change cannot be landed crate by crate.**
- **⚠ Nothing existing changes meaning.** `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"`: undecided is *absent* from the sidecar, never `null`, so a photo that was never asked writes the same bytes it always did. A pre-M5.4b sidecar with no key loads undecided (colorimetric, as before); `"rendering": "None"` — which in the buggy case was written by exactly the user who switched it off — loads as decided off, the reading they meant.
- **⚠ Preference resolution stays at first open (user's call).** A later preference change does not reach an already-opened photo, as before; the "resolve live at render time" alternative was declined as touching four render seams where forgetting one renders colorimetric silently.
- **⚠ `merge_from` copies the `Option`s verbatim.** So a paste, preset or sync from an undecided source makes the target undecided (in practice sources are seeded at open, so decided), and **Reset adjustments** — `merge_from` from a default `Edits` — leaves the lens corrections undecided, which renders off, as a reset does today. ⚠ The alternative of comparing neutrality against the preference *seed* rather than `Edits::default()` was rejected: the same sidecar would then mean different things on two machines.
- **⚠ `render_engine`'s JPEG strip and `thumbnail_edits` set `rendering: None`, undecided** — a stripped copy is a render input, not a choice anyone made, and `Some(off)` there would read as one.
- **⚠ The lens boxes read the applied value into a local and write the whole struct back as `Some` on any change.** Binding the checkboxes to the `Option`'s inside would need it to be `Some` first, which would turn merely *looking* at the panel into a decision. The Geometry panel's lens scale binds through `if let Some`, which is sound because it is drawn only while distortion is on, i.e. already decided.
- **⚠ Tests: the bug as a sidecar test** (`a_decided_off_rendering_or_lens_is_worth_a_sidecar`, plus persist-and-load round trip), whose sabotage — neutrality judged on the *applied* values — fails both; and the two panel writer tests, whose sabotage (`rendered.then_some(Camera)`, i.e. collapsing off to undecided) fails both. The regression tests ride the Task 1 commit rather than following it, because a `git add -A` re-staged them after they had been unstaged — recorded so the history reads right.
- **⚠ Three commits went in only after clippy's exit code was read from a file**, and the first attempt was refused by it twice: rewriting `let mut x = Edits::default(); x.lens_correction = Some(..)` into an initializer left two `mut`s unused in the examples. The guard held this time.
- **⚠ Two oversized files**: `crates/edit-model/src/lib.rs` (~3,800) and `crates/app/src/settings.rs` (~1,900); neither split.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) a preference change still does not reach already-opened photos; (2) a preset saved from an undecided photo carries "undecided"; (3) there is no UI state for "undecided" — the checkboxes show the applied value and any click decides; (4) ~~the remaining M5.4b leftover, `MEDIAN_TARGET`, is a judgement by eye and is the user's~~ — judged the same day, 0.20.

**Use-found fixes (2026-09-08) — two defects reported after the `MEDIAN_TARGET` retune. Merged fast-forward the same day (user's call); not pushed. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** *"I don't see the option in the settings you mentioned earlier to turn off the automatic camera rendering"* and *"when I unclick the jpeg files in the file format selection of the library, the setting does not persist over sessions."* 2092 → **2095 tests**; 59 goldens ran and none moved — no pipeline code is touched. No spec or plan: two root-caused bugs, each a test and a few lines.

- **⚠⚠ TWO PREFERENCES HAD NO CONTROL AT ALL, AND THIS FILE TOLD THE USER TO CHANGE ONE "IN SETTINGS".** `default_rendering` (M5.4b) and `auto_lens_correction` (M3.2) existed only in the settings file; the earlier handoff wording *"turn auto lens correction off in Settings"* described a checkbox that had never been drawn. Now two checkboxes in the Settings dialog — *Open new photos with the camera rendering*, *Apply lens corrections to new photos* — change-guarded like their neighbours, with hints saying the truth: they apply at the next photo **open** and an already-edited photo keeps its own. ⚠ **Never describe a control without reading the dialog that would draw it.**
- **⚠⚠ THE FORMAT FILTER HAD A WRITE HALF AND NO READ HALF.** The toggle in `shell.rs` wrote `settings.hidden_formats` and saved on every change; `RustroomApp::new` built its filter from `LibraryFilter::default()`. The file said "JPEGs hidden" and the app hid nothing. **`Settings::initial_filter()`** is the read half — the format only; search text and the mark sets start empty because they are about the question being asked now, not a standing preference — and `main.rs` seeds the session with it. ⚠ **The serde round-trip test `the_hidden_formats_survive_a_restart` passed throughout**: it proved the *file* and was blind to the *wiring*. `the_hidden_formats_reach_the_filter_at_startup` is the wiring half. **A persistence test that never reads the value back into the thing that uses it tests a file, not a feature.**
- **⚠⚠ THE `MEDIAN_TARGET` RETUNE (`5f3f128`, on `main`) SHIPPED WITH A FAILING TEST.** The tuning loop ran the goldens and the auto-tone tests, not the full suite, and `the_sessions_baseline_reaches_the_auto_solve` in `develop/panel/mod.rs` compared `None` against `Some(2.0)`: under a 0.20 target the fixture solves to −2.45 EV, a +2 EV baseline aims below the **−2.5 EV clamp**, and the two sat on the clamp 0.05 apart — failing for a reason unrelated to what it guards. Re-aimed at `Some(-1.0)`, which raises the target and lands well inside the range (`eefd072`). ⚠ **A constant retune is a code change and gets the full gate**, however small the diff; "small tweak in a tuning loop" excuses the GUI relaunch, not the test suite. ⚠ A test that asserts a *difference* must keep both values off every clamp, or a clamp turns the difference into noise.
- **⚠ Sabotage-verified, all three new tests:** the two dialog tests fail with the write-back lines deleted, the wiring test fails with `format: Default::default()`; restored from copies, `git status` checked.
- **⚠ One oversized file**: `crates/app/src/settings.rs` (~1,965); not split.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) a preference change still reaches only photos opened afterwards — the dialog's hint says so; (2) the format filter is the only part of the Library filter that persists, by design.

**M5.5c — custom aspect ratios and a Swap button (2026-09-08) — and the roadmap inconsistency this file raised on 2026-09-07 is closed by building the thing. Merged fast-forward the same day (user's call); not pushed. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE — after one defect found by using it, the tie-break, recorded below.** A `Custom` chip with two whole-number W : H fields, and a `Swap` button that turns the crop between landscape and portrait **in place**, inverting the lock. 2065 → **2083 tests**; 59 goldens ran and none moved — no pipeline code is touched. Spec `2026-09-08-custom-aspect-design.md`, plan `2026-09-08-custom-aspect.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THREE DECISIONS, ALL THE USER'S.** (1) **Swap transposes in place**, keeping the crop's centre and pixel size with width and height exchanged, shrinking uniformly only when the turned rect would leave the frame — Lightroom's behaviour, and the reason `crop_tool::swap_orientation` is not `snap_to_aspect(1/a)`. (2) **Button only, no shortcut** — the menu model and shortcuts dialog are untouched. (3) **One remembered custom ratio**, `Settings.custom_aspect: [u32; 2]`, not a list.
- **⚠⚠ THE UNIFORM SHRINK IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT COSMETIC.** When a turned rect must shrink to fit, both sides scale by the same factor, so the **transposed pixel ratio is preserved exactly** — which is what keeps the swapped crop consistent with the lock the panel inverts beside it. Shrinking one side would leave a "locked" 2:3 crop that is not 2:3. `a_rect_that_would_overhang_shrinks_uniformly_and_fits` pins it.
- **⚠ A chip is lit by its ratio OR ITS INVERSE, and Custom yields to a preset.** `geometry::chip_state` is pure and tested: after a swap the lock reads 2:3 and the 3:2 chip stays lit, with Swap showing which way it faces — a chip that went dark on swap would read as "no preset", which is false. ⚠⚠ **AND THE SPEC'S TIE-BREAK WAS A DEAD END, FOUND IN USE WITHIN THE HOUR.** It said a custom ratio equal to a preset lights the preset *instead* — but the W:H fields draw only while Custom is lit, so once the stored ratio was `[6, 4]` (which is 3:2) it could never be edited again: *"custom button worked the first time, but now by tipping on it it goes to 3:2 all the time."* Diagnosed from the **settings file**, not from theory — `custom_aspect = [6, 4]` said it in one line. One rule for all six chips now; two lit chips say truthfully that two ratios agree. `a_custom_ratio_equal_to_a_preset_stays_editable` is the report as a test, and reintroducing the tie-break fails it. ⚠ **A rule that decides what is *shown* from a value alone loses which control the user chose, and a control that hides its own editor behind that rule can lock itself away.** ⚠ A round-trip fixture was corrected before it shipped: on a 3:2 image a 0.2 × 0.3 rect turns into a 0.2 × 0.3 rect, so `swapping_twice_is_the_identity` would have passed with the swap deleted; it now asserts the first swap changes the rect.
- **⚠⚠ A `DragValue` HAS NO ACCESSKIT LABEL AT ALL, and the harness could not see the two fields.** egui gives it `Role::SpinButton` with a numeric value and nothing else (`response.rs`, `WidgetInfo::drag_value`), so `test_ui::labelled_centres` returned the whole panel without them. **`test_ui::numeric_values` is the query that can**, collecting every node's `numeric_value`, and `the_ratio_fields_are_drawn_only_while_custom_is_lit` asserts on 21 and 9 — values no other control in the panel sits at. The plan had predicted the failure and named the fallback.
- **⚠ A typed value settles at once; a dragged one on release.** `ed.absorb` alone would never settle a typed `DragValue`, because a `TextEdit` commit stops no drag — so the fields set `settled` on `drag_stopped()` **or** on `changed()` with no drag in progress. Either way one edit is one Ctrl+Z.
- **⚠⚠ A COMMIT WENT IN WITH CLIPPY FAILING, AGAIN — `show` reached eight parameters — and was amended before anything else was built on it.** The gate command was sequenced with `;` rather than read before committing. The answer was the project's standing one: **`geometry::AspectLock { lock, custom }`**, a params struct of two `&mut` into disjoint fields, the shape `FrameSpec` and `NameField` already gave. ⚠ Its rename swept `*crop_aspect` inside `lock_to`, which has its own parameter of that name — caught by the compiler, not by reading. **Read the clippy exit code before every commit** is now recorded for at least the twelfth time in this file.
- **⚠ `Swap` is a text label, not a glyph.** `⬍`/`⬌` already mean *flip* two rows up, and a swap that looks like a flip is the wrong model; no other suitable glyph is proven renderable, and probing one is not worth it for a word that says what it does.
- **⚠ Two files this milestone edits were already over the 600-line mark**: `crates/app/src/settings.rs` (~1,900) and `crates/app/src/develop/panel/geometry.rs` (~1,300, of which the tests are the larger half). Neither was split; both are named here.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) whole numbers only — 2.35:1 is 235:100; (2) one custom ratio, global, not per photo; (3) a swap under `Free` turns the rect and locks nothing, which is the stated rule; (4) no shortcut.

**M6.2 — metadata writing (2026-09-08) — and with it PHASE 6 IS CLOSED. Merged fast-forward to `main` the same day (user's call); not pushed. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE the same day — *"works, caption shows up in exiftool"*, the roadmap's done-when met on a real photograph.** Per-photo title, caption, copyright and creator, edited in the Information dialog, stored per **file** in the catalog (schema v7), written into every export together with rating, label, keywords and the capture facts under an "All metadata / Copyright only" choice, and round-tripped through the `stem.xmp` sidecar. 2035 → **2065 tests**; 59 goldens ran and every one is byte-identical, which is the proof no pixel path was touched. Spec `2026-09-08-metadata-writing-design.md`, plan `2026-09-08-metadata-writing.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THREE DECISIONS, ALL THE USER'S, DO NOT QUIETLY REVERSE THEM.** (1) The **wide** scope — not just the roadmap's four fields but the whole packet and the sidecar. (2) **Per file, not per copy**: a title says what is *in* the picture, which every copy shares — the keyword rule — while only marks describe the image you made. Lightroom gives copies independent metadata; recorded as a divergence in `01-lightroom-capabilities.md`. (3) **`MetadataScope::All` is the default for existing presets too.** An existing user's next export *changes bytes* — it gains camera, exposure, keywords and rating. That is the one deliberate exception to the M6.1 seam's "defaults to do nothing" rule, and `ExportMetadata`'s doc is where it is recorded.
- **⚠ The spec's header-dot rule was wrong and the plan corrected it before code.** §3 first said the Metadata section's "doing something" dot *"shows for `All` always"*. The existing test `a_section_that_is_doing_something_says_so_in_its_header` records why that cannot be: *"a marker that is always on and one that is never on are equally useless"* — the dot surfaces **hidden non-default state**, and the default is not hidden state. So `ExportMetadata::is_active()` is *a field filled OR the scope changed*, and the spec was corrected in place.
- **⚠⚠ THE SPEC MISSED A DATA-LOSS PATH THE PLAN CAUGHT: forget-folder-then-undo.** `forget_folder` cascades through the new table and its snapshot restored photos, marks, captures, memberships and keywords — but not descriptions, so an undone forget would have silently dropped every caption. *"Forgetting a folder is the only action that destroys metadata the user made by hand, and a restore that dropped the ratings would look like it had worked."* Same sentence, one more table: `DescriptionSnapshot` rides `CatalogSnapshot::Folder`. ⚠ When a per-photo table is added, check that snapshot **before** anything else.
- **⚠ Two accepted caveats, both recorded at the code.** `tiff:Model` carries the whole stored `"{MAKE} {model}"` string and there is **no `tiff:Make`** — the make cannot be split back off soundly (`NIKON CORPORATION NIKON Z 6`), and inventing one is worse than omitting it. And a foreign `dc:creator` with several entries **reads as its first and is written back as one**: the catalog holds one creator string, and a co-credit is not a field Rustroom has.
- **⚠ The scope is applied in exactly one place — `FileMetadata::resolve` — and the packet writer knows nothing about it.** `CopyrightOnly` clears everything but rights and creator *before* the writer sees the payload, so the writer has one rule (write what is present) and cannot forget a field the scope should have hidden. The preset's three strings are **defaults under** the photo's own values; the preset has no title and gains none.
- **⚠ No `Library.descriptions` map, deliberately.** `captures` is held in memory because the filter bar reads it every frame; nothing reads descriptions per frame. The dialog reads one row on its first frame (cached in the modal like `dims`), the export reads one per selected photo when the run starts. A per-folder map would be machinery for a consumer that does not exist.
- **⚠⚠ THE TWO CARRIER TESTS ARE THE W5 LESSON APPLIED.** `a_photos_own_caption_reaches_the_file_and_beats_the_presets` goes catalog row → `ExportJob` → worker → bytes in the TIFF; deleting the `file_metadata` call leaves the `export` crate green and this red. `closing_the_dialog_commits_a_changed_description` sets the draft directly (typing into an egui `TextEdit` is egui's to prove) and **clicks Close for real**, because commit-on-close is the wiring that would be forgotten. ⚠ And that test found a harness fact: on the modal's **very first frame** the Close button's reported centre is not where it is drawn from the second frame on, so `test_ui::click`'s own locate frame pressed beside it and the dialog silently stayed open. One warm-up frame (`settle`) fixes it; the comment on it is the record.
- **⚠ Commit only when changed, and the reason is the sidecar.** `description::commit` writes nothing for an untouched draft — *"rewriting the same bytes moves the modification time, which is the one value the whole conflict rule turns on"* (`apply_mark_to`). A sabotage that always writes fails `commit_writes_only_when_the_draft_changed`. ⚠ Unlike `apply_mark_to`, the sidecar rewrite is **not** gated on `is_original()`: a description is per file, so editing it from a copy's dialog changes what the sidecar says.
- **⚠ Two XMP packet skeletons remain** (`export::metadata::xmp_packet`, `xmp::write`) and the M6.3 note at both still stands; the six-line keyword-bag formatting is now in both. Sharing it would make `export` depend on `xmp` for six lines.
- **⚠ Rationals are `N/10`, not reduced; a shutter below a second is `1/N`.** exiftool renders `56/10` as `5.6` and `230/10` as `23.0 mm`; `1/250` is what a photographer wrote and `4/1000` is not. `exif:DateTimeOriginal` carries **no zone**, because `Capture::taken` is naive wall-clock by its own rule and a `Z` would claim a zone the value never had. `chrono` joined `export`'s dependencies for that one line — already in the tree, pure Rust, the CRC-32 argument again.
- **Done-when, checked with exiftool 13.55 on this machine** against the `deliverable` example's output (which now carries a full payload so every property can be read back): **every one of the fifteen properties reads back on JPEG, PNG and TIFF alike** — Title, Description, Rights, Creator, Rating 4, Label Blue, Subject `Munich, Probe`, Hierarchical Subject `Europe|Germany|Munich, Probe`, Camera Model Name, Lens, Date/Time Original `2026:08:27 10:00:00`, ISO 1600, Exposure Time `1/250`, F Number `5.6`, Focal Length `23.0 mm`. The example renders a synthetic chart, so there is no original to check "nowhere near"; that half is structural (no code path opens an original for writing), and the GUI round trip on a real photograph confirmed it the same day. ⚠⚠ **The third milestone in this file's record whose GUI wiring produced no defects at all**, after M7.2 and M6.5c — and like those two, the dialog is a straight extension of a layout that already worked, with the one wiring risk (commit on close) pinned by a headless click test before anyone used it. ⚠ The GUI round trip — type a caption in Information, export, `exiftool` the export and the original — is the user's to confirm; the app was launched for it.
- ⚠ Two files this milestone edits were already over the 600-line mark and grew a little more: `crates/catalog/src/db.rs` (~3,300 with its tests) and `crates/app/src/batch_export.rs` (~1,600). Neither was split; both are named here so the next split knows.

**Auto-advance after rating (M6.5c, 2026-09-07) — and the correction the spec needed before a line was written. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE (2026-09-08).** Rating a photo moves on to the next one, so a culling pass is one keystroke per frame instead of two. 2024 → **2035 tests**; every golden byte-identical, which is the proof no pixel path was touched. Spec `2026-09-07-auto-advance-design.md`, plan `2026-09-07-auto-advance.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE SPEC SAID "THE GRID AND THE FILMSTRIP" AND THE FILMSTRIP CANNOT RATE ANYTHING.** `interaction::culling_key` has exactly **three** callers — `library.rs`, `compare.rs`, `survey.rs` — and `filmstrip.rs` contains no `key_pressed` at all. That is not an oversight in the filmstrip: it follows from this file's own rule resolving the digit collision, *digits cull in Library, select tools in Develop*, and the filmstrip lives in Develop. **A third of the named scope did not exist**, found by grepping for the caller before writing the plan rather than by trying to implement it.
- **⚠⚠ THE ONE REAL HAZARD IS AN ORDERING ONE, AND IT IS NOW UNREPRESENTABLE RATHER THAN COMMENTED.** `RustroomApp::apply_mark` read its targets **out of the live selection**, after the frame — and auto-advance *moves* the selection. Rating a run of five frames would have rated the photo you moved *to* and left the run untouched: a data bug with nothing on screen to announce it. `library.rs`'s deferred local is now `Option<(Vec<PhotoId>, MarkAction)>`, so the ids are captured in the same expression that records the mark. **A mark without its targets cannot be constructed.** ⚠ `apply_mark` is deleted and its reasoning moved to `Library::selected_ids`, so exactly one place still decides which photos a culling key acts on. ⚠ Its sabotage — capture the targets *after* the advance — fails with `left: None, right: Some(3)`: the selected row was never rated.
- **⚠⚠ THE ADVANCE RUNS *BEFORE* THE MARK IS APPLIED, AND THAT IS WHAT LETS THE VIEW FOLLOW ON THE SAME FRAME.** `centre_on` is consumed when the `ScrollArea` is built, which happens after the input block and before anything deferred; advancing afterwards would need a field to carry the follow target to the next frame. **It is safe because of what `retain_visible` prunes**: `apply_mark_to` ends in `reload_marks` → `refilter` → `retain_visible`, which drops photos the *new* marks hid — which is the run just rated, no longer selected. The photo advanced *to* was not rated, so no filter change can reach it.
- **⚠ In the grid it steps past the WHOLE selection, not one from the active photo.** `apply_mark` deliberately acts on the selection — *"select a run of frames and press X"* — so a single step leaves you standing inside the run you just rated. `grid_nav::advance_past` takes the largest selected **visible** index and calls `step_by(.., 1)`. ⚠ `visible` is sorted ascending, so the largest selected visible index *is* the last one in visible order; a second notion of "last" is how the two would drift. ⚠ It clamps rather than wrapping, `step_by`'s rule: being thrown to the top of a folder mid-cull is disorienting, and the end of the list is information.
- **⚠⚠ IN COMPARE IT IS LITERALLY THE ARROW KEY'S STEP, AND THE TWO NOW SHARE ONE BLOCK.** The arrow loop used to carry its own copy of "step the bordered pane"; both it and auto-advance set a `step_forward: Option<bool>` and **one** block below acts on it. ⚠ That is what the sabotage proves: forcing it to always step the candidate fails `rating_advances_the_bordered_pane` **and** the pre-existing `the_arrows_move_the_bordered_pane` — one block, two guards. ⚠ Every pre-existing arrow test passed **unchanged** through the fold, which is the evidence it moved no behaviour.
- **⚠⚠ THIS IS NOT THE REMOVAL THAT WAS REVERSED ON 2026-09-02, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE REASON COMPARE IS IN SCOPE.** `P` once took the picked photo *out of the comparison*, which destroys the thing Compare exists for. **Removal takes a frame out; advancing steps to the next frame to compare against** — which is what the arrows already do.
- **⚠ Rating the reference advances the reference** (user's call in the spec). The border already means *"this is the pane the keys act on"*; advancing the candidate whichever pane was rated would move a pane the user did not act on, and refusing to advance on the reference would make the border a half-truth again — the exact fault round three of Compare fixed.
- **⚠⚠ `MarkAction::advances()` LIVES BESIDE THE TYPE, AND `Rating(0)` / `Flag::None` RETURN FALSE.** They are **corrections**: they clear a judgement rather than making one, and advancing off a photo you are in the middle of fixing is the one thing that would make this annoying rather than fast. ⚠ Survey already records the same reasoning about the same key — *"un-flagging undoes a decision, so a photo made undecided again belongs back on the wall"*. ⚠ **A deliberate divergence from Lightroom**, which advances on every rating including 0. ⚠ The test asserts over the **whole** vocabulary rather than one example, because a guard that enumerates a list stops guarding the moment the list grows — the sharpest finding of the masked-grade milestone, applied here in advance.
- **⚠ `Settings.auto_advance` defaults to TRUE**, and the correction rule is what makes that safe: a culling feature nobody finds is a feature that did not ship, and the case that usually argues for defaulting off is answered by `advances()` instead. ⚠ **No field-level `#[serde(default)]`** — that would use `bool`'s own default of `false` and silently ship the feature *disabled* for everyone who already has a settings file, which is most people. The trap `write_xmp` and `snapshot_strip_height` both document; sabotage-verified. ⚠ The exhaustive `Settings { .. }` literal forced the field at compile time, as it did for `drag_quality` the same day.
- **⚠ Survey is untouched.** It is already subtractive — `P` and `X` drop the tile off the wall — and adding advancing on top would mean a rated tile both stays on the wall *and* moves the focus, which is two behaviours for one act.
- **⚠⚠ A SABOTAGE THAT DID NOT APPLY REPORTED `1 passed`, WHICH IS THE RECORDED TRAP ARRIVING AGAIN.** The scripted edit's second pattern matched **twice** (the arrow block and the advance block have byte-identical closing structure after `cargo fmt`), so the `assert count == 1` fired, the file was never written, and the test ran against correct code. Caught only because the script printed its traceback. ⚠ **Anchor a scripted edit on something unique to the block being changed** — here, the `advance_past` call itself — and print the traceback rather than swallowing it.
- **⚠⚠ AND A PROCESS DEFECT THAT DID NOT REACH A COMMIT BUT WOULD HAVE REACHED THE NEXT SESSION'S: THE PLAN EMBEDDED A `Co-Authored-By` TRAILER IN ALL EIGHT OF ITS EXAMPLE COMMIT BLOCKS.** Every commit here is clean — checked, `git log origin/main..main` finds zero — but the *document telling a later agent how to commit* carried one, because both the writing-plans template and the harness's per-session attribution reminder show it. ⚠ **Every other plan in `docs/superpowers/plans/` opens with an explicit "no trailer, never push" header and this one did not**, which is exactly why the convention is written at the top of a plan rather than trusted to memory. Fixed in place, and the header added. **Grep any plan or doc for `Co-Authored-By` before committing it.**
- **⚠ The merged tree was verified by DIFF rather than by re-running the gates, and that is the stronger check.** The fast-forward's last three commits touched only `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/`, so `git diff <gated-commit> HEAD -- crates/ Cargo.toml Cargo.lock` came back **empty** — the merged code is byte-identical to what was gated. Re-running could only have confirmed what the diff already proved. ⚠ This does **not** license skipping the merged-tree run after a real *merge*: it holds because a fast-forward changes no code by construction, which the diff is what establishes.
- **⚠ `crates/app/src/library.rs` is 1,125 lines**, up from 970 and well past the ~600 marker; ~35 production lines and ~110 test lines of that are this milestone's. Reported rather than silently added to. `crates/app/src/settings.rs` is likewise 1,859.
- **⚠ Deliberately NOT changed: Compare's `INSTRUCTION` line**, which still reads *"digits, P, X and U rate it"*. Advancing is a setting that can be off, and a line that is true only half the time is worse than one that under-describes.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **rating the last photo in the list does not move** — the clamp, over wrapping; (2) **advancing past a multi-selection can jump a long way** — rate forty selected frames and you land after all forty, which is the stated rule and better than landing inside the run; (3) **Compare's reference advancing changes the pool the candidate steps through**, since the pool is "everything except the reference" — inherent to stepping the reference at all, which the arrows already do; (4) ⚠ **with a mark filter active, Compare's pool is computed before the mark is applied**, so rating the bordered pane can advance to a photo the filter has just hidden; it corrects itself at the next refilter, and the grid does not have this because its advance is recomputed against the live visible list; (5) a deliberate divergence from Lightroom on `0` and `U`; (6) **Survey keeps its own idiom**, so the app has two culling behaviours — subtractive there, advancing elsewhere — justified by what each view is *for* and recorded here so it is not later filed as an inconsistency. ✅ **Confirmed by use (2026-09-08)** — reported working. ⚠⚠ **The second milestone in this file's record whose GUI wiring produced no defects at all**, after M7.2. Do not read that as the class of bug being over; read it as both features being a rule applied through code paths the arrows had already proved — the grid's `step_by`-then-`centre_on` pair and Compare's own step block.

**The hue LUT (2026-09-05) — and the measurement that reshaped the milestone before a line was written. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** `HslStage` is ~1.5–1.7× faster and a photograph is unchanged. 1982 → **1991 tests**, **all 55 goldens byte-identical throughout**, which is the whole acceptance test — this changes how the stage computes, not what it computes. Spec `2026-09-05-hue-lut-design.md`, plan `2026-09-05-hue-lut.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE MILESTONE WAS SCOPED ON A BELIEF THAT MEASURING DEMOLISHED, AND IT WAS THE THIRD TIME HSL'S COST HAD BEEN MIS-REASONED HERE.** M7.2 recorded the cost as `atan2`/`cos`/`sin` and the fix as a LUT worth ~2×, from comparing HSL (+34.9 ms) against colour grading (+15.0 ms) — same Oklab round trip — and attributing the ~20 ms gap to the transcendentals, because they are the visible difference in the source. **Nobody looked inside `band_weights`.** Decomposed (`bench_hsl`, kept), above a harness floor:

  | step | cost | share |
  |---|---|---|
  | Oklab round trip — **irreducible, untouched** | 7.3–9.6 ms | ~27% |
  | chroma via `hypot()` | 0.6–2.6 ms | ~10% |
  | hue via `atan2()` | 3.4–3.5 ms | ~13% |
  | **band weighting** | **11.2–11.9 ms** | **~42%** |
  | chroma fade + `cos`/`sin` | 2.6–3.1 ms | ~10% |

  **The band weighting was the largest single cost — larger than the Oklab round trip — and `cos`/`sin`, the thing the LUT was named for, was under 10%.** The earlier spec first called masked HSL "per-pixel and nearly free", then claimed ~4×, then ~2× from `cos`/`sin`. Every one was reasoning where a measurement was available; **the decomposition took twenty minutes.**
- **⚠⚠ FLOAT `%` IS `fmodf`, A LIBM CALL, AND IT WAS IN THE HOT LOOP.** `band_weights` computed `(x + 360.0) % 360.0` **per band** until it found the bracketing pair — ~7 `fmodf` per pixel — and the `span` it recomputed each iteration is a **constant of `BAND_HUES`**. The caller then summed **24 multiply-adds over an array with exactly two non-zero entries.** ⚠ Removing all three is worth **−23%** and has no design content in it at all.
- **⚠⚠ AND `(x + 360) % 360` DOES NOT ROUND-TRIP IN f32 — measured, not assumed, over 3.6 M hues before anything was changed.** Adding 360 to a value near 330 moves it into a binade with twice the exponent spacing, and subtracting 360 does not recover it. **4 of the 8 spans differ**; **59% of weight vectors differ bit-for-bit** with a **worst difference of 1.07e-6** (0.0003 of an 8-bit level); and **exactly one hue in 3.6 M selects a different band pair**, at a boundary where that weight is ~0 so the output is continuous. **So the new code is the more accurate one**, and the goldens stayed byte-identical — checked, not hoped.
- **⚠⚠ THE ROTATION IDENTITY: `cos(hue)` AND `sin(hue)` WERE ALREADY IN HAND — THEY ARE `a/chroma` AND `b/chroma`.** Expanding `cos(hue + δ)` and multiplying by the new chroma, **the chroma cancels exactly**: `a' = k·(a·cos δ − b·sin δ)`, `b' = k·(a·sin δ + b·cos δ)`. Since `δ` is bounded to ±30° by `MAX_HUE_SHIFT_DEG`, `cos δ` and `sin δ` are 3-term Taylor polynomials (errors 1.4e-7 and 2.1e-6), not libm calls. It also drops the `atan2 → cos` round trip the old code performed, so the rotation is **more accurate as well as cheaper**; `hue` survives only as a table index.
- **⚠⚠ THE POLYNOMIALS' BOUND IS AN ASSUMPTION HELD BY A CONSTANT THAT DOES NOT MENTION THEM, SO IT IS A COMPILE-TIME ASSERTION.** `const _: () = assert!(MAX_HUE_SHIFT_DEG <= 30.0)`. Raise that constant and the approximation degrades **smoothly and silently** — there is no value at which a test suddenly fails. Same instrument as `MID_BUDGET_BYTES` and `MIN_RADIUS`, and it is the family this file already records: *a constant chosen for one reason moves once something else changes.*
- **⚠ A REAL BEHAVIOURAL DIFFERENCE, FOUND WHILE PLANNING RATHER THAN AFTER: the truncated pair does not satisfy `cos² + sin² = 1` exactly**, so a *pure hue shift* now moves chroma by ~1e-6 where the shipped code left it **bit-exact**. Two tests pin that budget — one on the polynomials, one on a pixel — because it is the kind of thing that otherwise gets discovered as a bug.
- **⚠⚠ THE TABLE HOLDS THE PARAMETERS, NOT A FINISHED ROTATION FACTOR, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING.** The chroma fade multiplies `dh`/`sat`/`lum` **before** they are used, so a table of finished factors would be valid only where `chroma ≥ CHROMA_FADE` and would need a second code path below it — and **on a dark or desaturated frame that "slow" path becomes the common one**, making the stage's cost depend on the picture. One table, one path, no branch. ⚠ It also makes interpolation across the 360°→0° seam simply correct; a table of complex factors would interpolate along a chord rather than an arc.
- **⚠⚠ SIZING THE TABLE CORRECTED THE DESIGN TWICE.** (1) The plan said pick the smallest `n` whose worst 8-bit drift is **0**. **No `n` reaches 0, 4096 included** — a worst of 1 is a handful of samples sitting exactly on a quantisation boundary, inherent to changing the last bits at all rather than a property of the table. What the size controls is the *share*: 0.0205% at 64, **0.0001% at 1024**, 0.0000% at 4096. (2) The worry that a 48 KB table would fall out of L1 and cost more than it saves was **unfounded** — cost is flat across the whole sweep, because hue is spatially coherent so the access stays cached. **1024** ships: the accuracy difference above it is invisible and the table is built **per mask** as well as globally.
- **⚠ `with_table_size` exists because nothing else can see the table at all.** A stage that reads a table and one that evaluates per pixel agree **by construction**, so the only observable consequence is a degenerate table: a **one-entry** table is forced to treat every hue alike, which a per-pixel evaluation would not. Its sabotage (hardcoding 4 entries) fails four tests.
- **⚠⚠ THE BENCH'S OWN CROSS-CHECK CAUGHT ITSELF GOING STALE, WHICH IS WHY IT COMPARES PIXELS AND NOT DURATIONS.** `pipeline::stages` was private, so `bench_hsl` could not call the real stage and instead asserted its *copied* kernel was bit-identical to it. The moment the stage changed, that assertion fired — correctly reporting the reference was stale before the table sweep could measure the wrong thing. `bench_hsl` now holds **three** kernels, each answering one question: the original (the ablation's baseline), an exact direct evaluation (the drift ground truth, so the sweep isolates the *table's* error from the rotation's), and the shipped one.
- **⚠ `lib.rs` re-exports `hue_table` ALONE rather than opening `stages`.** The plan said `pub mod stages;`, which would have made **every** stage public API for the sake of one benchmark. One `pub use stages::hue_table;` is the narrow version. ⚠ Consequence worth knowing: `band_weights` has no caller inside the crate and survives `-D warnings` only *because* of that re-export — remove it and the function needs `#[cfg(test)]`.
- **⚠⚠ THE DEAD-CODE TRAP FIRED TWICE AND THE PLAN WAS WRONG ABOUT IT BOTH TIMES.** `sin_small`/`cos_small` and `HueTable` are `pub` in a **private** module, so until their caller exists they are dead code under `-D warnings` — the trap this file records for *binary* crates, reproduced inside a library by a private module. Tasks 2+3 and 4+5 each had to land as **one** commit. ⚠ And the first commit of the milestone went in **with clippy failing**, because the script printed `CLIPPY=101` and committed anyway; every later commit is gated on both exit codes. **At least the tenth recorded instance of clippy catching what a green suite did not.**
- **Measured result** (X-T4, 1600 px fit, release, best of 7). ⚠ The machine is noisy — the *original* measured 29.1 and 33.8 ms above floor on two runs — so the ratio is quoted as a range rather than a single figure:

  | | above harness floor | vs original |
  |---|---|---|
  | original stage | 29.1–33.8 ms | — |
  | **shipped stage** | **19.7–20.4 ms** | **1.47–1.66×** |

  And what a user touches, from `bench_masked`: **one masked HSL at fit +33.1 → +23.0 ms (−30%)**; **three masked HSL +99.3 → +72.2 ms (−27%)**. ⚠ **Under the ~1.75× the spec predicted** — the table lookup plus rotation costs more than the ~2.5 ms estimated.
- **⚠ A photograph is unchanged, measured on real pixels rather than only on the charts:** against the original kernel, **worst 1 of 255, mean 0.00000, 0.0001% of samples differing**. ✅ **And confirmed by use the same day** — the GUI check was run and reported good. ⚠ The three things it was pointed at, because they are where a bug in *this* change would hide and no test here covers them well: **the 360°→0° seam in reds and magentas** (a wraparound bug shows as a hue discontinuity, not a shift), **±100 hue shifts on saturated colour** (the outer edge of the polynomials' domain), and **pastels and skin** (the chroma-fade region, where a wrong band selection reads as speckle rather than as a colour change).
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the Oklab round trip is untouched and is now the largest remaining cost at ~27%** — cutting it means approximating `cbrt`, a different accuracy question affecting every Oklab consumer (`hsl`, `noise_reduction`, `sharpening`, `local_contrast`, `color_grading`); (2) **`atan2` stays**, ~13% — a table indexed by a *pseudo-angle* would delete it, since a table can be built over any monotonic parameterisation of hue, and it was deliberately not taken because it needs a sampling-density argument the uniform table does not; (3) a pure hue shift moves chroma by ~1e-6 where it used to be bit-exact; (4) **the `hypot` → `sqrt` change may be worth nothing** — it measured −0.47 ms on one run and **+0.4 ms on another**, i.e. inside noise, and it is kept because it is also the simpler code; (5) nothing here helps colour grading, which already has its LUT.

**The interactive rung (2026-09-07) — a responsive drag on a large window, and the measurement that demolished the question it was asked to answer. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** A live gesture renders one or two ladder rungs lower and the release restores full quality: **137.9 ms → 40.0 ms**, 7.2 → 25 fps. 2017 → **2024 tests**; every golden byte-identical, which is the proof no pixel path moved. Spec `2026-09-07-interactive-rung-design.md`, plan `2026-09-07-interactive-rung.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE REPORTED BUG WAS "DRAGGING A MASKED EXPOSURE STUTTERS" AND THE MASK HAD NOTHING TO DO WITH IT.** Three rounds of measurement were spent inside `MaskStage` before the reporter closed it in one sentence: *"the global exposure actually exhibits the same behavior."* **`bench_masked` now prices a masked exposure at every mask kind, and none of it was the answer** — the clone is **0.4 ms of a 3.0 ms stage** where the backlog's headline suspect had been the clone; the blend is 60% and costs more than twice the adjustment it blends. ⚠ **A question that names a component is a hypothesis, not a scope.** The one datum that settled it cost the reporter a sentence and no measurement at all.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE MASK-KIND SPREAD IS A REAL FINDING THAT IS NOT THIS BUG.** Identical adjustment, identical pixels: gradient **+2.7 ms**, colour range **+16.9 ms** — 6×, because `hue_chroma` runs `prophoto_to_oklab` (three `cbrt`), `atan2` and `hypot` on **every pixel in the frame** to decide coverage, ahead of a one-multiply exposure. **A range mask's coverage is a pure function of the `base` pixel, which during a tail-only drag is the unchanging cached prefix output** — so it could be rasterized once and reused, exactly as `BrushCache` already does for a brush. Backlogged, not built.
- **⚠⚠ THE ROOT CAUSE, AND IT WAS INVISIBLE TO EVERY BENCHMARK IN THE REPO.** `fit_cap` is 1600, but `tier_max_dim` climbs a **doubling ladder** as soon as the viewport exceeds the fit texture — so a photo that *looks* fitted renders whole-image at **rung 3200, 6.8 Mpx**. `RUSTROOM_DEBUG_RENDER=1` over 167 real frames: **137.9 ms mean, 7.2 fps**, against **35.2 ms** at the fit rung. The time ratio (3.9×) matches the pixel ratio (4.0×), which is what identifies the rung rather than something incidental to it. ⚠ **`prefix_ran 0` on all 167 frames** closed the prefix, the projection and the checkpoint chain in one column.
- **⚠⚠ THE BENCH UNDERSTATED THE APP BY 2.6× — 53 ms predicted against 137.9 measured.** `bench_masked` renders a near-neutral photo; a real session carries the whole tail. **A synthetic bench prices a stage, not a session.** Read its rows as *deltas between rows*, never as a prediction of a drag frame.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE HIGH RUNG IS NOT WASTE, WHICH CORRECTS A FRAMING THIS WORK BRIEFLY ADOPTED.** `needed` is the viewport's long edge **in points**, so rung 3200 on a 2× display is *upsampled* — the 2026-08-13 spec measured the same thing and called it "a 1.44× residual". The 137.9 ms buys resolution the display can use. **There was nothing to reclaim**, which is why the fix trades resolution rather than removing waste.
- **⚠⚠ THIS FEATURE WAS BUILT AND DELETED ONCE — AND ITS DELETION REASON IS THE ARGUMENT FOR REBUILDING IT.** `2026-08-13-progressive-fit-resolution-design.md` rendered at 1600 while interacting and sharpened when idle; it was removed because *"the user could not see it on or off"*. **The question then was "is the sharper render visible?" (no). The question now is "is the slower render felt?" (yes.)** If a higher rung is not visibly sharper in a still frame, dropping one during a gesture costs little and buys 4×. ⚠ Honestly: that was measured at a **1.44× residual** and this drop is **2× linear**, so it is suggestive rather than conclusive. ⚠ **The problem also worsened since**: that spec's own table put rung 3200 at **59.5 ms**, against today's 137.9 — the tail has roughly doubled with display rendering, creative looks, masks, colour grading and soft proofing.
- **⚠⚠ THE GATE IS A QUESTION ABOUT THE POINTER, NEVER ABOUT THE RENDER — and that is what makes it structurally unable to oscillate.** The deleted feature asked *"is the showing resolution not yet sharp?"*, which the render itself answers: so the render answered it, the gate flipped, and it counted that as interaction and reset its own timer. `is_decidedly_dragging()` cannot be affected by anything a render does. ⚠ It also **excludes a plain click** (`!could_any_button_be_click() && !any_click()`), which is what stops a click costing two renders — verified in egui 0.35's source, not assumed.
- **⚠⚠ NO SETTLE TIMER, unlike the deleted feature's 200 ms.** `canvas/mod.rs`'s `max_dim != s.render_rung` disjunction already re-requests on any rung change, so the release edge triggers the full-quality render by itself, one frame later. **Do not "simplify" that disjunction away** — it is also what keeps a stale `rendered_region`, measured at a *different* rung, from being compared as though it were current.
- **⚠⚠ `rung_below` REBUILDS THE LADDER AND STEPS BACK; IT MUST NEVER HALVE.** The ladder doubles from `fit_cap` and **clamps** at `buffer_long`, so its top rung is **6246, not 6400** — halving gives **3123, a value `tier_max_dim` can never produce**. Feeding one to the renderer mints a new `max_dim`, hence a new `CacheKey`, and grows the prefix cache's key set without bound **while looking entirely correct on screen**. Sabotage-verified: a naive halving fails three tests, one reporting `3123` against `3200`.
- **⚠ `native` is computed from the DROPPED `max_dim`, the *opposite* of the 2026-08-13 spec's §2 warning** — and correct, because this override goes **down** where that one went up. Theirs could report native at fit zoom and make the whole image vanish; this one cannot.
- **⚠⚠ `Settings.drag_quality` IS THE DELETION MECHANISM, and that was the user's call rather than the design's.** `DragQuality { Full, Faster, Fastest }` → 0 / 1 / 2 rungs. **`Full` is zero, so when the GPU port lands the feature is switched off from Settings rather than removed from the code.** It also lets a machine pick its own point on the trade. ⚠ `n == 0` returns the rung unchanged, so **the disabled path is the same code** rather than a branch around it. ⚠ `Fastest` equals `Faster` in the fitted view, differing only when zoomed — a two-rung drop from 3200 clamps at `fit_cap`.
- **⚠⚠ DOES THE GPU PORT MAKE THIS REDUNDANT? Substantially, and it is still worth having** — the M7.2 note's three tests, applied again. The tail at 6.8 Mpx is the ideal GPU workload, realistically 5–15×, and a GPU tail deletes `pack` and the upload as well. **But Phase 8 is two phases away**; this *is* Phase 8's opening move, which is literally "cheap wins, then `wgpu`"; and it survives the port, since three masked HSLs is ~330 ms at 6.8 Mpx and still 33 ms after a 10× win. ⚠ The honest counter-argument, recorded: after the port the fitted case stops needing this, so the setting is what makes carrying it cheap.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT SHAPED THE FEATURE SAYS A SCALE-AWARE STAGE CAN BE PROPORTIONATE AND STILL INVISIBLE.** `rung_visibility` (kept) renders neutral against edited **within** each rung. Sharpening 70: **0.419 → 0.001 mean 8-bit levels between 3200 and 1600 — 420× weaker, 0.14% of samples moved.** It vanishes. Texture drops 3.1× and survives; clarity is only 8% weaker (σ 40 full-res px is still 10 px at 1600); NR and dehaze barely move. ⚠ **The control row is what makes the rest believable**: exposure has no kernel and reads flat to three decimals across the ladder, so the harness is sound before any spatial row is trusted. ⚠ The collapse is specific to the drop **into** the fit rung — zoomed, 6246 → 3200 leaves sharpening at 37.88% moved.
- **✅ CONFIRMED BY USE (2026-09-07), and the instrumented log shows the mechanism inverting the frame distribution.** 390 frames: **357 at rung 1600, mean 40.0 ms (25 fps)** during drags, against **31 at rung 3200** for releases and idle. The pre-fix log was the mirror image — **131 at 3200, 2 at 1600**. Reported as *"much better now, no stuttering anymore"*.
- **⚠⚠ `FINALS_CAP` AT 2 HELD, WHICH THE 2026-08-13 SPEC CONCLUDED IT WOULD NOT.** `prefix_ran 0` on **all 390 frames**, so alternating between rungs 1600 and 3200 never evicted a prefix. That spec raised it to 3 because its three rungs were live *within one mode*; ours are two per mode, and the instrument is what turned that from an argument into a fact. **+80 MB not spent.**
- **⚠⚠ THE VARIANCE WAS THEN MEASURED, AND IT IS NOT IN THE RENDER PATH — plus a correction to this file's own number.** "27.0 → 268.4 ms" is a **range statistic driven by one frame** and overstated it: the real distribution over 357 drag frames is **median 33.4 ms, p75 41.7**, with 92% between 27 and 60. ⚠ This file already records *"a whole-image mean is the wrong statistic"* twice; `max` is worse. **Separating the rungs matters too** — the last decile's alarming 93.6 ms was 18 legitimate full-rung *release* frames, not jitter.
- **⚠⚠ 156 CONSECUTIVE DRAG FRAMES AT 30–32 ms WITH ZERO OUTLIERS**, in the quiet middle of the session. The render path is steady at ~32 fps, so there is nothing to optimise in it. The bursts have two identified sources: **background contention** (frames 2–3 render at rung **3123**, the half-size buffer's top rung, so the full-resolution decode had not yet landed — ~3 s of libraw plus `ThumbLoader`'s 6 workers, which is exactly what that loader's own note predicted) and **rung transitions** at **103.8 ms mean against 48.1 ms**, with `prefix_ran 0` on both, pointing at allocator cost when the buffer size alternates rather than at the pipeline. ⚠ **`RUSTROOM_DEBUG_RENDER` logs no timestamps**, so "front-loaded" is inferred from frame index — add one before theorising further.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **sharpening reads at nothing mid-drag** and appears on release — the user's call, one gate over a per-slider table, and `CLAUDE.md` already directs that it be judged at 1:1 where the drop keeps it visible; (2) texture drops 3.1×; (3) **the image is visibly softer during a gesture** — at `Faster` roughly a 2.9× upsample on a large HiDPI window; that is the trade, not a side effect; (4) the first drag frame may render at the full rung, usually coalesced away; (5) `Fastest` equals `Faster` when fitted; (6) **`FINALS_CAP` stays at 2** against the old spec's 3 — its three rungs were live within one mode, ours are two per mode — and `prefix_ran` is the instrument that says whether that holds; (7) nothing here makes the tail faster, it renders less of it.

**Masked noise reduction and dehaze (2026-09-06) — Part B, and the whole "rest of the stages inside a mask" request closes with it. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** A mask carries **noise reduction** and **dehaze**, running in the **prefix** where the global pair run. 2003 → **2017 tests**, 57 → **59 goldens** with the 57 **byte-identical**. Spec `2026-09-06-masked-capture-corrections-design.md`, plan `2026-09-06-masked-capture-corrections.md`, `2026-09-07-interactive-rung.md`, `2026-09-07-auto-advance.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE RECORDED BLOCKER WAS FACTUALLY WRONG, AND CORRECTING IT WAS MOST OF THE MILESTONE.** `BACKLOG.md`, this file, M7.2's spec **and Part A's spec written the day before** all said NR and dehaze *"live in the prefix, **before geometry**"*, and concluded that a prefix-side mask would need coverage *"at prefix resolution before geometry — a different coordinate space from the one every mask is authored in."* **They run AFTER geometry.** `build_prefix_steps`' own tripwire spells the order out: `perspective → spot_removal → geometry → noise_reduction → dehaze`. The prefix output **is** the post-geometry image — this file says so in its own region-rendering section — which is exactly the space masks are authored in. **The load-bearing half of the blocker was never true, and it had propagated through four documents.**
- **⚠ The second objection was weaker than it read.** *"A masked one runs after the tone curve — a different operation from the global stage wearing the same name."* That is already true of **every** masked adjustment: global exposure runs before the global tone curve and masked exposure runs after it, because `MaskStage` sits in the tail. Accepted since M3.1a. It was never a property of noise reduction.
- **⚠⚠ THE REAL BLOCKER WAS DIFFERENT AND DEHAZE-ONLY: THE AIRLIGHT.** `MaskStage::apply` runs its sub-stages on **the buffer it is handed**, which in a region render is the *region*; `dehaze::airlight` reads the brightest 0.1% of a whole `ImageBuf`. **So a tail-side masked dehaze would re-estimate its airlight from whatever is on screen, and the photograph would change as you panned.** The global dehaze escapes this only because the prefix always runs whole-buffer — its own code comment says exactly that. **Placement answers it rather than working around it.**
- **⚠⚠ AND THE MASTER REGION INVARIANT IS WHAT PROVES THE PLACEMENT.** Masked NR and dehaze joined `a_region_render_matches_the_whole_render_cropped`'s edit set, **with the inset untouched** — they are not in the tail, so they contribute no reach. Moving the capture stages into the tail fails it with *"the region's interior must match the whole render bit for bit"*. ⚠ A weaker sabotage — a *constant* wrong region on the capture stage — **passed**, and that is a finding about the test rather than about the code: the invariant applies the same wrong region to both sides, so they still agree. What actually makes the bug unrepresentable is structural — `build_prefix_steps` has no region to give.
- **⚠⚠ MEASURED, AND THE SECOND COLUMN IS THE POINT.** X-T4, 3123×2085 half-size buffer, release, best of 5. Deltas against neutral (14.7 ms fit / 7.1 ms native region tail):

  | edit | fit, whole image @1600 | native region tail 1400×900 |
  |---|---|---|
  | **1 masked NR** (gradient) | **+29.9 ms** | **−0.8 ms** |
  | **1 masked dehaze** (gradient) | **+28.7** | **−0.3** |
  | 1 masked NR (**small radial**) | +27.9 | −0.3 |
  | *1 masked HSL, for scale* | +22.8 | +17.9 |
  | *1 masked grade, for scale* | +14.3 | +10.4 |

- **⚠⚠ THE NATIVE COLUMN READS ZERO, AND THAT IS THE MEASUREMENT THAT PROVES THE DESIGN.** Both stages are in the prefix, so the tail pays **nothing** for them — the small negatives are noise about zero. Masked HSL, in the tail, costs +17.9 ms on *every* render by comparison. The honest summary: **~29 ms when their own slider moves, and literally nothing on every other frame.** That is what the placement buys over and above the airlight correctness it was chosen for.
- **⚠⚠ THE PROJECTION IS WHAT STOPS THE PREFIX CACHE THRASHING.** `PrefixStepKey::MaskedCapture` holds a `capture_projection` of the mask — its shape plus those two fields — **not** the whole `MaskedAdjustment`. Keyed on the whole thing, every masked-*exposure* drag would invalidate the checkpoint and re-run a whole-buffer denoise. The test pins **both** directions, and only the "does not change" half can see it. ⚠ Its sabotage fails with *"a masked EXPOSURE change must not re-run a whole-buffer denoise"*.
- **⚠⚠ THE DOUBLE-APPLICATION SABOTAGE IS INERT HERE, AND SAYING SO IS BETTER THAN COUNTING IT.** Passing the real `noise_reduction`/`dehaze` from the tail's literal changes nothing, because **`adjustment_stages` does not build either stage** — verified, not assumed. The neutral pass is therefore **defence in depth rather than load-bearing**, and the real guard is `the_two_mask_builders_do_not_overlap`, which asserts the two builders **partition** a mask's adjustments. *That* sabotage — teaching `adjustment_stages` to build NR — fails with *"noise_reduction is built by both builders"*.
- **⚠⚠ THE PLAN WAS WRONG ABOUT THE BRUSH COVERAGE AND THE CODE SAID SO.** It had `build_prefix_steps` take an `Option<&BrushCoverage>` threaded from the render engine. **That cannot work**: the engine's `BrushCache` is sized from the *prefix output's* dimensions, which do not exist until the prefix has run. `MaskStage::apply` already rasterizes its own coverage when handed `None`, and with `region: None` that is the whole frame at exactly the right size — **at no per-frame cost, because the whole step is cached**. The parameter was added, found unbuildable, and removed.
- **⚠⚠ CLIPPY CAUGHT WHAT A GREEN SUITE DID NOT, AT LEAST THE ELEVENTH TIME.** `PrefixStepKey::MaskedCapture` is **296 bytes against the next-largest variant's 25**, so `large_enum_variant` fired — every entry in a `PrefixKey`'s `Vec` would have been that size, including the zero-sized ones. Boxed, the same answer `ActionKind::Sync` already gives.
- **⚠⚠ AND A DOCUMENTED TRAP RECURRED FOR THE SAME REASON AS LAST TIME.** `the_tail_native_sliders_edit_the_selected_mask` failed with *"dragging Amount reported no edit"* because five new sliders pushed Sharpening's Amount past the bottom of the **700 pt** harness screen. `test_ui::screen`'s own doc records that exact failure from when the Geometry panel grew by two rows: *"a widget laid out past the bottom of this rect is simply outside the window … the test reads as 'the button did nothing'."* Raised to **1400**, which is the accurate model of "the user scrolled to it" rather than a workaround.
- **⚠ Panel order deliberately does NOT follow pipeline order for these two, and `mask_adjust.rs`'s own comment had to be rewritten to say so.** Both run first in the pipeline, so strict order would put them above White Balance; they sit where the **global panel** puts them instead — Dehaze with Texture and Clarity, noise reduction above Sharpening — on this file's own rule that *panel follows workflow, pipeline follows maths*. "One layout learned once" decides it.
- **⚠ Two label collisions, kept deliberately.** A mask's section now has two sliders called **"Detail"** (noise reduction's and Sharpening's) and two called **"Luminance"** (noise reduction's and the colour grade's). The global Detail section already ships the first pair, separated the same way by sub-labels — parity beats uniqueness, since the alternative names one control two different things in two panels. ⚠ The carrier test therefore names only **Dehaze, Color and Smoothness**, which are unique: `test_ui` takes the first match, so a test naming "Detail" could pass against the wrong slider.
- **⚠ Accepted and pinned: a range mask selects on PRE-TONAL pixels in its capture half.** Coverage is computed from the buffer the stage is applied to, and the capture half runs before every tonal adjustment — so a luminance range selects the tones of the *scene* while the same mask's tail half selects post-tonal. One mask, two selections. For a correction of the *capture* that is arguably the more correct answer, and it is what the global stages already do. `a_luminance_range_selects_pre_tonal_tones_in_the_capture_half` makes it a documented property rather than something found later and filed as a bug.
- **⚠ A guard the goldens cannot give on their own.** A stage that never ran would produce a reference identical to a neutral render of the same chart, and the golden would pass against it forever — which is how creative LUTs shipped dead. `a_masked_capture_correction_really_changes_the_chart` renders each case against the *same chart with the mask removed* and asserts a substantial fraction of samples moved.
- **⚠ `mask_adjust.rs` is 440 lines, UP from 299**; `masks.rs` is unchanged at 624 and still over the ~600 marker.
- **✅ CONFIRMED BY USE (2026-09-06)**, masked dehaze included — which is the item that mattered, since **item 4 of the checklist (pan at 1:1 and check the masked area does not change) is the one no test fully stands in for.** Reported working.
- **⚠⚠ AND ONE REPORT THAT IS *NOT* THIS MILESTONE'S, BECAUSE THE PERSON REPORTING IT SAID SO: dragging a masked EXPOSURE "stutters a bit".** ⚠ Volunteered with the qualification that **it did so before this milestone, and does so without a masked dehaze active** — so it is a pre-existing property of masked adjustments generally, not a regression from the prefix work and not evidence about the projection. ⚠⚠ **That distinction was the reporter's, not a diagnosis made here, and it is the reason this is recorded as an observation rather than as a defect of Part B.** What is known: `MaskStage` **clones the whole image per mask**, so every drag frame pays a clone plus the sub-stages over the full frame — the same cost the bench shows as "three masks is ~3× one". Backlogged rather than scoped, because nobody has yet measured a masked *exposure* drag specifically; `bench_masked` has no row for it.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **whole-buffer by construction** — a small radial pays for the entire frame, measured at +27.9 against a frame-covering gradient's +29.9, which is the **strongest case yet for the deferred bounding box** and it is still deferred, a sixth time; (2) the range-mask asymmetry above; (3) two duplicated slider labels; (4) a mask's panel section is taller again; (5) **nothing here helps the first render after a photo opens** — the prefix cache is cold then, as always.

**Masked colour grading and the tone curve (2026-09-05) — Part A of "the rest of the stages inside a mask". ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE (2026-09-06).** A mask carries a **tone curve** and a **three-way colour grade** on top of everything M7.2 gave it. Dodge and burn with a curve; split-tone a sky; warm a face without warming the room behind it. 1991 → **2003 tests**, 55 → **57 goldens** with the 55 **byte-identical**. Spec `2026-09-05-masked-grade-and-curve-design.md`, plan `2026-09-05-masked-grade-and-curve.md`.

- **⚠⚠ IT RE-RAN M7.2's PLAYBOOK EXACTLY AND NO STRUCTURAL QUESTION AROSE**, which is what "tail-native" bought: `tail_kernel_radius`, `MaskStage`, `PrefixKey`, `EditGroups` and `merge_from` are all **untouched**, because neither stage reads a neighbour. M7.2's one structurally new part — the derived region margin — was simply not in scope.
- **⚠⚠ `LocalAdjustments` LOST `Copy`, AND THAT COST NOTHING — MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** `ToneCurve` holds a `Vec`, so `Copy` had to go; with both fields added and the derive changed, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` was **clean with zero call-site changes**. `MaskedAdjustment` has not been `Copy` since the brush landed, so nothing downstream depended on it. This was the one thing that could have made Part A structurally expensive, and a five-minute spike settled it before a line of the spec was written.
- **⚠⚠ THE DOC COMMENT ABOVE `LocalAdjustments` WAS STALE AND HAD BEEN SINCE M7.2 — a finding about the tree, not about this milestone.** It read *"the **per-pixel** adjustments, and only those … a spatial stage — noise reduction, sharpening, clarity, dehaze, HSL — … is out of scope here"* with `local_contrast`, `sharpening` and `hsl` declared **directly beneath it**. A comment that contradicts the fields it introduces. Rewritten to say what the set actually is — everything in the **tail** — and to name the **prefix** stages as the genuine remainder, with Part B's reason.
- **⚠⚠ THE COMPILE-TIME FORCING FUNCTION FIRED, WHICH IS THE BEST POSSIBLE PLACE FOR THE DECISION.** Adding the fields failed with `error[E0063]: missing fields \`color_grading\` and \`tone_curve\`` at `build_pipeline_split`'s exhaustive literal. The global tail places both stages itself, so it passes both **neutral**; `adjustment_stages`' doc now names **five** neutral-passed fields rather than three.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE DOUBLE-APPLICATION GUARD NAMED THREE OF FIVE, SO IT WAS BLIND TO THIS ENTIRE MILESTONE.** `the_global_tail_does_not_apply_the_mask_only_stages_twice` enumerates a **list** of stage names — and under the real sabotage (hand `edits.tone_curve` and `edits.color_grading` to the shared builder) it passed at **`1 passed`** while **six goldens failed**. Only the goldens caught it. ⚠ **A guard that enumerates a list stops guarding the moment the list grows, and the entries it silently drops are the newest ones — exactly the ones nobody has habits about yet.** Extended to all five and watched failing. **This is the single most useful finding of the milestone**, and it generalises to every enumerating guard in this repo.
- **⚠⚠ MEASURED, AND THE MEASUREMENT CORRECTED BOTH THE BACKLOG AND MY OWN SPEC.** X-T4, 3123×2085 half-size buffer, release, best of 5. `cargo run --release -p app --example bench_masked <raw>`, extended and kept.

  | edit | fit, whole image @1600 | native region tail 1400×900 |
  |---|---|---|
  | neutral | 12.2 ms | 6.0 ms |
  | **1 masked curve** (gradient) | 17.6 (**+5.4**) | 10.0 (**+4.0**) |
  | 3 masked curve | 29.4 (+17.2) | 17.8 (+11.8) |
  | **1 masked grade** (gradient) | 28.0 (**+15.8**) | 16.5 (**+10.5**) |
  | 3 masked grade | 59.0 (+46.8) | 40.3 (+34.3) |
  | 1 masked curve (**small radial**) | 18.9 (+6.7) | 10.5 (+4.5) |
  | 1 masked grade (**small radial**) | 28.8 (+16.6) | 18.6 (+12.6) |
  | *1 masked clarity, for scale* | 22.3 (+10.1) | 13.7 (+7.7) |
  | *1 masked HSL, for scale* | 35.9 (+23.7) | 23.6 (+17.6) |

- **⚠⚠ THE TONE CURVE IS THE CHEAPEST THING A MASK CAN CARRY, AND BOTH THE BACKLOG AND THE SPEC PREDICTED THE OPPOSITE.** The backlog flagged grading's 256-entry table as the thing to measure; the spec then "corrected" it by pointing out the curve builds a **1024**-entry one and was therefore the larger build. **Both were reasoning about the wrong quantity.** The table build is negligible at these magnitudes. What predicts a masked stage's cost is whether it does an **Oklab round trip**: curve (none) **+5.4**, clarity (spatial blurs) +10.1, grade (Oklab + LUT) **+15.8**, HSL (Oklab + `atan2` + table) +23.7. ⚠ That is the **fourth** time cost in this area has been reasoned wrong and measured right; the previous three were all HSL.
- **⚠ The curve was only ever more expensive in PANEL space**, which is what the collapsed blocks answer — and that half of the backlog's note was correct.
- **⚠⚠ THE NO-BOUNDING-BOX FINDING REPRODUCES ON BOTH NEW STAGES.** A small radial whose own bbox is ~5.8% of the frame costs **slightly more** than a frame-covering gradient — +6.7 against +5.4 for the curve, +16.6 against +15.8 for the grade — exactly as M7.2 measured for clarity and HSL. It is paying for 100% of the frame either way. The bounding box is deferred a **fifth** time, and the work is still in `MaskStage`, not in the masks.
- **⚠ Three masked grades take a fit render 12.2 → 59.0 ms, i.e. 17 fps**, and it is **almost exactly three times one mask** — so it is `MaskStage`'s per-mask clone and nothing shared. The row to watch in use.
- **⚠ Order inside a mask still mirrors the global chain**, now: wb → exposure → contrast → tone regions → **tone curve** → local contrast → saturation → hsl → **colour grading** → sharpening. The curve before local contrast because a local contrast measured before it would then be re-mapped by it; the grade after HSL because HSL corrects colours that are *in* the picture while a grade is laid on top. ⚠ Both have a test that asserts **order, not presence** — a masked stage in the wrong place still produces a plausible photograph, so a presence test would pass against the wrong chain.
- **⚠ `Blending` and `Balance` are carried inside a mask**, which is parity rather than padding: they are properties of the *range decomposition* shared by all four wheels, so omitting them would make a masked grade a different control from the global one wearing the same name — the Whites-slider failure. A deliberate choice, recorded so it is not later filed as bloat. Same reasoning M7.2 used for `Sharpening::masking`.
- **⚠ Both panel blocks are `CollapsingHeader`s, closed by default**, reusing `tone_curve::show` and `color_wheel::show` unchanged. ⚠ Their open/closed state lives in **egui's memory with the same id path for every mask, so it is shared across masks** — deliberate: you want the block to stay open while clicking through masks comparing them. ⚠ `RustroomApp` gained `mask_grade_range`, mirroring `mask_hsl_channel`, so a mask does not share the global panel's selected wheel.
- **⚠⚠ TWO egui 0.35 FACTS, BOTH LEARNED FROM THE COMPILER OR FROM A HANGING TEST.** (1) **`Context::style_mut` does not exist** — styles are per theme (`style_mut_of`), which this file already records for `set_style` and which bit again.
