(2) **A headless harness driving a `CollapsingHeader` must switch animation OFF.** The body animates open against wall-clock `stable_dt`, which a headless pass barely advances — left on, the body is still zero-height when the drag lands, the press hits nothing, and **the test passes for the wrong reason**, which is precisely how `a_left_drag_does_not_pan` went inert once.
- **⚠⚠ THE CARRIER TEST IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, AND W5's RECORD IS WHY.** The pipeline half is proven by the two ordering tests, and **nothing there proves the panel's value ever reaches `LocalAdjustments`** — which is exactly how creative LUTs shipped green and 100% non-functional. `dragging_the_masked_curve_reaches_the_masks_own_tone_curve` drives the real `mask_adjust::show`, opens the block **by clicking its header the way a user does** (so `test_ui::click`'s panic-on-missing-label also asserts the block is wired in), and aims the drag with the `egui::Rect` `show` returns — never a hardcoded coordinate. Its sabotage — draw the widget against a scratch `ToneCurve` — fails it with *"the drag never reached the mask's tone_curve"*.
- **⚠ Five sabotages, all caught, every one producing a `test result:` line** so none was inert or failed to compile: double application (the extended guard **and** 6 goldens), the grade moved before HSL, the curve moved after local contrast, the panel writing to a scratch curve, and the exhaustive fixture losing its curve.
- **⚠ `masks.rs` is 631 lines, UP from 614** — one parameter, one argument and three test locals. Everything else went into `mask_adjust.rs`, 60 → 299. That file is still over the ~600 marker and what is growing it is its test module, unchanged from M7.2's note.
- **⚠ Both goldens were MEASURED, not eyeballed.** Against `neutral.png`'s own baseline of 24 pinned samples: `mask_curve` 28 pinned (0.91%), distinct 64 → 182, 2784/3072 samples moved; `mask_grade` 52 pinned (1.69%), distinct 64 → 211, 1514 moved. Both were also compared against `neutral.png` and each other — **a stage that never ran produces a reference identical to neutral, and the golden test would then pass forever against it.**
- **✅ CONFIRMED BY USE (2026-09-06).** The masked curve was checked on a photograph — a graduated filter over a sky with the midtones lifted moves the sky and leaves the foreground alone — and reported working. ⚠ **Read that as the carrier being proven end to end**, which is the thing W5's record says to check; it is not a claim that every one of the seven checklist items was exercised.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **Part B — masked dehaze and noise reduction — is untouched and still blocked on its design question**, and Part A landing says nothing about it; that is the whole reason the two were split; (2) **no bounding box**, deferred a fifth time on a fresh measurement; (3) the collapsed state is **shared across masks**, deliberately; (4) a mask's panel section is taller again, mitigated by the blocks being closed by default but not removed; (5) **the curve widget draws its own "Tone Curve" label inside a block already headed "Tone Curve"** — a one-line redundancy, kept because the inner row also carries the Reset button; (6) three masked grades is 17 fps at fit.

**M7.2 — masked spatial adjustments (2026-09-05). ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** A mask carries **Texture, Clarity, Sharpening and HSL** in addition to the per-pixel adjustments it already had. Sharpen the eyes, soften the skin, add clarity to the sky, pull the greens down in one corner. 1975 → **1982 tests**, 54 → **55 goldens**, and **the 54 stayed byte-identical** — which is the acceptance criterion, not a nicety. Spec `2026-09-04-masked-spatial-design.md`, plan `2026-09-04-masked-spatial.md`.

- **⚠⚠ THE MILESTONE IS SMALLER THAN ITS NAME AND ITS ONE STRUCTURALLY NEW PART IS THE REGION MARGIN.** `MaskStage` already clones the image, runs sub-stages and blends by coverage, so a spatial stage dropped into that list works mechanically **and its semantics are already right**: the effect reads neighbours from *outside* the mask, which is what avoids a hard edge at the mask boundary. Lightroom's local sharpness behaves the same way. What was genuinely new is that **no fixed region margin can be correct any more.**
- **⚠⚠ `REGION_MARGIN` IS DELETED, AND NO CONSTANT REPLACED IT.** Mask *N* runs on mask *N−1*'s output, so *N* masks each carrying clarity reach *N* × 120 px into the input — **an unbounded stack has no worst case to write down**. The canvas now sizes its request from `pipeline::tail_kernel_radius(&edits, scale)`, the same function the pipeline uses. ⚠ **Deriving is the point, not a convenience**: a canvas computing its own margin would make the pipeline's reach and the canvas's request two facts that can disagree — the shape `render_max_dim`, `fit_image_size` and `geometry_mode` were each fixed for — and the failure is *silent*, a subtly wrong band at the edge of the visible area with nothing to error. `max_tail_kernel_radius` and `view_region`'s `REGION_MARGIN > worst` guard went with it, replaced by a comment saying a constant must not come back.
- **⚠⚠ `tail_kernel_radius` SUMS OVER MASKS; IT DOES NOT TAKE A MAXIMUM.** This is the M3.1a lesson restated and it is the one place the design could have been quietly wrong. Its sabotage — dropping masks from the sum — was caught by the **master region invariant**, the very test that stayed silent when sharpening landed and had to be re-armed then.
- **⚠⚠ THE RENAMED `adjustment_stages` HAS TWO CONSUMERS AND THE GLOBAL ONE PASSES THE NEW FIELDS NEUTRAL.** `per_pixel_stages` → `adjustment_stages(a, baseline, scale)` — it is no longer per-pixel, and it now takes a `RenderScale` the spatial stages need. The global tail places `LocalContrastStage`, `HslStage` and `SharpenStage` itself, so passing the real values there would apply all three **twice**. That exact problem was already solved in that function for saturation; three more fields now follow the identical rule. ⚠ **`build_pipeline_split`'s exhaustive literal is what forces the decision at compile time**, which is the best possible place for it — and the double-application sabotage failed its named test **and moved 4 goldens**, the strongest guard in the milestone.
- **⚠⚠ THE SPEC CLAIMED ALL FOUR EXHAUSTIVE SITES WOULD BREAK AND ONLY ONE DOES — the correction is the more useful finding.** `merge_from` destructures `Edits`, not `LocalAdjustments`, so a mask travels whole under `MASKS_GROUP` and correctly needs nothing. But **`pipeline::all_edits` and `edit_model::all_nonneutral` build their `LocalAdjustments` with `..Default::default()`**, so three fields whose entire purpose is to be non-neutral would have landed untested by the very fixtures that exist to force a decision, **silently, with nothing to remind anyone**. Both were updated by hand. ⚠ That is a finding about those fixtures, not only about this milestone: **a construct that names exhaustiveness does not deliver it through a nested struct.**
- **⚠ Order inside a mask mirrors the global chain** — wb → exposure → contrast → tone regions → **local contrast** → saturation/vibrance → **HSL** → **sharpening last**. HSL after saturation because it corrects colours that are *in* the picture and must see final tonality; sharpening last because it must not amplify what a later stage would stretch.
- **⚠ `Sharpening::masking` is carried inside a mask, which is a second gate inside a gate** — the mask has already answered *where*. Kept because parity costs nothing and an edge gate is defensible for a loose mask over mixed content. A deliberate choice, recorded so it is not later filed as an oversight.
- **⚠ `scale_from_full(full_long, buffer_long, max_dim)` is phrased from `full_long`, which is INVARIANT across the full-resolution handover** — `buffer_long` and `source_scale` both change there, and their product does not. That makes a whole class of handover bug unrepresentable rather than merely tested, which is better than what the plan asked for. ⚠ It is `pub`, not `pub(crate)`, and that is **forced**: `render_engine` is in the lib and the caller is in the bin, which is a separate crate.
- **⚠⚠ MEASURED (X-T4, 3123×2085 half-size buffer, release, best of 5), AND THE NUMBERS DECIDED TWO THINGS.** `cargo run --release -p app --example bench_masked <raw>`, kept.

  | edit | fit, whole image @1600 | native region tail 1400×900 |
  |---|---|---|
  | neutral | 12.7 ms | 6.6 ms |
  | 1 masked clarity (gradient) | 22.7 (**+10.1**) | 13.3 (**+6.7**) |
  | 3 masked clarity | 41.4 (+28.8) | 30.4 (+23.8) |
  | 1 masked HSL (gradient) | 45.8 (**+33.1**) | 34.4 (**+27.8**) |
  | 3 masked HSL | 111.9 (+99.3) | 88.8 (+82.2) |
  | 1 masked clarity (**small radial**) | 23.5 (+10.8) | 15.5 (+8.9) |
  | 1 masked HSL (**small radial**) | 45.9 (+33.2) | 34.7 (+28.1) |

- **⚠⚠ A SMALL RADIAL COSTS THE SAME AS A FRAME-COVERING GRADIENT — +33.2 AGAINST +33.1 — WHICH IS THE DIRECT MEASUREMENT THAT NO BOUNDING BOX EXISTS.** The radial's own bbox is 0.24 × 0.24, about **5.8% of the frame**, and it is paying for 100% of it. That one row is the bounding box's whole case, as a number rather than an argument.
- **⚠ The masked delta scales with the pixels the mask runs over**, which is the premise a bbox rests on — measured at two region sizes a quarter apart: clarity +7.2 → +2.4 ms (**3.0×**), HSL +27.9 → +7.1 ms (**3.9×**). **HSL is exactly linear in pixel count**, which is what "purely per-pixel" predicts. **Clarity is sublinear**, because `wide_blur`'s radius is fixed in pixels and does not shrink with the region — so a bounding box returns *less* on clarity than a naive area argument suggests.
- **⚠ Three masks cost ~18% more than three times one** at the region (+23.8 against 3 × 6.7 for clarity). `MaskStage` clones the image per mask, so three masks is three clones.
- **⚠⚠ DECISION 1 — THE BOUNDING BOX IS DEFERRED A FOURTH TIME, NOW ON A NUMBER RATHER THAN A GUESS.** It is worth roughly **17× on a small radial or brush mask** and **nothing at all** on a linear gradient, a luminance range or a colour range mask — three of the five kinds, and they include the commonest masked-spatial gesture there is: darken the sky with a gradient and add clarity to it. **The gradient rows are where a real edit sits, and a bbox cannot move them by a millisecond.** ⚠ For whoever picks it up: **a brush mask already carries its box** — `CoverageMap` has been bbox-bounded since M3.1c-A and `MaskStage` simply does not use it to bound the sub-pipeline; a radial's box is analytic. **The work is in `MaskStage`, not in the masks.**
- **⚠⚠ DECISION 2 — THE HUE LUT BECOMES THE NEXT MILESTONE.** Masked HSL is **3.3×** masked clarity and is the only thing here that is slow at *one* mask: +33.1 ms takes a fit render from 79 fps to 22, and three of them is 9 fps. Three properties point at the LUT rather than at the box: the cost is **exactly linear in pixel count** so it is per-pixel arithmetic and nothing else; it is **independent of mask kind**, so a LUT helps the three masks a bbox cannot touch; and it also helps the **global** HSL slider, +34.9 ms today, which every user moves. ⚠ `CLAUDE.md` predicted this moment in its own HSL section — *"a hue LUT is the obvious next optimization if it ever matters"*.
- **⚠⚠ ASKED AT THE MERGE, AND WORTH RECORDING BEFORE PHASE 8 RE-LITIGATES IT: DOES THE GPU PORT FIX MASKED HSL? Yes, substantially — but NOT by porting `HslStage` alone, and not soon enough to be the answer here.** Three things decide it. **(1)** This is the ideal GPU workload — per-pixel, independent, transcendental-heavy — and a GPU has *hardware* special-function units where a CPU has polynomials; order-10× the f32 throughput on this machine, realistically 5–15× on this kernel. **(2)** ⚠ **A single GPU stage inside a CPU pipeline is likely net-NEGATIVE**: a 1600 px fit frame is ~20 MB up and ~20 MB back per render. The win only materialises once the **whole tail** runs on GPU and the buffer stays resident — which is why `PipelineStage`'s consuming signature was designed as the seam. **(3)** ⚠ A bonus specific to this app: the tail's output already goes to the GPU as a texture, so a GPU tail makes `pack` **and** the upload disappear — 0.5 ms at fit, 2.2 ms at native, free. ⚠⚠ **None of that argues against the LUT, and three things argue for doing it anyway**: Phase 8 is two phases away (Delivery, then Depth); the LUT is a day against a phase; and it **survives the port** — a 1-D LUT on a GPU is a texture fetch from something permanently in cache. ⚠ What the GPU port will *not* fix, already on record: libraw's demosaic is upstream of everything `wgpu` would touch.
- **⚠ Nine sabotages, all caught**, and two were notably strong: the double-application sabotage failed its named test *and* moved 4 goldens, and dropping masks from `tail_kernel_radius` was caught by the master region invariant.
- **⚠⚠ THE FALSE-PASS TRAP WAS HIT TWICE IN ONE SESSION, BY SOMEONE WHO HAD JUST QUOTED IT.** (1) `cargo test -p app --bin Rustroom scale_from_full_agrees` returned **EXIT=0 with `0 passed; 325 filtered out`** — `render_engine` is a **lib** module, so `--bin` filtered it away. (2) A second filter matched no test at all: `0 passed; 326 filtered out`. Both runs looked green. **The habit that works is not knowing the trap but reading the passed-count on every filtered run** — `test result: ok` says nothing about what ran.
- **⚠ Clippy caught two things 1979 passing tests did not** — an orphaned `///` doc comment (`empty line after doc comment`) and an unused `use pipeline::RenderScale;`, both left by removing the guard test. At least the ninth recorded instance.
- **⚠ `develop/panel/hsl_bands.rs` is shared by the global panel and by a mask**, honouring `adjustments.rs`'s own "One layout learned once" comment; `mask_adjust.rs` holds a mask's sliders in pipeline order. ⚠ `RustroomApp` gained `mask_hsl_channel` so a mask does not share the global panel's selected channel. ⚠ **`masks.rs` is 614 lines, UP from 575** — the extraction removed ~20 lines of sliders and the wiring test plus channel locals added ~45. The split still earned its place (the file would otherwise be ~673) but it did not get under ~600, and what is growing it now is the test module.
- **✅ THE CHECKLIST WAS RUN ON REAL PHOTOGRAPHS (2026-09-05) AND EVERYTHING WORKS.** ⚠⚠ **This is the first milestone in this file's record whose GUI wiring produced no defects at all** — every comparable one (M3.1c-B six, M5.3b five rounds, the geometry work four) was reported broken in some way within the hour. Do not read that as the class of bug being over; read it as the panel being a straight extraction of a layout that already worked. ⚠ Nothing here could be self-checked: `AXIsProcessTrusted()` is false, so synthetic input is silently dropped and `screencapture` fails. The six items:
  1. A brush mask on a face, **Clarity −60**: the skin softens, the background does not.
  2. The same mask, **Texture −100**: skin smooths while the coarse markings survive. *This is the skin-softening tool, and it is why deferring masked NR costs nothing.*
  3. **Sharpening amount 80** on an eye mask with global sharpening also on: the eye gets **both**.
  4. **Masked HSL**, greens −40 saturation under a mask over foliage: the foliage desaturates and identically-coloured foliage **outside** the mask does not.
  5. ⚠ **Zoom to 1:1 and pan with two masked clarities active.** This is the one the derived margin exists for — watch the *edges* of the viewport for a band that differs from the interior.
  6. ⚠⚠ **Masked HSL at 1:1 is "a bit slow, but still usable" — reported, and the measurement predicted exactly that.** +33.1 ms on a 12.7 ms fit render is 22 fps, which is what "a bit slow" feels like. **That is the strongest evidence for the hue LUT there is**: the number said it would be the worst case in the feature, and the person using it independently named the same slider. It is a judgement about a photograph, so no test could have produced it.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **masked noise reduction and dehaze are a separate slice** — ⛔ **and the structural reason given here was WRONG; see the 2026-09-06 section above.** Both run *after* geometry, not before, so the coordinate-space objection never applied. The real obstacle was dehaze's frame-global airlight under a region render, and Part B answered it by placing both in the prefix; (2) **no bounding box**, so a small radial pays for the whole frame — measured above; (3) **no hue LUT**, so masked HSL is the expensive case; (4) ⚠ **skin softening is NOT among the gaps** despite the absence of masked NR — Texture is bipolar and −100 removes the fine band, so the commonest use of the feature is covered by what ships; (5) a mask's panel section is markedly taller, which is the visible cost of HSL parity.

**Creative 3-D LUTs (M7.5, 2026-09-04) — and the bug that proved the milestone was never wired up. ✅ CONFIRMED BY USE.** A `.cube` applied as a look, at a declared input space, with a strength slider, placed so a film emulation *replaces* the camera rendering and a grade *modifies* it. 1896 → 1934 tests, 53 goldens byte-identical.

- **⚠⚠ THE MILESTONE SHIPPED GREEN AND ENTIRELY DEAD, AND THAT IS THE LESSON WORTH MORE THAN THE FEATURE.** Every creative look failed to load in the running app, silently, with no error. The pipeline half was *thoroughly* tested — `a_look_runs_before_the_output_transform`, `a_look_with_no_table_leaves_the_camera_rendering_alone`, `an_inactive_look_inserts_no_stage`, plus the stage's own gamut test — and **nothing anywhere proved the table ever reached the pipeline.** ⚠ This file already records that shape more than any other ("the pure functions carry ~30 tests and produced no defect at all; every bug was canvas wiring"), and it happened again in a track that was otherwise exemplary. **A milestone whose maths is fully proven can be 100% non-functional.** Ask what carries the data to the tested code, and test *that*.
- **⚠⚠ THE DEFECT: the full-resolution handover rebuilt the render engine from `decoded.profile`, which is DEFINITIONALLY incomplete.** `decode.rs` sets `looks_dir: None` on purpose — *"the decode has no business knowing where looks live, and the directory is a session fact rather than a fact about this file"* — and `finalize_session` fills it in afterwards. The handover did not, so `LookStore` was `None`, the `.cube` never parsed and `CreativeLookStage` never ran.
- **⚠⚠ IT READ AS "LOOKS NEVER WORK" RATHER THAN "LOOKS EXPIRE", AND THE TIMING IS WHY.** `full_res::SETTLE` is 800 ms and the full decode ~3 s, so on **every RAW** the engine is replaced ~4 s after the photo opens — always before anyone can choose a look. Reported as *"I don't see any difference when selecting the Kodak Film simulation, sRGB display, and then moving the strength around from 0 to 100"*. ⚠ Read literally, that report names the whole bug: *no* difference, not a subtle one.
- **⚠⚠ THE COMMENT DIRECTLY ABOVE THAT LINE ALREADY WARNED ABOUT THIS EXACT HAZARD.** *"The profile must be carried across the full-resolution handover, or corrections would silently switch off the moment the upgrade lands."* It was written for the curve and the baseline; `looks_dir` was added to the same struct later and the warning did not cover it. **A warning attached to a struct's existing fields does not extend itself to the next field someone adds** — which is an argument for making the carry structural rather than remembered.
- **⚠⚠ THE FIRST TEST WAS NOT ENOUGH, AND FINDING THAT OUT IS THE PROCESS POINT.** `handover_profile` was extracted so a test could reach the rule — the `release_compare_panes` / `Settings::apply_delete_confirm` pattern for glue that cannot be driven. It passes, and **sabotaging the CALL SITE back to `decoded.profile.clone()` left it green** — i.e. the original bug reintroduced, undetected. That is precisely how this shipped. The second test drives the real `pump_full_res` against `test_app`'s injected stub decode, whose profile carries `looks_dir: None` exactly as the real decode does, and **that one catches the call-site sabotage.** ⚠ Both carry fixture checks ("the handover never ran, so this test proves nothing"), and the wait has a **deadline** so a regression fails rather than hangs CI.
- **⚠ The structural cure was considered and NOT taken, stated so it is not mistaken for an oversight:** a newtype that only `finalize_session` and `handover_profile` can build would make the wrong profile *unrepresentable* rather than merely tested, but it churns seven `RenderEngine::new` call sites across files. Worth doing alongside the deferred `ProfileParts` → `TailRendering` rename, which `decode_worker.rs` already records as owed.
- **⚠⚠ THE CHECKPOINT WAS WORTH ITS COST: FOUR REAL `.cube` FILES BROKE THINGS NO SYNTHETIC TABLE COULD.** Track A stopped at its planned Task 6 reporting that no third-party `.cube` existed on this machine; four were fetched from `aras-p/smol-cube` (MIT/Unlicense; the LUTs ship with Resolve and Tiny Glade) and are installed at `<config>/rustroom/looks/`. **(1)** `Cube::MAX_SIZE` was **64**, justified as *"beyond that a file is more likely corrupt than intentional"* — and **65³ is Resolve's high-quality default**, so a real ACES LMT was refused outright. Raised to 128 (~25 MB; 256³ would be ~201 MB and a malformed file must not be able to ask for it). **(2)** That same file carries a **1-D input shaper** whose 4 095 values sit *ahead* of the 274 625 cube values in the data stream; the parser knew nothing of `LUT_1D_SIZE`, so **raising the cap alone would have converted a loud refusal into a silent mis-parse.** Refused by name now. **(3)** `LUT_3D_INPUT_RANGE` and `DOMAIN_MIN/MAX` were skipped by the blanket "starts with a letter" rule — the genuinely silent one, indexing the cube over `[0,1]` whatever it declared. **(4)** A degenerate or NaN domain divided by zero; refused at parse, through `partial_cmp` rather than `hi <= lo`, because **NaN fails every comparison** and the positive form lets it through.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE FINDING THAT SHAPES THE UI: A WRONG SPACE DECLARATION DOES NOT LOOK WRONG.** Measured on real X-T4 frames. For a **grade** it is unmissable — `tinyglade-Sam_Kolder` declared Linear clips **91.9% → 100%** of the frame into the shadows at saturation 0.9705, Cold Ice 61.08%. For a **film emulation** all three spaces give **0.00% clipping** and a plausible photograph (Kodak 2383: mean L 0.4697 / 0.4496 / 0.2757). **Nothing in the image tells you the space is wrong**, so the control must be explicit and prominent rather than discoverable by eye. ⚠ Sharper still: that LUT's own header declares `Input: Cineon Log`, a space W5-0 deliberately excluded — so **all three of our options are wrong for it and it still looks fine.**
- **✅ The placement rule is confirmed exactly, on real content.** An identity cube declared at **sRGB display** or **Rec.709** reproduces the camera-rendering row to the digit (0.4514 / 0.06% / 0.2182); declared at **Linear** it reproduces the colorimetric row to the digit (0.3377 / 0.00% / 0.1414). A linear look really does run *in place of* the rendering. ⚠ And the shaper round trip is **0.000 of an 8-bit level, 0.000% of pixels** on real content — the measurement A's tolerance-versus-`assert_eq!` decision rests on.
- **⚠ `look_probe` has two faults of its own, found by using it, and both make it report confidently wrong things.** Its identity diagnostic rendered `cubes[0]`, so with `--cube` supplied it measured *the first file given* while still printing "identity round trip" — handed the Kodak LUT it reported `worst 55.972 of an 8-bit level, 100.000% of pixels`, which reads as a catastrophic shaper bug and is a film LUT doing its job. It also labelled rows with the path truncated to 30 characters, so four files in one directory printed four identical labels. Both fixed; **reach for it only after checking what it is actually measuring.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no log encodings** — W5-0's decision, so the commonest real film-emulation convention (Cineon Log) cannot be honoured, and such a LUT still renders plausibly at every space we do offer; (2) **no `.cube` ships with Rustroom** — installation is dropping a file into `<config>/rustroom/looks/`, and the panel shows no Look control at all until one is there, which reads as the feature being absent; (3) a shaper LUT is refused rather than supported; (4) "exact within ProPhoto" — a colour outside it is clipped by that encode; (5) **`rustroom render` applies no look**, since `pipeline` does no I/O — the same limitation camera profiles already have; (6) a Linear look on a JPEG/PNG/TIFF is applied as declared even though `Rendering::None` is forced for those, which is arguably meaningless — user's call, 2026-09-04, recorded rather than silently handled.

**🎯 COLOUR MANAGEMENT IS COMPLETE (2026-08-25)** — A (wide working space), B (output profile at export), C1–C4 (camera input profiles) and D (display profile, plus its macOS follow-up). Their sections are the eight immediately below. Before them, the layout/IA rework (2026-08-24) — the panel, the rail, the menu bar and a round of in-use fixes.

**⚠⚠ AND A NEW VARIANT, 2026-08-26: a broken sabotage HARNESS reported all seven guards "NOT CAUGHT".** The matcher looked for failure lines by bare test name while cargo prints them with module paths, so nothing ever matched. That is the mirror image of the finding below — a universal false *alarm* rather than a false pass — and both come from the same root: **trusting the harness's output shape instead of checking it produces what you grep for.** Check the harness against a sabotage you already know is caught.

**⚠⚠ A standing lesson, now ELEVEN instances deep: a test can name a bug it cannot detect.** Each time it looked rigorous and checked nothing. ⚠ **M3.1b alone produced three, all caught by the sabotage step and none by review** — which is the strongest argument yet that the step is not a formality.

**They share one shape: each asserted a property at inputs where the bug is inert.** That is the thing to check when writing the test, not after.

- **Rotate/flip**: "the generators reach exactly 8 states" passed against a *wrong abelian* composition rule — the count cannot tell a direct product from a dihedral group.
- **Masking 3.1a, `hit_test`**: `hit_testing_the_line_is_aspect_correct` used a horizontal axis with the probe directly below it, where the perpendicular distance is purely vertical — it passed with the aspect correction deleted.
- **Masking 3.1a, `tail_kernel_radius`**: the region-equality test was under-inset by ~9 px and passed because the residual differences happened to round the same way, until a masked gain pushed them over a quantisation boundary.
- **M3.1b, `feather_zero_is_a_hard_edge`**: probed either side of the ellipse boundary but never *on* it. Without the `FEATHER_EPS` guard the ramp is `(1−d)/0.0` — `+inf` inside, `−inf` outside, both clamping to the *correct* answer. The guard is load-bearing only at exactly `d == 1.0`, where it is `0.0/0.0`. ⚠ **IEEE infinity arithmetic can make a missing guard invisible.**
- **M3.1b, `dragging_an_edge_resizes_about_a_fixed_centre`**: dragged horizontal edges with `dy = 0` and vertical ones with `dx = 0`, so a cross-axis leak was a no-op it could not see. ⚠ **A zero component in a test's input is a blind spot; a real mouse drag never has one.** Every drag is diagonal now.
- **M3.1b, `an_existing_linear_sidecar_still_loads`**: asserted the *variant* and not the *payload*. Under `#[serde(untagged)]` serde reads `LinearGradient` from the whole object, and its container-level `#[serde(default)]` makes the unknown `"Linear"` key be **ignored** and every field default — so the variant still matched with `from`/`to` silently `[0,0]`. ⚠ **`#[serde(default)]` makes a struct deserialize from almost anything**, the same trap `edit_clipboard`'s tagged envelope exists for.

**The eighth is the one to read if you only read one, because the *harness* was at fault rather than the assertion.** The navigation rework's `a_left_drag_does_not_pan` — the regression guard for that entire change — passed vacuously because `drag_delta()` is per-frame and the harness moved the pointer on an earlier pass than the one it read. The assertion was right; it was fed zeros. ⚠ **When a test drives a UI framework, check that the harness produces the event on the frame you read.**

**M3.1c-A added three more, all found by the sabotage step and none by review**, and they name three *different* ways a test can be blind:
- **`frame_map_agrees_with_geometry_stage_on_a_straighten_angle`** — no **leverage**. A rotation sign error displaces a point in proportion to its distance from the centre; the probe sat at 0.34 of the half-width, where the flip moves it 1.34 px, inside a 1.5 px tolerance. ⚠ **A geometric test needs leverage, not merely a plausible input.**
- **`a_dab_is_round_on_a_wide_frame`** — the sabotage was a **no-op on that fixture**. On an 80×40 frame the long edge *is* the width, so scaling the radius by width changed nothing. A portrait case discriminates.
- **`overlapping_dabs_in_one_stroke_do_not_double_up`** — the assertion **band was too wide**. Accumulation gave 181, inside `> 0 && < 250`. Comparing against a single dab *exactly* is what discriminates.

**And two that asserted something FALSE about correct code** — a related but distinct fault worth separating: `a_soft_dab_ramps_from_the_centre_outward` demanded exactly 255 at a dab centre that falls on a pixel *boundary* (peak is 254), and `an_eight_bit_edge_has_no_visible_banding` asserted `<= 8` against a ramp whose own analytic slope is ~12. **When a new test fails, ask whether the assertion is right before touching the code.**

**⚠ PARTLY ADDRESSED 2026-08-26 — a fixture now drives the real `canvas::show`; see "Headless canvas tests". The finding below still holds for the PANEL, and for appearance anywhere.**

**⚠⚠ A DIFFERENT and larger finding, from M3.1c-B: the suite tests arithmetic well and interaction hardly at all.** Six bugs in that feature, **every one found by using the app and none by a test** — while the pure functions beside them (`FrameMap`, `inverse_point`, the rasterizer, decimation) carry ~30 tests and produced no defect at all. All six were canvas wiring: a crash from a mesh representation, a stale render, a cached screen rect, a bbox with no outside value. ⚠ **Headless `RawInput` tests of the drag lifecycle would have caught at least three.** The seam exists and works — `clicking_a_library_grid_thumbnail_opens_it`, `a_left_drag_does_not_pan`, `tool_drag_for` — it is simply not used for the canvas. **Treat canvas and panel work as untested territory.**

**⚠⚠ And a process finding that invalidates any sabotage result: a sabotage that fails to COMPILE looks exactly like one that passes.** Placing `#[serde(untagged)]` above the `#[derive]` instead of below made the edit an error; grepping for a test result found nothing, which reads as a clean pass. **Confirm the sabotage compiled before believing it.** Later tasks ran every sabotage through a helper that checks for compile errors first.

**A seventh, in a golden rather than a test:** M3.1b's exposure golden clipped at +1.0 EV — six of twenty samples pinned at 255, so it could not tell the ellipse's peak coverage from any larger value. That is the *identical* weakness 3.1a recorded for its linear exposure golden and did not fix. Now +0.55 EV, nothing clipped, 14 of 20 discriminating. ⚠ **Measure a generated golden, do not just look at it** — clipping is invisible to the eye and fatal to discriminating power.

**The habit that catches all of them: after writing a test, break the thing it names and watch it fail.** Every `⚠ watched failing` note in this file is one of those. When a sabotage step *doesn't* fail, that is a finding about the test, not a formality to wave through — one of this session's subagents refused to move on for exactly that reason and was right.

**M3.1c — the brush. Decisions already taken in brainstorming (2026-08-18), before the navigation rework preempted it:**

- **Storage: vector strokes in `Edits`, rasterized to a cached coverage buffer.** `Vec<Stroke>` (points, radius, hardness, erase) is small, exact, resolution-independent and remappable. The renderer rasterizes once per render into a frame-space coverage map. Rejected: a raster buffer *in* `Edits` (soft at 1:1, bloats every sidecar, makes "undo one stroke" impossible); and per-pixel evaluation over every stroke (O(strokes × points) per pixel).
- **⚠⚠ Anchoring: brush strokes live in PRE-geometry (source image) coordinates. Linear and radial keep frame coordinates.** This is the biggest finding of the brainstorm and it is a **latent bug the brush makes acute**, structurally identical to `invert` having no UI in 3.1b. `MaskStage` runs in the **tail**, after `GeometryStage`, so `u = (x0+x)/fw` is normalised against the *already-cropped, already-rotated* frame — **change the crop and every mask slides over the image content.** Invisible for a gradient, tolerable for a radial, ruinous for a stroke painted onto a subject. The asymmetry is principled, not a hack: **a gradient is compositional** ("darken the top" should stay at the top after a re-crop) **and a stroke is content-referred** (it must stay on the cat). Existing sidecars are untouched, which is the other reason not to convert all three.
- **⚠ The rasterizer, not `MaskStage`, applies the geometry transform.** Because coverage is rasterized into a *frame-space* buffer, `MaskStage` just samples it and never learns geometry exists. Rotation preserves circles and the crop does not stretch, so a dab of radius R source-pixels splats as a disc of radius R×scale frame-pixels — O(dabs × dab area), not O(pixels × dabs).
- **⚠ The cache key must NOT include the region**, exactly as `PrefixCache`'s does not — rasterize whole-frame, sample the sub-rect, so panning never invalidates.
- **⚠ Suggested seam, following the `Region` precedent:** `MaskStage` rasterizes internally when handed no pre-built coverage, so the CLI, export and all 35 goldens compile untouched; the engine passes a cached buffer when it has one. That is the pattern that gave the 1:1 region work "zero call-site churn".
- **Controls: size, hardness, erase.** Erase earns its place — overshooting is inevitable and undoing the whole stroke throws away the good part of the same drag.
- **⚠ Flow is deferred to a later roadmap milestone** (user's call, 2026-08-18). It complicates stroke-overlap handling in the rasterizer, which is the part worth keeping simple while 3.1c proves the architecture. **The roadmap file in the Obsidian vault still needs this note added.**
- **⚠ `Mask` and `MaskedAdjustment` lose `Copy`** — a stroke list cannot be `Copy`. Three sites depend on it today: `stages/mask.rs:35` (`masked: *masked`), and `develop/canvas.rs:309`/`330`. ⚠ `mask_tool::apply_drag(m: Mask, ..) -> Mask` should become `&mut Mask` in place rather than clone a stroke list on every drag frame.
- **⚠ Coverage map as `u8`, not `f32`** — a full-res frame map is 26 MB rather than 104 MB, and 256 levels across a soft edge is below the visibility threshold.
- **Still open:** whether `MaskStage`'s bounding-box optimisation (deferred at 3.1a and again at 3.1b) comes in here. Brush is the case that motivates it, but measure before assuming.

**M3.1c — the brush.** The last of M3.1's three primitives, and the one that **closes the milestone** — M3.1's own "Done when" names dodge/burn with a brush. Only then does the parked button/layout work resume.

⚠ **Read M3.1b's "did the architecture hold" verdict first** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-18-radial-mask-design.md`). Short version: it held, and the one structural item was `mask_tool`'s `Handle` enum. **The brush will test that decision much harder**, because a stroke's "handles" are not points at all — there is no fixed set of five positions to hit-test. That is where the rejected nested-`Handle` option becomes worth revisiting, and where the flat enum may genuinely stop paying.

⚠ **A brush is not a fourth analytic shape, and this is the real difficulty.** `Mask::coverage(u, v, aspect)` is a pure function of position for both existing variants; a brush is *sampled data*, so it needs storage (a stroke list or a coverage buffer), a resolution policy, and an answer to what happens on a crop or an orientation change. Expect this one to need architecture, unlike 3.1b. It is also the first mask whose `MaskStage` cost argues for the bounding-box optimisation deferred twice now.

⚠ **Two compile errors will greet the variant** — `Mask::coverage`'s match and `paint_mask`'s — plus `Mask::label()` and `Mask::invert_mut()`. Note 3.1b predicted three and got one, because Tasks 2 and 3 prepared the seams *before* adding the variant; that sequencing is worth repeating.

**⚠ Deferred, user's call 2026-08-13: rearranging buttons in the UI.** Asked for and parked until **after M3.1**, on the rule below — masking brings a mask list the layout has nowhere to put, so rearranging around its absence means rearranging twice. ⚠ The distinction that made the call: *reordering controls inside a container that already exists* is cheap and safe any time; *changing what containers exist or where they live* is what waits. Pick it up when M3.1 lands.

**⚠ Visual/cosmetic GUI work has a decided sequence — do not reopen it casually.** Recorded in the roadmap's sequencing notes and summarised here: **foundations** (spacing, palette, typography, conventions) are cheap now and expensive later, so they went *before* Phase 3 — **done**: `theme.rs` holds the palette, the density and the conventions. **Layout/IA** (where panels live, what the toolbar holds) must wait until *after* M3.1, because masking introduces a mask list, a top-level concept the current layout has nowhere to put; rearranging first means rearranging twice. The **visual identity** itself (actual colours, icons, symbols) is cheap to defer, because anything expressible in `egui::Style`/`Visuals` is one central change applied retroactively. What is expensive to defer is *structural* — whether buttons carry icons, whether sections have headers — since every feature added meanwhile multiplies the instances to convert.

**Virtual copies (M3.4) — several edit versions per file (2026-08-25).** Copies are Library rows, sessions, sync targets and exports. 858 → **888 tests**; 47 goldens untouched, which is the proof no pipeline code moved. **Snapshots are NOT included** — a separate slice, backlogged.

⚠⚠ **READ THE "Naming, renaming and the browser" SECTION BELOW BEFORE TRUSTING THIS ONE.** Three of its bullets were **overturned** by a week of using the feature — the "one number used everywhere" rule, "copy numbers are never reused", and what `_N` means in an export filename. They are marked ⛔ **SUPERSEDED** in place. Everything else here still holds.

- **⚠⚠ THE WHOLE FEATURE IS AN IDENTITY CHANGE.** `PathBuf` *was* a photo's identity in six places — the sidecar name, `BufferCache`, `HistoryStash`, `Session`, sync targets and export naming. `PhotoId { path, copy }` replaces it. Everything else here is small by comparison.
- **⚠⚠ `BufferCache` STAYS KEYED BY PATH, AND THAT IS WHAT DELIVERS "AT NO STORAGE COST".** Copies of one file share one decoded buffer (78–313 MB), so opening a copy of an already-decoded file is a cache *hit*. It required no work — only a decision not to change something, which is precisely the kind of decision that gets undone later. `copies_of_one_file_share_one_decoded_buffer` guards it, and ⚠ **the sabotage does not merely fail, it fails to COMPILE** — a stronger guard than a runtime assertion.
- **⚠⚠ THE MIGRATION'S DANGER NEEDS TWO THINGS, NOT ONE — the design said otherwise and was wrong.** The claim was that an untagged `{ Envelope, Legacy }` enum would read every existing bare-`Edits` sidecar as an empty copy list, destroying every edit with a green suite. **Measured: untagged alone is harmless** — `Sidecar::copies` is a *required* field, so a legacy object fails to match and correctly falls through; all 15 tests passed. It becomes data loss only when `#[serde(default)]` is **also** added to `Sidecar`, the obvious "forward compatibility" move. So there are **two independent guards**, both commented: explicit key dispatch in `load_all`, and the deliberate *absence* of `#[serde(default)]` on `Sidecar`.
- **⚠⚠ A PRODUCTION BUG THIS INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT: two readers of one format.** Once the sidecar became an envelope, `sidecar::load` still parsed files as a bare `Edits` and returned `None` for **every** file the new code writes. Its live caller was `reload_if_affected`, so **a synced photo would silently reset to neutral**. Found by two failing sync tests, not by reading. Fixed by removing the trap rather than patching the site: `load`/`save`/`persist` are now thin **copy-0 wrappers** over the copy-aware API, so one reader exists instead of two.
- **⚠⚠ THE STASH AND THE LOG HAD TO RE-KEY TOGETHER, and this file already said so.** From the per-photo-edit-history work: *"The storage was the easy half; the log was the feature. A per-path history alone does nothing, because `ActionKind::PhotoEdit` was anonymous."* Re-keying `HistoryStash` on `PhotoId` while `PhotoEdit` still carried a bare path would make Ctrl+Z on the original pop a *copy's* stack. One mechanism, one commit — and each half has its own sabotage-verified test.
- **⚠ The plan's own warning paid off: after the session work, every surviving `PhotoId::original` in `main.rs` was suspect. Six existed; FOUR were bugs** — including `stash`, which would have made two copies of one file share an undo stack. The two that remain are legitimate (single-file open, and a fallback when the pending id is gone).
- ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — ONE NUMBER USED EVERYWHERE.** The identity number *is* the displayed number *is* the export suffix. ⚠ **Deliberately not Lightroom's labelling**, which calls the original "Copy 1" — and the mockup this was chosen from followed Lightroom. A display number offset from the identity is two facts that can disagree, the failure `render_max_dim`, `fit_image_size` and `geometry_mode` were each fixed for. **⛔ Now: the number is still the identity, but a *named* copy shows and exports its own name instead. The rule survives in the form that matters — the name is an explicit, user-chosen override, not a second derived fact.**
- ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — Copy numbers are stable and never reused** (`max + 1`). Renumbering after a delete would silently change a copy's export filename and invalidate its history-stash key. **⛔ Still true of everything automatic; `Reindex` is the one explicit action allowed to renumber, and it drops the affected undo stacks for exactly the reason above.**
- ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — TWO NUMBERING SCHEMES IN EXPORT FILENAMES, with different separators on purpose.** `_N` is **identity** — which copy, stable forever. `-N` is **de-duplication** — the Nth file wanting this name in this folder, assigned at export time, and it already existed for Keep Both. They compose: copy 2 exported twice gives `a_2.jpg` then `a_2-1.jpg`. Merging them would make `a-2.jpg` ambiguous and would shift a copy's number whenever its plain name was taken. **⛔ Now: `_N` is only the *default* name of an unnamed copy. `-N` is unchanged, and it is what stops two copies sharing a name from overwriting each other — a second, stronger reason not to merge the two schemes.**
- **⚠ Sync's `prior` is per FILE and deduplicated, not per copy.** Two copies of one file are two read-modify-write cycles on one sidecar, so a second snapshot would capture the *first* target's change and undo would restore that. ⚠ And `SyncAction` had to keep its `targets` explicitly, because a per-file `prior` cannot say which *copies* were synced — redo would otherwise re-sync copy 0 of every file involved.
- **⚠ The name is envelope metadata, never an `Edits` field.** In `Edits` it would need an edit group and would **travel with a paste, a preset or a sync** — pasting a B&W recipe onto another photo would rename it — and "Reset adjustments" would wipe it. The cost is that `History<Edits>` cannot undo it, which is why renaming gets its own `ActionKind`. ⚠ Undo and redo call **one** function with the arguments swapped, so the two directions cannot drift. (The variant is now `CopyRename`; see the next section for why file renames needed a *second*, global one.)
- **⚠ The rename field edits a scratch draft**, like the export dialog's. Writing through would push one undo step per keystroke and commit a half-typed name on focus loss. Empty normalises to `None`; unchanged commits nothing, or Ctrl+Z would appear to do nothing.
- **⚠ `entries::build` is a pure function with the sidecar loader INJECTED**, so every expansion rule is tested without disk or a GUI — deliberately, because the Library is where this project's three sparse-index bugs happened. ⚠ **The original is always a row even when the sidecar omits it**: `persist_copy(.., 1, ..)` on an unedited photo writes copy 1 and no copy 0, and omitting it would make the photo vanish after copying it.
- **⚠ `rebuild_entries` rebuilds `thumbs` and the loader too**, because both are index-parallel and the indices have just shifted. `Library` keeps its `scan` so the re-expansion needs no second directory read and sees the same files.
- **Measured: reading every sidecar on folder open costs 4.6 ms for 830 files** (0.006 ms/file), against a 100 ms threshold that would have forced the read onto the loader thread. `cargo run --release -p app --example sidecar_scan <folder>` reproduces it.
- **⚠ ⌘' IS safe as a native accelerator, and knowing why matters**: the hazard is a **bare** key (stolen from every text field) or a text-editing chord (⌘Z/C/V/X/A). A plain apostrophe typed into the rename field is not the ⌘' chord. ⚠ `every_shortcut_maps_to_an_egui_key` caught the missing `'` → `Key::Quote` mapping, and the fix was to **complete the table, not weaken the test** — the rule already recorded for ⌘C/⌘V.
- **⚠ A test-isolation bug worth knowing: two tests reused the temp-dir tag `"absent"`, and `TempDir::new` does `remove_dir_all` — so in parallel they deleted each other's directory.** It surfaced as an *unrelated pre-existing test* failing during a sabotage run, which is the worst possible moment to be chasing a phantom. Fixed structurally (process id + monotonic counter), the same remedy `image-decode`'s `temp_path` needed after taking uniqueness from the clock.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **copies share a pixel-identical thumbnail** — Library thumbnails are the camera's embedded JPEG, so a black-and-white copy shows in colour, told apart only by badge and name; edit-accurate thumbnails are backlogged with this motivation attached; (2) **snapshots are not included**; (3) folder open now reads every sidecar, where it read none; (4) copy numbers are never reused, so a churned photo can reach `_47` with three copies; (5) two numbering schemes in export filenames; (6) **a copy is not a file** — nothing outside Rustroom knows it exists, and the sidecar is the only record; (7) single-file mode ("Open file…") lists no copies, since it has no Library.

**M5.3b — the spot-removal canvas tool, and Visualize Spots (2026-09-03) — PHASE 5 CLOSES WITH IT.** Click to place a patch, drag to size it, ⌥-drag to pick the source by hand; an auto-picked source otherwise; a Retouch rail tool on `4`; a high-contrast edge view for finding dust. 1765 → **1815 tests**; **53 goldens byte-identical throughout**, the proof no pipeline stage moved. Spec `2026-09-03-spot-removal-canvas-design.md`; two plans.

- **⚠⚠ FIVE ROUNDS OF DEFECTS FROM USING IT, EVERY ONE IN CANVAS/PANEL WIRING, NONE CAUGHT BY A TEST BEFORE IT WAS REPORTED.** The twelfth time this file records that about GUI work. The pure modules — `spot_tool` (16 tests), `spot_source` (7), `visualize` (5) — produced not one defect. In order: no size preview while dragging and sliders that edited nothing; a laggy move; the old heal showing under a moved patch; the sliders re-rendering live; and **the move flickering** — the last two being the same mechanism getting a second owner.
- **⚠⚠ THE MOVE MUST NOT RENDER WHILE THE DRAG RUNS, AND THE M3.1a RULE ("a drag must request a render on the drag frame") WAS RIGHT FOR MASKS AND WRONG HERE — structurally, not as taste.** `SpotRemovalStage` is in the **prefix**, so every drag frame re-ran noise reduction and dehaze whole-buffer (~117 ms each at native); `MaskStage` is in the cheap tail. The outline is the feedback; the render lands on release. The test that asserted the opposite was **inverted**, and ⚠ its name and doc comment were found a round later still describing the old behaviour — an inversion that changes only the assertions leaves a comment that lies.
- **⚠⚠ `Session.suppressed_spot` — a patch LEAVES THE VIEW while it is adjusted**, so the blemish underneath is visible to line up against; applied in `view_edits`, the way `preview_edits` strips the crop in geometry mode. Two renders per gesture. ⚠ The panel reads `dragged()`, not `changed()`: a slider reports no change on a frame the pointer held still, and treating that as the end of the drag flickers the patch back mid-gesture.
- **⚠⚠ AND THEN IT HAD TWO OWNERS, WHICH IS THE ROUND TO READ.** The panel suppresses during a slider drag, the canvas during a move — and moving a patch *selects* it, so the panel's release, keyed on "the selected spot is suppressed and no slider is dragging", fired on every frame of a canvas move, one frame after the canvas hid the patch. Reported as *"the unretouched image only flickers once"*. **An owner may release only what it suppressed**; the panel now releases on its own slider's `drag_stopped` and on nothing else. ⚠ The canvas test could not see it — it drives the canvas alone, and the bug lives in the two being drawn on one frame. Nothing drives the whole Develop body headlessly except `shell.rs`; recorded as a gap.
- **⚠⚠ FOUR OF SIX `spot_source` TESTS WERE BLIND, found by sabotage before the canvas existed.** Each placed its fixture where a wrong implementation had nothing to reach for — a destination no candidate crosses the edge from, a uniform field where a wrong score is indifferent, an obstacle where the search never looks. Every fixture now contains what a wrong implementation would be attracted to. ⚠ The ring is **outset** (1.2–1.6×), A's Wave 3 finding: the radius must *exceed* the blemish or heal measures a ring still lying on it.
- **⚠⚠ egui RUNS THE WHOLE DRAG LIFECYCLE FOR A PLAIN CLICK on a `Sense::drag()` widget**, so a click placed two spots until a movement guard (`CLICK_SLOP`) split the two. And the canvas `resp` never reports `clicked()`, so a second, click-sensing `ui.interact` exists only for placement — the eyedropper's trick, for the eyedropper's reason. ⚠ The drag state was a **local** in `canvas::show` at first — reset every frame, so the release saw no gesture; one `eprintln` found it after the lifecycle had read perfectly three times. It is `Session.spot_drag` now.
- **⚠ Size is in PIXELS on a logarithmic slider**, capped at `MAX_RADIUS = 0.05` because dust lives at 0.1–0.5 % and a linear 0–25 % slider crammed it into the first few pixels. `MIN_RADIUS` is a `const _: () = assert!(..)`. ⚠ A test probing at 0.12 — fine under the old 0.25 ceiling — would have silently clamped and stopped testing its property; moved to 0.03.
- **⚠ The sliders edit the SELECTED patch, else the tool defaults, and adjusting a default re-renders nothing and pushes nothing** — the `apply_view_demosaic` rule: no pixel changed. Found by the discriminating half of a test when `absorb` fired unconditionally.
- **⚠⚠ VISUALIZE SPOTS IS A VIEW MODE APPLIED IN `pack`, AFTER THE HISTOGRAM AND BEFORE THE DISPLAY TRANSFORM — never a pipeline stage.** The rule `display_space` established, inherited rather than reinvented; sabotage-verified by moving the histogram below it. `|luma − blur(luma)|` gain-mapped; **the threshold is a gain, not a cutoff**, or the slider shows nothing over most of its travel. σ is **6 rendered pixels** and takes no render scale — dust is a few pixels across wherever you look from. ⚠ `visualize` lives on **`Session`**, not `RustroomApp` as the plan said: `Session::request` is the one funnel and has no app access, exactly why `display_space` lives there; the cost is a reset on photo switch. ⚠ It clears itself in `select_tool` **unconditionally** — a `t != Tool::Retouch` guard's sabotage went uncaught, because the view can only be switched on *from* Retouch, and the matching "re-selecting keeps it" test half was vacuous (`select_tool` early-returns). An unreachable guard is an untested one.
- **⚠ `FrameMap` has two distinct problems and only one is fixable now.** *Anchoring* strokes and spots in sensor space is blocked on a **forward** distortion map lensfun does not expose — backlogged as "anchor strokes and spots in sensor space". *Sampling* the source colour under a pick is fixable and done: `sampler_for` composes `Homography::source_of` then `Corrector::source` then a buffer read, since the inverse is exactly what a search needs.
- **⚠ Phosphor's `bandaids` codepoint (`U+E0B2`) was verified by fetching the 2.1.2 CSS *and* parsing the bundled TTF's cmap** — the codepoints are assignment-ordered, not guessable, and a wrong one renders a different icon rather than tofu.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) a spot's pixel effect appears on release, never during a move or a slider drag — deliberate, above; (2) spots are frame-anchored like the analytic masks, so a crop change slides them — the backlog entry; (3) `visualize` resets on a photo switch; (4) no whole-Develop headless harness, so panel/canvas interactions are covered only piecewise; (5) the auto-picked source is a local search (16 angles × 5 distances), not a global one.

**M5.4b — a display rendering, and a baseline exposure to place the scene under it (2026-09-03).** A RAW opens looking like a photograph rather than a flat scene measurement. 1740 → **1761 tests**; **53 goldens byte-identical throughout**, which is the proof `Edits::default()` stayed neutral.

- **⚠⚠ THE DESIGN QUESTION THIS ANSWERS IS THE ONE THAT BLOCKED THREE OTHER FEATURES.** *"Where does a display rendering live in a scene-referred pipeline?"* — `ProfileToneCurve`, `ProfileLookTable` and creative 3-D LUTs were each backlogged on it. **`DisplayRenderingStage` runs last in the tail, immediately before `OutputTransform`**, which keeps every upstream stage's scene-linear tuning intact (`local_contrast`'s `MID_GRAY`, `sharpening`'s `LUMA_EPS`, `dehaze`'s airlight percentile) *and* is what bounds the data to `[0, 1]` — the seam the other three needed.
- **⚠⚠ THE OUTPUT TRANSFORM WAS DOING THE *ENCODING* JOB AND NOT THE *RENDERING* JOB, and the two got conflated because on an already-display-referred image they look alike.** Encoding is lossless and reversible; rendering is lossy and one-way. A scene carries 12–14 stops and a display 7–8, so something must decide which to spend — and nothing did.
- **⚠⚠ A FIRST MEASUREMENT SAID THE CAMERA *LIFTS* THE MIDTONES 1.5–2.3×, AND THAT WAS THE METERING, NOT THE CURVE.** It pooled frames raw, and each frame's JPEG carries its own exposure decision — across seven X-T4 frames the camera metered up by **+0.88 to +1.57 EV**, exactly the "lift" the first pass attributed to the transfer. `curve_probe` now rescales each frame so its own grey point lands on `GREY` before pooling, and what remains never exceeds 1.0×: **a compressive S anchored at grey, crushing below and rolling off above**. ⚠ The **0.69 EV spread** between those grey points is the evidence for the shape/level split — no fixed curve can encode a decision that moves 0.69 EV frame to frame.
- **⚠⚠ THE TOP THREE MEASURED BINS ARE DISCARDED AND THE SHOULDER CONSTRUCTED.** A bin above ~+2 EV holds a growing fraction of pixels the JPEG has **clipped to 255**, dragging its median up — so the raw data appears to *steepen* toward white (slopes 3.2, 5.2, 7.0). Read literally it would have given the rendering a contrast bump in the highlights. ⚠ Consequence, stated: **our shoulder is gentler than the camera's** — at +2.75 EV the camera reads 0.95 where we give ~0.68.
- **⚠⚠ THE STAGE CURVES EACH CHANNEL INDEPENDENTLY, THE ONE TONAL STAGE HERE THAT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT PRESERVE HUE.** That is where a camera rendering's shadow saturation and its roll toward white come from, and it is what the DNG spec does with `ProfileToneCurve`. A neutral grey still stays exactly neutral, since all three channels take the same curve.
- **⚠⚠ "IT JUST LOOKS DARKER WITH MORE CONTRAST" IS SUBSTANTIALLY RIGHT, AND WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** `rendering_vs_contrast` (kept) fits the closest Exposure + Contrast pair: it lands **within 3.2–4.4 of 255** on four frames. What the fit cannot reach is **saturation** — the rendering runs **+11% to +75% in the shadows and −24% to −27% in the highlights** against its own best match. ⚠ That gap is structural: Exposure, Contrast and the tone regions all compute a *scalar* gain and multiply RGB by it, which preserves `(max−min)/max` **exactly**, so no combination of them can change saturation at any tone.
- **⚠⚠ A PER-CAMERA `BaselineExposure` (DNG tag 50730) PLACES THE SCENE UNDER THE CURVE, AND WITHOUT IT THE FEATURE IS HALF-DELIVERED.** A RAW is metered to leave headroom above the subject, and libraw's `no_auto_bright` applies no normalisation — so 1.0 is *sensor saturation* and the subject's midtone lands at **0.061–0.098** where the display convention wants 0.18. Measured against the camera's own JPEG: **+0.67…+1.17 EV, mean 0.89** without it; **−0.28…+0.17 EV, mean 0.10** with it.
  - ⚠ **A consistent offset is the signature of a correct curve with a missing constant**, which is why the rendering alone made the *mean* gap worse (0.36 → 0.89) while halving its *span* (0.96 → 0.50 EV). Read the span, not the mean.
  - ⚠ **`None` and `Some(0.0)` mean different things** — "said nothing" against "declared no shift" — and the fallback applies only to the first. A test that only ever passes a number cannot tell them apart, which is exactly how the fallback would come to override every profile declaring zero.
  - ⚠ The fallback is **+1.13 EV, the median of the seven** — X-T4-derived like `BUILT_IN`, and for the same reason: it is the fallback. **The X-T4 profile carries no tag 50730**, so on this camera the constant is what runs. Verified by dumping the DCP's IFD.
  - ⚠ It applies whichever curve follows, including a profile's, because it describes the **sensor's levels** and not the look. The two fall back independently.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE BASELINE INTRODUCED A REGRESSION IN AUTO TONE: BOTH PLACE THE LEVEL, SO THEY STACKED.** Auto aims the *scene* median at `MEDIAN_TARGET`; the rendering then lifts the scene before its curve. Measured: camera+auto went **−0.58 → −1.00 EV** the moment the baseline landed. `auto_tone::median_target_for` divides the target by the rendering's gain — **divided, not subtracted**, because the rendering multiplies the scene *before* a curve that is near-identity around grey, which is what makes it exact. Now −0.75 EV, and the two worst frames are the two deliberately dark ones, where a histogram fit legitimately disagrees with the camera. ⚠ **Only the median is corrected**: whites and blacks place the tails in the *scene* and the rendering decides how they appear, and a rendered 0.98 white point is unreachable under a shoulder anyway.
- **⚠⚠ THREE SABOTAGES FOUND A GAP NO TEST COVERED: the fix was correct in `pipeline` and would have been UNREACHABLE FROM THE UI.** Passing `None` at either `adjustments::tone` or `SourceInfo` left every other test green. Both hops have a test now, the second driving the **whole panel** through `test_app` — the only thing that can see the `SourceInfo` field.
- **⚠ `Settings.default_rendering` (Camera) is a preference, not an op-list default** — `auto_lens_correction`'s rule for its reason. `Edits::default()` stays strictly neutral so the CLI, the 53 goldens and every sidecar written before today are untouched; only a photo with **no sidecar** opens rendered. Consequence: the GUI and the CLI disagree on a fresh photo, exactly as they already do about lens correction.
- **⚠ The "Camera rendering" checkbox sits ABOVE every slider**, because a rendering is not an adjustment: the sliders move a photograph already in a defined state, and this establishes that state.
- **⚠⚠ A THUMBNAIL NEVER RENDERS TWICE.** The strip shows the camera's embedded JPEG, which is already rendered, so `thumbnail_edits` strips the rendering and keeps every other edit. That is also what **closes** the "strip looks overexposed" report rather than widening it: the strip keeps the camera's rendering and the canvas gains ours, so the two converge.
- **⚠ `ProfileParts` groups the curve with the baseline** because the two must always travel together — a curve arriving without its baseline would render under our measured constant instead of the profile's, which is a wrong picture rather than an error. It also keeps `RenderEngine::new` inside clippy's limit. `pipeline::ProfileRendering` is its borrowed twin, and is `Copy` because a shared reference is.
- **⚠ The LUT is linear in scene value while the curve is defined in log-exposure, so its resolution is worst in the shadows — measured, not assumed.** At 1024 entries it drifted **0.61 of an 8-bit level** from the spline, so it is **4096** now and **shrinks its top with the baseline gain**, worth **4.6×** at the real baseline (0.05 of a level against 0.23). ⚠ The shrink's sabotage was **inert at a half-level threshold** and only became discriminating once it was tightened to a tenth — the bound the design actually delivers, with 2× margin.
- **⚠ An identity `ProfileToneCurve` is ignored.** The X-T4's is literally `[[0,0],[1,1]]`, so honouring it would produce no rendering at all — indistinguishable from `None`, and the setting would silently do nothing on the author's own camera.
- **⚠ `spline.rs` was extracted from `tone_curve.rs`** so both curves share one Fritsch–Carlson implementation. Its control points are clamped to `[0,1]`, which is why `BUILT_IN` is stored in **normalised** log-exposure rather than raw stops.
- **⚠⚠ A FOURTH VARIANT OF THE HARNESS-LIES TRAP: `cargo test $2` DOES NOT WORD-SPLIT IN ZSH.** All three Auto Tone sabotages reported `DID NOT COMPILE` while cargo was rejecting a malformed package name. Same root as the `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` and grep-shape variants already recorded — **use an array**, and the same bug bit `$FILES` when running the probes.
- **Kept probes:** `curve_probe` (pools frames, emits `BUILT_IN` candidates), `strip_vs_canvas` (the acceptance measurement, per rendering), `rendering_vs_contrast` (the exposure+contrast fit), `rendering_sheet` (contact sheets: colorimetric | rendering | camera JPEG).
- **⚠⚠ TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY USING IT, AND THE FIRST PREDATES THE RENDERING ENTIRELY.** Reported as *"the camera rendering toggle doesn't persist after switching photos, it's always reset to off"*. The seam assigned both preferences **unconditionally** — `edits.lens_correction = ...; edits.rendering = ...` — so **every photo that had ever been edited had its stored value overwritten on every open**: the rendering with `None`, and the lens corrections with all-off. ⚠ The old code's own comment claimed the opposite of what the line beneath it did (*"A photo already edited keeps exactly what it had"*), which is why it read as correct for months. `Settings::initial_edits(stored)` is the one decision now, and its test asserts **both** fields — a fix restoring only the reported one would leave the other silently broken, since the same line sets them.
  - ⚠ **Residual, stated rather than discovered:** turning the rendering *off* on a photo with no other edits makes the edits neutral, so `persist` deletes the sidecar and the preference turns it back on next time. Lens correction has always behaved that way. Fixing it needs `Edits.rendering` to become an `Option` — "not decided" against "decided off" — which is the `BaselineExposure` distinction one layer up, and is not built.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE SECOND: *"a bit desaturated and slightly darker compared to the jpeg"* — MEASURED, AND THE LUMINANCE WAS ALREADY RIGHT.** `sat_probe` (kept): pooled over 8 frames our render sat at **0.945×** the camera's saturation, and **0.855–0.931×** through the four best-sampled midtone buckets, while mean signed luminance was **−0.03 EV, i.e. marginally brighter**. ⚠ So "darker" is very likely the same cause: a less saturated image reads as darker (Helmholtz–Kohlrausch). The residual is the **film simulation the camera applies on top of its transfer**, which no tone curve can produce — what a profile's `ProfileLookTable` would otherwise supply.
  - **`CHROMA_BOOST` scales chroma about the pixel's own luminance**, so the tone the curve just decided is untouched and only the colour opens up. Pooled **0.945× → 1.001×**.
  - **⚠⚠ A CHROMA SCALE OF 1.18 DELIVERS A SATURATION GAIN OF ONLY ~1.12, AND THAT IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN A MIS-FIT.** Holding luminance fixed multiplies `(max−min)/max` by `s·max / (y + (max−y)·s)`, strictly less than `s` for every non-neutral pixel. The constant is therefore defined by what it **achieves** — swept over 1.16 / 1.18 / 1.20 for 0.996 / **1.001** / 1.007.
  - **⚠ It tapers at BOTH ends, each for its own reason.** Near white the measurement says we are **already 1.077×** the camera, because our shoulder is gentler, and boosting there would turn a documented caveat into neon clipping. Near black what reads as "saturation" is chroma **noise** — both renders measure ~0.83 there, which no real content produces — so a 12% boost is 12% more speckle, and NR is upstream and defaults to off. ⚠ The shadow taper is justified by **noise, not by the fit**, and the fit agrees: the deepest bucket needed no boost at 1.006×.
  - **⚠ A sabotage that moves the luminance along with the chroma is caught**, because a boost that also brightened would be a brightness change wearing a saturation label and nothing else here could tell them apart. ⚠ The taper test asserts a **fraction of the boost** rather than absolute numbers, so tuning the constant cannot silently invalidate the property.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the built-in curve and the fallback baseline are both X-T4-derived** — the shape family is universal but the numbers are Fuji's, and the proper per-camera route is a profile carrying tag 50940 and 50730, which most community DCPs omit; (2) the shoulder is gentler than the camera's, so bright highlights hold more detail and less punch; (3) below −4.75 EV the curve renders black, so shadow detail must be lifted by Shadows/Blacks *upstream*; (4) an already-edited photo keeps whatever it stored, so a catalogue edited before today opens colorimetric and looks inconsistent beside new work until each old photo is touched; (4b) ~~**turning the rendering off on an otherwise-unedited photo does not stick**~~ ✅ **fixed 2026-09-08** by the `Option` ("Decided off" section); (5) ~~**`MEDIAN_TARGET` was tuned without a rendering** and may want re-judging by eye now that an S-curve follows it~~ ✅ re-judged 2026-09-08, 0.22 → 0.20; (6) Auto Tone still disagrees with the camera on deliberately dark frames, by design; (7) no menu of alternative renderings (filmic/AgX/ACES) — that is the creative-LUT direction and reuses this seam; (8) **`CHROMA_BOOST` is a scalar with two tapers, not a look table** — it matches the camera's *mean* saturation, not its per-hue behaviour, which is what a real `ProfileLookTable` would carry.

**Wave 3 landed, then five fixes from using it (2026-09-02).** M5.3a spot removal · M6.3a/b XMP · M5.4a auto tone · three M7.6 tool gaps. 1627 → **1728 tests**, 51 → **53 goldens**.

- **⚠⚠ A THUMBNAIL REVERTED THE MOMENT YOU NAVIGATED AWAY, AND IT HELD FOR EVERY EDIT.** `thumb_request` prefers the live session's edits **only while the photo is open**; every other row renders through `PhotoEntry::edits`, which `entries::build` read at folder-open time and **nothing invalidated**. So resetting a crop updated the filmstrip immediately and then showed the old cropped thumbnail as soon as you arrowed on. ⚠ `refresh_entry_edits` re-reads the sidecar into the row, from the **three** places that write one — persist-on-switch, a paste or sync, and the `Sync` arms of undo and redo. ⚠ It **re-reads** rather than taking the edits as an argument, because a sync merges only the selected *groups* into each target and the result differs per photo. ⚠ **Not `rebuild_entries`**, which also re-sorts — under "last edited" that reorders the filmstrip under you mid-edit, which this file already records as worse than a stale order.
- **⚠⚠ THE WHITES SLIDER DID NOTHING ON MOST PHOTOGRAPHS, AND THE FIRST DIAGNOSIS ("the weight is too narrow") WAS THE WRONG ONE.** The region weights read as perceptual thirds — blacks under a third, whites over two thirds — but `l` arrives as **linear** luminance, where 2/3 is sRGB **0.85** and 1/3 is sRGB **0.60**. So Whites saw only the top sliver while Blacks covered more than half the visible range. In a scene-referred buffer the 99.9th percentile sits near 0.1–0.3 **even after an auto exposure**, so `max(0, 3l−2)` was exactly zero and Auto Tone could not solve for it at all. The weights are taken on the **display-encoded** luminance now.
- **⚠ The end pair is a cube, not a hard cutoff**, so the slider always has leverage — and `END_STRENGTH = 0.7` exists because a cube also *lowers* the weight everywhere: at the shared 0.5 that made **Blacks weaker in real shadows than the cutoff it replaced**, trading one defect for another. 0.7 restores its reach and still leaves Blacks below Shadows at mid-grey, so the two stay distinguishable; Whites overtakes Highlights only above linear ~0.46, which is what a white point should do.
- **⚠ Measured on three real frames after the fix: Whites and Blacks land *interior* where they used to park at 0 or a boundary.** ⚠ One frame still reads whites 0, and for a **different and correct** reason — Auto's `TAIL_SEPARATION` guard: its highlight is under one stop above the median, so there is no real highlight to set a point on.
- **⚠ `MEDIAN_TARGET` 0.16 → 0.22** (+0.46 EV) — the results read a bit too dark. ⚠ **`TEXTURE_GUIDED_EPS` 8e-4 → 2e-4** — at −80 the edges were still visibly softening. Measured, edge kept against texture removed: 8e-4 **94.9 %/53 %**, 2e-4 **98.6 %/29 %**. ⚠⚠ **That trade is inherent to a single-`eps` guided filter and cannot be tuned away** — the base preserves whatever has variance above `eps`, and fine texture has variance too, only about a decade below the edge's. Edge preservation is this half of the slider's whole stated purpose, so it wins.
- **⚠ Three goldens re-authored, each because the model moved**, and both tone goldens gained discriminating power (`tone_regions` pinned 100 → 44, distinct 129 → 144). `combined`'s 23 % pinning is **pre-existing**, checked rather than assumed.
- **⚠⚠ THREE TESTS WERE RE-AUTHORED AND TWO OF THEM COVERED GUARDS WHOSE TRIGGERING CASE NO LONGER EXISTS.** A stronger stage brought "0.98 from 3.0" into range, quietly turning a *boundary* test into a test of the solved path; its fixture is 10.0 now. And the no-leverage branch is **unreachable** for any input a photograph can produce — recorded as inert defence in depth rather than covered by an invented fixture, the handling A used for its degenerate-radius guard. ⚠ `texture_minus_100_removes_the_fine_band`'s threshold has now moved **twice** (0.2 → 0.35 → 0.55), each time because the model changed and never to make a red test green.
- **⚠ Two ownership exceptions during the wave, both correct and both reported rather than hidden:** A edited `adjustments.rs` (barred to it) because two assertions enumerate the edit groups the panel hides and a ninth group is exactly what they exist to flag — the instructions genuinely conflicted and it chose the green gate; D exposed `range_weights` on `ColorGradingStage` rather than as the free function the spec named, because reaching the free function needs `pub mod stages` in **A's file**. Verified: `mod stages` really is private.
- **⚠⚠ ALL FOUR AGENTS WERE KILLED MID-FLIGHT BY A SESSION RATE LIMIT AND EVERY ONE RESUMED WITH ITS CONTEXT INTACT** — `SendMessage` to the agent id, never a fresh spawn. Nothing was lost; each had already committed its plan. **That is the recovery path for a rate-limited wave.**

**Compare and Survey, round four (2026-09-02) — two corrections.** 1626 → **1627 tests**; 51 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ P PICK-AND-REMOVE BELONGS TO SURVEY, NOT COMPARE — I put it in the wrong view.** The subtractive act is Survey's: the wall you are narrowing *is* the point there. Both `P` and `X` now take a photo off it, because once you have said keep-this or lose-this there is nothing left to ask about it. ⚠ **A rating still does not drop**, and neither does `U` — un-flagging *undoes* a decision, so a photo made undecided again belongs back on the wall. Compare's keys mark and nothing else: it holds two frames still so you can decide between them, and removing one mid-comparison is the opposite of that.
- **⚠⚠ CENTRING ON EVERY KEYPRESS FELT ABRUPT, AND IT IS — the picture moved even when what you selected was already comfortably in view.** The grids follow a **band** now, `FOLLOW_BAND` = 25%–75% of the viewport: inside it nothing scrolls at all; outside it the view moves *just far enough* to bring the row back to the near edge. That is what every file browser and text editor does, and nobody notices it, which is the point. ⚠ **A band, not a point** — sabotaging `FOLLOW_BAND` to `(0.5, 0.5)` fails four tests, which is the guard on the whole idea.
- **⚠ `follow_offset` returns `Option`, and `None` means "do not move" rather than "move to where you already are".** Forcing `vertical_scroll_offset` even to its current value pins the scroll area for that frame and fights any smooth scrolling in flight. ⚠ A clamp landing back on the current offset therefore also reports no move — which at the top and bottom of the grid it always does.
- **⚠⚠ THE CURRENT OFFSET HAS TO BE KNOWN *BEFORE* THE SCROLL AREA IS BUILT**, because the band decision is an input to constructing it — so it is remembered on `RustroomApp` (`grid_scroll`, `survey_scroll`) from each frame's `ScrollAreaOutput` rather than read from the area being drawn. This is the one piece of state the feature genuinely needs.
- **⚠ A test asserted something FALSE about correct code, and then `cargo fmt` hid the fix.** Position 30 at 3 columns is row **10**, not row 9 — and the assertion had been reflowed onto three lines before the patch that corrected it ran, so the single-line match applied to nothing and the test still failed. **A scripted edit that matches nothing exits 0**, the trap this file already records for `sed`.
- **⚠ Removing the two Compare tests left orphaned doc comments**, which `cargo test` accepts and `clippy -D warnings` rejects (`empty lines after doc comment`). **Seventh recorded instance** of clippy catching what a green test run did not.

**Compare and Survey, round three (2026-09-02).** Four items, and one of them fixed a rule I had only half applied. 1610 → **1626 tests**; 51 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE ARROWS MOVED THE CANDIDATE WHICHEVER PANE WAS BORDERED, WHICH MADE THE BORDER A HALF-TRUTH.** Round two moved the culling keys onto the bordered pane and left the arrows where they were — *"the border is where the buttons work" has to mean **every** key or it means none of them.* ⚠ Stepping the reference is `compare_pair::step` **with the roles swapped**: the function's job is "walk the pool, skipping this one photo", and which of the two is being held still is the caller's business. No second traversal, no second rule.
- **⚠ Stepping the reference uses `focus`, falling back to `click`** — `click` replaces the whole selection, which would collapse "compare exactly these" to one photo and silently widen the pool to the entire folder. The rule the deleted `promote` already carried.
- **⚠⚠ P PICKS *AND* TAKES THE PHOTO OUT OF THE COMPARISON; X DELIBERATELY DOES NOT.** Compare exists to settle which of a run of near-identical frames to keep, so once one is picked there is nothing left to ask about it and leaving it in the pool means meeting it again on every pass. A reject is different — there you are choosing a keeper, so an `X` is a note about a frame you are *still comparing against*. ⚠ That asymmetry has its own test: one that only checked "P removes" would pass against a version where **every** culling key removed. ⚠ It fires only when the photo is genuinely in the selection, because below two selected the pool is the whole visible list and there is nothing to remove it from.
- **⚠ The captions are the photograph's own name.** "Reference — " and "Candidate — " prefixed every one with a word the border already says, and at a glance the label read first and the filename second — backwards, when the filename is what you are trying to keep track of.
- **⚠⚠ `grid_nav` HOLDS BOTH NAVIGATION RULES AS PURE ARITHMETIC, SHARED BY BOTH GRIDS.** `step_by` (move by ±1 or ±`cols` through the visible list) and `centre_offset`. A scroll position worked out inline in two places is how the two come to disagree about where a photo is — and neither function has ever seen a `Ui`.
- **⚠⚠ THE CLAMP IS WHAT GIVES "unless it is close to the border" FOR FREE.** Centring the first row wants a negative offset and centring the last wants one past the end; both are impossible, so those rows stay where they are and everything between centres. **No special case at all** — the behaviour the user described falls out of clamping to the real range.
- **⚠⚠ ARITHMETIC, NOT `scroll_to_me`.** egui computes a `scroll_to_me` target offset **once** and never recomputes it — the drift bug the filmstrip already paid for, where the centred photo slid away as thumbnails ahead of it loaded and reflowed the content. A grid of fixed-height rows can be placed exactly instead. ⚠ And the offset is forced **only on the frame a key moved the selection**: setting it every frame would fight the wheel, dragging you back the moment you scrolled away.
- **⚠ The pitch is the cell PLUS `item_spacing.y`**, because `show_rows` adds that gap itself. Using the bare cell height drifts further from the truth with every row and centres the wrong photo near the bottom of a long grid.
- **⚠⚠ `step_by` AGREES WITH `format_filter::step` AT EVERY INDEX IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, ASSERTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED — and the first version did not.** `binary_search`'s `Err(p)` is **already** the position *above* a hidden `cur`, so a naive `p + delta` skips one going forward; the grid would then disagree with the filmstrip about where the same arrow goes from the same photo. A parity test over 40 starting indices is what pins it.
- **⚠ The Library grid gained vertical arrows at all** — it stepped left-to-right only, so reaching a photo four rows down meant holding `→` through everything between. ⚠ `cols` moved *above* the keyboard block so one value serves both the arrows and the layout, rather than two derivations that could disagree about the grid's shape.
- **⚠ A test asserted something FALSE about correct code:** `centre_offset` with **zero columns** repairs to one (`cols.max(1)`) and returns a usable offset, where the test demanded 0.0. A repair that produces a usable answer is the point, and the assertion was written as though it were a refusal.
