- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **arrows on the reference pane change which photo is the reference**, which changes what "the pool minus the reference" contains — deliberate, and the only way the border can own every key; (2) picking removes from the *selection*, so a Compare entered without one (fewer than two selected) picks without removing; (3) the centring is per keypress, so scrolling manually and then pressing an arrow snaps back to the selection.

**Compare and Survey, round two (2026-09-02) — eight items, all from using the finished views.** 1599 → **1610 tests**; 51 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ ZOOM AND PAN ARE PER PANE, REVERSING A DECISION I SHIPPED ONE COMMIT EARLIER.** Sharing them was defensible on its own terms — *"which is sharper here"* wants the same part of both frames — and it is wrong for a reason the argument never reached: **the two photographs are not registered to each other.** They were shot seconds apart, handheld, with the subject moving. A shared view therefore puts the eye you are inspecting in one pane and an ear in the other, so the sharing *defeats* the alignment it was introduced to guarantee. **A property that would hold for two renders of one file does not hold for two frames of one moment.**
- **⚠⚠ THE X KEY DID NOTHING, AND THE CAUSE WAS `culling_key` REFUSING EVERY MODIFIER.** `Shift+X` is how anyone types the capital `X` the on-screen hints literally show, so the key the hint named was rejected. Shift is allowed now; **command, control and alt are not**, because ⌘X is cut and ⌘A is select-all. ⚠ **Nothing covered this until a sabotage restoring `modifiers.any()` went UNCAUGHT** — the hint said "X rejects", the test pressed `Key::X` with `Modifiers::NONE`, and the two never met.
- **⚠⚠ THE PLACEHOLDER JUMPED IN SIZE WHEN THE DECODE LANDED**, because the thumbnail was capped at `MAX_UPSCALE` = 2× while the render fills the pane. `TileImage::Placeholder` fits with **no cap**: it is not a picture in its own right, it is a stand-in for one, and it must occupy exactly the rect the render will. The 2× cap that is right for a Survey tile is wrong here for that reason alone.
- **⚠⚠ ENTER MOVES THE BORDER; IT DOES NOT MOVE THE PHOTOGRAPHS.** It used to promote the candidate to reference, which swapped the two panes — and a view whose whole job is holding two frames still while you look between them must not rearrange itself under you. The border was already saying which pane the keys act on, so `compare_focus` makes that the thing Enter changes. ⚠ Clicking a pane now focuses it on **either** side; the asymmetry that made a click on the reference a deliberate no-op went with the promotion.
- **⚠⚠ SURVEY SCROLLS, AND THE CAP IT REPLACES WAS THE BUG.** It filled one screenful, capped at `MAX_TILES` = 24, and said *"showing 24 of 39"* — which **named the problem and offered no way out**: the remaining photographs were simply unreachable. Virtualised, the cap is gone *and* the memory bound it existed for is **stronger**, because `retain_window` now pins only what is on screen. The Library grid solved the identical problem the identical way. ⚠ Its test was **replaced, not deleted** — it used to assert the cap — and the new one asserts the **want-list**, not the tile count, since a view drawing fewer tiles while still asking for all 300 would pass a "how many tiles?" check and blow the cache anyway.
- **⚠ Survey's tile size is its own setting, not the grid's.** The two are used for opposite things: the grid is for finding a photo among hundreds and wants small cells, a survey is for looking at a handful and wants large ones. `SURVEY_CELL_RANGE` runs to **900** against the grid's 320, because filling a large monitor with three photographs is the complaint that put the control there. Same pinch / ctrl+scroll gesture, same settle-then-save rule.
- **⚠ Up and down move by a whole row.** With a scrolling grid, left-to-right alone leaves a long selection unreachable by keyboard — you would hold `→` through forty photographs. ⚠ **The test needed the smallest tile size**: on the 400×300 harness screen the default 320 pt tile gives **one** column, where a row and a step are the same thing and the test could not see the difference it names.
- **⚠ Each tile carries a pick and a remove button**, shown on hover or on the focused tile — two glyphs on every tile of a forty-photo wall is more chrome than photograph. ⚠ Registered **after** the tile so they win the hit test, and the glyphs are `⚑` and `×`, the two the mark badges already use: `✓`, `✔`, `✕` and `✗` are in **no** bundled font.
- **⚠ `nearest_centre` was deleted with the shared zoom** — clippy caught it as dead code the moment the anchor moved into `Pane::navigate`, which is where it belongs now that each pane anchors against its own rect.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **Compare's arrows still move the candidate whichever pane is bordered** — the reference is changed from the grid, since letting the arrows move it would change the pool underneath the view; (2) the survey's tile size is global rather than per folder; (3) a survey larger than a screenful shows grey briefly while scrolling, inherent to loading on demand and the same trade the Library grid makes.

**Compare and Survey, finished (M6.5, 2026-09-02) — W2's track D closed.** 1590 → **1599 tests**; 51 goldens untouched. Both changes came from using the shipped views, and the two complaints had one cause between them.

- **⚠⚠ "THE IMAGES ARE QUITE SMALL" HAD A ONE-LINE CAUSE: `photo_tile` REFUSED TO UPSCALE AT ALL.** `.min(1.0)` — `thumb::cell`'s rule, so a grid thumbnail is never magnified into softness. But a Compare pane or a three-tile Survey gives each photo several hundred points, and a 512 px thumbnail then sat in the middle of it with grey all round.
- **⚠⚠ AND "IT SHOULD ZOOM, SAME AS IN DEVELOP" IS THE SAME PROBLEM ONE LAYER DOWN: A THUMBNAIL CANNOT ANSWER THE QUESTION COMPARE EXISTS FOR.** Choosing between two nearly identical frames is *precisely* about sharpness and focus, and zooming a 512 px thumbnail magnifies a blur. So **Compare builds a real decode per pane**; Survey keeps the thumbnails and merely fills its tiles (capped at **2×**), because composition and expression read fine at 512 px and a wall of decodes would not fit in memory. **The two views diverge here deliberately.**
- **⚠⚠ ONE ZOOM AND ONE PAN ACROSS BOTH PANES — THE FEATURE, NOT AN ECONOMY.** The question is "which of these is sharper *here*", so the two must show the same part of the frame at the same magnification; independent views would let you compare an eye against an ear and believe the answer. The gesture is read from whichever pane the pointer is over, through the **same `pan_delta`** the Develop canvas uses — which is why that function moved from `pub(super)` to `pub(crate)` rather than being copied.
- **⚠ `multi::pane::Pane` is a cut-down `Session`** — a buffer, an engine, a texture — carrying no history, no snapshots and no sidecar state. Nothing here edits anything, so a real `Session` would be most of a photo editor standing behind a picture you are only looking at.
- **⚠⚠ `draw_scale` MEASURES THE FRAME THE TEXTURE CAME FROM, NEVER THE RUNG THAT WAS ASKED FOR.** A render lands a frame or two after the request, and a crop makes the frame smaller than its cap — so a scale computed from `max_dim` sizes the picture by a number that does not describe it. **This project has paid for exactly that confusion twice, in `render_max_dim` and in `fit_image_size`.** Measuring the texture is *self-correcting*: a higher rung gives a larger texture and a proportionally smaller scale, so the picture holds still on screen and only gets sharper.
- **⚠ Whole-image renders, never regions**, which is the one place Compare departs from the canvas. The canvas renders a sub-rect at high zoom because a full-resolution buffer is 26 Mpx of tail work per slider move; nothing is being edited here, so the render happens once per rung and a whole image lets panning be a texture offset with **no re-render at all**.
- **⚠⚠ AT MOST ONE DECODE IN FLIGHT.** `DecodeWorker` caps concurrency and keeps only the **newest** queued request, so asking for both panes at once can drop one — and a dropped request is never retried, leaving that pane stuck on its thumbnail with nothing to say why. ⚠ A failed decode leaves `pending` set for that path, so it is asked for **once** rather than retried every frame. ⚠ `DecodeWorker::requests_issued` was added for the test, unconditionally, for `RenderEngine::requests_issued`'s reason: `in_flight` is not a substitute, since a decode that has finished or failed is no longer in flight and the assertion would pass whether or not the request was ever made.
- **⚠ The panes are released by ONE edge check** (`release_compare_panes`), not at each exit: `to_grid`, Escape, `G`, the segmented switch and `open_entry` all leave Compare, and one of the five would be forgotten. Each pane holds a ~78 MB decode and a render worker. ⚠ It is a method on `RustroomApp` rather than an `if` inside `shell.rs`'s `ui` **so a test can call it** — an `eframe::Frame` cannot be constructed in one.
- **⚠ The pair is derived ONCE, in `plan`.** Resolving it again inside the draw closure would be two computations of one fact — and while they read the same `lib` in the same frame and so agree today, that is the shape `render_max_dim`, `fit_image_size` and the crop feedback loop each were. ⚠ `pool` is still computed live inside, because the arrow keys step through it.
- **⚠ The pane image is clipped explicitly** with `Ui::set_clip_rect`: `paint_at` honours the `Ui`'s **clip** rect and not its layout rect, so without it a zoomed photo spills across the divider onto its neighbour — the one thing a comparison view must never do.
- **⚠⚠ SURVEY'S POINT IS A SUBTRACTIVE ACT.** Reported as "I don't get the point of survey" — and without one it really is a smaller Library grid. `X` now rejects **and** takes the photo off the wall, so the survey shrinks toward the keeper as you work. ⚠ **Rating must NOT drop**: a star is a judgement you go on to compare against the others, and a survey that emptied itself as you rated would destroy the very set you were choosing within. That is its own test, because a "does X drop it?" assertion structurally cannot see it. ⚠ The drop is applied **after** the mark — `drop_from_survey` moves the focus off the photo, and reversed the reject would land on whichever tile the focus had just moved to.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **Compare's top rung is the *preview* buffer's long edge** (3123 px on an X-T4), not the full-resolution decode — so it is 1:1 against a half-size decode, the same limit Develop had before its full-res upgrade; (2) a pane costs ~78 MB and a worker thread while Compare is open, ~350 MB for the pair with their prefix caches; (3) **arriving in Compare shows thumbnails for ~1 s** while the decodes run, which is better than grey but is the thing most likely to be reported as softness; (4) Survey's tiles are upscaled thumbnails and are *meant* to be soft — sharpness is Compare's job; (5) zoom and pan persist while stepping candidates, deliberately, since comparing the same detail across frames is the point; (6) `MAX_TILES` still caps Survey at 24.

**White balance is Kelvin and tint (M5.2, 2026-09-01).** An absolute instrument with presets and an eyedropper, serving RAW and non-RAW through one path. 1557 → **1584 tests**; **49 of 51 goldens byte-identical**, and the two that moved were re-authored rather than regenerated.

- **⚠⚠ THE PLAN'S TASK 3 WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** It threaded the source's **camera matrix** into the pipeline so gains could be computed in camera RGB. But `raw-decode` converts camera → **linear ProPhoto** before the pipeline ever runs, so by the time `WhiteBalanceStage` multiplies anything a neutral object already reads as equal RGB: **there is no camera space left to balance in.** What the stage performs is an *adaptation between two illuminants* — the one the decode balanced to, and the one the slider declares — and the matrix it needs is the **working space's**. ⚠ Verified before rewriting: `backend_rsraw` calls `camera_to_prophoto`.
- **⚠⚠ SO `RenderSpec` CARRIES A BASELINE `Illuminant`, NOT A MATRIX**, and that is the *better* per-source fact: after decode both a RAW and a JPEG have neutral at equal RGB, and all that differs is **what temperature to call that state**. RAW → the camera's as-shot white; anything else → D65, which is what a camera JPEG genuinely *is* rather than a fallback. `adaptation_gains(declared, baseline)` is `rgb(baseline)/rgb(declared)`, luminance-normalised — **exactly `[1,1,1]` when the two agree**, which is what gives the control a meaningful zero.
- **⚠⚠ `PROPHOTO_TO_XYZ` IS D65-REFERRED, COMPOSED FROM ITS TWO HALVES RATHER THAN RE-DERIVED.** `PROPHOTO_TO_SRGB` carries the Bradford D50→D65 step, so a neutral buffer pixel *displays* as D65 — verified, `[1,1,1]` → xy (0.31270, 0.32900) against published (0.31272, 0.32903). ⚠ **Do not substitute the inverse of `XYZ_D50_TO_PROPHOTO`**: same space, D50 frame, and the slider's no-op point silently moves to **5003 K**. Its sabotage fails three tests.
- **⚠⚠ THE AS-SHOT SOLVE MUST USE `cam_xyz`, NOT `rgb_cam` — AND THE WRONG ONE LOOKS PLAUSIBLE.** `rgb_cam` maps camera → sRGB **with the white balance already applied**, which is exactly why every row sums to 1: a balanced neutral goes to a balanced neutral. Put `1/cam_mul` through it and the sensor's green dominance is counted **twice**. Measured on a real X-T4 frame: `rgb_cam` lands **0.084 off the daylight locus — a tint of 140 on a ±100 control** — where `cam_xyz` gives **4762 K at 0.015 off**. ⚠ The Kelvin is plausible either way; **only the distance from the locus sees it**, which is what the gated test asserts. `cargo run --release -p raw-decode --example wb_probe <raw>`, kept.
- **⚠ `kelvin: None` inserts NO STAGE**, rather than computing gains that ought to be `[1,1,1]`. Neutral is then bit-exact rather than trusted, which is the rule the whole golden harness rests on. Sabotaging `is_active` to `true` fails **18** goldens.
- **⚠ No migration code.** Serde ignores the unknown `temp`, so an existing `{"temp":0.0,"tint":0.0}` reads as `kelvin: None` — exactly right, and every one of the 49 real copies is that. A *non-zero* legacy `temp` is silently dropped; that is the honest cost of a value that never had a Kelvin meaning, and none exists in this catalogue.
- **⚠ "As Shot" stores `None`, never the numeric baseline.** `None` keeps the photo *following* its file; freezing the number would strand it at a value nobody chose — the rule an unlabelled virtual copy already follows. It clears the tint too, because "as shot" is the camera's whole choice.
- **⚠ The Kelvin slider is linear in MIREDS**, not in Kelvin: equal mired steps are equal perceptual steps, so a Kelvin-linear slider spends most of its travel in the blues and crams the tungsten range into its first few pixels. ⚠ It is drawn **only once a temperature exists** — that absence *is* the "As Shot" state, and inventing a number would freeze it the moment anyone touched the slider.
- **⚠⚠ THE EYEDROPPER READS THE DECODE BUFFER, NEVER THE RENDERED TEXTURE.** The texture already has the white balance applied, so solving from it makes the pick chase its own correction: click a grey, the picture shifts, click the same grey again and it shifts further. `Session` keeps an `Arc` of the buffer for this — free, the worker owns the same allocation — and **it must be replaced at the full-resolution handover** alongside `engine`, or the pick reads the half-size buffer at full-resolution coordinates. The click maps through `FrameMap::inverse_point`, so it works on a cropped, rotated photo; it does not un-do lens correction or perspective, the caveat the brush already carries.
- **⚠⚠ THE CANVAS'S OWN SENSE WAS NOT CHANGED, AND TRYING IT BROKE TWO TESTS OUTRIGHT.** `Sense::drag()` never reports `clicked()`, so the obvious fix is `click_and_drag` — which also changes **when egui promotes a press to a drag**, and both mask-drag tests failed with `counts=[0,0,0,0,0]`. Instead a **second, click-sensing interaction** exists only while the eyedropper is armed, registered *after* the canvas response so it wins the hit-test tie. No other gesture can see it.
- **⚠ A mask gets the same control and the same baseline, but `picking: None`** — solving an eyedropper against a region is a different question from solving it against the frame, and `None` says so rather than drawing a control that quietly does nothing.
- **⚠ `SourceInfo`, `SourceFacts` and `RenderSpec` are three params structs this change had to add**, each because a function crossed clippy's seven-argument line. That is the **third, fourth and fifth** time this project has answered `too_many_arguments` that way, after `FrameSpec` and `NameField`. Clippy was right every time.
- **⚠⚠ `warm_wb` AND `combined` WERE RE-AUTHORED, NOT REGENERATED, AND BOTH VALUES WERE SWEPT.** `temp: 60` has no Kelvin equivalent, so regenerating would have baked whatever the new code happens to do into the reference. **5200 K newly pins exactly 116 of 3072 samples — the same as the reference it replaces — while moving 2412 where the old moved 2008.** A warmer choice reads more obviously warm and buys it by clipping (3200 K pins 360), and a clipped sample cannot tell this effect from a stronger one. `combined` at 3600 K is the closest the sweep gets to its predecessor; its clipping is driven by the exposure and contrast beside it and barely moves with Kelvin.
- **⚠ A commit went in with clippy failing again** — an unused `want` binding in a new test. `cargo test` being green is not evidence about clippy. **Sixth recorded instance.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the same Kelvin does not give the same result on a RAW and its own camera JPEG** — the RAW's baseline is the light it was shot under, the JPEG's is D65, and the data to reconcile them does not exist (checked: of **433 JPEGs, zero** carry `ColorTemperature`, and 430 of 430 were shot on Auto); (2) a non-zero legacy `temp` is dropped; (3) **"Auto" is deliberately absent** — it is an analysis pass, not a preset value, and the spec marks it as the one item that could be cut; (4) no baseline readout in the panel (user's call); (5) McCamy's ~2% approximation rides along in the forward solve; (6) the eyedropper does not un-do lens correction or perspective; (7) non-RAW correction is limited by 8-bit data the camera has already rendered.

**Lens correction, a row at a time and in parallel (2026-09-01).** The crop-then-slider stall, measured and mostly removed. 1554 → **1557 tests**; **51 goldens byte-identical**, which is the proof this is a performance change and nothing else.

- **⚠⚠ THE REPORT WAS "after cropping the image is unresponsive for several seconds, then fluid" AND THE MECHANISM IS WORTH KNOWING.** Crop mode **pins** the render to the fit rung. On exit a crop makes the image smaller, so `fit` is larger and `tier_max_dim` **doubles** — and `max_dim` is a **pre-geometry** cap, so the whole prefix re-runs on **4× the pixels** with a cold cache. Everything after that is fast because the prefix is then cached. Nothing was broken; the first frame after a crop is simply the most expensive one the app ever renders.
- **⚠⚠ MEASURED BEFORE OPTIMISING, AND THE MEASUREMENT NAMED A DIFFERENT CULPRIT THAN THE PLAN DID.** On an X-T4 frame at the 3200 rung the prefix was **1178 ms — of which lens correction was 98%** (26 ms with it off). But splitting *that* gave **400 ms in lensfun's coordinate calls and 765 ms in the resampler**: `CLAUDE.md` had recorded row batching as the fix, and row batching alone could only ever have reached a third of it. ⚠ The resampler half is M7.3's doing — Catmull-Rom is **3.2× Bilinear here** (765 vs 242 ms), 16 taps per channel and three channels per pixel, and lens correction is on by default for every RAW. `cargo run --release -p app --example bench_crop_rung <raw>`, kept.
- **⚠⚠ `Corrector::fill_row` MAKES ONE LENSFUN CALL PER ROW WHERE THERE WERE THREE PER PIXEL.** `apply_geometry_distortion`, `apply_subpixel_distortion` and `apply_color_modification_f32` all take `width`/`rows` and are built to fill a row; a 1×1 grid pays their setup — normalisation constants, the `Option` unwrap, the kernel dispatch — once per pixel.
- **⚠⚠ THE BATCH IS EQUIVALENT ONLY AT `applied_scale == 1.0`, AND THAT IS A GUARD RATHER THAN A LIMITATION.** The API steps x by exactly one **output** pixel; `Corrector::scaled` steps by `1/applied_scale`. Scale 1.0 is the default (the Scale slider sits at 0) and therefore what every RAW pays; above it `fill_row` falls back to the per-pixel path rather than being silently wrong. ⚠ `a_scaled_row_matches_the_per_pixel_source` is the only test that can see the guard removed — the failure would be a smooth, plausible image that is simply not the correction asked for.
- **⚠⚠ BIT-IDENTICAL SOURCES NEEDED THE TCA DISPLACEMENT ASSOCIATED EXACTLY AS `source` DOES IT.** `base + (tca − p)` and the mathematically equal `tca + (base − p)` disagree in the **last ulp**, which the first version shipped and a test caught immediately (`-31.909601` vs `-31.909603`). ⚠ Green matched and red/blue did not, which is what pointed straight at the displacement rather than at the batching.
- **⚠ Gains are NOT bit-identical, by ~1e-5, and that is upstream's behaviour rather than ours.** `apply_color_modification` advances `r²` by an **incremental update along the row** instead of recomputing it, so a batched row accumulates f32 rounding that `w` separate one-pixel calls cannot. lensfun always processes a row, so the batched value is the *more* faithful one. ⚠ 5.6 ppm on the gain, ~0.0014 of an 8-bit level — **no golden moved**, which was checked rather than assumed.
- **⚠ `RowMap` exists so the scratch is allocated once per WORKER, not once per row.** The stage uses `for_each_init`, whose initialiser runs once per rayon job; allocating inside `fill_row` would have meant zeroing ~100 KB per row for nothing.
- **⚠ Rayon over OUTPUT rows is sound because this is a gather**: an output pixel reads the whole source and writes only itself. Bit-identical to the sequential form, which is what lets the goldens stand as the check on it. ⚠ `data.par_chunks_exact_mut(stride)` with the `stride == 0` guard — `par_chunks_exact_mut(0)` panics, the same note `map_pixels_xy` already carries.
- **⚠⚠ A SABOTAGE THAT WAS NOT CAUGHT, AND THE REASON IS THE C3 LESSON AGAIN: AN UNREACHABLE GUARD IS AN UNTESTED ONE.** Deleting the identity fill that stands in when there is no distortion pass left the suite green, because the fixture had **all three corrections on** and that branch cannot be reached. The panel has three independent checkboxes, so the test now sweeps four `Corrections` variants — one with each correction off — and the sabotage fails. **Eighteenth instance of a test naming something it could not see.**
- **Result: the 3200 rung's prefix goes 1178 → 309 ms, and the fit rung 310 → 87 ms.** ~3.8×, and it helps **every** render at every zoom, not only the frame after a crop.
- **⚠ RECOMMENDED FOLLOW-UP, deliberately not taken here: the resampler is now ~2/3 of what is left.** Catmull-Rom costs 3.2× Bilinear on this stage, and `Resampler::default()` changed to Catmull-Rom in M7.3 — so every RAW quietly got ~3× more expensive in the pass it always pays. The options are a per-context default (Bilinear while interacting, the chosen kernel on settle) or separating the *lens* resampler from the *geometry* one. **Do not just change the default back**; it is a quality decision the user made.
- **⚠ A commit on `main` had shipped with a clippy warning** — `bench_crop_rung`'s unused `prefix`/`tail` bindings, caught here by `clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`. `cargo test` being green is not evidence about clippy. **Fifth recorded instance.**

**Wave 2 (2026-09-01) — four tracks, zero conflicts, 1454 → 1553 tests.** M7.1a luminance range mask · M6.5a/b compare and survey · M6.1b/c/d metadata, watermark, output sharpening · M5.2 white balance (specced separately).

- **⚠⚠ THREE STANDING DECISIONS FROM M6.1, TAKEN DELIBERATELY (user's call, 2026-09-01) — do not quietly reverse them.**
  1. **Output sharpening runs in the file's DISPLAY space, not linear light**, and this is a *deliberate departure* from the rule `sharpening.rs` follows. Capture sharpening measures the **photograph**, so it is linear; output sharpening is applied to the **file**, in the space the file is written in — which is what Lightroom, Capture One and Photokit Sharpener all do, because linear-light sharpening produces halos brighter than the eye expects. ⚠ Its weights are therefore Rec.709 **Y′** (luma, on gamma-corrected components), **not** `color::luminance`, and that is *not* another stray Rec.709 copy. The technique is otherwise verbatim: high-pass on an achromatic channel, scalar RGB gain, `LUMA_EPS` near-black guard.
  2. **`epaint_default_fonts` is a dependency of `crates/export`.** Data-only, zero dependencies of its own, already compiled in this tree. ⚠ It is an egui-repo crate inside a crate whose rule is "zero GUI dependency" — accepted because that rule exists so `export` can be driven headless by the CLI and tests, which a `static [u8]` of font bytes does not threaten. Alternatives were vendoring ~200 KB (the vendored `Phosphor.ttf` has **no Latin letters**) or moving the watermark into `app` and losing CLI reach.
  3. **`export` depends on `pipeline`** for `spatial::gaussian_blur`, read-only. Acyclic, and it honours "reuse the blurs" rather than writing a second Gaussian — but ⚠ **it inverts the obvious layering**, and that is hard to undo later.
- **⚠ `img-parts` was evaluated and rejected on COVERAGE, not cost.** Pure Rust, permissive, and its three deps are already here — but it handles **JPEG/PNG/RIFF only**, and two of the five formats are TIFF, so it would have left TIFF hand-rolled as a *second* code path. Metadata is one hand-rolled XMP packet for all three containers instead. ⚠ The TIFF half rewrites the IFD **at the end of the file** and patches the header pointer, because growing it in place would shift every byte after it and invalidate every offset.
- **⚠⚠ `CoverageSource` HAD A CATCH-ALL AND IT WAS A LIVE TRAP.** `MaskStage::new_in_region` read `other => Analytic(other.clone())`, so a new `Mask` variant whose `analytic_coverage` returns `None` fell through to `unwrap_or(0.0)` — **a mask covering nothing, silently.** Exactly the failure the `Option` exists to prevent, defeated one layer up. The match is exhaustive now, so a fifth variant is a compile error there.
- **⚠ A luminance mask draws NOTHING on the canvas, and a coverage tint would be actively misleading** — the only pixels the canvas has are the rendered texture, which **already has this mask's adjustment applied**, so a tint from them would show the mask selecting the tones it has just moved. Doing it honestly needs coverage against the pre-mask buffer, which lives on the render worker: a different feature.
- **⚠ `MAX_TILES = 24` in Survey is a MEMORY bound before a legibility one.** `retain_window` **pins** the window and never evicts it, so an uncapped survey would pin past the 512 MB budget with nothing sheddable. A `const _: () = assert!(..)` makes raising it a build error, and the cap's test asserts the **want-list length**, not the tile count — capping the drawing alone would still pin the whole cache.

**Resampling is a setting (M7.3, 2026-09-01).** 1422 → **1446 tests**; 6 of 49 goldens moved.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED ON REAL FRAMES BEFORE ANYTHING WAS ADOPTED, at the user's instruction** — the agent was scoped to *report only*. Acutance retained after one resample: bilinear **54–58%**, Mitchell 56–60%, **Catmull-Rom 72–74%**, Lanczos3 82–84%. Ringing, in 8-bit levels: **0.0 / 2.7 / 19 / 28**. ⚠ **Acutance alone rewards a kernel for keeping grain**, so detail-vs-flat was measured separately: the unresampled frame is 2.17 and Lanczos3 (2.38) and Catmull-Rom (2.44) sit *closer* to it than bilinear (2.69) — the sharp kernels' gain is genuine detail, and **bilinear is the one that distorts the original balance most**.
- **⚠ Mitchell is not an interpolating filter** — `B = 1/3` puts weight 0.0556 on each neighbour at zero phase, so it **softens even where nothing moves**. Found by a failing test, not by reading.
- **⚠⚠ BILINEAR IS COMPUTED EXACTLY, NOT FROM THE LUT**, and that is what makes it bit-identical to the sampler it replaced: the table quantises the phase, and the generic tap distance gives `1.0 - (1.0 - f)`, which is **not** `f` in f32 (0.100000024 at f = 0.1).
- **⚠ The kernel rides the three resampling `PrefixStepKey` variants**, not `PrefixKey` beside `scale` — so a kernel change is an ordinary cache miss, and on a photo that resamples nothing it invalidates **nothing**.
- **⚠ Two sabotages caught what every other test structurally could not**: hardcoding a kernel inside `build_pipeline_with` or `render_cached` leaves all 49 goldens *and* the whole suite green, because every other test compares two renders passing the **same** kernel. Both guards use a hard edge; a uniform buffer or a smooth ramp cannot see it, since every kernel agrees on those.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) thumbnails always use the default; (2) Lanczos3 rings — measured, not asserted; (3) the LUT quantises the phase to 1024 (max error 4e-4, invisible at 8-bit).

**Copy labels (M6.0, 2026-09-01) — the naming half of M3.4, replaced.** A virtual copy carries a **label**, not a name that is also its export filename. 1415 → **1422 tests**; 49 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE OLD DESIGN WAS CORRECT AND ITS PREMISE WAS REMOVED — this is not a correction of a mistake.** *"A copy's name IS its export name"* followed from the name determining the filename, and so did every clause under it, **including the field showing a whole filename with its extension**. M6.1a's template took that job, leaving a field that looked like a filename and decided nothing. ⚠ This is the **third** shape copy naming has taken (append → replace → label); record why "replace" was right at the time, or the next reader restores it.
- **⚠⚠ THE EVIDENCE CAME FROM REAL SIDECARS, NOT FROM ARGUMENT.** Of three named copies in the author's folders, **only one was a label anyone chose** — the other two stored `"DSCF6712_1.RAF"`, the *derived default*, frozen because the field displayed a whole filename and committing it wrote that string back. **A field that looks like a filename invites you to freeze a name you never picked.** ⚠ The user deleted all three (backup at `~/Desktop/rustroom-sidecar-backup-20260901-131723/`), so nothing needed migrating; the read rule exists for that backup and for anyone else.
- **⚠ A READ RULE, NOT A MIGRATION.** `normalise_label` discards a stored value equal to the old derived default; anything else loses a trailing extension and becomes the label. No version bump, no backfill, and it normalises on the next write. ⚠ Deliberately **not** "strip a leading `{stem}_` prefix" — same outcome by a rule that can be wrong.
- **⚠ `#[serde(alias = "name")]` is what keeps every existing sidecar loading.** Validated end to end on the backup with `cargo run --release -p app --example label_probe <dir>` (kept): `"DSC"` survives, both frozen defaults resolve to `None`.
- **⚠⚠ COPY 0 IS LABELLABLE, and that is what removed the field's last excuse for guessing.** The old refusal existed because a copy-0 "name" would be a second filename; a label is not a filename. So "Colour" beside "B&W" is expressible — **and with copy 0 carrying both a filename and a label, nothing distinguishes them but an explicit choice**, which is why `name_edit::Editing` exists and `rename_draft` split into two.
- **⚠ Caption = the file's name on every row; badge = label-or-number.** `shown_name` split into `entries::caption` and `label_of`. That fixed a defect nobody had named: a labelled copy previously showed *only* its own name and gave no sign of which photograph it came from.
- **⚠ "Edit what you see": the caption renames the FILE, the badge sets the LABEL.** One field per cell, with which string it edits decided by which affordance opened it — two fields would fight for the same space and the same click. The Develop panel's field is the label's discoverable home, since a badge is small. ⚠ Two entry points for one **string** is fine; the Presets panel was removed for being two homes for one **feature**.
- **⚠ `PhotoId::export_stem` is DELETED.** `{name}` is the bare stem, `{copy}` is label-else-number-else-absent. **The old rule survives as the template `{copy}`** — promoted from hardcoded to chosen, which is what templating was for.
- **⚠ Typing back the copy number the badge shows stores `None`**, so the copy keeps *following* its number rather than freezing "2" as a literal label — identical on screen, then wrong if `Reindex` renumbered it.
- **⚠ `NameField` groups four parameters** because `field` reached eight and clippy objected. Fair, and the same answer `FrameSpec` gave when `render_cached` hit the same limit.
- **⚠ Eleven existing tests asserted the replaced rule; two had to be INVERTED** — one required searching `"dscf"` *not* to find a copy, one required a name on the original to be ignored. Each rewrite says what it used to assert, or it reads as broken.
- **⚠ The badge number is deliberately NOT searchable.** The filter matches the stored label, not the rendered badge: matching "1" against every first copy is noise. A test asserting otherwise was wrong about correct code.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) a labelled copy exports as `DSCF1234_B&W.jpg` rather than `B&W.jpg` unless the template is `{copy}` — zero files affected in this catalogue; (2) badge-click-to-edit is not self-advertising, the panel field is the mitigation; (3) labels are not unique; (4) stale `photos` rows for the deleted copies remain, harmless; (5) `copy_hwm` untouched, so numbers are still never reused.

**⚠⚠ A PANEL SECTION COLLAPSED BY THE ARROW DID NOT PERSIST (fixed 2026-09-01, found in use).** `collections_panel::section` had **two click targets that disagreed**: egui's arrow toggled the visual state and never the stored flag, while the title toggled the flag and never the visual. Collapsing by the arrow — what anyone actually clicks — looked right all session and sprang open on the next launch.

- **⚠⚠ THE EXISTING TESTS PASS AGAINST THE BUG, AND ONE OF THEM IS THE GUARD WRITTEN AFTER THE PREVIOUS INCIDENT.** A **title**-click test structurally cannot see an **arrow**-click defect. This is the **third** time this one function has lost persistence invisibly, and every time only *persistence* broke — the section collapses and stays collapsed all session, so no screenshot and no single sitting can show it.
- **⚠ The fix is one path, not two:** the title toggles egui's state, and the flag **mirrors** egui rather than competing with it. egui owns the state within a session — it is what the arrow writes and what the animation reads — so mirroring is what makes either affordance survive a restart. In-memory every frame is free; the **file** write stays gated on an actual change.

**The Library panel's sections are reorderable (2026-08-27).** Drag a section's title to move it; the order persists. Asked for as *"it would be nice to be able to rearrange the left outer panel, e.g. by dragging folders below collections."* 1232 → **1249 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE BLOCKER WAS NEVER THE DRAG — IT WAS THAT THE SECTIONS WERE NOT DATA.** Five `section(..)` calls, each a closure over a different set of `app` fields, with no list to permute. `PanelSection` plus a `match` is the whole trick: it lets all five bodies keep their **exact previous form** while their order becomes a value. The ~350-line move compiled with **no fallout at all**, which is the evidence the bodies really were independent.
- **⚠ The trap the backlog warned about had already been defused.** `picked` and `selected` were once locals of the Collections block that Keywords happened to see; they were hoisted above the panel when Smart collections landed between them. Anything shared between sections must be read there, or the order stops being permutable.
- **⚠⚠ I BUILT A SEPARATE DRAG GRIP, AND IT WAS BOTH UNNECESSARY AND WRONG — THE USER REMOVED IT.** The spec argued a single widget could not both toggle and drag, citing that `dnd_drag_source` senses over its whole rect and that a drag over the header would swallow the toggle click. Both facts are true and **neither implies the conclusion**: `Sense::click_and_drag()` on one widget makes a click and a drag **mutually exclusive by construction**, because egui reports `clicked()` only for a press-release that never became a drag. ⚠⚠ **That guarantee was already recorded in this file**, for the Library's rubber-band selection — *"the two are mutually exclusive by construction rather than by a threshold"* — and I reached for a new glyph instead of for the note. Reported as *"it looks very cluttered"*, and the simpler design is also the safer one.
- **⚠⚠ AND REMOVING IT EXPOSED A REGRESSION THE PREVIOUS COMMIT HAD SHIPPED.** `CollapsingState::show_header`'s response comes from a plain `horizontal` scope, whose sense is `Sense::hover()` — so **`clicked()` on it is always false**, and the `open` flag that persists the collapsed state silently stopped updating the moment `section` moved off `CollapsingHeader`. ⚠ **Collapsing still looked perfect**, because egui's own arrow toggles in-session; only *persistence* was lost, which no screenshot can show and which the user would have met as "my panel forgets its state". Two headless tests guard it now, and reinstating the old read fails one.
- **⚠ `Label::selectable(false)` is load-bearing on the title.** An egui label senses click-and-drag for **text selection** by default, which fights the reorder drag and shows an I-beam — the same reason `theme::empty_state` sets it.
- **⚠⚠ PLACEMENT IS TWO PURE FUNCTIONS, AND THAT WAS THE REASON FOR REJECTING EGUI'S DND PRIMITIVES.** With `dnd_drag_source`/`dnd_drop_zone` the logic lives inside the framework and there is nothing to test; `panel_order::{drop_index, moved}` have no `Ui` in sight. ⚠ `moved` carries a **permutation property over all 30 from/to pairs** — nothing lost, nothing duplicated — because an off-by-one only shows at particular indices. ⚠ `drop_index`'s exact-on-a-boundary test is the one a naive implementation fails and no manual test would ever notice, and it is the only test the `>=`→`>` sabotage breaks.
- **⚠ `Settings::panel_order()` REPAIRS rather than trusts.** `settings.json` is hand-editable, so the stored order can omit a section, list one twice, or be empty; duplicates are dropped and anything missing is appended in default order, so **a section can never become unreachable**. `FormatFilter`'s hidden-by-exception rule: the safe state is the one you get by default, not one you have to maintain. ⚠ The field and the method share a name deliberately — the field is the raw stored value, the method the usable one — and **both read sites go through the method**, verified by grep.
- **⚠ A `Vec`, not `[PanelSection; 5]`**, precisely because the file can hold any list at all. The round-trip test stores an unrepaired two-element order, which is what proves the repair has not leaked into serde.
- **⚠ Two egui facts the plan got wrong, both caught by the compiler:** `show_header` takes `self` **by value** and returns a `HeaderResponse` that **borrows `ui`**, so the planned `show_header`-then-`show_body_indented` pair cannot compile; and `HeaderResponse`'s fields are **private**, so the header's response comes back out of `body(..)` — meaning the toggle click must be read *after* the body is drawn, which reads backwards without the comment that now explains it.
- **Verified against a hand-damaged settings file**: `panel_order` set to `["Dates"]` by hand, relaunched, and all five sections came back with Dates first.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no reset to default order** — an unwanted drag is undone by dragging back; (2) **no undo for a reorder** — the log is scoped to photos and global actions, and a layout change is neither; (3) **the title is now a drag handle**, which is not signposted beyond the grab cursor on hover; (4) the order is **global**, not per folder or per catalog — the same scope `panel_sections` has; (5) only these five sections, since the panel has no other content to order.

**Edit-accurate thumbnails (2026-08-27).** The Library grid and the Develop filmstrip render each photo's edits over the camera's embedded JPEG, so a black-and-white virtual copy finally shows in black and white beside its colour original. 1214 → **1232 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE MEASUREMENT CHOSE THE DESIGN BEFORE ANY QUESTION WAS ASKED.** `cargo run --release -p app --example bench_thumb_render <raw> [dir]`, kept: **rendering over the embedded JPEG is 4.9 ms on top of the ~90 ms extraction already paid**, against **934 ms** for a half-size decode. The 9.8× ratio *understates* it — the render is ~5 ms either way, so the entire difference is the decode, and it would be paid again on **every edit**, uncacheable at 78 MB a buffer. For an 830-photo folder across six workers: ~13 s against ~2 min, plus that again on every sync.
- **⚠⚠ THE CACHE KEY IS `(PhotoId, edits fingerprint)`, WHICH IS WHAT MEANS NOTHING HAS TO INVALIDATE.** A changed edit is an ordinary cache miss, so a sync, a paste, a preset click, an undo, a redo and a snapshot restore are all correct **without any of them knowing this cache exists**. That is `PrefixKey`'s pattern, which deliberately fails toward a miss. ⚠ The alternative — `cache.forget(id)` at every edit site — was rejected because there are seven such sites and the one that forgets shows a stale picture with nothing to notice it.
- **⚠⚠ THIS INVERTS A DECISION `thumb_cache` DOCUMENTED DELIBERATELY.** It was keyed by `PathBuf` *so that copies of one file shared one texture* — right while a thumbnail was the camera's JPEG, and exactly the bug once thumbnails show edits. ⚠ Two of the three properties that fell out of path-keying survive and both are load-bearing in the Library: `rebuild_entries` still invalidates nothing, and **indices never leave the UI**. Both have their own test now.
- **⚠⚠ THE FINGERPRINT HASHES THE SERIALIZED EDITS, NOT FIELD BY FIELD, AND THE EXHAUSTIVENESS LIVES IN THE IMPLEMENTATION RATHER THAN IN A TEST.** `Edits` has ~40 fields and cannot derive `Hash` (it holds `f32`), so a hand-written hash would silently omit any field added later — and the symptom is a *stale thumbnail*, which nothing would notice. ⚠ No test can honestly enumerate forty fields; `edit_model::all_nonneutral` and `pipeline::all_edits` are both **private to their own crates' test modules** and unusable here. The tests instead verify the implementation *is* the serde-based one, by moving fields a hand-written hash would forget (the eighth HSL band's luminance, a nested crop edge). The discriminating sabotage is "hash only `exposure`".
- **⚠ A SABOTAGE EXPOSED A TEST THAT COULD NOT SEE THE HALF IT NAMED.** `two_copies_with_different_edits_get_different_keys` passed with the fingerprint stubbed to a constant, because those keys also differ by *copy number*. `one_photo_with_two_different_edits_gets_two_keys` holds the `PhotoId` fixed so only the edits can distinguish them. **Fourteenth instance.**
- **⚠ The edits come from TWO places, deliberately.** The **open** photo's live in `Session`, because `persist_current_session` writes only on a photo switch or on quit — so the sidecar is stale *exactly while you are editing*, which is when the filmstrip most needs to update. Every other photo's come from `PhotoEntry::edits`, read when `entries::build` loaded the sidecar (already measured at 4.6 ms for 830 files, so it costs nothing new).
- **⚠ `live_edits` must be hoisted above every `&mut app.*` binding.** It takes `&self`, so it borrows the whole of `app` and cannot follow a mutable borrow of one field — an `E0502` in `library.rs` until it moved.
- **⚠ Both window builders stopped de-duplicating by path.** Each skipped a row whose file was already queued, which was right when copies shared a texture and is exactly wrong now.

**⚠⚠ AND THE BUG THIS SHIPPED WITH, FOUND BY USING IT — READ THIS ONE.** Reported as *"without any edits, the preview of the jpeg and raw file of one and the same photo look very different, the saturation of the raw file is very exaggerated."*

- **The cause: `render_edits` did `srgb_to_linear` and stopped.** The pipeline's working space is linear **ProPhoto** and its output transform converts ProPhoto→sRGB on the way out, so that matrix was applied to data that had never been in ProPhoto. `image_decode::decode` does `srgb_to_linear` **then** `srgb_to_prophoto`, and its own comment warns about precisely this: *"every JPEG rendering heavily oversaturated."* Measured by sabotage: **mean saturation 0.5247 → 0.8255, a 57% rise.**
- **⚠ It bit only *edited* photos**, because a neutral one takes the early return that skips the pipeline entirely. That is why the effect looked arbitrary rather than systematic — and why the particular pair the user compared differed for a reason nothing on screen explained.
- **⚠⚠ MY OWN MEASUREMENT POINTED THE WRONG WAY, AND THE REASON IS A LESSON THIS FILE ALREADY RECORDS TWICE.** I compared whole-image *means* of the decoded canvases and concluded the RAW was *less* saturated than the JPEG — true of the canvas, and irrelevant to the thumbnails he was actually describing. **A global mean is the wrong statistic for a colour question**; the HueSatMap and colour-grading work both recorded that and I reached for it anyway. What reached the answer was **re-reading his exact words**: he said *preview*, and previews are what the new code path touches.
- **⚠⚠ THE EXISTING TEST COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT IT.** `a_saturation_edit_really_reaches_the_pixels` uses −100 saturation, which collapses the channels to grey **whether or not the colour space is right**. A *strong* edit is blind to a wrong working space; only a near-neutral one is not. `a_negligible_edit_does_not_change_the_colours` uses `exposure: 0.01` — large enough that `is_active` puts a stage in the pipeline, small enough that any colour change is the bug. **Fifteenth instance of a test naming a property it cannot see.**
- **⚠ The end-to-end test exists because the obvious sabotage was inert.** Skipping the render entirely in `load_thumbnail` left the whole suite green — the feature silently absent. `a_saturation_edit_really_reaches_the_pixels` writes a real red PNG with `export::save`, loads it back through `load_thumbnail`, and asserts the channel spread collapses; it is the only test that proves edits reach actual pixels.

- **Measured on the real catalog**: RAW and JPEG thumbnails of the same shot agree to three decimal places (mean saturation 0.4006 vs 0.4013), which is what confirmed the thumbnail *paths* were sound and sent the search to the render. `cargo run --release -p app --example thumb_pair <raw> <jpg>` and `--example render_pair <raw> <jpg> [dcp] [dir]`, both kept.
- **⚠ The canvas still changes when a decode lands, and that is inherent.** With no edits at all, the camera's embedded JPEG and Rustroom's own render differ by **mean 30–45/255, max 91–255, with 86–93% of samples differing by more than 8** across three photos — Fuji's film simulation against a scene-linear→sRGB rendering. It was always there; this change made it *smaller* for edited photos, since the placeholder now carries the edits too. The same thing Lightroom does.
- **⚠ The placeholder's geometry got better**: it predicts the decoded size from the thumbnail's aspect, and a cropped photo's thumbnail is now cropped, where an uncropped one used to make a hard-cropped photo snap shape on decode.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **highlight recovery is invisible in the grid**, and shadow recovery likewise — the 8-bit JPEG has no headroom, and this is the most likely thing to be reported as a bug; (2) the thumbnail shows the camera's rendering plus your edits, not Rustroom's own render, so it never matches the export exactly; (3) **entries can outnumber files** — a file with three copies holds three textures against the same 512 MB budget, so a heavily-copied folder evicts sooner; (4) an edit costs a re-extract (~90 ms, off-thread) rather than being served from a kept source; (5) still no on-disk thumbnail cache; (6) JPEG/PNG/TIFF sources have no embedded preview and render from a downscale of themselves, which is *more* accurate than the RAW path.

**Dialog sizing (2026-08-27) — one convention for all fourteen modals.** Reported as *"all pop up windows in general are very small and cramped."* Three width classes, roomy padding, a resize handle whose result persists per dialog, a viewport-bounded height that scrolls, and aligned label columns. 1205 → **1214 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ SEVEN FACTS WERE READ OUT OF EGUI'S SOURCE BEFORE ANY DESIGN, AND THREE OF THEM CHANGED IT.** (1) **`egui::Modal` has no width knob at all** — its only sizing surface is `frame` and `area`, so width comes entirely from content, which is *why* every dialog was a different width. (2) Its default frame is `Frame::popup`, whose inner margin is `style.spacing.menu_margin` = **`Margin::same(6)`** — egui's default, not a choice this project made, and the bulk of the "cramped". (3) `Modal` anchors `CENTER_CENTER`. (4) **`Resize::State::desired_size` is `pub(crate)`**, so the size cannot be read back out of egui. (5) `Resize::show` returns only the inner value, not a `Response`, so there is no `drag_stopped()` to persist on. (6) `theme::tune` does not touch `menu_margin`, so overriding the frame per-modal affects no real menus. (7) `Context::style()` and `Context::screen_rect()` **no longer exist in egui 0.35** — they are `style_of(theme)` and `viewport_rect()`.
- **⚠⚠ FACTS 4 AND 5 TOGETHER CHOSE THE PERSISTENCE MECHANISM: read on open, write on close, and *measure* rather than read back.** `dialog` records `ui.available_size()` on the first line of the `Resize`'s content closure — the same quantity `default_size` sets, so feeding it back cannot drift.
- **⚠⚠ THAT ROUND TRIP WAS THE ONE MECHANISM THE DESIGN COULD NOT CONFIRM FROM SOURCE, AND IT TURNED OUT TO BE TESTABLE HEADLESSLY** — better than the eyeball check the plan had scheduled. On a **large** viewport the recorded size equals the class default *exactly*, three opens running; on a small one it clamps once to 80% and holds.
- **⚠⚠ THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT TEST COULD NOT SEE WHAT IT NAMED.** On the harness's 400×300 screen the size came back `320` against a `340` default, which *looks* like drift and is the viewport clamp. Worse, sabotaging the measurement by 4 pt converged to a **stable but wrong 336** — stable, so a "does it creep?" assertion alone passes it. **The equality-to-the-class-default assertion is what discriminates**, and it needs a viewport large enough that nothing is clamped. Thirteenth instance of a test naming a bug it cannot detect.
- **⚠ The close hook is ONE place, not fourteen.** Each dialog sets `app.modal = Modal::None` from its own path — a button, the backdrop, Escape — so persisting at each would be fourteen edits and a fifteenth waiting to be forgotten. `modals::draw` already dispatches every modal, so it compares open-ness before and after and calls `save_settings` on the transition. ⚠ `Settings` is kept current in memory every frame, which is cheap; only the **file** write is gated — the `apply_view_demosaic` rule.
- **⚠ `dialog_frame` uses `style_of(ctx.theme())`, not `global_style()`.** A frame's fill, stroke and shadow are theme-dependent and this app tunes dark and light **separately**, so the global style would give light mode the wrong frame.
- **⚠ `DialogSize::ALL` is `#[cfg(test)]`.** Only the tests iterate it, and in a **binary** module a `pub` item with no production caller is a `dead_code` error under `clippy -D warnings` — caught at commit time, and the same reason `theme::base_fill` already lives in its test module. **Tenth instance of that trap.**
- **⚠⚠ THREE INNER SCROLL AREAS HAD TO BE REMOVED** — `info` (`max_height(460)`), `rule_edit` (`360`) and `shortcuts` (`420`). `dialog` now provides one, and **nested vertical scroll areas fight over the wheel**: the inner one under the pointer eats it. That is the exact bug the Library panel's three inner scroll areas were removed for on the same day.
- **⚠ `shortcuts` and `about` were folded into the funnel**, which is what makes the convention universal rather than merely widespread — they built their own `egui::Modal` and would have missed the padding and sizing entirely.
- **⚠⚠ EXPORT GOT ONLY *TWO* OF ITS THREE LABELLED CONTROLS IN A GRID, DELIBERATELY.** `Demosaic` is also a `label: control` row, but it is separated from Format and Colour space by the banding hint, the quality slider, the long-edge checkbox and its slider — folding it in would **reorder the dialog** and detach the banding hint from the two controls it is about, whose placement `export.rs` has a comment explaining. **Alignment is not worth silently rearranging a dialog someone reasoned about.** ⚠ The two sliders carry their labels inside the widget (`Slider::text`) and are not label rows at all.
- **⚠ Information was already half-solved and the other two were not** — worth stating so the change is not oversold. `info::row` used `add_sized([130.0, ..])`, which aligns until a label is longer than that. `Settings` and `Export` used bare `ui.horizontal` with no alignment; those were the ragged ones. ⚠ Each `info` block gets its **own** grid salt: `Grid` sizes columns per id, and "Path" is far longer than "ISO".
- **⚠ The compact panel metrics were deliberately NOT touched.** `tune` tightens `item_spacing.y` and `slider_rail_height` for the *panel*, where forty sliders make vertical space scarce. A dialog is the opposite case and now gets its room from its own frame instead.
- **Verified on the real settings file**: `export_settings` persisted at `[555, 343]` — a dragged value, not its 480×320 class default — beside `edit_rule` and `save_preset` at theirs.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the resize handle drifts from the pointer at half rate**, inherent to `Modal`'s centred anchor and unfixable without giving up the centring that was chosen over `egui::Window`; (2) **the buttons scroll with the content** rather than pinning, because `dialog` does not own the button row — pinning means restructuring all fourteen call sites, and they stay *reachable*, which is the bug being fixed; (3) the size is per dialog, so widening one Standard dialog does not widen its siblings, and the settings file can accumulate up to fourteen entries; (4) a dialog sized on a large monitor is clamped to 80% of a smaller one on open, so it self-corrects rather than stranding; (5) **no per-dialog position** — modals stay centred and cannot be dragged; (6) the class is a compile-time choice with no UI.

**Copy numbering gets the same high-water mark (2026-08-27).** The latent bug the snapshots work found in `next_copy`, fixed in the mechanism snapshots had copied their numbering from. 1203 → **1205 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE BUG: deleting the *newest* virtual copy freed its number.** Make two copies, delete copy 2, make another — it took `2` again; then undo the deletion (`ActionKind::CopyDelete` keeps the removed entry precisely so it can come back) and the file held **two copies numbered 2**. `max + 1` over the survivors structurally cannot see a number that only the undo log still holds.
- **⚠⚠ THE SYMPTOM WOULD HAVE BEEN A VANISHING COPY, NOT AN ERROR.** The two share an export filename and a `HistoryStash` key — and `Catalog::add_copy` upserts `ON CONFLICT(folder_id, file_name, copy)`, so the **Library shows one row for the pair** and the other is unreachable while still sitting in the sidecar. Silent, and permanent.
- **⚠ `Sidecar.copy_hwm`, with a FIELD-level `#[serde(default)]` only.** A *container*-level one on `Sidecar` is the documented data-loss trap — `copies` staying **required** is what stops a legacy bare-`Edits` sidecar being read as an empty envelope. Adding a defaulted field beside it is safe and does not weaken that. Verified on the real catalog: 20 sidecars, 21 copies, all edits intact.
- **⚠⚠ `compact()` RESETS the mark rather than raising it, and a PRE-EXISTING test enforces that.** Reindex exists to close gaps, so keeping the old high would make the next copy `_10` on a file that now runs 1, 2, 3 — the mess the button removes. `the_next_copy_number_follows_a_compaction` fails without the reset, which is why the interaction was checked before writing the fix rather than after.
- **⚠ `Sidecar::restore_copy` is a funnel, and the undo arm goes through it.** The arm used to `copies.push` directly, which bypasses the mark — the one thing standing between an undo and a duplicate. The raise also lives inside `set_edits`/`set_name` rather than at their call sites, so every route that brings a copy into existence keeps the mark true without remembering to.
- **⚠ A methodology note worth keeping: a bulk regex over test fixtures churned 21 unrelated lines**, then the *revert* regex rewrote five pre-existing inline literals into calls to a helper their module does not have. Caught by the compiler and by reading `git diff` for removed lines. **Check `git diff | grep '^-'` after any scripted edit** — the intended change here removes exactly five lines, and anything else is churn.
- **Accepted caveat:** existing sidecars carry no mark, so they read as `0` and degrade to the old `max + 1` behaviour — a file whose highest copy was already deleted can still reuse that one number, once. Nothing backfills it, because nothing knows what was deleted before today.

**Snapshots (M3.4's remaining slice, 2026-08-27) — M3.4 IS CLOSED, and with it Phase 3's last outstanding milestone half.** Named saved states within one photo, in a resizable strip pinned to the bottom of the Develop panel. 1165 → **1203 tests**; 47 goldens untouched, which is the proof no pipeline code moved.

- **⚠⚠ THE BLOCKER THE BACKLOG NAMED WAS ANSWERED BY MAKING THE TWO MECHANISMS ORTHOGONAL, AND THE ANSWERS ARE BORING — which is the point.** *Taking* a snapshot pushes **no** undo entry, because it changes no `Edits` and a `History<Edits>` holds nothing else. *Undoing past* one cannot touch it, because it was never in the stack. It survives the per-photo stash **trivially**, since `take_matching` compares only `Edits`. And *restoring* one **does** push, because that genuinely changes the edits — so Ctrl+Z undoes a restore for free. That independence is what makes a snapshot unable to vanish silently, which is why it replaced the dropped "persistent undo history".
- **⚠⚠ A COLLISION FOUND BY A TEST THAT FAILED, AND IT IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE PART.** `next_snapshot` was `max + 1` over the survivors — the `next_copy` rule, copied. But **deleting the *newest* snapshot frees its number**: take two, delete 2, take another and it is `2` again; then undo the deletion and the copy holds **two snapshots numbered 2**, where a rename or a delete hits whichever comes first. ⚠ **The live maximum structurally cannot see a number that only the undo log still holds.** Fixed with a per-copy `snapshot_hwm` raised inside `add_snapshot`, so every route in — taking one, and undoing a deletion — keeps it true without remembering to. ⚠ **`next_copy` has the identical latent bug for virtual copies** and is left alone here rather than fixed in passing; it is worth its own change.
- **⚠ The first failing run looked like a wrong test and was not.** The app-level assertion was stronger than the `Sidecar` unit test, which removes a **middle** snapshot and so never exercises the case. **A test that deletes from the middle cannot see a high-water-mark bug** — the same shape as the eleven "a test can name a bug it cannot detect" entries above, arriving from the other direction.
- **⚠⚠ THE STRIP IS A DELIBERATE DEPARTURE FROM THE EXCLUSIVE-PANEL RULE.** Every other control in that panel belongs to exactly one rail tool; this one draws under all four. A snapshot list is **navigation, not a tool** — the point is that the other states of the photo are always in reach, and behind a fifth rail tool it would be hidden precisely when wanted. ⚠ `the_strip_is_present_under_every_tool` loops `Tool::ALL` and is **the only test that can see that requirement**; drawn under `Tool::Edit` alone it looks perfectly correct in any screenshot of the Edit panel.
- **⚠⚠ ALLOCATED BEFORE THE PANEL'S SCROLL AREA, AND THAT ORDERING IS THE WHOLE FEATURE.** egui allocates in call order, so the strip takes its height from the panel's bottom edge and the scroll area gets what is left — which is what pins it while forty sliders scroll behind it. Moved after, it scrolls away with them. ⚠ **Recorded honestly: that sabotage is INERT** — no headless test can see pinning, and inventing one that appeared to would be worse than saying so.
- **⚠⚠ THE TWO SCROLL AREAS ARE SIBLINGS, NOT NESTED, and the code says so** — because it looks exactly like the nesting the Library panel's three inner scroll areas were **removed** for on the same day. The strip's list scrolls inside the bottom panel, the sliders inside the right panel's own area, and the rects are disjoint, so the wheel is never ambiguous.
- **⚠ `Settings.snapshot_strip_height` is a `u32` of points, not an `f32`** — `Settings` derives `Eq`, which no float satisfies, and an integer makes a NaN height *unrepresentable* rather than something to guard. `library_cell`'s reasoning, verbatim.
- **⚠⚠ IT TAKES NO `serde` ATTRIBUTE, AND THE CLAIM WAS VERIFIED RATHER THAN REASONED.** `Settings` carries a **container-level** `#[serde(default)]`, so a missing field comes from `Settings::default()`. Adding a **field-level** one instead makes it `u32`'s default — measured by sabotage as `left: 0, right: 110` — silently handing every existing user a collapsed strip.
- **⚠ Persisted on the resize's release frame only.** egui **deliberately recomputes the panel size on `drag_stopped`** so the released size is the one stored (`panel.rs`: *"We also recompute the size on the release frame"*), and `InnerResponse::response.rect` is the panel's **outer** rect — the same quantity `default_size` takes, so feeding it back cannot drift. Writing per frame would be the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake at 60 Hz.
- **⚠ `ui.set_min_height(ui.available_height())` is load-bearing**, the note the filmstrip already carries: a resizable egui panel stores its *content's* rect as its size, so content smaller than the panel shrinks it back every frame.
- **⚠⚠ `Sidecar::is_neutral` HAD TO LEARN ABOUT SNAPSHOTS, or "take a snapshot, then Reset adjustments" deletes the sidecar and every snapshot in it.** `persist_all` removes the file when neutral. That function already documented this hazard for names — *"a name or a second copy is worth a file even with neutral edits"* — so snapshots are the **third** case of a rule already written down.
- **⚠ `Session` caches the open copy's snapshots**, read once in `finalize_session` from **one** `load_all` shared with `name` — two reads are two chances to disagree. The panel body runs sixty times a second, so a `load_all` there is the `apply_view_demosaic` rule in a new costume.
- **⚠ `Snapshot::label()` is the one rule** — the strip's row, the rename field's initial text, the name seeded into a virtual copy. `entries::shown_name`'s rule, and the reason it exists: this project has already shipped a bug where the Library printed `file_name` while a copy's real name lived elsewhere.
- **⚠ Restoring clears `selected_mask`.** It is an index into `edits.masks` and a restored state has a different list. Every read site guards with `.get(i)`, so a stale index is harmless *today* — but a mask added later would land at that index and appear selected without anyone selecting it.
- **⚠ `restore_snapshot` pushes no `ActionKind` of its own**, and returns `bool` like a slider: `develop::body` already records exactly one `PhotoEdit` from `photo_edited`, so a second push would make one restore cost two presses of Ctrl+Z.
- **⚠ Taking a snapshot is not undoable; deleting one is.** That follows the precedent already in the code rather than inventing one — `create_virtual_copy` pushes nothing while `delete_virtual_copy` does. Creation is additive and reversed by deleting; deletion destroys the only record of a state.
- **⚠⚠ RENAMING SHIPPED BROKEN, AND THE CAUSE WAS ONE LINE I ADDED — reported in use, reproduced headlessly, fixed with three regression tests.** Neither Enter nor a click elsewhere accepted a typed name. **A singleline `TextEdit` calls `surrender_focus` *inside* `ui.add` when Enter is pressed**, so `focused()` is `None` immediately afterwards — and the autofocus guard on the next line (`if focused().is_none() { request_focus() }`) **took the focus straight back**. `Response::lost_focus()` reads **live** memory, so it then reported `false` and `theme::committed`'s `lost_focus() && key_pressed(Enter)` never fired. The same steal defeated the click-away path.
  - ⚠ **`name_edit::field` had the identical focus steal and was never broken by it**, because its condition is `lost_focus() || (has_focus() && entered)` — the `has_focus() && entered` half survives the re-steal. `theme::committed` is safe **only for a field that never re-requests focus**, which is true of the three collections fields and false here.
  - ⚠ The gesture is now **`name_edit::commit_gesture`**, extracted from `field` and used by both, so the two cannot drift. `name_edit`'s three tests passed **unchanged** through the extraction, which is the evidence it moved no behaviour.
  - ⚠⚠ **A deliberate reversal of the spec: clicking away now COMMITS rather than abandons.** The design said abandon; the report said otherwise, and the filename field people already use commits on click-away. One idiom beats two.
  - ⚠ **Instrumentation settled it in one run after reasoning had failed twice** — reading egui's source showed `surrender_focus` *is* called and `filtered_events` does *not* consume the key, so both hypotheses were wrong. One `eprintln` printing `has_focus/lost_focus/enter` showed `lost_focus=false` on the Enter frame and named the culprit immediately. **Twelfth instance of reaching for a print too late.**
- **⚠ `theme::committed` moved out of `collections_panel`** now it has a fourth caller. `lost_focus() && key_pressed(Enter)`, never a bare `lost_focus()`: clicking away abandons a name.
- **⚠ Typing the default label back in stores `None`**, so the snapshot keeps following its number — the rule an unnamed virtual copy already follows. Freezing the derived string would strand it.
- **⚠ No keyboard shortcut for "Take snapshot", deliberately.** A bare key is stolen from every text field and a text-editing chord breaks editing inside one; Lightroom binds none either.
- **Validated on the real catalog**: all **18 sidecars in `~/Desktop/fuji` parse with their edits intact** (`cargo run --release -p app --example sidecar_check <folder>`, kept), and four snapshots taken through the running GUI on a real `.RAF` came back numbered 1–4 with `snapshot_hwm: 4`. ⚠ The timestamps round-trip correctly under the wall-clock convention — stored 21:17:46 against a local clock reading 21:24:13 six minutes later.
- **⚠ Two commits went in with clippy failing and were amended**, both caught immediately: `field assignment outside of initializer`, and an unused `use super::*`. `cargo test` being green is not evidence about clippy. **Third and fourth recorded instances.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) the strip costs panel height on photos with no snapshots unless dragged away — one rule with no exceptions, where a height that collapsed when empty would be two facts that can disagree; (2) **no snapshot is visible outside Develop**, so a photo with ten saved states looks identical to one with none in the Library; (3) taking one is not undoable; (4) **a restore replaces everything**, crop and masks included — a snapshot is a whole state, not a preset with edit groups, and this is the caveat most likely to surprise; (5) no cap on how many a photo may hold; (6) **no "update with current settings"** (user's call) — taking a new one and deleting the old does the same thing visibly; (7) snapshots do not travel with a paste, preset or sync, and a new copy does not inherit them.

**Guided perspective (2026-08-31, reworked over four rounds of use 2026-09-01).** Draw up to four lines onto features that should be vertical or horizontal; Rustroom solves for the camera rotation and writes it into the Vertical, Horizontal and Straighten sliders. 1295 → **1322 tests**; 49 goldens untouched, which is the proof no pipeline stage moved.

- **⚠⚠ `mod_pc` IS NOT USED, AND THAT REVERSES THE BACKLOG NOTE I HAD WRITTEN THE SAME MORNING.** It said guided *"is the half `mod_pc` actually serves"*. Working the maths through, using lensfun would have been **strictly worse**: it returns a 3×3 matrix built from its own three angles and its own *estimated* focal length, so writing the answer into our sliders would mean **decomposing** that back into our pitch and yaw at the fixed `F_NORM` — not exactly representable, and the image would visibly shift the moment the solve finished.
- **⚠⚠ SOLVING IN OUR OWN MODEL IS EXACT AND CLOSED-FORM.** Our rotation's bottom row is `[−cθ·sφ, sθ, cθ·cφ]`, and a family of lines becomes parallel exactly when `(R · ray)_z = 0` for its vanishing-point ray. Two families give two equations in two unknowns: `tan φ = −f(b−d)/(ad−bc)`, `tan θ = sin φ·(a−c)/(b−d)`. ⚠ **The single-family cases fall out of the same equations rather than being special-cased** — with `φ = 0` it reduces to `tan θ = −f/b`, which is *literally the formula already in `perspective.rs`* and already had a test.
- **⚠ Roll comes free and goes to Straighten.** After `R` the vanishing-point ray has `z = 0` by construction, so it is a direction *in the image plane*; the angle between it and screen-vertical is the residual. Guided therefore levels the horizon too, as Lightroom's Upright does.
- **⚠⚠ GUIDES ARE VIEW STATE ON `Session`, NEVER `Edits` — and that is what makes "solve into the sliders" ONE representation rather than two.** A guide is scaffolding for arriving at a slider value, not part of the photograph's description: `selected_mask`'s rule. So they never reach the sidecar, never travel with a paste, preset or sync, and reset on a photo switch — and the resulting `Edits` are **indistinguishable from the same values dialled in by hand.**
- **⚠ `show_guides` lives on `Session` beside them, not on `RustroomApp`**, which is what lets `view_edits` read it directly and leaves **all seven of its call sites untouched.**
- **⚠⚠ FOUR ROUNDS OF USE RESHAPED THIS, AND THE FEEDBACK-LOOP ANSWER CHANGED TWICE.** The problem is real: guides sit on the image and the solve transforms that image. **v1** hid the correction while drawing (the crop-mode trick) — reported as *"I don't really understand what the guides are doing"* and *"very unresponsive"*, because nothing visibly happened until you toggled out. **v2** solved live — reported as *"jiggling too much"*: the whole photo shifts under the cursor on every pixel of movement, and with two guides a small nudge swings the correction hard. **v3, which stands**: the correction stays visible, the guides are **mapped through it** so a line stays on the feature it was traced onto, and **the image is frozen for the whole drag**, solving on release. ⚠ The freeze costs nothing to implement — no request is issued during a drag, so the transform the overlay maps with cannot move.
- **⚠ The toggle is `Guide Mode` under its own header**, not a control tacked onto the Perspective sliders.
- **⚠ Exclusive with the crop overlay.** The Geometry tool gives every drag to the crop rectangle, so while Guide Mode is on the crop overlay is neither drawn nor interactive — otherwise the two fight over one gesture.
- **⚠⚠ GUIDE MODE UNPINS THE FIT RUNG so it can zoom and pan**, which the crop overlay cannot: the crop rectangle is placed by fit-mode maths, while the guide overlay is anchored to `frame_rect` and survives a region render. ⚠ **Every guide coordinate goes against `frame_rect`, never `img_rect`** — the mask overlay's rule, for its reason — **including the aspect**, since a region's aspect is not the frame's. `img_rect` stays the *clip*.
- **⚠ A loupe** — a 5× crop of the texture around the endpoint being placed, in the corner **furthest from the cursor** so it never covers what is being traced, with crosshairs rather than a dot so the placed pixel stays visible. ⚠ It samples in `img_rect` coordinates, the one place in that overlay where `img_rect` is right: it is where the texture is actually painted, so the crop is exact whether the frame came out whole or as a region.
- **⚠⚠ THE OVERLAY IS DRAWN AGAINST `shown_geo` — WHAT THE VISIBLE TEXTURE WAS RENDERED WITH — NEVER `s.edits`.** Perspective is a **prefix** stage, so changing it drops the prefix cache: at fit that re-renders in ~15 ms and nobody notices, but at native zoom it re-runs geometry and perspective whole-buffer, hundreds of milliseconds during which the guides sat at their new positions over the *previous* image. `Session` carries `shown_geo` beside `pending_geo` (what was asked for); the frame-landing site advances one to the other. ⚠ `pending_geo` is a `Cell` so `request` stays `&self` — it is called from places already holding a shared borrow.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE LAST ONE: `fit_image_size` WAS REFRESHED ONLY BY WHOLE-IMAGE FRAMES.** A region frame carries `full_w`/`full_h`, the same post-geometry dimensions the `rendered_long` line beside it already used, so the restriction was never necessary — merely harmless while geometry mode pinned the fit rung and every render was whole-image. **Unpinning that rung for Guide Mode made it a bug.** ⚠ The concrete case: entering geometry mode **with a crop** resets the crop for the preview, so the frame grows while the stale value still described the cropped one — `frame_rect` was the wrong size and every frame-relative overlay sat slightly off, righting itself only when you zoomed out far enough for a whole-image frame to land.
- **⚠⚠ TWO CHEAPER HYPOTHESES FOR THAT WERE MEASURED AND RULED OUT RATHER THAN "FIXED"**, which is the part worth copying: the aspect the stage uses versus the canvas's (**0.000 px** — the autoscale is insensitive to it) and pixel-centre versus extent normalisation (**0.25 px** — invisible). Chasing the second is what put the search in the code path where the real one was. Either would have shipped as a confident fix.
- **⚠ The family is INFERRED from the line (`|dy| >= |dx|`), not chosen from a menu** — you draw along a building edge and it is obviously a vertical. ⚠ And the inference is **shown**: verticals paint blue, horizontals amber, so a misclassified 45° line is visible and fixed by nudging an endpoint. A rule the user cannot see is one they cannot correct.
- **⚠⚠ TWO SABOTAGES WENT UNCAUGHT AND EACH NAMED A REAL GAP — the step earning its place again.** (1) Swapping `(b−d)` for `(a−c)` in the yaw formula passed **everything**, because `a_horizontal_family_engages_the_yaw` only asserted both angles were *non-zero* — and **a wrong angle is still an angle**. The replacement derives the vanishing points from a known rotation and asserts the solver recovers it. (2) Nothing pinned that **two guides per family are required**, so a solver inventing a vanishing point from one line would have shipped. ⚠ A third sabotage was **inert and had to be redone**: `vanishing_point(a, a)` has a zero determinant and returns `None` anyway, so it changed no behaviour and said nothing.
- **⚠ `guide_endpoint_at` takes the NEAREST endpoint, not the first.** Two endpoints can overlap after a drag, and "whichever was drawn first" would make the other permanently unreachable.
- **⚠ A zero-length guide is dropped on release.** A tap that never became a drag leaves a line with no direction, which would classify arbitrarily and be invisible.
- **⚠ A stale hover text was caught in passing**: `Scale` still described its pre-bipolar behaviour, because an earlier `sed` had silently missed a line `cargo fmt` had reflowed. ⚠ **A `sed` that matches nothing exits 0** — check that a scripted edit actually landed.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the estimated focal length is unusable** — two vanishing points determine it (`f² = −(ac + bd)`, what lensfun's `mod_pc.rs:344-347` computes), but the stage's `F_NORM` is fixed; the verticals still come out exactly parallel, which holds for *any* `f`, and the residual stretch stays Aspect's job; (2) **guides are not saved to disk** — quitting or switching photo discards them, while the slider values they produced persist; they *do* survive a tool switch, and **editing Vertical, Horizontal or Straighten by hand clears them**, since the solve no longer describes what you see (Aspect and Scale do not, because a solve never writes them); (3) **exactly two guides per family**, so no least-squares fit over more lines; (4) **the solve overwrites** whatever those three sliders held, undoable in one step; (5) **no automatic line detection** — that is the remaining slice and needs a Hough transform; (6) the **loupe shows the rendered texture**, so at fit resolution it magnifies preview pixels rather than real detail — it is genuinely sharp only once the zoom has triggered a native render; (7) **the first guide of a family does nothing**, and the second does everything at once, since roll needs two like everything else.

**Perspective correction (M3.2's remaining half, 2026-08-31) — M3.2 IS CLOSED.** Four sliders — Vertical, Horizontal, Aspect, Scale — building one homography, applied as its own prefix stage. 1260 → **1285 tests**; goldens 47 → **49**, and the 47 stayed byte-identical, which is the proof the stage is inert at neutral.

- **⚠⚠ THE BACKLOG WAS HALF WRONG ABOUT `mod_pc`, AND CHECKING IT REFRAMED THE FEATURE.** It said *"the maths is `mod_pc` in the `lensfun` crate … the work is the UI."* But `enable_perspective_correction(x, y, d)` takes **4–8 pixel coordinates** on lines that should be vertical or horizontal (`modifier.rs:226`) and runs a Jacobi SVD — there is no slider input anywhere in it. **So the slider half cannot use lensfun at all**; it builds its own homography, a few dozen lines and no new dependency.
- **⚠ Two facts recorded for the guided slice, because they are easy to get wrong later:** `build_perspective_state` is a **public free function** taking normalised coordinates (`mod_pc.rs:546`), so guided needs no `Modifier` and therefore **no matched lens profile**; and **at 8 control points lensfun estimates the focal length itself** (`mod_pc.rs:341-348`). So guided would work on unprofiled lenses and on JPEGs — the RAW-only limitation the other three lens corrections carry need not apply to it.
