- **⚠⚠ `F_NORM` IS A FIXED CONSTANT (2.0, ≈24 mm), NOT READ FROM EXIF — and that is a decision, not a shortcut.** For **any** assumed `f` there is a `θ` that sends the vertical vanishing point to infinity, `tan θ = −f / y_v`, so the verticals come out **exactly parallel whichever value is picked.** Only the residual stretch differs, and **that is precisely what the Aspect slider corrects.** ⚠ Reading `Capture.focal_len` would instead make the tool behave one way on a RAW and another on a JPEG with no EXIF — two behaviours differing invisibly, the failure `render_max_dim` and `Session::geometry_mode` were each fixed for. ⚠ The consequence, stated so it is not later filed as a bug: **the same Vertical value is not the same physical pitch on a 16 mm and a 200 mm shot.** Lightroom's manual sliders behave the same way.
- **⚠⚠ THE AUTOSCALE ITERATES, AND ONE PASS IS NOT ENOUGH — measured, not assumed, and this was a real bug in the first implementation.** `Corrector::compute_autoscale` gets away with a single ratio pass because lens distortion is radial and nearly linear in scale. **A projective map is neither: scaling the output coordinates does not divide the source coordinates by the same factor.** On a 60 Vertical at 3:2 one pass returns **1.97 where the true answer is 3.31** — and at 1.97 the bottom corners still read source y = 1.26, so the empty wedge the slider exists to remove would simply have stayed. `one_autoscale_pass_is_not_enough` is the guard.
- **⚠⚠ THE NEUTRAL CASE RETURNS THE IMAGE UNTOUCHED, and that is NOT merely an optimisation.** The homography is the identity only to within f32 rounding, so resampling through it displaces samples by ~1e-7 and bilinear interpolation then blends neighbours — **softening the entire frame for nothing.** Caught by a bit-exact test, not by reading. `GeometryStage` guards its rotation and its crop the same way.
- **⚠⚠ IT SITS UPSTREAM OF `GeometryStage`, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON FOR THE PLACEMENT: `FrameMap` NEEDED NO CHANGE AT ALL.** Brush strokes are anchored to `GeometryStage`'s **input**, so perspective inherits the caveat lens correction already documents rather than requiring new code. ⚠ The rejected alternative was fusing it into `GeometryStage` as one homography with `straighten` — better quality, but `FrameMap` would then invert a homography rather than a rotation, and **that agreement test is exactly what caught the rotation sign error in M3.1c-A.** It also loses the cache checkpoint.
- **⚠ Being in the prefix buys three things that would each otherwise be work:** it always runs whole-buffer, so **no `Region` and no kernel margin** despite being a spatial resample; it is normalised, so **no `RenderScale`** and preview and export agree exactly; and it gets **its own `PrefixStepKey`**, so dragging the crop, Straighten or any tail slider never re-runs it.
- **⚠⚠ THE SOFTNESS MEASUREMENT THE SPEC DEFERRED, AND IT FOUND SOMETHING BIGGER THAN THE QUESTION.** `cargo run --release -p app --example persp_sharpness <raw>`, kept. Mean-absolute-Laplacian acutance on a real X-T4 frame: neutral **0.03186**, one bilinear resample **0.01661** (−48%), two **0.00591**. ⚠⚠ **The FIRST resample costs ~46% and the second only ~20% more** — so fusing would recover about a fifth, real but secondary. **The dominant cost is that these stages interpolate bilinearly at all**, which already applies to straighten and lens correction today and is the larger and more general win. Both are backlogged, with the second flagged as the better one.
- **⚠ Two tests asserted something FALSE about correct code and were fixed in the test, not the code.** Positive yaw expands the **right** (mirroring positive pitch expanding the top) — measured, 1.238 against 0.907; and a keystone empties the **bottom** corners while pulling the top ones inward, so the scale-0 test must ask about *any* corner rather than the top-right one it originally probed. **A keystone moves two corners out and two in**, which is exactly the kind of asymmetry a single chosen probe cannot see.
- **⚠ `Perspective` is its own `Edits` field, NOT a member of `Geometry`** — that is what buys the separate checkpoint; sharing `PrefixStepKey::Geometry` would make every crop drag re-run the resample. It rides the **Geometry group** (`GEOMETRY_GROUP`, index **6**), which defaults **off** for presets, paste and sync. ⚠ Three exhaustive constructs forced that decision at compile time: `merge_from`'s destructure, `pipeline::all_edits`, `edit_model::all_nonneutral` and `edit_clipboard`'s payload.
- **⚠⚠ `scale` IS BIPOLAR — −100 FITS, 0 IS RAW, +100 FILLS — AND IT TOOK TWO ROUNDS OF USE TO GET THERE.** It shipped at **100** and was reported as *"moving vertical seems to simply be a crop of the image"*, which it was: **the scale needed to fill the frame is enormous** — measured at 3:2, Vertical 40 needs **1.92×, keeping 27% of the image**, and 60 needs **3.31×, keeping 9%**. ⚠ The spec's reasoning was backwards: it argued *"a keystone empties far more than pincushion, so default to 100"*, when the opposite follows — **the more it empties, the more important it is to see.** Moved to **0**. ⚠⚠ **That was still wrong, and the second report is the one that found the real gap**: *"moving the vertical or horizontal slider far to the side moves parts of the photo outside of the crop range, so it's simply gone."* A keystone pushes two corners **out** of the canvas (clipped away) while pulling two **in** (black) — and the original scale could only ever go **up**, so **nothing in the feature could recover the lost content.** `fit_scale` is the missing mirror, and the default is **−100**, so nothing is ever silently discarded.
- **⚠ The two directions are computed differently, and the asymmetry is real.** `fill_scale` **iterates** (see below) because it works through the *inverse*, where scaling is not linear. `fit_scale` needs **one step**: it maps the four source corners *forward* and takes `1/extent`, and scaling the output *is* linear in the output.
- **⚠ Of the two fixes offered — "the crop range grows" or "the picture gets smaller" — the second was taken.** Growing the canvas would change the image dimensions as the slider moves, which shifts what the normalised crop rect refers to and breaks the fixed-canvas property that keeps `FrameMap` untouched. Scaling down gets the same visible result with none of that.
- **⚠ Three assertions in this feature got an INVERSE direction wrong**, the last being `the_scale_slider_fits_below_zero_and_fills_above_it`: `source_of` is the inverse map, so **fitting shrinks the picture and therefore makes the frame reach *further* into the source**, which reads backwards. The comment lives in the test rather than in a commit message for that reason.
- **⚠ The goldens deliberately use DIFFERENT scale values**: `perspective_vertical` at `scale: 0` (raw) so the reference is mostly image — at the default it comes back **66% black**, leaving only a third of the pixels able to witness a change — and `perspective_aspect` at the default, so the pair covers both scale paths. 32.5% and 66.9% black, no clipping, 155 and 137 distinct values.
- **⚠ `Default` is now DERIVED, with a doc comment saying to hand-write it the moment any field's neutral value stops being `0.0`** — `PanelSections`' rule. ⚠ `is_active` stays corrections-only: putting `scale` in it would insert a whole-image resample for a slider that only zooms.
- **⚠⚠ THE RESET BUTTON'S TEXT WENT STALE THE MOMENT PERSPECTIVE JOINED THE GROUP.** "Reset crop" hovered *"Resets the crop, the straighten angle and any rotation or flip"* — an enumeration that silently became wrong, the same failure as the "Reset adjustments" text that named five panel sections and broke when a sixth arrived. It is **"Reset geometry"** now, and **the rule is written at the call site**: anything added to `GEOMETRY_GROUP` must be named in that hover text.
- **⚠⚠ A PRE-EXISTING TEST CAUGHT A REAL UI AMBIGUITY: two sliders both labelled "Scale".** `the_lens_scale_appears_only_when_a_profile_matched_and_distortion_is_on` failed because the perspective Scale is always drawn. Fixed by renaming the **visitor** — the lens one is **"Lens scale"** *in this panel only*, since its home is the Edit panel's Lens section. Two adjacent sliders called "Scale" say nothing about which is which once the headings scroll out of view. A new test pins that they stay distinct.
- **⚠⚠ ASPECT STAYS ONE BIPOLAR SLIDER, RELABELLED — and the reason two would be wrong is a rule this codebase already follows.** Reported as *"aspect goes from vertical to horizontal when moved from − to +. Shouldn't there be simply two aspect sliders?"* The two directions are `diag(s, 1/s)`, **inverses of one quantity rather than independent axes**: two sliders would be `diag(a, b)`, which factors exactly into a uniform scale × an aspect ratio — so the second slider's extra freedom **is the Scale slider**, and the same look becomes reachable two ways. `Orientation`'s single `mirror` flag exists for precisely that reason. ⚠ The real complaint was **labelling**, so the slider now reads `Aspect ⬍ ⬌` and every one of the four carries hover text.
- **⚠⚠ `↔` AND `↕` ARE ABSENT FROM EVERY BUNDLED FONT and would have shipped as tofu boxes — the fourth time probing first has paid off**, after the rail's four glyphs, the mark badges and the keyword checkmarks. Present and used: **`⬌ ⬍`**, which are already the flip buttons' glyphs in this same panel, so they carry the right meaning. Also present: `‹ › « » < > | — –`. `every_perspective_glyph_is_renderable` is the standing guard.
- **⚠ That guard FAILED first, for the recorded reason**: `test_ui::ctx()` neither installs the fonts nor runs a frame, so it was asking about **egui's defaults, which production no longer uses**, against an atlas that had never been built. Both lines are load-bearing and the test says so.
- **⚠ `Edited::slider` now RETURNS its `Response`** so a call site can attach hover text. Additive — every existing caller ignores it — which is why it beat adding a `slider_hint` beside it.
- **⚠⚠ THE CROP OVERLAY IS DRAWN TWICE — a dark halo, then the light mark over it — because a white line is invisible against a bright photograph.** Reported as *"the white cropping border which is exactly at the boundary of the image is sometimes very hard to see, especially the white dots in the corners… some people might simply miss that."* ⚠ The **full-frame** case is the one that matters and the one that was worst: the border then lies exactly on the image boundary, so nothing distinguishes the control from the edge of the picture. Now a 3 px black-alpha stroke under a 1 px white one, and the same doubling on the handles.
- **⚠ Corner BRACKETS replaced the 4 px dots.** They are the conventional crop affordance, they read as "grab me" where a dot reads as decoration, and they advertise a far larger target — the hit test uses a 0.03 normalised tolerance, which a 4 px dot badly under-sold. ⚠⚠ **Every arm points INWARD**, and that is load-bearing rather than aesthetic: at a full-frame crop `painter_at(img_rect)` clips to the image boundary, so an outward arm would be **half-clipped** and read as a stray tick. `every_bracket_arm_stays_inside_the_rectangle` is the guard. ⚠ The arms also shorten on a small crop so opposite brackets can never meet and turn the corners into a solid outline.
- **⚠⚠ THE MARKS ARE PAINTED INTO A *WIDER* CLIP THAN THE IMAGE, and the first fix was incomplete without it.** Making the bracket arms point inward was not enough: **`line_segment` centres its stroke on the path**, so a 6 pt halo drawn along the crop boundary puts 3 pt of itself on each side — and at a full-frame crop that boundary *is* the image edge, so `painter_at(img_rect)` cut the outer half off **every** mark, the halo worst of all. Reported as *"parts of the lines are outside and cannot be seen"*. `canvas_geom::overlay_clip` expands by half the widest stroke. ⚠ The **dimming rectangles keep the tight clip** — they darken what falls outside the crop, and there is no photograph beyond the image to darken.
- **⚠ Expanded rather than inset, deliberately.** Insetting the *geometry* also keeps the marks whole, and was rejected because it moves them off the crop line they exist to mark. `the_overlay_clip_contains_the_image_rather_than_shrinking_it` pins the direction.
- **⚠ Red, not white and not the theme accent.** White vanishes against a bright photograph — the original complaint. The **accent was rejected because it is user-switchable, including a `Neutral` setting whose entire point is to have no colour**, which would defeat the purpose exactly when someone had chosen it. The crop overlay was already hand-painted with fixed colours and deliberately outside the accent system (visual-identity caveat 4), so this keeps one rule rather than adding a second.
- **⚠ The bracket geometry is a pure function in `canvas_geom`**
- **⚠ The rail entry is renamed `Crop` → `Geometry`.** Its hover text already read "Crop & Geometry", the edit group is `Geometry` and the module is `geometry.rs`, so the name now agrees everywhere. `Tool::Crop` the variant is unchanged, and `2` still selects it.
- **⚠ The goldens reuse the EXISTING `grid_chart`** the lens-correction goldens already use rather than adding a second grid, so a keystone golden and a distortion golden stay directly comparable. **Measured rather than eyeballed**: max 237, **zero clipped samples**, 141 and 138 distinct values, and the two differ from one another.
- **⚠ Seven sabotages, all caught** — pitch about the wrong axis, a one-sided pitch sign flip (negating a matrix *and* its inverse stays self-consistent, the trap hit twice in the colour-management work), `is_active` including scale, a zeroed default scale, `from_edits` ignoring the slider, a single-pass autoscale, and a no-op stage. ⚠ **One sabotage did not compile on the first attempt and would have read as a pass** — the recorded trap; the runner checks for a `test result:` line before believing anything.
- **⚠ A process note: `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features` without `-- -D warnings` exits 0 on warnings that fail CI.** Two real lints (`needless_range_loop`, `manual_is_multiple_of`) sat in an already-committed task before this was noticed. **CI runs `-- -D warnings`; so must every local check.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no guided control points** — the more powerful half, backlogged with the two `mod_pc` facts above; (2) no automatic vertical detection; (3) the assumed focal length is fixed, so a slider value is not a physical pitch — Aspect is the complement; (3b) **a corrected photo shows black corners until you raise Scale or crop**, by design; (4) **toggling perspective after painting shifts brush strokes**, as lens correction already does; (5) **a third bilinear resample** when lens, perspective and straighten are all active — measured above, and backlogged behind the more general interpolator question; (6) no X/Y offset, since the crop applied immediately after already chooses what survives; (7) no separate Rotate — Straighten is it; (8) **at Vertical 100 the autoscale clamps at 8× and the frame is still not fully filled**, which has its own test so it is not later found as a bug; (9) strong corrections lose real resolution at the stretched end, inherent to keystone correction.

**Undo for catalog deletions (2026-08-27) — and the backlog's own framing of it was wrong.** Ctrl+Z restores a deleted collection, smart collection, keyword subtree or forgotten folder, and a keyword deletion that takes a subtree now says so first. 1252 → **1260 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE BACKLOG SAID A RESTORE "MUST RECOVER ROW **IDS**, OR EVERY MEMBERSHIP POINTING AT THEM STILL DANGLES." THAT IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE TRUTH, AND PROBING SQLITE IS WHAT SETTLED IT.** Explicit-rowid restore *works* — and **collides**: without `AUTOINCREMENT` SQLite hands a freed rowid to the next insert, so deleting a collection, creating another, then undoing fails with **`UNIQUE constraint failed: collections.id`**. Sabotage-verified, that exact message. ⚠ What must survive is the **relationships**, not the ids — and because a cascade removes *exactly* the dependent rows, **a snapshot is closed under references**, so remapping is total. The alternative was an `AUTOINCREMENT` migration recreating four tables on live user data, for a worse outcome.
- **⚠⚠ THE MEASUREMENT THAT MADE THE DESIGN DECISION IS ONE SQL PROBE, NOT AN ARGUMENT.** `a_restore_survives_its_id_being_taken_by_something_else` asserts the fixture's own premise (`assert_eq!(other, id, "fixture check: the freed id really was reused")`) before testing anything — without that line the test could pass on a database that never reused the id and would say nothing at all.
- **⚠ Every delete now RETURNS what it removed, and `let _ =` still compiles.** That is the trap this change had to be careful about: the four call sites discarded a `()` before and would happily discard a `CatalogSnapshot`, leaving the feature silently absent. Each site is now an explicit push.
- **⚠⚠ `ActionKind::CatalogDelete` IS GLOBAL, NOT SCOPED TO A PHOTO — and that is the test that matters.** Forgetting a folder can **close the very library the deletion happened in**, and a collection belongs to no photograph, so scoped the undo would be unreachable exactly when it is wanted. Deleting from the Library grid with no photo open is the concrete case: `open` is `None`. `PresetDelete`'s rule for `PresetDelete`'s reason; sabotaged with `open.is_some()` and watched to fail.
- **⚠⚠ REDO LOOKS THE ROW UP BY *NAME*, NEVER BY THE ID IN THE SNAPSHOT.** The restore gave it a fresh id, so the old one either names nothing or — worse — names something else that has since taken it. Same reasoning `PresetDelete` already carries for being keyed by name rather than "whatever is newest in the trash".
- **⚠ A row whose *other* end has since gone is SKIPPED, not fatal.** Forget a folder, delete the collection its photos were in, then undo: the memberships cannot be satisfied and the **photos still come back**. `add_to_collection`'s rule — the catalog must never be a reason an action fails. ⚠ Guarded by `WHERE EXISTS (...)` rather than by `INSERT OR IGNORE`, because **`OR IGNORE` does not swallow a foreign-key violation**; sabotage-verified by removing the guard and watching the test fail.
- **⚠ The whole restore is ONE transaction** (`unchecked_transaction`, which takes `&self` where `transaction()` needs `&mut Connection`). A half-restored folder — photos back, marks missing — would be worse than one that failed outright.
- **⚠ The keyword tree is snapshotted PARENTS-BEFORE-CHILDREN**, by the `ORDER BY depth` on the same recursive CTE `keyword_members` uses. That ordering is the whole of the remapping: a child's parent has always already been re-inserted, so its new `parent_id` is simply a map lookup. Its test asserts the **shape** — Europe root, France under Europe, Paris under France — not merely that three rows came back.
- **⚠⚠ A CONFIRMATION BEFORE A KEYWORD TAKES ITS SUBTREE, BECAUSE UNDO DOES NOT HELP IF YOU NEVER NOTICE.** One click removed a whole subtree and every assignment of any of them, silently. ⚠ Shown **only when the deletion removes more than the keyword itself** — a leaf nobody has used is unsurprising, and a dialog for it is the false alarm `banding_warning` already rejects: *one that fires on ordinary practice trains people to dismiss the one that matters.* ⚠ `a_bare_keyword_is_deleted_without_a_dialog_and_a_parent_is_not` asserts **both halves**, since a dialog that never fires and one that always fires are equally wrong and only the pair distinguishes them; a third test covers an assignment with **no** descendants, which the descendant tests structurally cannot see.
- **⚠ Deleting a COLLECTION stays unconfirmed**, deliberately: its scope is not surprising — the panel shows its member count beside its name — and it destroys only membership, which undo now restores.
- **⚠ `delete_keyword_now` is the ONE implementation both routes share**, so the confirmed and unconfirmed paths cannot drift — including the "if it was on screen, close the library" tidy-up, which would otherwise exist twice. `apply_cell_action` and `name_edit`'s rule.
- **⚠ The dialog-class completeness test caught the new dialog at COMPILE time** (`[(&str, DialogSize); 14]` → 15), which is exactly what that fixed-size array is for.
- **⚠ No cap on the payload.** A forgotten 830-photo folder is a few hundred KB in the undo log. `PresetStore`'s trash caps at 20 because those are *files on disk* that accumulate; this is memory, and ten forgotten folders is a couple of megabytes.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the restored rows have new ids**, so anything outside the catalog holding one would dangle — nothing does today, and the sidecars key on paths; (2) a membership whose collection was deleted after the folder was forgotten is **not** recovered, only skipped; (3) the undo log is session-only, so quitting loses it — the same rule photo edits already follow; (4) **redo matches by name**, so renaming a restored row between undo and redo makes the redo report that it is no longer in the catalog rather than deleting something else; (5) **no confirmation on a collection or a smart collection**, by design.

**The Library panel's sections collapse (2026-08-27).** Folders · Collections · Smart collections · Keywords · Dates, each a `CollapsingHeader`, in that order. 1161 → **1165 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE OPEN STATE IS PERSISTED IN `Settings`, NOT LEFT TO EGUI, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT.** This app implements no `eframe::App::save`, so egui's collapsing memory **dies with the process** — a section collapsed today would spring open on every launch and the control would be worth nothing. `PanelSections` is five bools beside `library_sort` and `library_cell`.
- **⚠⚠ ALL FIVE DEFAULT TO CLOSED, AND THE FIRST VERSION HAD IT THE OTHER WAY.** Defaulting them open was argued as "an existing user loses no discoverability" — reversed by the user the same day, and rightly: the point of the control is that the panel does not show every option at once, so a default that shows them all makes it something you must set up before it helps. A collapsed header still names its section, so nothing is hidden, only folded. ⚠ `Default` is **derived**, which is correct only while every field is `false`; the doc comment says to hand-write it the moment one flips, the way `RadialGradient::default` and `ColorGrading::default` already are, and the test asserts the **value** rather than the mechanism so it catches either way round.
- **⚠ `Settings` persists; egui owns the state within a session.** `default_open` seeds egui from the flag on the first frame after a restart and the header's click updates the flag, so the two agree at every moment both are defined. The alternative — `.open(Some(flag))` every frame — **overrides the user's click** and is not a `set_open`.
- **⚠ The flags are copied into a local and written back ONCE, after the panel.** The closure already borrows half of `app`, and writing the settings file per frame is the `apply_view_demosaic` rule, now cited **eleven** times in this file.
- **⚠⚠ REORDERING BROKE A HIDDEN DEPENDENCY, AND THE COMPILER CAUGHT IT.** `picked` and `selected` were locals of the *Collections* block that the *Keywords* block happened to see, because they were sequential statements in one closure. Once each section became its own closure — and Smart moved between them — Keywords could not reach them. Both are read once before the panel now. ⚠ **A section that compiles only because of where it sits in the file is a section that cannot be reordered**, which is exactly what this change wanted to do.
- **⚠ `id_salt` on every header**, the rule the date tree already follows: egui's auto-ids shift with what was drawn before them, and this panel's contents change with the catalog.
- **⚠ `#[serde(default)]` at the container level, with its own test.** Without it a hand-edited or older file naming one section would silently collapse the other four — and `SettingsStore::load` is infallible, so a parse failure reverts *every* setting. Sabotage-verified.
- **⚠⚠ REVERSING THE DEFAULT DID NOT TAKE EFFECT, AND THE CAUSE IS A TRAP ANY PERSISTED SETTING HAS.** Reported as *"only Folders was closed, the rest were open"* — and the code was correct. The **open-by-default build had already written all five flags to `settings.json`**, because the write-back stores the whole struct whenever any one section is clicked; a stored value beats a changed default, forever. Fixed by deleting the key from the user's `settings.json` (an artifact of a default reversed within the same unreleased session, never a choice anyone made about four of the five). ⚠ **The general lesson: changing a `Settings` field's default is invisible to anyone whose file already holds it** — which is most people, since these files are written on the first interaction. A default that must actually change for existing users needs a version marker, not a new default.
- **⚠⚠ THE PANEL SCROLLS AS A WHOLE, AND GETTING THAT MEANT REMOVING THE THREE INNER SCROLL AREAS.** Collections, Keywords and Dates each had their own `ScrollArea::vertical()`, and **nested vertical scroll areas fight over the wheel** — the inner one under the pointer consumes it, so the panel could not be scrolled past whichever list you happened to be over. With every section collapsible, an inner scrollbar buys nothing that folding the section does not. ⚠ `auto_shrink([false, false])` pins the scrollbar to the panel's edge rather than letting it track the widest row — the same fix the Library grid needed.
- **⚠⚠ `theme::empty_state` EXISTS FOR A CURSOR BUG, AND THE CAUSE IS NOT OBVIOUS.** egui's labels are **selectable by default**, so a `Label` senses click-and-drag for text selection and shows the **I-beam over its whole rect** — and inside `centered_and_justified` that rect is the *entire panel*. So the empty Library ("Open a folder to browse…") turned the cursor into a text caret across the whole window, which reads as the app being in an editing state it is not. ⚠ Fixed at the three justified call sites with `Label::selectable(false)`, **not** by turning `interaction.selectable_labels` off globally: selection is wanted on ordinary labels, and it is *justification* that makes it a problem. ⚠ Its test asserts `Response::sense` **and** asserts the contrast — that an ordinary label still senses a drag — or it would be a test about egui's defaults rather than about our call.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) no drag-to-reorder of the sections themselves; (2) the state is global, not per folder or per catalog; (3) a very long keyword tree now lengthens the panel rather than scrolling within its own section — which is what the outer scroll area is for.

**Keyword and menu polish (2026-08-27) — six items, every one reported from using the finished M4.3c.** 1155 → **1161 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE TRI-STATE KEYWORD TOGGLE IS WHAT MAKES "REMOVE A KEYWORD" A FEATURE RATHER THAN A SECOND BUTTON.** The photo context menu's **Keywords ▸** submenu shows `■` when every selected file carries a keyword, `–` when only some do, `☐` when none does; clicking makes the selection uniform. One control assigns *and* removes, and it also answers *"what keywords does this photo have"* — the other recorded gap — with no new panel.
- **⚠⚠ `keyword_counts` COUNTS DIRECT ASSIGNMENTS, NEVER THE SUBTREE, AND THAT IS THE TRAP.** `keyword_members` deliberately expands descendants, because a *search* for `Europe` must find a photo tagged `Paris`. A menu toggle removes an **assignment**, so a photo tagged only `Paris` must show `Europe` **unchecked** — a ticked box that unticking cannot clear reads as the control being broken. Its own test, and the sabotage (recursing to ancestors) fails it.
- **⚠⚠ `✔` AND `✓` ARE IN NO BUNDLED FONT, CONFIRMED BY PROBING — the third feature to produce that finding**, after the rail's four glyphs and the mark badges. Also absent: `▪ ▫ ☑ ◼ ◻ ✖ ✘ ✗ ● ◉ ⬛`. Present and usable: `■ ☐ ◎ ○ – − · • ± -`. `every_keyword_mark_is_renderable` is the standing guard, sabotage-verified with `✔`. **Probe before choosing, never after.**
- **⚠⚠ `menu_targets` RETURNS THE CLICKED PHOTO ALONE WHEN IT IS NOT SELECTED, and the reason is a frame-ordering subtlety.** `menu_select` is applied *after* the frame, so while the menu is being drawn `lib.selection` still holds the set the right-click is about to **replace**. Reading it unconditionally would build the menu for photos the click has already discarded. Its own test, and the sabotage (`if true`) fails exactly that one.
- **⚠ Keywords are per FILE and collections per COPY, so the two target lists differ deliberately** — `distinct_files` de-duplicates for keywords while the collection path does not. Two copies of one photo are one file to tag and two rows to collect. That asymmetry is M4.2b's, not an oversight, and it has its own test.
- **⚠ The whole menu body is ONE shared function**, `library::cell_menu`, called by the grid and the filmstrip — which had duplicated three buttons between them. The same rule `thumb::cell`, `name_edit` and `apply_cell_action` already follow.
- **⚠⚠ THE CATALOG QUERIES LIVE INSIDE THE MENU CLOSURE**, which egui runs only while the menu is open. Reading the collections, the keywords and the counts beside the grid would run three queries at 60 Hz — the `apply_view_demosaic` rule, now cited **ten** times in this file.
- **⚠ Two `E0502`s, both from the same cause, and the fix is worth knowing.** Adding `menu_targets(lib, i)` inside the cell closure makes it capture `*lib` **whole** rather than disjoint fields, which then conflicts with the `&lib.entries[i].id` argument of the enclosing `push_id`. Cloning the id into a local fixes it in both files. ⚠ **A function taking `&Library` defeats edition-2021 disjoint capture**; a function taking `&lib.entries` would not.
- **⚠ Rename and Information are DISABLED above one photo, not hidden**, with `on_disabled_hover_text("One photo at a time")`. A menu that changes shape is harder to learn than one that greys out.
- **⚠ `committed(ui, resp)` is written ONCE and serves all three name fields** — collection, keyword, smart. ⚠ It is `lost_focus() && key_pressed(Enter)`, never a bare `lost_focus()`: clicking away is abandoning, not committing, the rule the copy-rename field already follows. ⚠ And **the same `named` guard drives Enter and `+`**, so the two cannot disagree about what is a valid name — two guards for one rule is how they drift.
- **⚠ The panel's `−` completes an existing mechanism rather than adding a second one.** The `+` was already there and `unassign_keyword` already existed and was tested; both routes go through one pair of functions the context menu also calls.
- **⚠ The filmstrip hover shows `entries[i].label()`, never `file_name`** — the one rule the Library caption, the rename field and the default export name share. ⚠ The grid needs no hover: it draws the caption already.
- **⚠ Dialog sizing was BACKLOGGED, not fixed** (user's call). ⚠ The entry says why it is a **`theme`** job rather than a per-dialog one: nine dialogs each growing their own spacing is how they got cramped, so fixing them one at a time reproduces exactly that. ⚠ It also says the compact metrics are **not** the cause and must not be reverted — they exist for a panel of forty sliders, which is the opposite case.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no keyword is shown on a thumbnail** — the menu is the only place a photo's keywords appear; (2) the tri-state is per *selection*, so there is no "remove from only those that have it" separate from making it uniform; (3) collections in the menu are **add-only**, since a collection has no per-photo view to remove from; (4) still **no re-parenting or renaming** of a keyword; (5) dialog sizing is backlogged.

**The rule editor and multi-select dates (M4.3c, plan 2 of 2, 2026-08-27) — M4.3 IS CLOSED, and Phase 4's catalog half with it.** 1138 → **1155 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠ The criterion dropdown is GROUPED under headings — Marks / Library / Capture / File — because sixteen flat entries is more than a dropdown can be scanned for.** ⚠ The grouping lives on **`Criterion::group()`, beside the criteria themselves**, never in the dialog, so a new criterion cannot land in a heading the editor does not draw. Two completeness tests pin it over every variant: each reports a group from the declared list, and no declared group is empty. ⚠ Marks comes first because culling is the commonest reason to build a rule.

- **⚠⚠ THE EDITOR'S ROW MODEL WAS DECIDED BY INSPECTING THE REAL SAVED RULE, AND A NAÏVE DESIGN WOULD HAVE REFUSED ITS OWN OUTPUT.** `rule_from_current` maps a star **set** to `Any([Is, Is, …])`, so *every rule plan 1 saves is nested*. The one saved during testing is literally `{"All":[{"Any":[{"Is":{"Rating":{"op":"Exactly","value":3}}}]}]}`. A one-criterion-per-row editor could not have opened it. **So a row is a *kind* plus one-or-more values**, serialising to `Is(c)` for one and `Any([Is…])` for several — which matches the filter bar's multi-select and keeps the stored shape unchanged.
- **⚠⚠ A RULE THE FORM CANNOT SHOW IS REFUSED, NEVER FLATTENED — and never opened as an empty form.** The form is a strict subset of `Expr`; hand-written or future nesting has no flat equivalent, and an empty form that replaced a working rule on Save would be invisible data loss until the collection stopped matching. The dialog says so and offers no editing.
- **⚠ A single-value row emits a bare `Is`, not `Any([Is])`** — it keeps the stored JSON readable and a hand-written `{"Is":…}` editable. **An empty row is dropped**, or it would become `Any([])`, which matches *nothing* and would silently empty the collection.
- **⚠ A multi-value row must hold ONE kind.** `Any([Rating, Text])` is not a row; splitting it silently into two rows would turn an OR into an AND. `Criterion::kind()` exists for that check **and** for the editor's dropdown, so it lands with two callers.
- **⚠⚠ SIXTEEN CRITERION KINDS NEED ONLY SIX WIDGETS, which is what made the dialog finite.** One operator-plus-number arm covers **Rating, ISO, Aperture, Shutter, Focal length and File size**; the rest are a string dropdown (camera/lens/format), an id dropdown (collection/keyword), a folder dropdown, free text, an enum dropdown (flag/colour), and year/month/day. ⚠ Every dropdown is built from the catalog, so a value that cannot exist cannot be chosen.
- **⚠ Changing a row's kind resets its values.** A Rating cannot carry a Camera's string, and keeping a stale one would be a criterion nobody chose.
- **⚠ The form and the six dropdown lists are read ONCE when the dialog opens.** Rebuilding per frame would discard every keystroke *and* run six catalog queries at 60 Hz — the `apply_view_demosaic` rule, now cited **nine** times in this file.
- **⚠⚠ SAVING REOPENS THE COLLECTION IF IT IS THE ONE ON SCREEN.** Without that the grid keeps showing the *old* rule's results with no sign anything changed, which reads as Save not having worked.
- **⚠ Multi-select dates reuse `apply_click`'s three rules** — plain replaces, ⌘ toggles, Shift extends — so the app has **one** multi-select idiom rather than two. ⚠ Shift extends in **tree order**, which `date_tree::flatten` defines, so "everything between" means what the eye sees rather than what a tuple comparison would say. ⚠ `range` is order-independent, because Shift-clicking upwards must select what downwards does.
- **⚠ `LibrarySource::Period` became `Periods`, holding a set** — a single click is the one-element case, so there is no second code path. ⚠ `rule_from_current` maps it to `Any([Period, …])`, which is exactly a multi-value row, so the capture and the editor agree by construction.
- **⚠ De-selecting the last date closes the view** rather than showing an empty grid with no way back: `photos_in_periods(&[])` matches nothing **by design**, since that is the state the panel is in before anything is clicked.
- **⚠ `photos_in_periods` de-duplicates explicitly**, because the periods can overlap — a day and the month containing it are both legitimate selections, and a photo in both must appear once.
- **Eight sabotages, all caught**: four on the form's round trip (kind check, bare `Is`, the missing `Not`, an empty row), two on the date tree (range direction, flatten omitting years), one on de-duplication, and one earlier on the evaluator.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the editor is one level deep** though the model is a tree — nesting is reachable only by hand-editing the JSON, and is then read-only; (2) no drag-to-reorder rows; (3) a `Period` row's day is inert without a month, which the row says rather than silently ignoring; (4) no live re-evaluation — a smart collection re-runs on open; (5) rules are catalog-only.

**Smart collections (M4.3c, plan 1 of 2, 2026-08-27).** A saved boolean rule that auto-populates, created from whatever is on screen. 1124 → **1138 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified on the real catalog:** schema v5 with all seven existing tables row-identical to a backup.

- **⚠⚠ A CORRECTION THAT THIS FILE ITSELF ASSERTED TWICE AND GOT WRONG: `LibraryFilter` IS NOT THE RULE REPRESENTATION.** M4.3a-ii and M4.3b both recorded that it "already **is** the rule representation smart collections need". It is not, and the type split shows why: **`Collection`, `Keyword` and `Period` are `LibrarySource` variants, not `LibraryFilter` fields.** A saved filter therefore cannot express *"in my Portfolio collection, show me July"* — the very query the milestone exists for. The filter half of a rule was free; **the source half did not exist.**
- **⚠⚠ `text` CANNOT BE EVALUATED IN SQL, AND THAT CHOSE THE ARCHITECTURE.** It matches `entries::shown_name`, which reads a copy's own name from the **sidecar**. A pure-SQL engine would need copy names duplicated into the catalog — two facts that can disagree. So evaluation is **in memory**, which the measurement makes affordable: 4.6 ms per 830 sidecars, ~60 ms at 10,000. ⚠ The payoff is that the evaluator is **one pure function**, testable exhaustively with no database and no GUI — which is what a boolean engine needs most.
- **⚠⚠ A TREE IN STORAGE, ONE LEVEL IN THE EDITOR.** `Expr::{All, Any, Not, Is}` is a real tree; the editor (plan 2) ships Lightroom's flat `Match [all|any|none] of:`. The serialized shape then never has to change when nesting arrives — only the editor grows — and a hand-written nested rule in the JSON already works. ⚠ The editor producing a strict *subset* of what the model allows is not a two-facts-that-disagree problem: it is a subset, and the evaluator is the single authority.
- **⚠⚠ `All([])` IS TRUE AND `Any([])` IS FALSE, and this is the first test rather than a curiosity** — those are the identity elements *and* the state a half-built rule is in. Backwards, an empty rule hides or shows everything, which reads as the feature being broken rather than empty. The sabotage that swaps them fails two tests.
- **⚠⚠ EVERY OPTIONAL FACT FAILS WHEN ABSENT** (`is_some_and`), the rule the camera filter established: "show me the X-T4 shots" must not include a photo whose camera is unknown. Applied uniformly to camera, lens, period, ISO, aperture, shutter, focal length and file size.
- **⚠ `Rating` is an OPERATOR in a rule (`>= 3`) and a SET in the filter bar** — because an operator is what composes under `Any`. A real divergence between how the transient filter and the saved rule model one field, and **the same one Lightroom has** between its filter bar and its smart-collection editor. Stated as a decision so it is not later found as an inconsistency.
- **⚠ `Criterion::Folder` matches the PARENT, not a path prefix**: `/x` must not match `/xy/a.RAF`, which a `starts_with` would. Its own test, and the sabotage fails it.
- **⚠ `Keyword` matches descendants**, reusing `keyword_members`' recursive CTE — searching `Europe` finds photos tagged `Paris`, M4.2b's rule. The expansion happens where the membership map is built, so the evaluator stays a plain `contains`.
- **⚠ Two membership maps keyed differently, and that is M4.2b's asymmetry rather than an oversight** — collections are per `(file, copy)`, keywords per **file**. ⚠ Built with one query *per collection and per keyword*, never one per photo: a catalog has a handful of each and may have ten thousand photos.
- **⚠⚠ RE-EVALUATED ON EVERY OPEN, NEVER CACHED AS A MEMBER LIST.** A photo rated *into* the rule must appear without a restart — the same reason `open_collection` re-reads its members. It is what makes the collection "smart" rather than a snapshot.
- **⚠ A rule that will not parse yields an EMPTY collection and a message, never a panic.** The text is user-editable, in a file, by hand.
- **⚠ `rule_from_current` is what makes plan 1 usable with NO EDITOR** — browse, filter, save. ⚠ Its limit is structural: the UI can only be in *one* source at a time, so the captured rule has at most one source criterion. **Composing two ("Portfolio ∩ July") needs the editor, which is plan 2 and the actual point of the milestone.** ⚠ Saving *from* a smart collection captures only the filter: nesting a rule inside itself **by id** would dangle when the inner one is deleted.
- **⚠ The mark filters are sets, so each becomes an `Any([...])`** — which is precisely why the rule needed `Any`, and why the checkbox model maps onto it cleanly.
- **⚠ Smart rows show NO COUNT**, unlike Collections and Keywords: computing one means evaluating the rule over every photo, every frame. The row does not pretend to know something expensive.
- **⚠ Deleting a smart collection does not confirm.** It is a **query** with nothing behind it, so it follows `delete_collection`'s precedent rather than `forget_folder`'s, which takes ratings and keywords with it.
- **⚠ Both migration-simulation drop lists needed `smart_collections`** — there are two, which the v4 sabotage revealed. Sabotage-verified again here.
- **Seven sabotages, all caught.** Five on the evaluator (All/Any swapped, a missing fact passing, `Folder` as a prefix, `Text` on `file_name`, `Period` ignoring the day) and two on storage (no UNIQUE name, a forgotten drop list).
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **a smart collection builds all entries before filtering** — ~60 ms at 10k, plus windowed thumbnails; a SQL pre-narrowing pass for the criteria the catalog *can* evaluate is possible later and deliberately not attempted; (2) **no editor yet**, so a rule can only be captured from the current view — plan 2; (3) no live re-evaluation: rating a photo out of the rule leaves it on screen until you reopen; (4) rules are catalog-only, lost with the catalog; (5) `FileSize` is a criterion the evaluator honours but nothing yet supplies — the map is empty until a caller fills it.

**Library polish (2026-08-27) — persistent filters, a photo context menu, and rubber-band selection.** 1114 → **1124 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. Both changes came from using the finished M4.3b.

- **⚠⚠ A REVERSAL: THE FILTER NOW SURVIVES A SOURCE SWITCH, AND THE EARLIER REASONING WAS WRONG.** M4.3a-ii reset the search text and mark filters on every switch, argued as *"a stale search that empties a newly opened folder reads as breakage."* In use it is the opposite: **browsing replaces the source**, so filter-then-pick-a-date is the *only* way to say "five-star picks from the 9th", and the reset destroyed exactly the composition that browse-replaces-source otherwise denies. ⚠ **The `Filter: …` label is what makes persistence safe** — a filter hiding everything is visible and one click from fixed — and that label did not exist when the reset was chosen.
- **⚠ The filter moved to `RustroomApp`, it was not copied across in `open_scan`.** Copying works and was rejected: it leaves two places that can disagree about which filter is current, the shape this project has paid for three times (`render_max_dim`, `Session::geometry_mode`, the thumbnail loader's shadow copy). `Library::refilter` takes the filter **by reference**, which keeps `Library` ignorant of the app — that is what lets `library_of` build one in a test.
- **⚠ `open_scan` applies the filter explicitly rather than leaving it to `reload_marks`**, which early-returns without a catalog. The filter must hold across a switch whether or not one is open.
- **⚠ Session-only, deliberately.** The *format* half stays separately persisted in `Settings`, because a format preference is standing where a search string is about the question being asked now.
- **⚠⚠ A NEW `Capture` FIELD CANNOT BE BACKFILLED UNDER THE ROW-PRESENCE DESIGN — found while planning this, and it is the reason dimensions are read live.** `files_without_capture` queues files with **no capture row**, so adding `width`/`height` columns would leave all 832 already-scanned files NULL *forever*: the new fields would apply only to photos nobody owns yet. Fixing that needs a `capture_version` column and a re-read pass. **Reading one photo's dimensions when a dialog opens needs neither**, costs ~15 ms once, and can never be stale.
- **⚠⚠ A RAW AND ITS JPEG SIBLING LEGITIMATELY DIFFER IN SIZE, and the first version of that test asserted otherwise.** Measured: the RAW is **6246×4170**, the camera's JPEG **6240×4160** — libraw's active area includes border pixels the camera crops, and 6246×4170 is exactly what a full decode produces. ⚠ **This is where dimensions differ from camera and date**: those are facts about the *shot* and are normalized to agree exactly; dimensions are a property of the *file*. The test asserts the **orientation** agrees and the sizes are within 2%. Fourth "assertion false about correct code" this session.
- **⚠ Both readers apply orientation, by different routes.** libraw reports the *sensor's* dimensions, so `dimensions` swaps them for `sizes.flip` of 5 or 6 — CLAUDE.md records `sips` doing the same thing sending an investigation down a blind alley. `image_dimensions` likewise ignores EXIF, so the four transposing `Orientation` variants are **named rather than a numeric `5..=8` range**, which would silently mean something else if the crate reordered the enum.
- **⚠ The Information dialog reads its dimensions ONCE, into the modal's own state.** A dialog body runs sixty times a second, and a whole-file read there is the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake in its most expensive costume yet. ⚠ Every absent value shows as `—`, never a blank row: a missing field is *information*, and two of the author's exported JPEGs carry no EXIF at all.
- **⚠ Shutter is shown as a fraction below one second.** The stored value is `0.0199999995529652`; showing that is technically honest and practically useless.
- **⚠ A secondary click does NOT fire `clicked()`**, so the context menu selects the photo explicitly — otherwise Edit and Rename would act on whatever was selected before rather than on what was right-clicked.
- **⚠ One `apply_cell_action`, shared by the grid and the filmstrip**, and Rename reuses `name_edit`'s draft rather than adding a second path. Two editable-name mechanisms drifting is the bug that made copy names invisible in the grid.
- **⚠ A commit went in with clippy failing again** — `field assignment outside of initializer`, in two tests. `cargo test` was green, which is not evidence about clippy. Amended. **Check clippy's exit code before every commit**; this is the second recorded instance.
- **⚠⚠ TWO RENAME-FIELD BUGS, BOTH FROM USING IT, AND THEY HAD DIFFERENT CAUSES.** (1) **Returning from Develop to the Library activated the rename field.** The Develop panel draws its name field on *every* frame, so `get_or_insert_with` leaves a draft behind for whatever photo was open — harmless there, since autofocus is deliberately off in Develop, but the Library grid draws the field for whichever row owns the draft **and autofocuses it**. Fixed by clearing `rename_draft` on a view switch, beside the status line, which is per view for the same reason. ⚠ Discarded rather than committed: renaming is explicit, and navigating away is not a way to confirm a half-typed name. (2) **Clicking empty space did not close the field** — it commits on `lost_focus()`, and clicking empty grid space moves focus *nowhere*, because nothing there is focusable. `field` now surrenders focus on a press outside its own rect, which makes `lost_focus()` fire on the next frame and reach the one commit path. ⚠ The pointer is read into a local **before** `r` is touched — the re-entrant context lock this very function already paid for once.
- **⚠⚠ RUBBER-BAND SELECTION, AND THE HIT-TEST ORDERING IS THE WHOLE TRICK.** A drag-sensing background is registered **before** the cells, because egui breaks a tie by registration order (`hit_test.rs`: *"In case of a tie, take the last one = the one on top"*). Registered after, it would swallow every click meant for a thumbnail. The scrollbar is drawn by the `ScrollArea` afterwards and is therefore on top, so dragging it still scrolls. Cells sense `click_and_drag` too, so a band can also start on a photo.
- **⚠ `banded` takes the drag's two CORNERS, not a rectangle — and that was a correction.** With `Rect::from_two_pos` at the call site, the "backwards drag" test built *both* rectangles itself and so exercised **egui's** normalisation rather than ours: it passed with ours deleted. Moving the normalisation into `banded` put the behaviour where the test can reach it. Another test naming a bug it could not detect, caught by the sabotage step.
- **⚠ Intersects, not contains.** A band must catch a photo it merely clips, or selecting a run would mean drawing the rectangle precisely around every thumbnail.
- **⚠ The additive case unions and re-selects rather than toggling.** A toggle would *deselect* a photo the band passed over twice, so sweeping back and forth would flicker the selection.
- **⚠ Only what is drawn can be banded**, because the grid is virtualised — which is also all a band can cover, since it cannot leave the viewport. There is no auto-scroll while dragging.
- **⚠ A click on empty space clears the selection, and only a PLAIN click does.** A ⌘- or Shift-click there is part of *building* a selection, not a request to throw it away — `apply_click`'s rule. ⚠ A click and a band cannot both fire from one gesture: egui reports `clicked()` only for a press-release that did not become a drag, so the two are mutually exclusive by construction rather than by a threshold.
- **⚠⚠ CLIPPY CAUGHT A FEATURE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED DEAD.** `cell_rects` was flagged as never-mutated: the edit adding the rect collection had not landed, because the surrounding indentation differed from the pattern it was applied with. The band would have compiled, run, and **selected nothing** — and no test would have noticed, since the `banded` tests use a synthetic strip. Third commit-time clippy save this session; the earlier two were `field assignment outside of initializer` and an unused variable.
- **⚠ Deferred by the user's call: shift/⌘-click multi-select of dates**, folded into **M4.3c** because multi-select periods and smart collections both want *"a source described by a rule"*, and building it first risks building it twice.
- **⚠ Six fields are stored and still not filterable** — ISO (11 values), aperture (10), shutter (8), focal length (33), file size, and edited-state. ⚠ Focal length wants a **range slider** rather than checkboxes at 33 values; the rest are list-sized. Held back deliberately until the Information dialog has been used, since seeing the values per photo is what says whether filtering on them is worth it.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) the filter is session-only, so it resets on restart while the format half does not; (2) **Information reads the file**, so opening it on a RAW costs ~15 ms; (3) no Delete, Export or Copy in the context menu — three actions, not an exhaustive menu; (4) dimensions are not stored, so they cannot be sorted or filtered on; (5) **no auto-scroll while banding**, so a selection cannot run past the viewport in one gesture; (6) a rename left half-typed is discarded by switching view, not committed.

**Filtering on capture metadata (M4.3b, plan 2 of 2, 2026-08-27) — M4.3b IS COMPLETE.** Browse by capture date from the left panel; narrow by camera and lens from the Filter popup. 1096 → **1114 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE DESIGN WAS CHOSEN FROM THE REAL DATA PLAN 1 COLLECTED, AND IT INVERTED THE ROADMAP'S PRIORITY.** Measured on the author's catalog: **1 camera, 2 lenses, 2 months, 6 days**. So the **camera filter — the criterion the roadmap actually named — has zero discriminating power on this library**, and a *month* filter nearly none, since 738 of 830 photos fall in one month. **Date at day granularity is the one that does real work.** ⚠ A ten-year archive inverts that, which is why no single granularity could be chosen and the tree expands instead.
- **⚠⚠ DATES ARE BROWSED FROM THE PANEL, NOT FILTERED — and that is what makes them need NO DATE PICKER, which egui does not have.** The tree is a `GROUP BY` over what is in the catalog, so it shows the six days that exist rather than asking anyone to type a range. It is also the idiom the panel already uses four times (All Photos, Folders, Collections, Keywords: *a list with counts, click to browse*). ⚠ **The cost, stated: "in my Portfolio collection, show me July" is not expressible** — the limitation keywords already have, and exactly what **M4.3c** exists to fix.
- **⚠⚠ `strftime` OVER THE STORED VALUE IS THE PAYOFF OF PLAN 1'S WALL-CLOCK DECISION.** `capture_days` groups with `strftime('%Y'|'%m'|'%d', taken, 'unixepoch')` and gets back **exactly the date the camera wrote**, with no timezone arithmetic anywhere in the app. Had `taken` been a true instant, every query here would need a zone.
- **⚠ The catalog returns DAYS and the app aggregates upward** (`date_tree::group`), rather than the catalog building a tree. The useful granularity depends on the library, and a flat day list is the one shape that serves both ends of that range. ⚠ `group` relies on the rows being **ascending**, which `capture_days`' `ORDER BY` guarantees — the alternative is a `HashMap` and a re-sort for no gain.
- **⚠ `date_tree` matches on the YEAR, not just the month.** Grouping on the month alone folds July 2025 into July 2026; `the_same_month_number_in_two_years_does_not_merge` is the guard, and the sabotage fails it.
- **⚠⚠ A CAMERA/LENS SECTION IS SHOWN ONLY ABOVE ONE VALUE.** With a single camera it would be one checkbox that can only hide everything, which is not a filter — the same reasoning that hides the format chips below two formats. On this library that means **the Camera section does not appear at all**, which is the correct response to the data rather than a gap.
- **⚠ A photo with no camera recorded FAILS a non-empty camera filter** (`is_some_and`). "Show me the X-T4 shots" must not include a file whose camera is unknown — and with no filter set it is shown like anything else.
- **⚠ `cameras`/`lenses` are `BTreeSet<String>`, not bitmasks like the mark filters** — the values are open-ended strings discovered from the catalog, not a fixed small enum.
- **⚠ `Library.captures` is keyed by PATH where `Library.marks` is keyed by `PhotoId`, and both carry a comment saying so.** Capture is per *file* and every copy shares it; a rating is per copy. Read **once per rebuild** via `captures_in` — one query per folder, never one per photo, the rule `marks_in` already follows.
- **⚠⚠ `reload_captures` MUST ALSO RUN AFTER THE SCANNER DRAINS**, not only where `reload_marks` runs — otherwise a folder that has just been scanned stays unfilterable until something unrelated rebuilds. ⚠ Guarded on there being results, or it re-queries every folder sixty times a second: the `apply_view_demosaic` rule, now cited **eight** times in this file.
- **⚠ `id_salt` on every `CollapsingHeader` in the tree.** egui's auto-ids shift with what was drawn before them — the hazard the grid's `push_id` fixed — so two years with the same month number would otherwise collapse together.
- **⚠ `distinct_capture_column` interpolates its column name and is PRIVATE for exactly that reason**: SQLite cannot bind an identifier, so the safety rests entirely on both callers passing a literal.
- **⚠ One sabotage was inert and is recorded as such**: removing `WHERE taken IS NOT NULL` from `capture_days` changes nothing, because a NULL date makes `strftime` return NULL, the `i32` deserialization fails, and `filter_map(Result::ok)` drops the row. The clause is kept as defence in depth — relying on a swallowed error is not a thing to build on — but **no test can distinguish the two**, and inventing one that appeared to would be worse than saying so.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **a date cannot be combined with a collection or a keyword** — browse replaces, and composition is M4.3c; (2) **no arbitrary date range** — only a year, a month or a day from the tree; (3) the camera section is invisible on a one-camera library, by design; (4) **ISO, shutter, aperture and focal length are stored but not filterable** — they are `Capture` fields with no UI, and adding one is a `capture_section` call each once there is a reason; (5) a photo with no capture date is absent from the tree entirely, though it still appears in every other source.

**Capture metadata (M4.3b, plan 1 of 2, 2026-08-27).** Date taken, camera, lens, ISO, shutter, aperture and focal length, read from every photo into the catalog by a background pass. 1078 → **1096 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified on the real catalog:** schema v4 migrated with every row preserved, 832 files scanned, and **415 of 415 RAW+JPEG pairs agreeing on every field**.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED BEFORE DESIGNING, AND THE MEASUREMENT CHOSE THE ARCHITECTURE.** Reading a RAW's metadata is **~11–15 ms/file, of which libraw's parse is 0.2 ms** — 98% is pulling the ~29 MB file into memory, because `RawImage::open` takes a `&[u8]`. That is ~10–13 s for 830 files, which rules out doing it inline on folder open (today: instant, plus 4.6 ms of sidecar reads). `cargo run --release -p raw-decode --example bench_metadata <folder>`, kept.
- **⚠⚠ A PREFIX READ WAS TESTED AND REJECTED, AND *HOW* IT FAILED IS THE REASON.** At 64 KB, 256 KB and 1 MB libraw opens **nothing**; at 4 MB it opens **20 of 40**. A truncated read would work for part of a library and silently miss the rest — the worst failure shape, invisible until someone noticed a date filter quietly omitting photos.
- **⚠⚠ THREE FINDINGS FROM RUNNING BOTH READERS AGAINST THE SAME SHOT, none guessable from the documentation.** `cargo run --release -p app --example pair_check <raf> <jpg>`, kept, is the check the whole design rests on:
  1. **The readers disagreed about the camera's name** — libraw `"Fujifilm"`, EXIF `"FUJIFILM"`. A RAW+JPEG pair from one body would have appeared as **two cameras** in a filter. Same class as C4's profile matching, except here it is our own two readers disagreeing. **Fixed by normalizing at capture: `camera` is `"{MAKE} {model}"` with the make uppercased** — deterministic, no alias table, and what most bodies write in EXIF anyway.
  2. **`LensModel` comes back as the value plus 51 empty strings.** `display_value()` renders every one of them, so the naïve implementation stores `"XF23mmF2 R WR", "", "", …`. Read from `Field::value` and take the first non-empty. ⚠ `first_non_empty` is split out from `string_of` **so the padding is testable without building an EXIF container** — a test that cannot reach the finding is not a test of it.
  3. **RAW carries a timezone and JPEG carries none.** ⚠⚠ **Both store the local wall-clock as naive seconds**, via `.naive_local()` on the RAW side. Two reasons: the readers then agree *by construction* on a pair, where true UTC would make the RAW exact and the JPEG a guess about the machine's zone; and **wall-clock is what a photographer means by "date taken"** — the shots from the 17th means the 17th where they were standing. ⚠ Cost: photos from two timezones interleave by local clock rather than by true instant.
- **⚠⚠ THE PASS IS ITS OWN, NOT PIGGYBACKED ON THE THUMBNAIL LOADER — even though that already reads every file.** M4.3a-i made thumbnails **windowed**, so metadata attached to them would exist only for photos that had been scrolled past, and **you cannot filter by a date that was never read.** Accepted cost: a file is read twice on a first visit.
- **⚠⚠ `MetadataScanner::scan` is ADDITIVE where `ThumbLoader::want` REPLACES, and that difference is the point.** The thumbnail queue is "what is on screen now", so replacing it *is* the cancellation. This queue is "what still needs reading", and dropping it on a source switch would leave folders permanently half-scanned. It de-duplicates against everything ever queued, so re-opening a folder mid-pass costs nothing.
- **⚠⚠ AN ALL-`None` CAPTURE IS STILL STORED, and the real catalog shows why.** Two exported JPEGs in the author's folder carry no EXIF at all; skipping them would mean re-reading them on **every folder open forever**, 11–15 ms each, for nothing. `Some(Capture::default())` and `None` mean different things — *read and had nothing* versus *never read*. An unreadable file likewise yields an empty capture rather than being dropped.
- **⚠ Per FILE (`photo_capture` keyed on `(folder_id, file_name)`), like `photo_keywords` and unlike marks.** Capture metadata describes the *exposure*, which every copy of a file shares; a rating describes the image you made, which they do not. Third table to follow that split, and it carries the same comment because it looks like an oversight. ⚠ `files_without_capture` therefore needs **DISTINCT**: `photos` has a row per (file, copy), so without it a photo with three copies is read three times.
- **⚠ Schema v4 is a new gate (`if from < 4`), never an edit to 1–3** — third time that rule has met real user data. ⚠ **Both** migration-simulation tests needed `DROP TABLE photo_capture` added to their drop lists; the sabotage revealed there are two, not one, which reading alone had missed.
- **⚠ `Capture` lives in `core-image`, beside `Thumbnail` and for the same reason** — both decoder crates produce it. ⚠ `catalog` gains a dependency on `core-image` as a result; the alternatives were an eight-parameter `set_capture` (which trips `too_many_arguments`) or a duplicate row type.
- **⚠ libraw reports 0 for a value it does not have**, not an absence — `non_zero_u32`/`non_zero_f32` exist so "ISO 0" and "f/0" never reach a filter as real-looking options.
- **⚠ One new dependency, `kamadak-exif` 0.6** — pure Rust, **no C library, no `pkg-config` entry, no CI change**, the property that made pure-Rust `lensfun` acceptable. RAW needs nothing new: `rsraw` already exposes everything, `chrono` included.
- **⚠ Eleven sabotages, all caught**, including `Drop`-wakes-parked-workers — which a test counting *reads started* structurally cannot see, because a parked worker starts none. It asserts on `Arc::strong_count` of the shared queue, which **is** the live-worker count. Copied deliberately from `ThumbLoader`'s fifteenth-instance lesson rather than re-learned.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) ~10 s of *background* work the first time a folder is opened, then never again; (2) a file is read twice on a first visit; (3) **no re-read**, so a replaced file keeps its old metadata until the folder is forgotten; (4) wall-clock, not true instants; (5) camera and lens are free text with no alias table beyond the uppercased make; (6) **GPS is available from `rsraw` and deliberately not captured** — it needs a map to be worth anything; (7) PNG/TIFF get whatever EXIF they carry, usually none; (8) **nothing filters on any of this yet** — that is plan 2.

**The filter bar (M4.3a-ii, plan 2 of 2, 2026-08-27) — M4.3a IS COMPLETE.** Search by name, rating, flag and colour label, over whatever the Library is showing. 1052 → **1078 tests** (1067 as first shipped, plus eleven from two rounds of in-use fixes below); 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ ONE `LibraryFilter`, NOT FIVE MORE FIELDS ON `Library`.** It replaces `filter: FormatFilter` *and* `min_stars: u8` with a single value carrying format, text, stars, flag and label, and `refilter` became one call over the entries. ⚠ It also happens to be the **rule representation M4.3c needs** — a smart collection is a saved `LibraryFilter` — so that slice gets cheaper for no extra work here.
- **⚠ `FormatFilter` is CONTAINED, not absorbed.** Its *hidden-by-exception* rule is deliberate: it stores what is **hidden**, so a format appearing later is visible by default and needs no maintenance. Merging it would churn a tested thing for nothing. It gained one method, `is_active`, which landed with its caller per the bin-crate dead-code rule (tenth instance).
- **⚠⚠ TEXT MATCHES `entries::shown_name`, NEVER `file_name`.** That is the one rule the Library caption, the rename field and the default export name already share. Matching `file_name` would mean searching for what a copy is *called* and not finding it — precisely the bug that made copy names invisible in the grid, where the Library was the last consumer bypassing the shared rule. The sabotage fails two tests.
- **⚠⚠ STARS MATCH EXACTLY, VIA A MULTI-SELECT `RatingSet` — it shipped as a *minimum* and that was wrong, found in use within the hour.** A minimum cannot express *"only the one-star ones"*, which is a real culling question; and it made stars the odd one out, since flag and label already match exactly (`Some(Flag::Pick)` means picked, not "picked or better"). A set **subsumes** a minimum — 3-or-more is 3, 4 and 5 — at the cost of two extra clicks in a popup you are already in. Empty means "any", the convention `flag: None` and `label: None` already use, and `0` selects the unrated exactly as `Some(Flag::None)` selects the unflagged. ⚠ `RatingSet` is a `u8` bitmask whose `toggle` **ignores out-of-range values** rather than letting `1 << 9` wrap into another rating's bit.
- **⚠⚠ THE FILTER BUTTON IS ALWAYS PREFIXED `Filter: `, and it shipped without that.** Active, it read as `3★+` alone — so the button stopped naming itself and looked like a different control depending on what was set. Reported in use. `the_filter_button_reports_an_active_filter` asserts the prefix, and the sabotage fails with `the button stopped calling itself Filter: "3★"`.
- **⚠ The Filter button sits BESIDE the search field, not in the right-aligned cluster.** They are one idea — *narrow what I am looking at* — and splitting them across the row put half of it next to the sort order and the grid size, which are not. That also leaves the right cluster at its original five controls, which was the layout worry in the first place.
- **⚠⚠ ALL THREE MARK FILTERS ARE MULTI-SELECT SETS — `RatingSet`, `FlagSet`, `LabelSet` — and stars shipped as a minimum with flag and label as `Option`s before use corrected all three.** Empty means "any" in every one; the unset *value* (unrated / unflagged / unlabelled) is a real member, so "show me what I have not judged yet" is expressible and consistent. ⚠ Each is a `u8` bitmask whose `toggle` **ignores out-of-range values**, or `1 << 9` would wrap into another value's bit.
- **⚠⚠ ONE ORDERING ACROSS EVERY SECTION: the unset value first, then the values.** Flag read "Any, picked, rejected, unflagged" while colour read "Any, unlabelled, colours" — two orderings for one idea, reported in use. There is no "Any" row any more either: an **empty set is "any"**, which is the same convention in all three and one fewer thing to explain. ⚠ The flag ordering lives in **`FlagSet::ALL`**, beside the type, so the UI cannot re-invent it — the rule `EDIT_GROUP_NAMES` and `Tool::ALL` already follow.
- **⚠⚠ THE POPUP USES `PopupCloseBehavior::CloseOnClickOutside`, NOT egui's DEFAULT `CloseOnClick`.** Every row is a checkbox and the point is ticking several; a popup that shut on the first tick made setting two filters a four-click job of reopening it. Reached through `MenuButton::new(..).config(MenuConfig::default().close_behavior(..))` rather than `ui.menu_button`, which offers no config.
- **⚠⚠ THE SPEC'S LAYOUT WAS CORRECTED AFTER READING THE TOOLBAR, and the reason generalises.** The spec put the bar *"right of sort, left of the format chips"* — written before reading `shell.rs:143`, which is a single `Layout::right_to_left` cluster already carrying five controls **plus the status line**. A ~180 px text field there squeezes the status out, which is the *exact bug that cluster was created to fix*. So the **search field and the Filter popup both went on the LEFT** of the row, in the space the Develop toolbar occupies and which is empty in Library. ⚠ **The cost, stated: the star filter goes from one click to two**, and it is the most-used of the three.
- **⚠ The Filter button's label reports the active state** (`3★+ ⚑ unlabelled`, else `Filter`). A control that hides rows while looking idle is invisible state — the problem the crop aspect lock was fixed for — and `mark_filter_label` is a pure function so that property is testable without a window.
- **⚠ The glyphs were probed, not assumed** — `★ ⚑ ×`, the set M4.2a already shipped. ⚠ `every_filter_glyph_is_renderable` calls `theme::install_fonts` *and* runs a frame first: a bare `Context::default()` has not built its atlas and answers about egui's defaults, which production no longer uses.
- **⚠ No new keyboard work was needed, and that was checked rather than assumed.** The Library's bare culling keys (`0`–`9`, `p`, `x`, `u`), its arrow navigation and `pressed_command` are all already gated on `memory().focused()` — exactly the gate a text field needs. Escape to leave the field was added so the keyboard is recoverable without the mouse.
- **⚠⚠ THE FORMAT FILTER PERSISTS; THE REST OF THE FILTER DOES NOT — and that split is the point.** It shipped resetting on every source switch, which made *"I shoot RAW+JPEG and only want the RAWs"* something to re-set constantly. It lives in **`Settings`** now, beside `library_sort` and the cell size, so it survives a restart too. The search text and the mark sets still reset by **construction** — `open_scan` builds a fresh `Library` — because they are about the question you are asking right now, and a stale one that empties a newly opened folder reads as breakage. ⚠ `FormatFilter` gained `#[serde(transparent)]`, and `a_settings_file_from_before_the_format_filter_still_loads` guards the forgiving load, since `SettingsStore::load` is infallible and one unparseable field silently reverts *every* setting.
- **⚠ Both bodies guard on an actual change** before calling `refilter` — they run every frame, and refiltering hundreds of rows sixty times a second is the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake in another costume. That rule is now cited **six** times in this file.
- **⚠ A test that asserted something FALSE about correct code, the third this session.** `the_search_text_narrows_…` searched `"0001"` on twenty rows and expected one match; the right answer is **eleven** — `00001` plus `00010`–`00019`. Fixed in the test with a substring that occurs exactly once. **When a new test fails, ask whether the assertion is right before touching the code.**
- **⚠ Six sabotages, all caught, each by the tests that name it**: the four `matches` criteria individually, `file_name` for `shown_name`, and removing `refilter`'s `retain_visible` — that last one being the hazard where a filter hides a selected photo and a sync then writes to photos that are not on screen.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the mark filters are two clicks now**, and "3 or more stars" is three ticks rather than one pick — the price of expressing *only* one star at all; (2) **no keyword in the bar** — panel only, since browsing a keyword *is* that operation once All Photos exists, and composing keyword-with-collection is what M4.3c is for; (3) **no saved searches** — M4.3c; (4) text is a plain case-insensitive substring: no globs, no regex, no field prefixes; (5) every mark filter is a set, so a *range* like "2 to 4 stars" is three ticks rather than a range control; (6) the search text and the mark sets reset on every source switch — only the format filter persists.

**"All Photos" and folder management (M4.3a-ii, plan 1 of 2, 2026-08-27).** The whole catalogued library is browsable as one view, and a folder can be removed from the catalog. 1044 → **1052 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ A TEST NAMED THE CASCADE IT COULD NOT SEE — the sixteenth instance, and found by the sabotage step within minutes.** `forgetting_a_folder_cascades_…` asserted on `all_photos`, `collection_members` and `keyword_members` — **all three JOIN through `folders`**, so with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` the orphaned rows simply drop out of every query and the test passed vacuously. The discriminating assertions are the **raw counts**: `photo_count()` is `SELECT count(*) FROM photos` with no join, and the counts inside `collections()` and `keywords()` likewise count their own tables. ⚠ The real damage of a missing cascade is invisible-but-permanent: orphans accumulate, and a folder re-added at the same path gets a **new id**, so the old orphans are unreachable forever.
- **⚠⚠ `mtime` IS FINALLY READ, after being stored and unused since M4.1.** CLAUDE.md recorded it as *"recorded and read by NOTHING… what a future re-link needs, and it costs nothing during a scan the caller is already doing."* A whole-library view is what it was for: `open_all_photos` builds a path→time lookup from the catalog and **does not walk the filesystem**, where `scan_of` used to `stat` every member — 10,000 stats on a view that otherwise touches no files.
- **⚠ `collections::scan_of` now takes `modified_at` injected, exactly as it already took `edited_at`.** One function, one rule — *the caller supplies the times*. Collections and keywords pass a `fs_modified` helper and are unchanged; All Photos passes the catalog lookup. The three existing tests kept their assertions verbatim, which is the evidence the refactor moved no behaviour.
- **⚠⚠ A FOLDER ROW RE-SCANS ON CLICK; IT DOES NOT BROWSE FROM THE CATALOG — and this reverses "one idiom for the whole panel".** That was the first instinct and it is wrong here: collections and keywords *span* folders and genuinely cannot scan, while a folder row names one real directory and can. Three consequences: there is no such thing as a stale panel-browsed folder; **refreshing needs no new button** (click the row again); and `Reindex` keeps working because the source is a `Folder`.
- **⚠ Deliberately NOT added: a lightweight "Refresh" beside "Reindex".** `Reindex` bundles two things — it re-scans *and* renumbers copies, which changes unnamed copies' export filenames and drops their undo stacks. Two buttons whose difference is "one of them also silently renumbers" is worse than one with a clear hover. Splitting `Reindex` into refresh-and-renumber is a real cleanup and belongs in its own change.
- **⚠ The re-scan had NO test until a sabotage said so.** Forcing `open_catalog_folder` down its catalog-fallback path left the entire suite green. `a_catalog_folder_row_rescans_the_directory` writes a file, loads, writes a second, loads again, and asserts the count went 1 → 2 — plus that the source stays a `Folder`, or `Reindex` would start refusing on it.
- **⚠ An unreadable directory falls back to the catalog's last-seen rows** rather than showing an empty folder. An unplugged drive must not look like the folder emptied itself — the rule `sync_folder` already follows in never deleting a photo row.
- **⚠⚠ FORGETTING DELETES CATALOG ROWS AND NEVER TOUCHES A FILE**, and that has its own test *with real files on disk* — the in-memory tests structurally cannot see it. This is the M4.1 split doing its job: *delete the catalog and you lose organisation, never work.* Its ratings, flags, labels and keyword assignments **do** go, which the dialog says plainly.
- **⚠ The forget dialog has no "don't ask again".** A deleted preset goes to a 20-entry trash and Ctrl+Z restores it; this has neither. Suppressing a guard on the *irrecoverable* action is exactly the distinction `the_two_delete_guards_are_independent` exists to protect.
- **⚠ Forgetting the folder on screen closes the Library; forgetting one while All Photos is open reopens it.** Two cases and both need handling — leaving either would show rows the catalog no longer has.
- **⚠ `open_scan` is now the ONE place a `Library` is constructed.** Three loaders had grown three copies of the same fifteen lines, and a fourth was the point at which they became one — a new field on `Library` had to be added in three places, which is how two of them drift. `open_folder` also split into the file dialog and `open_folder_at`, which is what lets a folder row reuse it.
- **⚠ The bin-crate dead-code trap fired exactly as predicted, for the ninth time**: `AllPhotos`, `open_all_photos` and `open_catalog_folder` are all dead until the panel calls them, so Tasks 2–4 landed as one commit.
- **⚠ Two sabotages were inert and are recorded as such**: deleting `photos` explicitly before the folder (the cascade would do it anyway) and — before the new test — forcing the catalog fallback. A sabotage that does not change behaviour is not evidence about the test.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **forgetting is destructive with no undo**, deliberately, and confirmed every time; (2) All Photos' mtimes are as fresh as each folder's last scan — clicking the folder row refreshes them; (3) All Photos lists photos whose files have vanished, the same *absence is not deletion* rule as collections; (4) no keyword filter in the bar — panel only; (5) **no filter bar yet at all** — that is plan 2; (6) no re-linking of moved files, unchanged from M4.1.

**Windowed browsing (M4.3a-i, 2026-08-27).** The Library grid and the filmstrip draw and load only what is on screen, bounded by a byte budget. 1019 → **1043 tests**; 47 goldens untouched, which is the proof nothing in `pipeline` moved.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED, AND THE OLD COST WAS BEING PAID ON EVERY FOLDER OPEN — this is not speculative work for a library nobody has.** On the real 830-file folder (`cargo run --release -p app --example bench_library_open <folder>`, kept): **42.0 s and 552 MB** to fill the whole folder, against **1.5 s and 15 MB** for a first screenful. 28× and 37×. A 120-thumbnail window — a very large one — is 6.6 s and 79 MB. The steady state is capped by `THUMB_BUDGET_BYTES` at 256 MB however large the folder, so the ~7 GB a 10k source would have cost never happens.
- **⚠⚠ THE 10k "DONE WHEN" OF M4.3 LANDS HERE, NOT ON SMART COLLECTIONS, and that reframed the milestone.** "All Photos" is the first `LibrarySource` that can be large; `library.rs` drew *every* entry each frame, `Library.thumbs` held a texture per entry forever, and `ThumbLoader` ground a fixed list in ascending order with no notion of the viewport. Sliced out on its own so a grid bug could never be confused with a search bug — the M3.1a precedent.
- **⚠⚠ `ThumbCache` IS KEYED BY `PathBuf`, NOT BY AN `entries` INDEX, and three things fall out of one decision.** **(1)** Copies of one file share one texture — `entries` repeats a path once per copy, so a file with three copies held three pixel-identical 0.7 MB textures. **(2)** `rebuild_entries` invalidates nothing; it used to *have* to carry the textures across by hand, and getting that wrong greyed out the whole folder. **(3)** Indices never leave the UI, which removes the sparse-versus-dense bug family at the boundary instead of guarding against it downstream. `thumbnail_for` also collapsed from a position scan to a lookup.
