- **⚠⚠ THE BUDGET WAS 256 MB AND THAT WAS TOO SMALL — found by using it, within an hour, and it is now 512 MB.** A **415-file folder measures 276 MB**, 20 MB over, so scrolling to the end and back evicted the top ~30 thumbnails and they visibly reloaded. Reported as *"the first like 20 thumbnails are greyed out"* — which is diagnostic, because generic scroll latency would grey whatever you land on, not specifically the *first* ones after a round trip. ⚠ The boundary was landing on ordinary folders; at 512 MB it holds **749** thumbnails, so any folder up to ~700 photos is browsed end-to-end and back with no eviction at all. ⚠ 512 also matches this project's own sizing precedent — `MID_BUDGET_BYTES` is 512 MB, and a *single* full-resolution prefix checkpoint is ~313 MB, larger than this whole cache was.
- **⚠ The sizing is a COMPILE-TIME assertion, not a comment** — `const _: () = assert!(THUMB_BUDGET_BYTES / TYPICAL_THUMB_BYTES >= 700)`, so lowering the budget back is a build error rather than a silent return of the eviction. Verified by doing exactly that and watching `E0080` fire. Same rule `MID_BUDGET_BYTES` already follows, and module-level rather than associated for the reason below.
- **⚠ The Library's scrollbar is pinned to the window edge (`auto_shrink([false, false])`).** It had tracked the *rightmost thumbnail*, moving whenever the column count or cell size changed, because egui's default puts the leftover width **outside** the scroll area. ⚠ **Pre-existing, not a regression from virtualisation** — the old `ScrollArea::vertical().show(..)` had the same default.
- **⚠⚠ THE WINDOW IS PINNED, NOT MERELY PREFERRED.** Eviction may never touch a path on screen, however far the budget falls, so the cache legitimately exceeds its budget under a very large window. Without it a big window silently blanks the cells being drawn, and the failure looks like a slow loader rather than a bug. Sabotage-verified.
- **⚠ `ThumbCache::get` takes `&self`; recency is set by `retain_window`.** What makes a thumbnail recent is *being on screen*, which is exactly what `retain_window` is told — and a `&mut self` accessor inside the draw loop would fight the shared borrows of `entries` and `visible` the loop already holds.
- **⚠ Generic over its value (`ThumbCache<T>`) so it can live in the lib**, which `lib.rs` says must not depend on egui. The bin instantiates `ThumbCache<egui::TextureHandle>`; the tests use `String`.
- **⚠ Replacing the want-list IS the cancellation.** `loader.want(&paths)` discards whatever was wanted before, so a scrolled-past request is simply never popped — "abandon, don't abort", the trade `DecodeWorker` and `batch_export` already make. An **in-flight set** stops a path being fetched twice, which matters because consecutive want-lists overlap almost entirely as the window moves.
- **⚠ The UI owns "what is loaded", not the loader.** The UI owns the cache, so it is the only thing that knows; a loader keeping a shadow copy would be two facts that can disagree — the failure `render_max_dim` and `Session::geometry_mode` were each fixed by deriving rather than storing alongside.
- **⚠⚠ WORKERS NOW PARK, SO `Drop` IS LOAD-BEARING — AND THE OBVIOUS TEST COULD NOT SEE IT (the fifteenth instance).** The old loader stopped because `tx.send` failed once `rx` was dropped; a worker asleep on the condvar never reaches `send`. `a_dropped_loader_stops_its_workers` counts *loads started*, and a parked worker starts none either way — so deleting the entire `Drop` impl left every test green while leaking up to six threads per folder open. `dropping_the_loader_wakes_parked_workers` asserts on `Arc::strong_count` of the shared queue, which **is** the live-worker count rather than a proxy for it, and it fails under that sabotage.
- **⚠⚠ VIRTUALISATION SILENTLY BREAKS THE RENAME FIELD, AND THIS HAZARD DID NOT EXIST BEFORE.** `name_edit::field` adds a `TextEdit` with no `id_salt`, so its id comes from egui's auto-id counter. `show_rows` calls `skip_ahead_auto_ids(min_row)` — it skips `min_row` ids while each row holds `cols` widgets, so the counter does **not** line up. Scroll one row with a rename open and every id shifts: the `TextEdit` loses its state, and worse, the *stale* id keeps focus so `autofocus`'s `focused().is_none()` guard never fires and the box goes dead. Fixed with `ui.push_id(&PhotoId)` per cell in both views. ⚠ **No test covers it** — found by reading egui's source while verifying the API, not by running anything, and confirmed only by hand.
- **⚠ `scroll_to_me` → `thumb_window::centre_offset`, and that is an improvement rather than a workaround.** egui computes a `scroll_to_me` target offset **once** and never recomputes it (`scroll_area.rs`), which is the M2.2 drift bug: the centred photo slid left as thumbnails ahead of it loaded and reflowed the content. Arithmetic placement has no such failure mode — and it is the only option once an off-window cell is never drawn.
- **⚠ The row-break bug is now structurally impossible**, so its test was *replaced*, not deleted. `cells_in_row(row, cols, n)` computes positions from the row directly; `rows_consume_the_visible_positions_densely_and_in_order` pins that the rows cover every position exactly once, in order, with only the last partial. `egui::Grid` is gone from the Library.
- **⚠ One window at a time**, which holds only because Library and Develop are never on screen together. If that changes they must contribute to a *union* rather than overwriting each other.
- **⚠⚠ TWO SABOTAGES ARE INERT, AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER.** Calling `want` unconditionally instead of on change, and `MARGIN_ROWS = 0`, both leave the whole suite green — because one is a per-frame cost and the other is latency, neither is correctness. Inventing a test that appeared to cover them would be worse than saying so. The five substantive sabotages (the pin, byte accounting on replace, LRU direction, the in-flight set, list replacement, the `Drop` flag, the window) were each caught by exactly one test.
- **⚠⚠ A HARNESS BUG, CAUGHT IMMEDIATELY, AND IT IS THE THIRD VARIANT OF ONE ALREADY IN THIS FILE.** My first sabotage runner grepped for `^error:` to detect a failed compile — but cargo prints `error: test failed, to rerun pass ...` for an ordinary *test* failure, so all three sabotages were reported as "DID NOT COMPILE" when in fact they had compiled and caught the bug. **The robust check is whether a `test result:` line appears at all.** Same root as the two variants already recorded: trusting the harness's output shape instead of checking it produces what you grep for.
- **⚠ A test that asserted something FALSE about correct code.** `a_replaced_want_list_abandons_what_is_no_longer_wanted` waited with `collect(&loader, 1)` — which returns on the *first* result to arrive, and that is one of the already-in-flight abandoned paths, never the newly wanted one. It waits for *that path specifically* now. Fixed in the test, not the code.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **fast scrolling shows grey cells briefly** — inherent to loading on demand; the margin softens it and does not remove it, and it is the caveat most likely to be reported as a bug; (2) one window at a time, as above; (3) the budget is a constant, not a setting — 256 MB from screen arithmetic, and it is the one number to turn down if memory proves tight; (4) an evicted thumbnail is re-read from disk, since there is still no on-disk thumbnail cache; (5) **nothing here is visible as a feature** except that folders become usable ~28× sooner.

**Keywords (M4.2b, 2026-08-26) — M4.2 IS COMPLETE.** Hierarchical keywords, assigned per file, browsable across folders. 1009 → **1019 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified on the real catalog**: schema v3, a live `Europe > France > Paris` tree, and every folder, photo and collection membership intact.

- **⚠⚠ KEYWORDS ARE PER *FILE* WHILE MARKS ARE PER *COPY*, AND THAT IS NOT AN INCONSISTENCY.** A rating describes *the image you made* — a B&W copy can be better than the colour one. A keyword describes *what is in the picture*, which every copy of a file shares. So `photo_keywords` is keyed on `(folder_id, file_name)` while marks hang off `photos.id`. ⚠ **Both tables carry a comment saying so**, because it looks like an oversight.
- **⚠⚠ THAT KEYING MAKES DRIFT UNREPRESENTABLE.** Per-copy storage would need *two* rules — "assigning applies to every copy" **and** "a new copy inherits its source's keywords" — which can disagree. The failing case is concrete: tag copy 0 "Paris", browse a collection holding copy 1, search Paris, and copy 1 does not match. `a_copy_created_after_tagging_still_matches` pins it, and needs no inheritance code at all.
- **⚠⚠ `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON keywords(COALESCE(parent_id, 0), name)` IS LOAD-BEARING.** A plain `UNIQUE(parent_id, name)` allows **two root-level "Paris" keywords**, because **SQLite treats NULLs as distinct in a unique constraint** — silent duplicates that look identical in the tree with no way to tell them apart. Sabotage confirmed the index is the only thing preventing it. ⚠ And `the_same_name_under_different_parents_is_allowed` is the discriminating other half: "Paris" under both France and Texas is legitimate.
- **⚠⚠ IMPLIED PARENTS ARE DERIVED BY A RECURSIVE CTE, NEVER STORED.** Searching `Europe` finds photos tagged `Paris`. Storing the implication would mean rewriting every affected row whenever a keyword is re-parented — two facts that can disagree, the failure `render_max_dim` and `Session::geometry_mode` were each fixed for by deriving.
- **⚠ Hierarchy was chosen over flat because FLAT → HIERARCHICAL IS NOT AN AUTOMATABLE MIGRATION.** Nothing can infer that `Paris`, `Lyon` and `France` form a tree; the user would rebuild their taxonomy by hand. ⚠ "Flat now, unused `parent_id` in the schema" was rejected too — that hedge is justified for `size`/`mtime`, where a *scan* captures data that would otherwise be lost forever, but here it is speculative schema that does not spare the user the re-parenting, which is the actual cost.
- **⚠⚠ `rename_photo` NOW MOVES THREE THINGS** — the `photos` rows, and the `photo_keywords` rows; collection membership rides on the row id and needs nothing. Third instance of that integration. A rename that forgot would silently strip a file's keywords.
- **⚠⚠ EVERY NEW MIGRATION MAKES THE OLDER DOWNGRADE-SIMULATION TESTS INCOMPLETE.** `a_version_one_catalog_upgrades_and_keeps_its_rows` fakes a v1 catalog by *undoing* what later versions added — and it did not know about v3's tables, so `migrate` re-ran a step whose objects still existed and failed with "table already exists". **The failure is loud, but only because that test exists.** Add the new objects to its drop list whenever a version lands.
- **⚠ Deleting a keyword cascades to its children**, and their assignments. Destructive and deliberate: re-parenting orphans to the root would silently flatten a taxonomy you built, which is worse than an obvious deletion.
- **⚠ One panel, two sections — not two panels.** Collections and Keywords share one idiom: a list with counts, `+` to apply to the selection, click to browse. Two files would invite two idioms for one concept. Browsing a keyword reuses `collections::scan_of` and `LibrarySource` gained a `Keyword` variant, so the grid, filmstrip, format filter, sort orders and star filter all work unchanged.
- **⚠ `assign_keyword_to_selection` de-duplicates by path** — two copies of one file are one file. Assigning twice is a no-op anyway, but the status count would otherwise lie about how much was tagged.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **deleting a parent deletes its children**; (2) **no drag-and-drop** — a parent is chosen when the keyword is created; (3) **no renaming or re-parenting** once created, though the schema supports it; (4) **per file**, so one copy cannot be tagged differently — by design; (5) **catalog-only**, lost with the catalog, never travels with the photos; (6) **no keyword suggestions** — needs usage data and a heuristic, and it is polish on a feature that did not exist; (7) `unassign_keyword` exists and is tested but **has no UI** — the panel gains a remove control when re-parenting does.

**Ratings, flags and colour labels (M4.2a, 2026-08-26) — culling from the keyboard.** Three marks per virtual copy, badges in the grid and filmstrip, and a star filter. 993 → **1009 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **The v1→v2 migration was verified against the real catalog**: 2 folders, 860 photos and 8 collection memberships all survived.

- **⚠⚠ M4.2 WAS THREE SUBSYSTEMS AND WAS DECOMPOSED BEFORE DESIGNING.** The roadmap bullet reads *"EXIF/IPTC display + edit; star ratings, flags, color labels; hierarchical keywords"*. Ratings shipped first (smallest, highest daily value — you cannot cull without them); **keywords are their own slice**; and **EXIF/IPTC editing went to `docs/BACKLOG.md` as a design conflict, not effort** — *writing* metadata means either modifying the original (which violates the thing Rustroom is built on) or a second XMP sidecar alongside the JSON one, reopening the interop question `02-architecture-and-tech` parked.
- **⚠⚠ THE V2 MIGRATION IS THE FIRST TO MEET REAL USER DATA.** A **new gate** (`if from < 2`), never an edit to version 1 — a migration that has already run on someone's machine cannot be changed. `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` means existing rows acquire "unmarked" with no backfill, and `0` is the neutral value for all three marks, so "no mark" needs no special case anywhere. `a_version_one_catalog_upgrades_and_keeps_its_rows` builds a v1 catalog *with a collection in it* and asserts the membership survives.
- **⚠⚠ `1`–`4` ALREADY SELECT RAIL TOOLS — exactly where Lightroom puts stars.** Resolved **by view**: digits cull in Library, select tools in Develop. The rail exists only in Develop and culling happens in the grid, and the two views already diverge on keyboard handling. Rejected: different keys for stars (nothing else is muscle memory — every photo tool uses digits, so it trades a real collision for an unlearnable binding) and moving the rail (breaks a shipped binding for an unshipped feature).
- **⚠⚠ THE CULLING KEYS ARE BARE, SO THEY SIT BEHIND THE EXISTING `memory().focused()` GATE.** The Library now has an editable filename field *and* a new-collection field; a `5` typed into either must not rate a photo. The **same** gate the grid's arrow navigation uses, not a second one. Also `!i.modifiers.any()`, so ⌘X stays cut and ⌘A stays select-all.
- **Marks are per COPY**, on the row `photos` already keys on — a rating describes *the image you made*, which is why copies exist. ⚠ **Accepted incoherence: rejecting a frame means rejecting each of its copies.** Splitting the rules (stars per copy, reject per file) was considered and rejected — two rules to explain, and a grid showing a rejected badge on rows whose stars differ. Lightroom is per-virtual-copy for all three.
- **⚠⚠ THE GLYPH GUARD EARNED ITS PLACE IMMEDIATELY: `✓` AND `✕` ARE IN NO BUNDLED FONT** and would have shipped as tofu boxes. Probing found the usable set is `★ ☆ × ■ ⚑ ⚐ ! ✚ ⊗ ▓`. Pick is `⚑` and reject `×` — and the flag is the *better* symbol anyway, since Lightroom's "pick" literally is a flag. Same finding the rail's four glyphs produced; **probe before choosing, never after**.
- **⚠⚠ A SABOTAGE EXPOSED A TEST THAT COULD NOT SEE THE LAYER IT NAMED — the thirteenth instance.** `an_out_of_range_rating_is_clamped_rather_than_stored` read the value back through `marks()`, which clamps on the **read** path too — so it passed with the *write* clamp deleted. It asserts on the **stored column** now. Defence in depth is fine; a test that cannot see the layer it names is not, and a stored `9` would still be visible to any future `WHERE rating >= 3`.
- **⚠ Colour labels are red, blue, yellow, green — deliberately not red/orange/yellow/green.** Orange and yellow are barely distinguishable at badge size, which defeats the point of a colour label. ⚠ And there are **exactly four**, matching the four keys that reach them: a fifth colour shipped briefly with no key bound to it, which is unreachable and invisible as a bug. `MAX_LABEL` is 4.
- **⚠ `marks_in` is one query per folder per rebuild, never per cell.** The grid draws sixty times a second over hundreds of thumbnails. Only *marked* rows come back, so an unmarked folder costs almost nothing.
- **⚠ The badge is painted into the ALLOCATED rect**, so a marked photo does not resize its cell — the reflow bug the filmstrip already paid for.
- **⚠ Filtering composes through `Library::refilter`**, the single derived `visible` list every consumer iterates — **not** a second filtering mechanism. Sparse-versus-dense index confusion is this project's third-most-repeated bug. `reload_marks` refilters, or a newly-rated photo would not appear or disappear under an active filter until something unrelated rebuilt.
- **⚠ Marks apply to the SELECTION**, the rule a paste, a preset and a sync already follow. Culling is where it matters most: select a run and press `X`.
- **⚠ Nothing in Develop.** The panel is for the photograph; a rating widget there would be a second place to set what the keyboard already handles — the "two homes" mistake the Presets panel was removed for.
- **⚠ A RAW+JPEG pair is two photos, so it takes two marks.** Correct by the model and a real cost if you shoot both routinely; Lightroom's answer is a "treat JPEG as sidecar" import setting. Not built.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) rejecting a frame means rejecting each copy; (2) **no auto-advance** after rating; (3) colour labels are numbers, not names; (4) no "delete rejected" action — nothing here deletes files; (5) **marks are catalog-only**, so they do not travel with the photos and are lost with the catalog — the M4.1 split, deliberately; (6) **filtering by minimum stars only**, not by flag or label — additive, same path.

**Collections (M4.1, plan 2 of 2, 2026-08-26) — M4.1 IS COMPLETE.** Manual collections, created and browsed from a left panel in the Library. 981 → **993 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified end to end on two real folders**: 860 photos auto-catalogued, a collection spanning them.

- **⚠⚠ A COLLECTION IS BROWSED, NOT FILTERED — and this deliberately overrides the spec.** The spec said plan 2 would *"filter the grid"*. **That would not have delivered the feature**: filtering only the folder you happen to have open reproduces exactly the fragmentation that choosing one catalog over one-per-folder was meant to avoid. Selecting a collection **replaces the Library's contents** with its members, from any folder ever opened.
- **⚠ It cost almost nothing because everything downstream keys on paths.** `collections::scan_of` builds a synthetic `CatalogEntry` scan from the stored paths, and `entries::build`, `ThumbLoader`, `Selection`, the format filter, the six sort orders and the filmstrip all needed **no change at all**.
- **⚠⚠ THE MODEL, STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE IT IS TWO THINGS:** the **catalog** is one, global and **automatic** — every folder opened is recorded with all its photos, no action required. **Collections** are many and **manual** — subsets within that one catalog, nothing added unless you add it.
- **⚠ There is NO "all photos" view.** The catalog accumulates everything, but the only two loaders are `open_folder` and `open_collection`. Cross-folder *queries* work; browsing the whole library does not exist yet. That is M4.3.
- **⚠ `Library.dir` became `LibrarySource::{Folder, Collection}`**, which made one latent problem explicit: **`Reindex` now refuses on a collection** and says so. It renumbers copies in a *folder*; a collection has no single folder to re-read, and renumbering photos you are merely viewing would be surprising.
- **⚠ Deleting a collection never touches photos.** `ON DELETE CASCADE` removes the *memberships* and stops there — a collection is a view onto photos, not a container of them. It has its own test because it is the thing people actually worry about.
- **⚠ A member whose file has vanished is still listed.** Dropping it would make an unplugged drive look like the collection had emptied itself — the same rule as "a scan never deletes a photo row".
- **⚠ A duplicate collection name returns the existing one rather than erroring.** The name is the identity a user thinks in; failing the second time "Portfolio" is used would be a bug, not a safeguard.
- **⚠ Membership is by row id, so a rename carries it** — `a_renamed_file_stays_in_its_collections` asserts end to end the integration plan 1 built `rename_photo` for.
- **⚠ A SABOTAGE THAT WAS INERT, AND SAID NOTHING.** Changing `ON CONFLICT(name) DO NOTHING` to `DO UPDATE SET created = created` still cannot create a second row under a UNIQUE name — so "NOT CAUGHT" was meaningless rather than a finding. The discriminating version removes the conflict clause entirely, making the insert error. **A sabotage that does not change behaviour is not evidence about the test.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no smart collections** — M4.3, and they need a query language; (2) **no nesting**; (3) **a collection cannot be renamed**, only created and deleted; (4) **no drag-and-drop** — photos are added by selecting them and pressing `+`; (5) **no "all photos" view**, as above.

**The catalog foundation (M4.1, plan 1 of 2, 2026-08-26).** One invisible SQLite catalog recording every folder opened and every photo in it. 968 → **981 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified on a real folder: schema v1, four tables, 30 photos with real sizes and mtimes.**

- **⚠⚠ EDITS DO NOT LIVE HERE, AND THAT SPLIT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** Edits stay in the per-photo JSON sidecars, so they travel with the photograph and survive this application entirely; only *library* metadata — folders, photos, collections — is in the database. The property it preserves: **delete the catalog and you lose organisation, never work.** The rejected alternatives were sidecars-authoritative-with-a-rebuildable-index (which forces ratings and keywords into the sidecars, bloating a per-photo file with library concepts) and DB-authoritative (Lightroom's model, and the most-complained-about part of it).
- **⚠⚠ ONE INVISIBLE CATALOG — AND A CATALOG *PER FOLDER* WAS REJECTED FOR A CONCRETE REASON.** Three shoots in `2026-08-holiday/`, `2026-09-wedding/`, `2026-10-portraits/` would be three databases, making *"show me every 5-star frame this year"* impossible. **The single thing a DAM exists for, defeated by the default behaviour** — and getting a unified library would then require ceremony while the frictionless path produced permanent fragmentation. ⚠ The discussion initially leaned the other way; the scenario is what turned it. Lightroom users overwhelmingly run **one** catalog and Adobe's own guidance discourages more, for exactly this reason.
- **Consequence: no import step, no catalog picker, no catalog concept in the UI at all.** Opening a folder works exactly as before.
- **⚠⚠ A SCAN NEVER DELETES A PHOTO ROW.** An unplugged drive or unmounted share scans as *empty*, and deleting rows then would silently empty every collection referencing them — with no collections UI yet, nothing would catch it for weeks. `last_seen` records what was present; **absence is not deletion**. `a_folder_whose_files_have_vanished_keeps_its_rows` is the guard.
- **⚠⚠ RENAMES AND `Reindex` UPDATE THE CATALOG IN THE SAME OPERATION**, and a rename moves **every copy** of the file, because copies share it. This is the same class as the rule virtual copies needed twice — *the stash and the log must let go together*. A catalog row and the file it names are two facts that must move as one.
- **⚠ Every call site degrades to doing nothing when the catalog is absent** (`Option<Catalog>`, errors logged not propagated). A database that will not open must never stop you editing photographs, because edits live in the sidecars and do not need it.
- **⚠ `migrate` is forward-only by construction** — every step gated on `from < n` — so a catalog written by a *newer* Rustroom is left alone rather than downgraded, which would destroy whatever that version added. `a_catalog_from_the_future_is_left_alone` pins it.
- **⚠ All four tables are created in the first migration**, including `collections`, which plan 2 uses. A migration that has already run on someone's machine cannot be edited afterwards, so the choice is to write it once now or add a second migration later for no benefit.
- **⚠ `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` is per connection and OFF by default in SQLite.** Without it, deleting a folder leaves orphaned photo rows and orphaned collection memberships pointing at nothing. It has its own test because nothing else would notice.
- **⚠ `size` and `mtime` are recorded and read by NOTHING.** They are what a future re-link of a moved file needs, and they cost nothing during a scan the caller is already doing — the alternative is discovering later that the data was never captured for any photo anyone owns.
- **⚠ `rusqlite` with `bundled`** compiles SQLite from source (14 s), which is a C dependency — but **no system library, no pkg-config entry, no CI change**, strictly fewer build prerequisites than libraw already imposes. Linking the system sqlite would add a fourth entry to a `PKG_CONFIG_PATH` line already three deep.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the library accumulates every folder ever opened**, including accidents — "Forget this folder" deferred until it annoys; (2) **no re-linking of moved files** — a moved photo appears as new and its membership stays with the old row; (3) **no import step**, so no moment at which to apply presets or metadata on ingest; (4) **one catalog**, so no client/personal separation — collections are the answer; (5) **plan 1 is invisible** — if you can tell it shipped, something is wrong.

**Headless canvas and panel tests (2026-08-26) — the eleven-times-recorded gap, closed for wiring.** A fixture that drives the real `canvas::show`, label-based widget finding via AccessKit, a request counter, and three lifted coordinate functions. 933 → **968 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE BARRIER WAS ALWAYS CONSTRUCTION, NEVER INTENT — and measuring that reframed the job.** The harness was *not* unused: `canvas.rs` already had 11 tests. They covered exactly two ~30-line helpers (`tool_drag`, `pan_delta`), because those were the only functions small enough to call. `show` (238 lines), `draw_mask_overlay` (224) and `draw_crop_overlay` (92) had **zero**, and all of them take `&mut RustroomApp`.
- **⚠⚠ A `RustroomApp` NEEDS NO DISK, NO LIBRAW AND NO GPU.** `crates/app/src/test_app.rs` builds all 43 fields: the two `DecodeWorker`s already take an *injected* decode fn, the two stores an injected path, `RenderEngine` a plain `Arc<ImageBuf>`, and `ctx.load_texture` works headlessly. **Nothing in production had to change to make this possible** — which is why it should have existed a year ago.
- **⚠ The fixture's 43-field literal is exhaustive on purpose**, following the `Settings` precedent: a new field on `RustroomApp` is a **compile error there**, forcing a decision about what a test should see. Do not reach for `..Default::default()`.
- **⚠⚠ `RenderEngine::requests_issued()` is UNCONDITIONAL, not `#[cfg(test)]`, and that is forced.** `render_engine` is in the **lib**; `canvas.rs` is in the **bin**. When the bin's test target builds, the lib is a dependency compiled *without* `cfg(test)` — a gated field would simply not exist where the tests need it. Two `usize` of production surface, and the alternative (asserting on `requested_region`) is a proxy that misses exactly the whole-image fit case where the bug occurred.
- **⚠⚠ THREE OF THE FIVE SABOTAGES FAILED FIRST, and each exposed a different way the harness lied.** This is the most valuable part of the work:
  1. **The press landed away from the handle.** The harness moved the pointer to `to` on the *same pass* as the press, so every drag missed — and `dragging_a_mask_handle_asks_for_a_render` **passed anyway**, because `show` issues renders for reasons of its own. Found because its sibling failed. The harness is **five passes** now: move to `from`; press *while still there*; move; release; settle.
  2. **Sampling after the gesture could not see a missing request.** Once `Edits` changes, a *later* frame issues a request regardless, so the count rose even with `s.request` deleted. ⚠ **In the real app that later frame never comes — egui idles after a drag — which is precisely why the bug was visible as "the image does not update until you touch a slider".** The test samples the counter **on the drag frame itself** now.
  3. **Asserting on `Edits` could not see a missing release commit.** The edits change during the *drag*, from `apply_drag`; only the undo push happens on release. The test asserts on **`history.can_undo()`** now. Twelfth instance of a test naming a bug it cannot detect.
- **⚠ `a_drag_that_misses_every_handle_asks_for_nothing` is the guard on the guard.** "The count went up after a drag" means nothing unless it *stays put* when the drag grabs nothing. It is what would have caught fault 1 above directly.
- **`develop/canvas_geom.rs`** holds `frame_rect`, `normalized_point` and `frame_aspect`, lifted out of the canvas and **replacing their inline originals in the same commit** — a `pub fn` with no caller in a *binary* crate is dead code that fails `clippy -D warnings`, the eighth instance of that trap. They make the drift and aspect bugs testable with **no fixture at all**.
- **⚠ A commit went in with clippy failing**, caught immediately after: `cargo test` only *warns* on an unused variable while clippy's `-D warnings` rejects it, so the green test run was not evidence. Amended. **Check clippy's exit code before every commit, not just the test one.**
- **⚠⚠ ACCESSKIT IS ALREADY IN THE BUILD, AND IT REMOVES HARDCODED COORDINATES ENTIRELY.** eframe turns egui's `accesskit` feature on, so `ctx.enable_accesskit()` plus `FullOutput::platform_output.accesskit_update` gives every interactive widget's **label and bounds** — found by probing, not assumed. `crates/app/src/test_ui.rs` is ~100 lines on top of that: `find`, `click` and `labelled_centres`, all by label. **No new dependency.** ⚠ `egui_kittest` **0.35.0 exists**, matching our egui exactly, and is a wrapper over this same mechanism plus wgpu snapshotting — **adopt it rather than reimplement** the moment the queries here outgrow "find an interactive widget by its label".
- **⚠ Plain `ui.label` text produces NO AccessKit node** — only interactive widgets are findable. A test that wanted to assert on an empty-state label had to be narrowed to the *absence of buttons* instead. That is the exact boundary where kittest starts to pay.
- **⚠⚠ THE FIRST CANVAS TESTS HARDCODED `(200, 150)`, AND THAT WAS FIXED BEFORE IT SPREAD.** The point came from instrumenting the layout once — brittle in the worst way, because a layout change moves the target silently and the failure mode is a test that still passes while grabbing nothing. `canvas_centre` measures it by running one pass and reading `ui.max_rect()`.
- **Covered now: `canvas.rs` 20 tests, `panel/masks.rs` 5, `panel/geometry.rs` 4, `panel/presets.rs` 6.** The crop overlay's four guards mirror the mask overlay's, ⚠ **including the asymmetry**: `the_crop_overlay_does_not_ask_for_a_render` pins that a crop drag deliberately issues none, because `preview_edits` strips the crop in geometry mode and the overlay *is* the feedback. Assuming the reverse for masks is the bug that shipped.
- **⚠ `every_orientation_button_moves_the_crop_with_it` guards the invariant that panel's own comment names** — each button must move the crop by the same transform it applies to the orientation, or the crop lands on different content. A button that turns the image and leaves the crop behind looks right on an uncropped photo and is silently wrong on every cropped one.
- **⚠ Two tests asserted something FALSE about correct code, both f32 equality after a rigid transform.** `1.0 - (1.0 - 0.1)` is `0.100000024`, so a crop flipped twice does not compare exactly. Fixed in the tests with a 1e-6 tolerance — a millionth of the frame, sub-pixel on any image — not in the code.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **appearance is out of scope** — no pixel assertions, no golden screenshots, so the 711 MB vertex buffer and the wrong-looking tint remain uncatchable and would still be found by using the app; (2) the render worker really spawns in every fixture test; (3) two `usize` of test-only surface on `RenderEngine`; (4) **still uncovered by choice — `shell.rs`, `library.rs`, `filmstrip.rs`, `modals/`** (~960 lines, tier 2), deferred because they are overwhelmingly glue whose testable logic was already extracted (`Settings::apply_delete_confirm`, `batch_export::destination_ready`, `format_filter`); (5) plain labels are invisible to `test_ui`.

**Naming, renaming and the browser (2026-08-26) — M3.4's follow-up, and every bit of it came from using the feature.** File renaming, per-copy names, `Reindex`, six sort orders and a resizable grid. 888 → **933 tests**; 47 goldens untouched throughout. **Not one of these was found by a test.**

- **⚠⚠ THE ORIGINAL COPY-NAME FIELD WAS WRONG IN A WAY THE PLAN COULD NOT SEE: it was a *second* name.** The filename showed as static text and a separate "copy name" box sat beneath it — so a photo had two names, only one of them editable, and typing in the editable one changed nothing visible. Reported as "the name edit does not seem to work", which was the right description. **There is one name field per row now, and *which* name it edits depends on the row.**
- **⚠⚠ THE ASYMMETRY IS THE FEATURE, not a compromise.** On the **original** the field is the **filename on disk** (`std::fs::rename`, sidecar moved with it); on a **copy** it is that copy's **own name**. A copy has no file of its own, so a first attempt that renamed the file from either row dragged *every sibling copy* along with it — correct by the model, and surprising in use. Two intentions, two targets.
- **⚠⚠ A COPY'S NAME *IS* ITS EXPORT NAME — it is not appended to the file's.** Naming copy 2 "Warm" gives `Warm.jpg`, not `DSCF6223_Warm.jpg`. The first build appended, which means a copy can never have a name of its own. **This is also why the field shows a whole filename** (`DSCF6223_1.RAF`) rather than a bare label: what you see is what you get.
- **⚠ An unnamed copy's name is DERIVED, never stored.** `None` in the sidecar is what makes it follow the file — rename `a.RAF` to `b.RAF` and its copies become `b_1.RAF` on their own. ⚠ Typing the *default* back into the field also stores `None`, deliberately: freezing the derived string would silently strand the copy on the old stem, and its export filename with it.
- **⚠ One rule, three consumers.** `entries::shown_name` feeds the Library caption, the editable field in both views, and the default export name. It had briefly grown **two** implementations, which is exactly how the grid ended up printing `file_name` where a copy's name belonged.
- **⚠⚠ THE BUG BEHIND TWO SEPARATE REPORTS WAS ONE LINE: `library.rs` printed `entries[i].file_name` and never called `label()`.** Every copy of a file carries the same `file_name`, so the grid showed the file's name on every copy row — copy names could not appear at all, and renaming the original looked like it only partly took. **The Library was the last consumer still bypassing the shared rule.**
- **⚠ Two renames, two `ActionKind`s, scoped differently — and the difference is forced.** `Rename { from, to }` is **global**: after it the photo is at a *different* path, so scoping it to the old one would make it unreachable the moment it took effect. `CopyRename { id, from, to }` is **scoped to its copy**, like `PhotoEdit`, because the file has not moved.
- **⚠ `Reindex` is the one thing allowed to renumber copies, and it is a button for that reason.** Re-reads the folder (which is also the reorder, and picks up files changed outside Rustroom) and closes gaps, so deleting copy 1 of two makes the remaining one copy 1 again. ⚠ Three consequences, all deliberate: **unnamed** copies get new export filenames (a **named** one does not — its name is its own); **undo stacks for renumbered copies are dropped**, because a renumbered copy is a different `PhotoId` and keeping the stash would hand old copy 1's stack to the new copy 1; and the open photo is reopened at its **original**, since its copy number may no longer exist.
- **⚠⚠ THE STASH AND THE LOG MUST LET GO TOGETHER** — `HistoryStash::forget` plus `ActionLog::forget_edits_of`. A stash kept without its log entries is unreachable; log entries kept without their stash pop nothing. The same pairing M3.4 already needed for re-keying.
- **⚠ Sorting is applied to the SCAN, never to `entries`.** That is what keeps a file's copies adjacent to it and ascending under every ordering — sorting `entries` would scatter them. Six orders: name ↑↓, modified ↑↓, **last edited** ↑↓. The filmstrip follows for free; both views read one `entries` list.
- **⚠⚠ EVERY ORDER ENDS IN THE SAME NAME TIE-BREAK, and that is what makes the grid stable.** Two files sharing an mtime is ordinary — a burst, a copy, an unzip — and without it they swap places between one folder open and the next for no visible reason. Case-insensitive comparison alone is likewise **not a total order**, so names differing only in case need it too.
- **⚠ Sorting was case-SENSITIVE and nobody noticed for a week.** A byte comparison puts every capital ahead of every lowercase letter — `'A'` is 65, `'a'` is 97 — so `Zebra.RAF` sorted before `apple.RAF`. ⚠ **Testing it needed the comparator extracted from `scan_folder`**: macOS is case-insensitive, so a test writing `a.RAF` and `A.RAF` gets *one* file and can say nothing about how two such names order.
- **⚠ `SortOrder` lives in `catalog`, beside the comparator**, so the ordering rule exists once — the `Demosaic`-in-`raw-decode` trade, and it cost `serde` as that crate's first dependency. ⚠ But **`CatalogEntry::edited` is filled in by the CALLER**: "last edited" means the sidecar's mtime, and the sidecar naming convention belongs to `app`. Carrying the field while staying ignorant of where it comes from is what keeps the layering intact. `needs_edited()` lets the other four orders skip a stat per file.
- **⚠ A never-edited photo sorts to the far end**, not jumbled among the edited ones: `None` means "never edited", not "unknown, put it anywhere".
- **⚠ The Library grid resizes by pinch, ctrl/cmd+scroll or two buttons, and the cell is a `u32`.** Not `f32`, for two reasons: `Settings` derives `Eq`, which no float satisfies; and it makes a NaN cell **unrepresentable** rather than something to guard — NaN defeats `clamp` entirely, since it fails every comparison and comes back unchanged. Thumbnail, cell and caption all scale together, the caption from the theme's own Small size so a later type-scale change carries through.
- **⚠⚠ A PLAIN MULTIPLY WOULD HAVE LOOKED LIKE THE PINCH WAS NOT WIRED UP.** `zoom_delta` reports ~1.01 per frame; against a whole-point cell that rounds straight back to the same integer, so the grid sits still until you pinch hard. `zoomed_cell` forces at least one point of travel while a gesture is under way. ⚠ **The failure mode is silence, not wrongness**, which is why it has its own test. And `zoom_delta` is a **factor that is 1.0 when idle**, not a delta — the trap the canvas-navigation work already recorded.
- **⚠ Settings are written when a gesture SETTLES**, not per frame: a pinch runs at 60 Hz. Likewise the sort combo and the size buttons guard on an actual change, because their bodies run every frame — the `apply_view_demosaic` rule, now invoked four times in this file.
- **⚠⚠ A HARNESS BUG MADE ALL SEVEN SABOTAGES REPORT "NOT CAUGHT" — a new variant of a lesson already here.** The matcher looked for failure lines by bare test name while cargo prints them with module paths, so nothing ever matched. CLAUDE.md already records that *a sabotage which fails to compile looks exactly like one that passes*; this is the mirror image, a universal false alarm. **Both come from trusting the harness's output shape instead of checking it produces what you grep for.** The harness now checks for compile errors *and* was re-run once fixed.
- **⚠ Three tests were written that asserted something FALSE, or measured the wrong thing, and each was fixed in the test rather than the code:** `forget` stashed histories with nothing to undo (which `stash` drops, so it stored nothing and would have passed vacuously); a click-to-edit test used `draft.is_some()` as the signal for "a field was drawn", which is true for a *foreign* draft too — it reads keyboard focus now, which is both honest and what the user experiences; and `every_order_reaches_a_different_arrangement`'s first fixture made two orders produce the same list. ⚠ **That last one is the pairwise check doing its job**: three files have exactly six permutations in three reverse-pairs, and each ascending/descending pair of orders *is* one such pair, so the fixture must hand a different pair to name, modified and edited.
- **⚠ The Library name is a CAPTION until clicked, and that also fixed the keyboard.** Drawing the field for whichever row was selected meant merely clicking a photo put you in a text box — which then owned the arrow keys and Enter, so browsing stopped working. ⚠ The grid's navigation is now gated on `memory().focused()`, the same gate the filmstrip has always had and that was simply missing here. ⚠ And the field is drawn whenever the draft belongs to that row **regardless of selection**: tied to the selection, clicking another photo would remove the field before it could lose focus and the typed name would vanish uncommitted.
- **⚠ Autofocus is scoped to the on-demand field only.** The Develop panel draws its field every frame, so autofocusing there would grab the keyboard the instant a photo opened and send every keystroke into the filename.
- **⚠ `name_edit` is a shared bin module** holding the field and the commit, so the Develop panel and the Library grid cannot drift. They had one editable field between them; growing a second copy in the grid is how "the same logic" becomes two subtly different logics.
- **⚠ `safe_name::sanitize` is the guard on both paths** — a typed name reaches the filesystem here exactly as a preset's does. The **shown** name keeps what you typed; only the filename is sanitized, so "B&W" is labelled "B&W" and exports as `B_W`.
- **⚠ `file_rename::target` always preserves the original extension**, whatever is typed: the field shows the whole filename, so `.RAF` is easy to mangle, and a RAW without its extension stops being openable by this app or any other. ⚠ Its extension strip is deliberately **not** shared with `default_copy_name`'s — `".RAF"` must be *refused* as a typed name and *kept whole* as a derived one, and sharing the rule made a blank entry silently acceptable.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **edit times refresh on folder open, Reindex and any rebuild — not while you are editing**, because reordering the grid and filmstrip under someone mid-edit is worse than a slightly stale order; (2) renaming a **file** still moves every copy of it, which is correct — they share the file; (3) `Reindex` renumbering changes unnamed copies' export filenames and drops their undo stacks; (4) two copies may be given the same name, and only the pre-existing `-N` de-duplication keeps their exports apart; (5) the grid size is one setting, not per folder.

**Lens corrections (M3.2) — distortion, TCA and vignetting (2026-08-25).** Profile-driven optical corrections, matched from LensFun's database. 824 → **854 tests**; goldens 43 → **47**, and the 43 stayed byte-identical. **M3.2's automatic half is done; perspective correction is a separate slice.**

- **⚠⚠ THE C-DEPENDENCY RISK THIS FILE RECORDED FOR M3.2 IS GONE, AND THAT WAS FOUND BY PROBING BEFORE ANY DESIGN.** `lensfun = "0.7"` is a **pure-Rust port** — zero dependencies, LGPL-3.0-or-later (compatible with our GPL-3.0), and it **embeds the whole database** (56 XML files, gzipped at build time). No C library, no glib, no pkg-config entry, no CI change. ⚠ **`brew install lensfun` is NOT a dependency** — the C library was installed during probing only, to compare against, and it drags in glib. Do not add it to the setup line.
- **⚠⚠ THE BARREL/PINCUSHION DIRECTION WAS BACKWARDS IN THE FIRST DRAFT OF THE DESIGN, AND IN THE MOCKUP THE BEHAVIOUR WAS CHOSEN FROM.** Pinned empirically against a synthetic profile: **barrel correction expands the compressed periphery, so the output reads from *nearer* the centre — the corners always have data and its autoscale is exactly 1.0**; **pincushion reads from *further out*, so the corners want pixels beyond the sensor and come back empty**. Confirmed on the real XF16-80: barrel at 16 mm (source *inside* the frame), pincushion from ~35 mm (source outside). ⚠ The consequence is the design decision: **a fixed autoscale is wrong at both ends** — useless at the wide end while discarding real data, essential at the long end — which is why the scale is a slider. `barrel_correction_needs_no_scaling` and `pincushion_correction_autoscales_above_one` pin both halves.
- **⚠⚠ THE PORT HAS NO AUTOSCALE API — ours is our own.** No `get_auto_scale`, no `enable_scaling`, nothing (verified: zero matches across the crate). The C library has `lfModifier::GetAutoScale`. Ours follows its approach — probe the four corners and four edge midpoints, ask what scale each needs, take the largest — and is **validated against synthetic profiles, not against the reference implementation**. ⚠ An earlier statement that "both expose the scale factor" was wrong and fed a user decision; it changes A-vs-C, not B-vs-C, since a fixed autoscale needs the identical computation.
- **⚠⚠ THERE IS NO COMBINED SUBPIXEL+GEOMETRY CALL EITHER.** `apply_subpixel_distortion` carries **TCA only** — it seeds coordinates from the pixel grid and applies the TCA kernel, with no distortion term. So the two are **summed as displacements**: `source_c = distortion(x,y) + (tca_c(x,y) − (x,y))`. Exact to first order, and the neglected cross term is bounded at **~0.08 px** at realistic coefficients (TCA ~0.06% of radius, distortion ~3.5%, radius 3700 px) — far below the error two sequential resampling passes would introduce. `disabling_tca_leaves_the_distortion_map_untouched` pins the degenerate cases *exactly*.
- **⚠⚠ `Database::load_str` TAKES XML, AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES ANY OF THIS TESTABLE IN CI.** Tests define a **synthetic lens with coefficients we chose** — no RAW fixture, no gating, deterministic. It is why lens correction has real **goldens** at all: a real profile needs a real camera and lens, which a synthetic chart does not have, and a fixture-gated test is no evidence in CI. Every `lens-profile` test and all four goldens run on this seam.
- **⚠ The stage builds its own `Corrector` from the image it is given.** A corrector is tied to specific dimensions, and the preview and the 1:1 view render at different sizes through the same `Edits`. Handing one in would make a size mismatch **representable** — and silently wrong rather than an error. Found while wiring the GUI, where the rendered dims are not known at `build_prefix_steps` time; all 47 goldens stayed byte-identical through the refactor, which is the proof it changed no behaviour.
- **⚠ Placement: FIRST in the prefix, before `GeometryStage`** — the calibration is defined in the uncropped, unrotated sensor frame. Prefix membership means it runs whole-buffer, so **no `Region`, no kernel margin, no `REGION_MARGIN` change** despite being a spatial resample. New `PrefixStepKey::LensCorrection`. ⚠ `PrefixKey::new` passes `None` for the corrector deliberately: the key is derived from the *edits*, and the key variant carries them.
- **⚠ It needs no `RenderScale`** — the correction is normalised, so passing the rendered dimensions makes preview and export agree exactly. Same property the creative vignette has.
- **⚠ NO DOUBLE-CORRECTION RISK, verified rather than assumed.** libraw's generated bindings contain **no** distortion, geometry or vignetting fields — zero matches across 6,389 lines. `dcraw_process` does demosaic, WB, colour and gamma and no geometric correction, so Fuji's embedded correction parameters are not being applied by the decoder.
- **⚠ Two `rsraw::LensInfo` fields are unreliable and are deliberately not carried.** `min_focal`/`max_focal` report `16-16` for the 16-80 zoom, and `mounts` returns the garbage string `"IL UM"`. The per-shot `raw.focal_len()` is correct and is what the interpolation needs; the mount is unnecessary because matching goes through the camera.
- **⚠⚠ IT HAS ITS OWN EDIT GROUP AND ITS OWN PANEL SECTION — it was in Detail first, and that was wrong.** The initial placement leant on this file's own note that Detail is the capture-specific group ("noise, lens, output size"), which is true but was a one-line aside rather than a considered placement. The concrete cost of bundling: **syncing Detail to another photo also synced lens corrections**, so noise reduction dragged an unrelated capture correction along with it. `EDIT_GROUP_NAMES` is now **8 entries** with `Lens` at index 4, and `GEOMETRY_GROUP`/`MASKS_GROUP` moved to 6/7 — ⚠ safe for existing presets because `EditGroups` serializes by **field name**, not index. `lens_corrections_travel_on_their_own_group_not_with_detail` pins both directions.
- **⚠ Placed *after* Detail rather than first, despite running first in the pipeline.** It is on by default and visited only to turn something off, so depth costs nothing — panel follows workflow, pipeline follows maths. It is also where Lightroom puts its own Lens Corrections panel, and where perspective correction will go when that slice lands.
- **⚠⚠ THE SCALE SLIDER APPEARS IN THE CROP TOOL TOO, AND THAT IS NOT DUPLICATION FOR ITS OWN SAKE.** Scale is only *judgeable* against the uncropped frame — crop mode previews with the crop reset, which is the one view where the empty corners pincushion correction leaves are visible. In the Edit panel the crop is applied, so **if you have cropped even slightly the gaps are already outside it and the slider looks inert**. One field, two decisions, edited from whichever view can see its effect. ⚠ Contrast with the Presets panel, which was removed for having two homes: that was a duplicated *feature*, this is a single value.
- **⚠⚠ THE `Effects` GROUP IS NOW `Vignette`, AND RENAMING A SERIALIZED FIELD IS A MIGRATION, NOT A RENAME.** It held exactly one thing and said otherwise. `EditGroups.effects` → `.vignette` carries **`#[serde(alias = "effects")]`**, which is load-bearing: the struct has a container-level `#[serde(default)]`, so without the alias an existing preset storing `"effects": false` would drop that key and fall back to the field's default of **true** — a preset deliberately built to leave the vignette alone would silently start applying one. `a_preset_that_excluded_effects_still_excludes_the_vignette` pins it, and the sabotage (deleting the alias) fails it.
- **⚠ A stale string caught by the same change: the "Reset adjustments" hover text named the five sections literally** and went wrong the moment a sixth was added. It is now **derived from `SECTIONS`**, like the button's group set already was. ⚠ And `the_masks_group_transfers_masks_and_nothing_else` hardcoded `EditGroups::only(6)` — exactly what the named `MASKS_GROUP` constant exists to prevent; it uses the constant now.
- **⚠ `Edits::default()` stays strictly neutral; the GUI applies a preference.** `Settings.auto_lens_correction` (default on) turns the three corrections on for a photo with **no sidecar yet**; an already-edited photo keeps exactly what it had. Defaulting the op-list instead would dent that invariant **in a way the golden harness structurally cannot see** — the goldens are synthetic buffers with no lens metadata, so the stage stays inert there while every real photo changes. ⚠ Accepted consequence: **the GUI and the CLI disagree on a fresh photo.**
- **⚠ The profile is resolved on the DECODE thread and captured by `RenderEngine`**, the shape C4 used for the camera profile. `Catalogue::shared()` is a `OnceLock` because the first parse decompresses 56 XML files — the `srgb_icc()` precedent — and a database load in a panel body would be the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake in a new costume. ⚠ The profile must also be carried across the **full-resolution handover**, or corrections would silently switch off when the upgrade lands.
- **Validated on a real X-T4 frame, bucketed by radius rather than globally** (`cargo run --release -p app --example lens_diff <off> <on>`, kept). The XF23's **4.79× corner lift is real**: the uncorrected frame falls to **61% of centre brightness** at the periphery and lands at **106.3 against a centre of 102.9** corrected — within 3% of flat, monotonic in radius (1.015 → 1.079 → 1.212 → 1.443 → 1.725 by band), neutral at the centre. ⚠ A whole-image mean would have hidden this entirely.
- **⚠ Measured cost, and it points at a specific inefficiency: a full-resolution render goes 4.59 s → 6.15 s (+1.56 s on 26 Mpx), which is 7.7× `GeometryStage`'s 203 ms for a whole-buffer rotation.** The cause is not the maths — the lensfun API takes `width`/`rows` and is built to fill a **row** at a time, and the stage calls each of the three `apply_*` functions with a 1×1 grid per pixel. Batching per row is the fix; backlogged.
- **⚠ A false green worth remembering: `cargo test --all | grep -c "test result: FAILED"` returns 0 when the build FAILS**, because a failed build emits no test-result lines at all. It reported clean while three test targets did not compile. **Check the exit code**, the same lesson already recorded for clippy.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **brush strokes shift if corrections are toggled after painting** — up to ~3.5% at the corners, ~0 at the centre, because strokes are anchored to `GeometryStage`'s input and lens correction now precedes it; the principled fix needs a **forward** distortion map the crate does not expose, and is backlogged; (2) **RAW only** — JPEG/PNG/TIFF carry no lens metadata through `image-decode`; (3) the **bundled database goes stale with the crate version** rather than tracking the user's system; (4) a lens absent from the database gets nothing — no manual profile picking; (5) **the calibration is volunteers' data measured on other bodies** — the XF23's vignetting was taken with an X-T2 and its distortion with an X-T10, and lensfun's own comment on that lens reads *"the target was not really trustworthy."*; (6) the GUI/CLI disagreement above; (7) **perspective correction is not included.**

**Colour grading (M3.3) — three-way wheels (2026-08-25).** Shadows / midtones / highlights, plus a Global wheel and the Blending and Balance sliders. 792 → **824 tests**; goldens 39 → **43**, and the 39 stayed byte-identical. **M3.3 is closed with this.**

- **⚠⚠ THE WHOLE THREE-WHEEL GRADE COLLAPSES TO A 1-D LUT ON OKLAB *L*, and that is the finding that settled the milestone's one open architectural question.** The tint at a pixel depends on nothing but its lightness and on constants, so `ColorGradingStage::new` precomputes a 256-entry table of `(Δa, Δb, ΔL)`. **This answers "one stage or three?" definitively: one.** Only one stage can fold three ranges into one table, and three sequential stages would mean three Oklab round trips with each range re-measuring a lightness the previous had already moved — the interaction `LocalContrastStage` sums deltas against one source specifically to avoid. Not a micro-optimisation either: direct evaluation is three `exp` per pixel, ~5 M on a preview frame.
- **⚠⚠ THE RANGES ARE SELECTED ON OKLAB *L*, NOT `color::luminance()` — this file said otherwise and was wrong.** The parked note assumed physical *Y* would be the selector. Two reasons it must not be: we are already in Oklab for the tint, so *L* is free; and **mid-grey sits near 0.6 in *L* but at 0.18 in *Y***, so selecting on *Y* puts "midtones" where most people would say highlights.
- **⚠ Weights are three normalised Gaussians, and every property wanted falls out of that shape rather than being bolted on.** They **sum to exactly 1 at every lightness**, so three equal wheels reproduce that tint uniformly with no seams — which is also why **Global is a plain fourth constant added after the weighted sum**, not a fourth range. Blending maps to the Gaussian width: at 0 the nearest centre wins outright, at 100 all three approach ⅓ and the grade becomes a uniform tint. Balance slides all three centres together.
- **⚠ The softmax `max(e)` subtraction is LOAD-BEARING, not tidiness.** Without it an `l` far from every centre at small sigma underflows all three exponentials to zero and the normalisation divides by zero. `an_extreme_lightness_at_the_narrowest_width_still_normalises` is the only test that catches its removal — sabotaged and watched to fail with `sum=NaN`.
- **⚠⚠ THE INDEX CLAMPS TO [0,1]; THE PIXEL'S OWN `L` DOES NOT.** Scene-referred values above white have `L > 1`, and clamping the *value* rather than the *index* would drag every highlight back to white — destroying exactly the headroom the wide working space exists for. **Identical trap to `ProfileHueSatMap`'s `v`**, and it has its own test for the same reason.
- **⚠⚠ A GOLDEN'S HUE CONVENTION CANNOT BE GUARDED BY A GOLDEN, and this is the sharpest lesson of the feature.** Swapping `a` and `b` in `wheel_offset` leaves `the_table_agrees_with_direct_evaluation` **green** — it calls that function on *both* sides — and leaves `three_wheels_alike_reproduce_a_uniform_tint` green for the same reason, and `a_grey_pixel_is_tinted` green because a swap preserves chroma *magnitude*. A golden is no help either: **a golden only ever catches a change**, and would have baked the wrong convention into its reference on the first run. `the_tint_lands_at_the_hue_the_wheel_names` derives the answer independently, by measuring the chroma the stage actually produced, and is the **only** thing that fails. It was added because the plan's sabotage step predicted the blind spot in advance.
- **⚠⚠ THE FIRST GOLDEN RAMP CLIPPED — 144 of 3072 samples pinned at 255 — and a permanent test caught it rather than an eyeball.** The plan called for measuring the generated golden; there is no PIL on this machine, so it became `the_grading_goldens_do_not_clip` instead of a throwaway script, which is strictly better: it guards the goldens' discriminating power against any future change to the chart or the constants. ⚠ The top was then chosen **by measurement, not by eye**: at 0.85 `grade_split_tone` pinned 144 samples; at 0.74 `grade_balance_high` still pinned 16; at **0.68** the brightest sample across all four cases is **243**. A first pass at 0.60 was clean but reached only *L* ≈ 0.66, leaving the highlight range weakly exercised — 0.68 with a gentler grade reaches *L* ≈ 0.72.
- **⚠ `the_four_grading_goldens_differ_from_one_another` exists because four valid-looking PNGs prove nothing.** Each case varies exactly one axis, so any two rendering alike means that axis is inert.
- **⚠ The golden edit sets are looked up from one `grading_cases()` table, not written twice.** They were duplicated at first — between the four `golden_grade_*` tests and the clipping guard — which would have let the guard measure an image no reference contains.
- **⚠ `ColorGrading::default` is hand-written, not derived**, because `blending` is 50 at neutral and the container-level `#[serde(default)]` fills a missing field from it. A derived one gives every partial sidecar hard-edged ranges, silently. Same trap `RadialGradient::default` records; sabotaged and watched to fail with `left: 0.0, right: 50.0`.
- **⚠ `is_active()` is wheels-only** — `blending` and `balance` are modifiers, and **a hue with zero saturation is a direction with no magnitude**. The rule `NoiseReduction`, `Vignette` and `Sharpening` already follow.
- **Placement: tail, after `HslStage`, before the masks loop.** After the tone curve and local contrast is *forced* — the ranges are selected on lightness, so anything remapping lightness later would slide pixels between ranges. After HSL because HSL corrects colours that are *in* the picture while a grade is laid on top. Before masks because a mask "should act on the global look rather than be re-mapped by it"; the rejected alternative would let a masked +1 EV on a face push it into the highlight range and silently pick up the highlight tint.
- **⚠ Being per-pixel and position-independent bought a very small change**: no `Region`, no `RenderScale`, no kernel margin, **no `PrefixStepKey`**, and no golden movement. It lands in the existing **Color** group, so no new edit group and no new panel section either.
- **Measured** (X-T4 `.RAF`, 1600 px preview, release, best of 5): neutral 14.2 ms → **29.2 ms with a split tone, i.e. +15.0 ms** — against HSL's +34.9 ms, despite both doing an Oklab round trip. **The LUT is the whole difference**: one lookup instead of per-pixel `atan2`/`cos`/`sin` and band weighting.
- **Validated on a real X-T4 frame, bucketed by lightness rather than globally** (`cargo run --release -p app --example grade_probe <raw>`, kept). ⚠ **A whole-image mean is the wrong statistic and would have understated it badly** — a split tone pushes shadows one way and highlights the other, so they largely cancel in a global average; the same mistake the HueSatMap work nearly drew a wrong conclusion from. On DSCF6291 with all three buckets populated: **shadows −36.7, midtones +2.5, highlights +61.1** (R−B shift). Directions and magnitudes exactly as designed.
- **The widget is painted from scratch — egui has no colour wheel.** A triangle fan with per-vertex colours, centre neutral, rim at constant Oklab chroma; out-of-gamut hues clip at the rim, which honestly shows what is reachable. A segmented `Shad │ Mid │ High │ Global` selector follows the `HslChannel` pattern. Chosen over three 62 px wheels side by side and over a stacked list, from mockups drawn at **the panel's real 240 px** — at that width three-up leaves about 30 px of usable drag radius.
- **⚠⚠ THE DRAG PATH AND THE PAINT PATH COMPUTE THE ANGLE INDEPENDENTLY, and the sabotage step is what exposed it.** The drag reads `(-d.y).atan2(d.x)`; the paint writes `offset(hue, ..)`. Un-negating *one* of them leaves every hue **value** correct while the handle renders mirrored from the point you dragged to — and neither the angle test nor the drag test can see it alone. `the_handle_returns_to_where_you_dragged` is the round trip, and sabotaging each half separately fails it both times. Same shape as the `render_max_dim` and `fit_image_size` disagreements this project has already paid for twice.
- **⚠ `dragged_by(Primary)`, never bare `dragged()`.** egui reports a drag for **every** button, so a bare check would let the middle-drag pan binding rewrite the grade as it passed over the wheel — the trap the canvas-navigation work recorded. `a_non_primary_drag_leaves_the_wheel_alone` guards it, and its failure under sabotage is also **what proves the headless harness produces a genuine drag** rather than passing vacuously.
- **⚠ The hue is only re-read while off-centre** (`d.length() > 1.0`): at the exact centre `atan2(0, 0)` is 0, which would snap the hue to red every time saturation is dragged to zero.
- **⚠ Seven headless widget tests, and this is deliberately answering the standing complaint** that the suite "tests arithmetic well and interaction hardly at all". The `RawInput` three-pass harness — lay out, press, move — is the same one `clicking_a_segment_switches_the_channel` uses, and the same one whose single-pass version made `a_left_drag_does_not_pan` inert.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **masks carry no grading** — `LocalAdjustments` is per-pixel-only and a grade is global look; (2) **two Oklab round trips when HSL and grading are both active** — fusing them would break the `PipelineStage` seam that exists for the GPU port; (3) ~~**no visual feedback of the range boundaries** on canvas or histogram, so Blending and Balance are judged by their effect, as in Lightroom~~ ✅ **2026-09-08** — the panel strip (W3) and the grade range view, see its section; (4) **the tuning constants are starting values** — `SAT_MAX`/`LUM_MAX` 0.15, `SIGMA_MIN` 0.08, `SIGMA_MAX` 3.0, `BALANCE_SHIFT` 0.25 — and the vignette took three revisions before its feel was right; (5) out-of-gamut hues clip at the wheel's rim; (6) grading is **not** chroma-gated, unlike HSL — it *assigns* a hue rather than reading one, so there is no noisy hue to fade out, and tinting neutrals is the entire point of shadow toning.

**Colour management, piece C4: choosing a camera profile (2026-08-25).** Profiles are discovered from `<config>/rustroom/profiles/`, matched to the open photo's camera, and chosen from the Edit panel. 783 → **792 tests**; all 39 goldens byte-identical. **Colour management is complete with this.**

- **⚠⚠ RUSTROOM SHIPS NO PROFILES, AND THAT IS A DECISION.** Adobe's `.dcp` files are Adobe's; this project cannot make a legal determination about redistributing them and does not pretend to. RawTherapee bundling 162 of them is evidence about *their* risk assessment, not permission for ours. Discovering what the user already has sidesteps it entirely — and is independently better: no payload in a GPL-3.0 repo, nothing that goes stale when Adobe revises a profile. **Installation is dropping a `.dcp` into a folder.**
- **⚠⚠ MATCHING IS CONTAINMENT AT WORD BOUNDARIES, and plain containment is a real hazard.** libraw reports `"Fujifilm X-T4"` where the profile is `"FUJIFILM X-T4"`, so equality finds nothing — but plain containment matches `"X-T3"` against `"FUJIFILM X-T30"`, silently applying a different body's profile. Requiring a non-alphanumeric character (or the string's end) on both sides handles the make prefix *and* multi-word models like `"EOS 5D Mark IV"`. Verified on the real data: `"Fujifilm X-T4"` matches, case difference and all.
- **⚠ A test asserted something the rule cannot deliver, and the honest fix was to document the behaviour rather than force a fragile rule.** `"EOS 5D"` *does* match `"Canon EOS 5D Mark IV"` — both boundaries are spaces, and separating them needs an alias table encoding that "Mark IV" continues the model. **It is accepted because the match only ORDERS, never filters, and never auto-selects**: the default stays Built-in, so the worst case is a profile listed too high, not a photo rendered through the wrong profile. `a_model_that_is_a_whole_word_prefix_does_match_and_that_is_accepted` records that, and says where to start if matching ever begins selecting.
- **⚠ No alias table, deliberately.** RawTherapee ships `camera_model_aliases.json` because it must match hundreds of bodies unattended; we can be wrong and let the user pick by hand. A table we cannot test against real files for every camera is a liability.
- **⚠ Per CAMERA, not per photo** — keyed by `Session::make_model`. A profile is a calibration of the body, and changing it re-decodes exactly as `view_demosaic` does. ⚠⚠ **Per-photo is a recorded follow-up, not a rejected idea** — it is what Lightroom does in its Calibration panel, and it becomes wanted the moment someone holds both "Camera Velvia" and "Adobe Standard" for one body. It would put a *decode* parameter into `Edits`, which must then travel through the sidecar, presets, copy/paste, sync **and the buffer-cache key** — that key is where the work is.
- **⚠ `BufferCache` gained `clear()`, and it is load-bearing.** Every cached buffer was decoded with the old profile, so serving any of them looks exactly like the setting not working. Cleared wholesale rather than per entry because a folder of one camera's files is the normal case.
- **⚠ The profile is CAPTURED by both decode workers, not passed per request** — `DecodeWorker`'s callers have no business knowing about it, and rebuilding the worker is what makes a change take effect. Same shape `full_res_worker(tier, ctx)` already used. Dropping the old worker disconnects its channel, so an in-flight decode discards its own result.
- **⚠ The profile list is cached when a photo opens, never scanned in the panel body** — that body runs sixty times a second, and a directory scan there is the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake in a different costume.
- **⚠ The dropdown is drawn OUTSIDE the session borrow**, like `presets::show`, because it needs `&mut RustroomApp` for the settings and the re-decode — not disjoint from `&mut app.session`.
- **⚠ "Built-in (libraw)" is a real choice, not a fallback label.** It selects C1's matrix, the well-tested path and the oracle C2 and C3 are measured against; someone who dislikes a profile must be able to get back to it.
- **⚠ Seventh instance of the binary-crate dead-code trap.** `apply_camera_profile` is a method on a bin-crate type with no caller until the dropdown exists, so the decode wiring and the UI had to land in one commit.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no bundled profiles**; (2) **per-camera only** — the follow-up above; (3) a mixed-camera batch export uses the *open* photo's profile for every file, since the export worker captures one; (4) no alias table, so an unusual model string means picking by hand; (5) RAW only — a JPEG has no camera profile; (6) unchanged from C2/C3: no embedded DNG profiles, no `CameraCalibration`, no `ProfileToneCurve`, no `LookTable`, and no independent oracle.

**Colour management, piece C3: the profile's HueSatMap (2026-08-25).** The dual-illuminant hue/saturation table is applied after C2's matrix. 770 → **783 tests**; all 39 goldens byte-identical.

- **⚠⚠ THE SCOPE CAME FROM INSPECTING REAL PROFILES, NOT THE DNG SPEC'S CONTENTS.** C3 was scoped as "HueSatMap + LookTable + ProfileToneCurve". In `FUJIFILM X-T4.dcp`: the HueSatMap is present (90×30×1, dual-illuminant), the **LookTable is absent**, and the **ProfileToneCurve is `[0,0,1,1]` — the identity**. So for this camera the HueSatMap *is* all of C3. (`FUJIFILM X-T1.dcp` does have a LookTable, so other bodies differ.)
- **⚠⚠ `ProfileToneCurve` IS DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, and it is an architectural reason not an effort one.** It is Adobe's *display* rendering. Rustroom's decode produces **scene-referred linear**, and every downstream stage is tuned against linear luminance — `local_contrast`'s `MID_GRAY = 0.18`, `sharpening`'s `LUMA_EPS`/`GAIN_MAX`/`HALO_KNEE`, `dehaze`'s airlight percentile. Baking a display curve into the decode would leave that buffer non-linear while every constant still assumed otherwise: invisible per stage, wrong everywhere. It is the identity here anyway.
- **⚠ `ProfileLookTable` is deferred for the ordering, not the effort.** The spec applies it *after* the tone curve; with no tone curve it would land in the wrong space, which is worse than not applying it. It is also absent from the X-T4 profile, so it could not be tested on the files this project validates against. The machinery is shared — a LookTable is the same 3-D HSV table.
- **⚠⚠ "MEAN DIFFERENCE" WAS THE WRONG STATISTIC, and reaching for it nearly produced a wrong conclusion.** Adding an 11°-mean-hue-shift table made the whole-image mean |diff| vs libraw *fall* (0.00175 → 0.00145), which reads as "it is not being applied". It is: **64.5% of this frame sits below saturation 0.2, where the table is the identity by construction**. Split by saturation, the effect is **2.4× stronger on the saturated 20.1%** (0.00352 vs 0.00145), with a peak of **0.148 = 38/255**. Probe colours confirm it directly: green −22° hue, skin-tone saturation 0.333 → 0.419.
- **⚠ `v` is `max(r,g,b)` and is NEVER clamped.** The table *index* clamps at the top while the scale applies to the real value — clamping `v` itself would drag every highlight above 1.0 back to the table's edge and quietly compress the headroom piece A's wide working space exists to preserve. `a_value_above_one_keeps_its_headroom` guards it; sabotaging the clamp fails four tests.
- **⚠ Hue WRAPS, saturation and value CLAMP.** Hue is circular, so a clamped index puts a discontinuity at 360° — a visible seam on magentas, exactly where the wrap sits. `the_hue_axis_wraps_with_no_seam_at_zero` catches it.
- **⚠ One illuminant weight, two consumers.** The matrices and the table are interpolated on the same weight C2 computes from the camera neutral; two weights would let the colour transform and the look disagree about the scene's light.
- **⚠ The table is blended ONCE and applied in ONE pass.** Blending per pixel would be 2700 multiplies per pixel for a constant; a second `map_pixels` would be a second traversal of ~313 MB at full resolution.
- **⚠ A THIRD test that could not detect what it named.** The parser's dims/length guard was untested — real profiles are always self-consistent, so removing the guard left everything green. Fixed with a **synthetic** DCP built in the test (dims 4×4×1, three entries), plus its mirror image asserting a consistent table is still accepted. ⚠ **An unreachable guard is an untested one**, and the only way to reach this one is to construct the malformed file.
- **⚠ Neutrals are unmoved by construction** — saturation is 0, so the scale multiplies zero. Tested anyway: if a grey ever moves, the HSV round trip is wrong, not the table.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) still opt-in — nothing in the GUI sets `camera_profile`, which is C4; (2) **no tone curve, no LookTable**, for the reasons above; (3) the **oracle is still unavailable** (`rawtherapee-cli` exit 133), so this is judged by eye and by construction, not diffed against a reference implementation; (4) `CameraCalibration` still unparsed; (5) the 3-D table path has a unit test but no real profile here exercises it.

**Colour management, piece C2: a DCP's matrices (2026-08-25).** A new `camera-profile` crate parses `.dcp` files, and `raw-decode` renders through the profile's dual-illuminant `ForwardMatrix` when given one. 758 → **770 tests**; all 39 goldens byte-identical.

- **⚠⚠ OPT-IN, AND THAT IS THE SAFETY DESIGN.** `DecodeOptions.camera_profile: Option<PathBuf>` defaults to `None` = C1's libraw matrix, so **no existing render changed** and the C1 oracle stays live for comparison. Discovery and the UI are C4.
- **⚠⚠ THE INTENDED ORACLE WAS NOT AVAILABLE, and this is the most important caveat.** The spec leaned on `rawtherapee-cli` as an independent DCP implementation; it is installed but **crashes on this machine — exit 133 (SIGTRAP), no output, empty log**. C2 therefore ships with weaker validation than planned, which is why `camera_profile` stays off by default — the plan required exactly that in this situation.
- **⚠⚠ WHAT REPLACED IT, AND WHY A NEUTRAL TEST COULD NEVER HAVE DONE THE JOB.** Both ForwardMatrices have rows summing to D50 white by construction, so **they agree on a grey to 1e-4** — an inverted illuminant choice is *completely invisible* on neutrals. On coloured content they differ by **0.020–0.034**. Our render differs from libraw's by a mean of **0.0018**, an order of magnitude less, so the daylight matrix was selected correctly. `the_illuminant_choice_is_invisible_on_neutrals_and_visible_on_colour` pins that reasoning so nobody mistakes the neutral test for verification.
- **⚠⚠ `ForwardMatrix` rows sum to the D50 white point `(0.9642, 1.0, 0.8249)` — the DNG spec's own constraint, and the strongest parser self-check available.** A transposed read or a mis-parsed rational fails it. Verified by sabotage.
- **⚠ DNG interpolates in RECIPROCAL temperature (mireds), not linearly in kelvin.** Linear-in-kelvin agrees at both endpoints and is visibly wrong in between — the worst way to be wrong, and why the test probes the middle. Between 2856 K and 5003 K the mired midpoint is ~3635 K, not 3930 K.
- **⚠ No chromatic adaptation after the ForwardMatrix, and that is not an omission.** It outputs XYZ **D50** and ProPhoto *is* a D50 space. `XYZ_D50_TO_PROPHOTO` was derived and verified to map D50 white to exactly `(1, 1, 1)`; a Bradford step here would be a second, wrong adaptation.
- **⚠ The camera neutral is `1/cam_mul`, not `cam_mul`.** The multipliers scale channels *up* to make a neutral object neutral, so the object's own camera reading is the reciprocal. Getting this backwards solves for the inverse temperature. Measured on the X-T4 file: the neutral solves to **5165 K**, just above the D50 calibration point, so the weight clamps to `ForwardMatrix2` — correct for a daylight frame.
- **⚠ The temperature solve is iterative and uses McCamy's approximation** (~2% over the range profiles use), with a fixed 30-pass bound rather than a convergence loop that a malformed profile could hang. The 2% moves the blend weight far less than the gap between the two matrices.
- **⚠ A TEST THAT COULD NOT DETECT WHAT IT NAMED — the fourteenth instance.** `a_file_that_is_not_a_profile_is_refused` fed `b"not a dcp at all"`, which is rejected by the **byte-order** check before the magic number is ever read — so deleting the magic check left every test green. `a_valid_byte_order_with_the_wrong_magic_is_refused` builds a file starting `II` with magic 99, and catches it.
- **⚠ `DecodeOptions` lost `Copy`** — a `PathBuf` cannot be `Copy`. Zero call sites broke, unlike `Mask`'s equivalent loss for the brush.
- **⚠ A malformed or ForwardMatrix-less profile falls back to libraw's matrix rather than failing the decode.** A photo that renders slightly differently beats a photo that does not open, and the fallback is the well-tested C1 path.
- **⚠ `CameraCalibration` is neither parsed nor applied.** The X-T4 profile has none; a profile carrying one renders as though it were the identity. Recorded rather than silently mis-rendered.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no `ProfileHueSatMap`, `ProfileLookTable` or `ProfileToneCurve`** — C3, and the visible half; (2) no discovery, UI or bundling — C4, with the licensing question about Adobe's files; (3) no embedded DNG profiles, only a `.dcp` the caller names; (4) the DCP tests are gated on `DCP_FIXTURE` and cannot run in CI; (5) **the interpolation is bounded, not independently verified** — see the oracle note above.

**Colour management, piece C1: the camera input transform is ours (2026-08-25).** libraw is asked for camera-native RGB and `raw-decode` applies camera→ProPhoto itself. 750 → **758 tests**; all 39 goldens byte-identical; **no visible change, by design**.

- **⚠⚠ C IS FOUR SLICES AND C1 HAS NO PAYOFF ON ITS OWN — that is not a failure.** libraw's `cam_xyz` is derived from Adobe's own `ColorMatrix2`, so applying it ourselves produces the same image. The visible half of camera profiles ("Adobe Standard" vs "Camera Velvia") lives in **C3**'s `ProfileHueSatMap`/`ProfileLookTable`. C1's acceptance criterion is *"the render is unchanged"*.
- **⚠⚠ THE PROBE DECIDED THE IMPLEMENTATION, AND IT WAS NOT WHAT THE PLAN GUESSED.** `examples/color_probe.rs` prints libraw's colour data: **`rgb_cam` comes out identical at `output_color` 0 and 4**, with every row summing to exactly 1.0. That identifies it — `rgb_cam` is camera → linear **sRGB**, already normalised, because dcraw applies the output space *separately* (`out_cam = out_rgb[output] × rgb_cam`). So the transform is `rgb_cam` then our own `SRGB_TO_PROPHOTO`, and no derivation from `cam_xyz` was needed at all. **The row-normalisation and illuminant questions the spec worried about evaporated** because libraw had already answered them.
- **⚠⚠ THE ACCEPTANCE TEST FAILED FIRST, AND THE FAILURE WAS AN IMPROVEMENT.** Raw comparison gave **max 0.254 with mean 0.000017** — a near-zero mean rules out a cast, so a few pixels differed a lot.
